# Appendix B — Findings register (detail)

Companion to [BLUEY_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-09-28.md](../BLUEY_DEEP_AUDIT_2026-09-28.md). One entry per distinct issue; IDs are stable.

### Credentials & Keychain

#### CRIT-001

**Ad-hoc-signed Latest/Nightly auto-updates give Bluey a new code identity each time, so every Keychain item asks for the login password after each update**  
Severity **Critical** · confidence verified · effort L · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Partially implemented** — WS-A: Each update still gets a new code identity, but it now costs far fewer prompts. The Keychain backend reads attributes only (secrets/backend.rs, secrets/keychain.rs) and caches values in the process (secrets/mod.rs), so there is at most one decrypting read per item per process and none at boot: accounts and auth restore only continue when the item is Present (fix-pass commits 1cdb252, 2162c4e). A locked item shows up as Locked, with a deliberate Allow access (commit dbcb485). Opt-in stable signing for local builds uses BLUEY_LOCAL_SIGNING_IDENTITY in scripts/release.sh (718f0f8). Developer ID signing of Latest/Nightly updates is a release decision and is out of scope here.

- **User impact:** After every auto-installed update (nightly updates can arrive daily), the first access to each Bluey item (each provider key, the Clerk session, each subscription account) shows a login-password dialog. On this Mac that is at least 6-8 dialogs per update. Together with findings no-secret-cache-and-data-read-has and token-rewrite-every-request-and-boot, the dialogs repeat many times.
- **Root cause:** keyring 3 apple-native stores items in the legacy file-based keychain, where access is authorized by the code identity recorded in the item's ACL and partition list. An ad-hoc signature's identity is its cdhash, which changes with every build. Bluey ships only ad-hoc builds and auto-updates them.
- **Evidence:** scripts/release.sh:100-104: sets APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- (ad-hoc) for the developer/nightly path; .github/workflows/nightly.yml builds through release.sh; docs/RELEASING.md:194 'ad-hoc Apple signature, no Apple secrets'; gh releases v0.1.0-v0.1.2 and nightly are labelled 'unsigned build' ('no Apple Developer Program membership yet'); UPDATES.md:54 checks 30 s after launch and every 6 h, then auto-installs; Apple securityd clientid.cpp:187-288 partitionIdForProcess: Developer ID / Apple Development / App Store -> 'teamid:<TEAM>'; unsigned -> 'unsigned:'; any other signed code, INCLUDING ad-hoc and self-signed -> 'cdhash:<hash>' (per build); TN3127: 'Ad hoc signed code ... has a DR but it's tied to that specific version of the code'; securityd acls.cpp:111-200 validate/validatePartition: a partition mismatch on a read op -> extendPartition :204-240 -> KeychainPromptAclSubject.validateExplicitly = the 'enter the login keychain password' (XARA) dialog; /tmp/bluey-audit/kc-probe/FINDINGS.md: an ad-hoc rebuild reading an item created by the previous build -> -25293 errSecAuthFailed (the prompt); an Apple Development-signed rebuild -> 0 (no prompt); bluey_acl_raw.txt (ACL dump, no values): trusted-app lists on provider:openai/gemini and auth:clerk items keep accumulating /Applications/Bluey.app cdhash entries (44be.., 5ac2.., 9e4a..) plus target/debug entries: one entry per build a user clicked 'Always Allow' for; `docs/SECURITY.md and docs/RELEASING.md say nothing about Keychain prompts after updates`
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Product/infra: sign every published channel (Latest and Nightly) with a Developer ID Application certificate and notarize. The partition becomes teamid:<TEAM> and the DR stays stable across updates, so there are no per-update prompts. Add a CI guard so a publish job fails when `codesign -dv` shows no TeamIdentifier (once credentials exist). (2) One-time migration on the first team-signed launch: for each Bluey item, read it (one prompt per item, or one for a vault, see vault-consolidation), then do an attribute-only delete plus a fresh add so the item is owned by the stable identity. (3) Until membership exists, ship the code mitigations (cache, attribute-only has, write-only-when-changed, delete+add, vault) so an update costs one prompt, and document in UPDATES.md/SECURITY.md that ad-hoc updates require one 'Always Allow' click. Consider whether auto-install of nightlies should stay on by default while builds are ad-hoc. Do not weaken ACLs, do not add a plaintext fallback, and do not use env workarounds.
- **Test plan:** Manual macOS QA matrix rows U1-U4 (install build A, store keys, install ad-hoc build B: count dialogs; repeat with Developer ID builds A'/B': expect zero). While testing, watch `log stream --predicate 'subsystem == "com.apple.securityd" AND category == "integrity"'` for 'ACL partition mismatch' / 'asking user about XARA partition'. CI: a release-workflow assertion on TeamIdentifier for signed channels.
- **Verification (confirmed):** scripts/release.sh:100-104 falls back to APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- when no identity is set. nightly.yml:55/96 builds through that path. `gh release list` shows v0.1.0 to v0.1.2 as 'unsigned build' and the nightly as a pre-release. The v0.1.2 body says auto-update is on by default. The kc-probe ran without user interaction: an ad-hoc rebuild's data read of an item created by the previous build returns -25293, and an Apple Development rebuild returns 0. That is the primary cause of the per-update prompts: one dialog per item, per build, for every user, and the nightly can update daily. It is rated Critical because of its reach and because, combined with keychain-errors-collapsed-to-absent, one Cancel disables features. It is not a security or data-loss bug. The only fix that works is Developer ID signing. The code mitigations only reduce the number of prompts. — Lead override: first verifier rated Critical; the refuter lowered it to High only because it is not data loss. Every user of either channel hits it on every update, it is the user-reported top issue, and together with MAC-003 it silently breaks core features after updates.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). I couldn't refute this. Every piece of evidence checks out, and nothing in the code prevents the prompts.

- **Ad-hoc signing:** scripts/release.sh:100-104 sets APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- when no identity is configured. .github/workflows/nightly.yml:55 notes "ad-hoc Apple signature (no Apple secrets)", sets PUBLISH_RELEASE="false" at line 69 and builds through release.sh at line 96. It still publishes a nightly latest.json through scripts/release/nightly.py publish (nightly.yml:140). The Developer ID gate in scripts/release/verify_macos.py:48-52 (TeamIdentifier, "Developer ID Application", no Signature=adhoc) only runs when PUBLISH_RELEASE=true (release.sh ~line 122). It never runs for nightly.
- **Stable updates:** The Latest feed (tauri.conf.json:115, releases/latest/download/latest.json) currently resolves to v0.1.2 "unsigned build". Its assets include latest.json and the .app.tar.gz/.sig updater bundles, so ad-hoc builds are being delivered as auto-updates today.
- **Auto-install:** src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:7 says automatic updates are on by default, lines 192-195 install without asking, and line 35 sets a 6 h check interval.
- **Every access reads the secret:** SecretsStore::has_sync (src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:132-133) calls get_sync, which calls get_password, which reads the data and can prompt. set_sync (line 142) and delete_sync (line 150) go through keyring and security-framework paths that also read the data first, per the probe's library facts. There is no cache, no read that fetches only attributes, and no "write only when changed" guard.
- **Other apps' items:** src-tauri/src/accounts/claude.rs:302-303 and antigravity.rs:451-453 read items owned by other apps. With an ad-hoc build, an "Always Allow" on those only trusts that one cdhash, so it expires at the next update too.
- **Probe:** /tmp/bluey-audit/kc-probe/FINDINGS.md shows an ad-hoc rebuild gets -25293 on data reads of items made by the previous build, while an Apple Development rebuild gets 0.

**One mitigation, which narrows but does not refute:** release.yml's publish path requires Developer ID (verify_macos.py:48-52). If maintainers use it for the next stable release, Latest-channel users hit one migration prompt per item and are then stable. The Nightly channel stays ad-hoc for good and re-prompts on every nightly. v0.1.0-0.1.2 were published unsigned even though release.sh:103 prints "NOT eligible for publication", so the gate has already been bypassed in practice.

**Why High rather than Critical:** Nothing is lost and nothing is exposed. A denied prompt surfaces as a storage error, or as has()=false ("not configured"). There is no security hole. The damage is repeated login-password prompts after each update, one per Keychain item, plus features that look unconfigured until the user allows access. That is a significant UX and trust problem hitting every current user on the default settings, but it is not data loss or a vulnerability. Fix critique: The main fix is right: sign every published channel with Developer ID, which gives a teamid partition and a stable designated requirement, and add a CI guard on TeamIdentifier. Two gaps:

1. **The guard must cover nightly.** Today verify_macos.py runs only when PUBLISH_RELEASE=true, and nightly.py publish has no signature check. Put the check inside nightly.py publish, or run verify on the nightly artifacts, so an ad-hoc bundle can't reach any latest.json feed. Also block manual `gh release` uploads of ad-hoc builds to Latest. That is how v0.1.x shipped.
2. **The move to Developer ID still costs a one-time migration prompt per existing item** (probe row 4). Plan for it: on the first signed launch, read each item once, then delete it by attributes only and re-add it so the new identity owns it. Tell users one set of prompts is expected.

The interim mitigations are sound, with these adjustments:

- **has() by attributes only:** Needs a direct Security.framework call or kSecReturnAttributes through SecItem, not keyring's get_password.
- **Never modify in place:** The probe showed an untrusted build that modifies data in place locks the item for everyone, the creator included. So set must be "delete by attributes, then add", never keyring's set_password (which finds with data and then modifies).
- **Delete:** keyring's delete_credential also reads data first, so it has to be replaced too.
- **Session cache:** Needs a clear invalidation rule when Settings changes a key.
- **Tri-state errors:** Needed so a denied prompt (-25293 or user cancel) isn't read as "no key", which would lead the UI to ask the user to re-enter the key and overwrite a good item.
- **Third-party items:** For the Claude Code and Antigravity items Bluey doesn't own, none of these avoid a prompt on ad-hoc builds. Only a stable signature helps.
- **Nightly auto-install:** Turning it off by default while builds are ad-hoc is reasonable. Stable matters more because it is the default channel.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/adhoc-updates-reprompt-every-item)_

#### PERF-001

**No in-process secret cache, and has() is a full decrypting read: provider keys are re-read on every AI request, every settings save and every AI-tab open**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-A: SecretsStore has an in-process Zeroizing value cache plus attribute-only presence, so has() never decrypts and provider keys are read at most once per process (src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs, 79ff256).

- **User impact:** Whenever the running build is not yet trusted for an item, and permanently for users who click 'Allow' rather than 'Always Allow', each of these reads shows the password dialog. That means N dialogs per settings toggle, one per ask, and one per live suggestion. This is the most direct cause of 'Bluey keeps asking for my password'.
- **Root cause:** SecretsStore is a thin pass-through to keyring. Existence checks decrypt the item, and callers re-query instead of relying on state that is already known.
- **Evidence:** secrets/mod.rs:113 entry() creates a new keyring::Entry per call; :132-134 has_sync = matches!(get_sync(..), Ok(Some(_))); :168 async has = get().is_some(); keyring-3.6.3 macos.rs:79-83 get_password -> find_generic_password with data (the ACL-gated decrypt); ai/mod.rs:194-197 adapter_for: secrets.get(provider_key) for every model call (ask, live suggestion via engine.ts:664 prepare -> runPipeline, classify refine, embeddings, summary, test_connection, list_models); settings/mod.rs:150-156 replace(): has_sync for every provider on every settings_update (settingsStore.ts:35: any toggle); :38 at boot; :160-176 after every secrets_set; SecretKeyField.tsx:41-58 has() per mounted field; ProviderCard.tsx:196 (one per provider) + AITab.tsx:451-464 (Exa, Firecrawl, Anthropic), even though ProviderCard already receives provider.hasApiKey; auth/mod.rs:137 + :141: about 5 Clerk data reads per boot (has_stored_session, restore load, publish status, 3 WebViews via initStores.ts:71); research/mod.rs:125-133: 3 has() reads per research-eligible ask; :66/:99 a get per search/scrape; securityd acl_keychain.cpp:270-277: only 'Always Allow' (query.remember) is persisted; 'Allow' authorizes the single operation; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:118-133 `has_sync` = `matches!(self.get_sync(key), Ok(Some(_)))`, i.e. get_password (reads the secret data); keyring 3.6.3 apple-native uses legacy SecKeychain `find_generic_password` (~/.cargo/registry/.../keyring-3.6.3/src/macos.rs:31-33, 81); settings/mod.rs:150-156 replace() and :160-176 refresh_provider_keys() call has_sync for every provider on every settings write; app/mod.rs:274 has_stored_session at boot; auth status() on every window init; Release/nightly builds are ad-hoc signed (task facts; docs/RELEASING.md:194). An ad-hoc designated requirement is its cdhash, so each update is a new app to keychain ACLs (e.g. github.com/omesser/fidget/issues/283, github.com/snowflakedb/gosnowflake/issues/1847); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:194-198 `adapter_for`: `self.secrets.get(&provider_key(&config.id)).await?` for every non-OAuth, non-mock provider; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:77-79 `SecretsStore { service }` has no cache; :159-165 `get` = spawn_blocking keyring `get_password` (SecItemCopyMatching); Callers per ask: drive_provider (ai/mod.rs:591) and, when embeddings are on, embed (ai/mod.rs:723, via documents/mod.rs:141-147). Research availability adds 2–3 more reads (research/mod.rs:126-128, agent/mod.rs:99-118); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:606 request_sent is stamped after the read, so it shows up inside 'prompt built → request sent'
- **Proposed solution:** Inside SecretsStore (the Rust-side architecture stays the same): add `cache: Mutex<HashMap<String, CachedSecret>>` with the states Present(Zeroizing<String>) / PresentUnread / Absent. get(): return on a cache hit; on a miss, read the Keychain and cache Present/Absent; never cache errors. has(): return from the cache; on a miss use an attribute-only lookup (security_framework::item::ItemSearchOptions class generic_password + service + account + load_attributes(true), no load_data -> SecItemCopyMatching without kSecReturnData, which the probe showed never prompts) and cache PresentUnread/Absent. set(): write, then cache Present. delete(): delete, then cache Absent. Clear everything on data reset. At boot, run one attribute-only query with kSecMatchLimitAll for the service to fill every has_api_key flag and has_stored_session with zero prompts. SecretKeyField can use provider.hasApiKey instead of its own has() call. Log key CATEGORY + operation + OSStatus, never values.
- **Test plan:** Add a SecretBackend trait (see no-secret-backend-seam) with a counting fake. Assert: 10 adapter_for calls -> 1 backend read; 5 settings updates -> 0 data reads (the existence cache/attribute query only); secrets_set -> 1 write and 0 reads; delete invalidates the cache (the next get hits the backend). Boundary test: the real Tauri command secrets_has through the invoke layer returns a cached value without a backend call. Manual QA rows C1-C3 (click 'Allow' only, then ask 5 times: expect at most 1 dialog per item per process).
- **Depends on:** no-secret-backend-seam-for-access-count-tests
- **Verification (confirmed):** secrets/mod.rs:132-134 defines has_sync as matches!(get_sync) and :168 defines has as get().is_some(). keyring-3.6.3 macos.rs:79-83 get_password calls find_generic_password with data. Call sites: ai/mod.rs:195 (a get for every API-key request), settings/mod.rs:38/152/165, auth/mod.rs:137/141, research/mod.rs:126-127, and SecretKeyField.tsx:46 (a has() on every mount). Once the user clicks 'Always Allow', these reads no longer prompt. The amplification hits the untrusted window after each update, and any user who clicks plain 'Allow' gets one dialog per call. High is right.
- **Also reported as:** journey-onboarding-settings/keychain-has-reads-secret-data, performance-latency/api-key-keychain-read-per-request
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/no-secret-cache-and-data-read-has)_

#### SEC-001

**Account tokens are rewritten to the Keychain after every subscription-backed request, and Clerk tokens on every boot, even when unchanged. Each rewrite is a prompting read plus an in-place modify that resets the item's partition**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Account tokens are written only after a real refresh (credential_for with a refreshed flag, then persist_refreshed). Clerk tokens are written only on sign-in or refresh; restore never rewrites them (b106a65, a675eb7).

- **User impact:** Every ask on a ChatGPT/Claude/Google subscription does a Keychain write. After an update, or when the dev build and the installed app are both used, each write prompts. It then moves the item's partition to the writer, so the other build prompts on its next access, and the cycle repeats. A failed write is silently ignored (`let _`), so a token rotated by a refresh can be lost and the account later needs re-auth.
- **Root cause:** Persist-after-fetch is unconditional, and keyring 3's set path modifies the existing item in place, which requires decrypting it first.
- **Evidence:** accounts/mod.rs:781-790: on every Ok from credential_for, `if let Some(expires_at) = tokens.expires_at { ... let _ = self.secrets.set(&account_tokens_key(account_id), raw).await; }`, with no 'changed' check (the SQLite expires_at compare at :783 is not applied to the Keychain write); callers: ai/mod.rs:185 (every model call on a subscription provider), accounts/mod.rs:604 (catalog), :636 (probe), :834 (restore at boot); auth/mod.rs:541-545 restore -> store(tokens,&user) -> :495 secrets.set(CLERK_OAUTH_TOKENS_KEY) on every boot, even without a refresh; security-framework-3.7.0 passwords.rs:269-279: set_generic_password = find_generic_password WITH data (a prompt when untrusted), then item.set_password in place; if the find fails -> add_generic_password -> errSecDuplicateItem; kc-probe FINDINGS.md: 'After untrusted B modifies data in place: B read -25293 AND creator A read -25293 (item locked for everyone)'; ACL dump: account:chatgpt:oauth_tokens apps [5ac2,44be] but partition_id only cdhash:5ac2 after the 09-27 rewrite; account:antigravity and auth:clerk mdat identical to the second (a boot-time rewrite); src-tauri/src/accounts/mod.rs:780-791: after `cache.fresh(...)` succeeds, `if let Some(expires_at) = tokens.expires_at { ... if let Ok(raw) = serde_json::to_string(&tokens) { let _ = self.secrets.set(&account_tokens_key(account_id), raw).await; } }`; the write sits outside the `expires_at changed` check; src-tauri/crates/bluey-oauth/src/tokens.rs:114-115: `fresh` returns the cached token unchanged when it is not expiring, so most calls persist identical data; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:185 `adapter_for` → `credential_for` on every stream/embed/list call (ai/mod.rs:591, 723, 774, 817, 931), awaited before `rust.request_sent` (:606); src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:171-176 `set` = spawn_blocking keyring `set_password` (find + modify in the login keychain); src-tauri/src/accounts/mod.rs:779-790 on Ok(tokens) with expires_at: `let _ = self.set_account(updated).await;` and `let _ = self.secrets.set(&account_tokens_key(account_id), raw).await;`; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:136-145,172-178 SecretsStore::set = unconditional keychain set_password on the blocking pool (no compare with the current value); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:185 adapter_for -> credential_for runs before every subscription-backed stream (hot path, awaited)
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Have TokenCache::fresh report whether a refresh actually happened (or compare the returned TokenSet with the cached one) and call secrets.set only then. (2) In auth.restore, call store() (Keychain) only when the tokens were refreshed; still upsert the user row. (3) Implement set as attribute-only SecItemDelete followed by SecKeychainAddGenericPassword/SecItemAdd. Deletion needs no decrypt (the probe shows 0 with no prompt), and the new item is owned by the current build, so a partially trusted build can never lock the item for everyone. The value being written is already in memory, so nothing is exposed. (4) Log (never the value) when a token write fails, and surface it as account degradation instead of discarding it.
- **Test plan:** Counting fake backend: 20 credential_for calls with non-expiring tokens -> 0 writes; a forced refresh -> exactly 1 write; auth.restore with fresh tokens -> 0 writes, with expiring tokens -> 1. A backend write failure after a refresh -> the error is logged/propagated to account status. Manual: run the dev build and the installed app alternately (row D3): expect no repeated dialogs after the first Always Allow on each.
- **Verification (confirmed):** accounts/mod.rs:781-790 calls secrets.set whenever expires_at is Some, with no check for a changed value (only the SQLite write is compared). auth/mod.rs:541-545 calls store(), and with it secrets.set at :495, on every successful boot restore even when nothing was refreshed. security-framework-3.7.0 passwords.rs:269-279 set_generic_password does a find WITH data and then SecKeychainItemModifyAttributesAndData in place. The probe shows an in-place modify by an untrusted build locks the item for the creator too. The result is extra prompts, not data loss.
- **Also reported as:** performance-latency/oauth-keychain-write-per-request, error-handling-stubs/token-rotation-persist-silent
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/token-rewrite-every-request-and-boot)_

#### SEC-002

**Denied, cancelled or locked Keychain access is reported as 'no key' or 'not signed in': a single Cancel disables a provider or subscription for the whole session, and re-entering the key prompts again and can fail with a generic write error**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-A: Denied, interaction-not-allowed and unavailable statuses are no longer reported as missing: they map to storage.keychain_access_denied, storage.keychain_interaction_not_allowed and storage.keychain_unavailable, with present.ts copy. They also give the SecretState Locked. A locked account is not marked disconnected. Fix pass: restore never does a prompting read at boot, and Allow access reads only a locked item. Device check: Cancel the real prompt once and confirm the key field shows Locked with Allow access (not 'no key').

- **User impact:** A user who dismisses one dialog sees their provider as keyless, their subscription as broken, or is asked to sign in again. Their natural fix (re-entering the key or signing in) causes more dialogs, and the errors do not explain what happened. Support cannot diagnose it because no OSStatus is logged.
- **Root cause:** The error model has only Present/Absent/generic-error, and several call sites convert errors into Absent (has_sync, load_tokens) or cache them.
- **Evidence:** keyring-3.6.3 macos.rs:257-265 decode_error: -25293 errSecAuthFailed, -128 errSecUserCanceled, -25308 errSecInteractionNotAllowed -> PlatformFailure; secrets/mod.rs:124-129 any Err -> storage('failed to read from the keychain'); :132-134 has_sync -> false on any error; :141-145 set error -> 'failed to write to the keychain'. The OSStatus is never logged; settings/mod.rs:38/152/165 has_api_key=false on a denied read -> router.rs:256 provider unusable -> app/checks.rs:28 'no provider'; app/mod.rs:274 authenticated = !auth_required || has_stored_session() -> a denied read shows the sign-in gate although a valid session is stored; accounts/mod.rs:742-750 load_tokens `.ok().flatten()` -> :752-763 token_cache caches None for the process -> credential_for -> RefreshError::NoTokens -> 'the account has no stored sign-in' on every request while status stays Connected (cleared only by disconnect :547, reset_all :864, finish_connect :461, or a restart); SecretKeyField.tsx:41-58 on a has() error: setSaved(false) + error toast -> an empty field invites re-entry -> secrets_set -> set_generic_password finds WITH data (another prompt); if denied -> falls through to add -> errSecDuplicateItem -> 'failed to write to the keychain'; `auth/mod.rs:524: restore returns silently when load_tokens fails`; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:118-133 get_sync maps any non-NoEntry keyring error to Err; has_sync collapses Err into false; src-tauri/src/settings/mod.rs:150-153, 160-172 has_api_key recomputed from has_sync on every settings replace/refresh, so a denied or cancelled prompt flips providers to has_api_key=false; router.rs:255-257 provider_usable requires has_api_key → config.no_model 'No model assigned' (present.ts:75-78); auth/mod.rs:141 the same denial makes auth status() fail → AuthGate ConfigurationScreen (AuthGate.tsx:102); src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:121-133: get_sync returns Err on non-NoEntry errors; has_sync = matches!(…, Ok(Some(_))) → false; src-tauri/src/settings/mod.rs:160-176 refresh_provider_keys writes that false into provider.has_api_key and publishes settings.changed
- **Proposed solution:** Add a SecretAccessError { NotFound, Denied (-25293, -128), InteractionNotAllowed (-25308), Locked (-25292 etc.), Other(i32) }. Extract the code by downcasting keyring::Error::PlatformFailure's inner error to security_framework::base::Error::code() (or use the direct security-framework backend). Make has_sync/has return tri-state (Present/Absent/Unknown). Keep has_api_key true-ish ('locked') on Unknown, and let the boot gate treat Denied as 'session present, Keychain locked' rather than signed out. Never cache errors in token_cache. Show UI copy such as 'macOS blocked Bluey from reading this key. Choose Always Allow when asked. [Retry]'. In set, do not fall through to add after a denied find (use delete+add, see token-rewrite). Log `tracing::warn!(category="provider_key", op="read", status=-25293)` with no key id suffix beyond the category and no value.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit tests with a fake backend returning each code: has_api_key becomes Unknown (not false); token_cache is not poisoned (the next call re-reads); the boot gate does not show sign-in on Denied; the set after a denied read returns Denied, not DuplicateItem. Boundary test: a Tauri command error payload carries a stable code (e.g. storage.keychain_denied) that the WebView maps to copy (vitest on SecretKeyField with the real event/command error shape). Manual QA rows E1-E4 (click Deny or Cancel at each prompt).
- **Depends on:** no-secret-backend-seam-for-access-count-tests
- **Verification (confirmed):** secrets/mod.rs:124-129 maps every error except NoEntry to a generic storage error, and has_sync (:133) turns an error into false. accounts/mod.rs:742-749 load_tokens discards the error with .ok().flatten(), and :752-763 caches the resulting None in token_cache for the whole process. app/mod.rs:274 turns a denied read into the sign-in gate. SecretKeyField.tsx:51-54 sets saved=false on a has() error, which invites the user to re-enter the key. auth/mod.rs:524 returns silently. One Cancel click leaves the subscription unusable until restart or reconnect.
- **Also reported as:** journey-onboarding-settings/keychain-deny-silently-unkeys-providers, testing-verification-debt/keychain-error-reads-as-missing-key
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/keychain-errors-collapsed-to-absent)_

#### PERF-004

**refresh_provider_keys holds the settings write lock across N blocking Keychain reads, so a pending dialog stalls every settings reader**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Provider-key presence comes from the cache or attribute probes, and the settings write lock is no longer held across Keychain I/O (settings/mod.rs, 79ff256).

- **User impact:** After saving a key while Bluey is untrusted for other items, a Keychain dialog can stay up. Until it is answered, every component that reads settings (AI adapter, HUD, audio) blocks, and the app looks frozen.
- **Root cause:** The flags are computed under the lock with a potentially interactive call. replace() already computes them before locking.
- **Evidence:** settings/mod.rs:160-176: `let mut settings = self.current.write();` then `self.secrets.has_sync(&provider_key(&provider.id))` per provider inside the lock; commands/settings.rs:37-43/51-56: async secrets_set/secrets_delete call the sync refresh_provider_keys on a tokio worker thread; `ai/mod.rs:200 `self.settings.get()` and other readers block on the parking_lot RwLock`
- **Proposed solution:** Compute the flags first (on spawn_blocking), then take the write lock and apply them. With the cache/attribute-only has this becomes a non-interactive O(1) lookup anyway.
- **Test plan:** A unit test with a fake backend that blocks has() for 500 ms: settings.get() on another thread returns within 50 ms during refresh_provider_keys.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed that settings/mod.rs:162-172 holds current.write() across has_sync calls. The scope is wider than reported: the async settings.update (mod.rs:57, via replace() at :66) and the async commands secrets_set and secrets_delete (commands/settings.rs:41/55) all run synchronous, possibly interactive Keychain reads directly on a tokio worker thread without spawn_blocking. While a dialog is open, each call stalls one runtime worker. The fix should also move replace() and refresh_provider_keys onto spawn_blocking, or onto the cache.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/settings-write-lock-held-across-keychain-reads)_

#### SEC-005

**Imported Claude Code sessions are refreshed by Bluey after the first expiry, although the docs promise 'read-only, no refresh'. Rotation would sign Claude Code out**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Rotating imports (Claude, ChatGPT) are never refreshed. Fix pass (1cdb252): the origin (import or browser) is stored per account under accounts:origin:<id>, and it is persisted before the Keychain write. If it cannot be persisted, the connect fails (Disconnected plus an AppError). An account with no origin record (connected by a pre-upgrade build) is treated as an import: it is never refreshed and it returns imported_session_expired. Only a Bluey browser sign-in is revoked on disconnect. refreshes_imported_session is now an allow-list (antigravity).

- **User impact:** A few hours after importing, the first Bluey request refreshes the shared Claude session. If Anthropic rotates refresh tokens (which the module's own comment asserts), Claude Code on the same Mac is signed out. Docs and UI tell the user this won't happen.
- **Root cause:** 'No refresh' is enforced only at import time. The account record does not remember that its tokens are borrowed.
- **Evidence:** docs/SECURITY.md:81-82: Claude import '... read-only, no refresh'; docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:191-193 '...without refreshing (token rotation would sign Claude Code out)'; accounts/claude.rs:5-7 says the same; accounts/claude.rs:468-509 refresh() posts the copied refresh_token to claude_code::TOKEN_URL and stores the new TokenSet; accounts/mod.rs:768-777 credential_for -> cache.fresh(..., profile.refresh) for every account; rg finds no import-origin/imported field in accounts/mod.rs; ChatGPT is documented honestly (SECURITY.md:77-80 shared session; AccountCard.tsx:196 tooltip); Antigravity (Google) refresh tokens are not expected to rotate
- **Proposed solution:** Product decision. Option A: store origin=imported on the account and, on expiry, do not refresh. Either re-import from Claude Code's item (one legitimate foreign prompt unless Always Allow) or set NeedsReauth with a 'Sign in with Claude in Bluey' CTA. Option B: keep refreshing, change the docs and the Import button copy to warn like ChatGPT does, and ideally offer 'sign in separately' as the default.
- **Test plan:** Rust: a counting fake ProviderProfile. An imported account with an expired token -> credential_for does not call refresh (option A) / calls it once (option B, docs updated). Manual: import from Claude Code, wait for expiry (or fake expires_at), ask, then run `claude` CLI: verify it is still signed in (row F3).
- **Verification (confirmed):** The claude.rs:386-390 import copies refresh_token into the TokenSet. Nothing marks the account as imported. accounts/mod.rs:768-777 credential_for calls profile.refresh, which at claude.rs:468-509 posts the copied refresh token. This contradicts claude.rs:5-7 and SECURITY.md:81-82. Whether Anthropic rotates refresh tokens is asserted only by the code comments; the audit did not verify it. This is an account and documentation integrity issue, not a cause of Keychain prompts.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/imported-claude-session-refreshed)_

#### DEBT-002

**`tauri dev` binaries are unsigned (Intel) or ad-hoc (Apple Silicon) and use the same Keychain service as the installed app, so they prompt on every rebuild and take items from the installed app**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-A: Debug builds use their own Keychain service, com.codewithabdul.bluey.dev (secrets/mod.rs). An opt-in dev-sign-runner plus src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml signs `tauri dev` binaries with a stable local identity (718f0f8). Documented in DEVELOPMENT.md and ADR 0011. Device check: With BLUEY_LOCAL_SIGNING_IDENTITY set, rebuild twice and confirm no new prompt for .dev items.

- **User impact:** The developer (the reporting user) gets a password dialog per item after every Rust rebuild. Because dev and prod rewrite shared items (see token-rewrite-every-request-and-boot), the installed app then prompts again. Every dev 'Always Allow' also leaves another trusted-app entry on production secrets.
- **Root cause:** There is no separation between the dev and prod secret namespaces, and there is no stable signing step for dev binaries.
- **Evidence:** storage/mod.rs:12 BUNDLE_ID used as the Keychain service (secrets/mod.rs:90) in both debug and release; `codesign -dv src-tauri/target/debug/bluey` -> 'code object is not signed at all'; the ACL dump lists several target/debug/bluey trusted entries ('requirement: none') and partition 'unsigned:' on production items; tauri.conf.json has no build.runner/signingIdentity; no .cargo/config.toml; tauri-cli dev = `cargo run` (runner receives the exe path, per cargo config docs); clientid.cpp:187-288: only Apple-CA team certs (Apple Development / Developer ID) get a stable 'teamid:' partition; self-signed certs get 'cdhash:' -> a self-signed local identity does NOT stop the rebuild prompts; kc-probe: Apple Development-signed rebuild reads with no prompt; 'Apple Development: ... (<PERSONAL-ID>)', Team <TEAM-ID> exists on this Mac
- **Proposed solution:** (1) `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` service `com.codewithabdul.bluey.dev`. env_import (app/env_import.rs:79-97) already seeds dev keys from .env.local, so the dev workflow keeps working and production items are never touched by dev builds. (2) Optional stable dev signing: src-tauri/.cargo/config.toml `[target.'cfg(target_os = "macos")'] runner = "scripts/dev-codesign-run.sh"` (resolved relative to src-tauri/). The script, when $BLUEY_DEV_SIGNING_IDENTITY is set and basename($1) == bluey, runs `codesign --force --sign "$BLUEY_DEV_SIGNING_IDENTITY" --identifier com.codewithabdul.bluey.dev "$1"`, then `exec "$@"`. It passes cargo test binaries through unchanged and is a no-op when unset. Document that the identity must be an Apple Development certificate (a free personal team works); a self-signed certificate is not enough.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit test: the debug-build service name differs from release. Manual rows D1-D3: rebuild twice with and without BLUEY_DEV_SIGNING_IDENTITY; run dev and installed app alternately; count dialogs.
- **Verification (confirmed):** storage/mod.rs:12 BUNDLE_ID is also the Keychain service (secrets/mod.rs:90), and there is no cfg(debug_assertions) switch. src-tauri/.cargo does not exist, so dev binaries have no signing runner. Only developers are affected directly. Because the dev app sets values with an in-place modify, a dev run can also leave the installed app's items prompting.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/dev-builds-share-keychain-namespace-unsigned)_

#### SEC-006

**Deleting a secret decrypts it first (prompt), and a failed delete aborts sign-out, so after an update, sign-out/disconnect/reset each prompt and a denial leaves secrets behind**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Delete is attribute-only and checks its status. Sign-out and disconnect revoke using in-memory tokens only (peek), and a failed delete does not abort them; the leftover item is reported in Saved credentials (a675eb7, b106a65). Fix pass: auth restore probes the item before reading, so boot never holds the io mutex behind a prompt (2162c4e).

- **User impact:** 'Reset all data' after an update can show one dialog per item. If the user denies any, the key stays in the Keychain while the UI suggests it was reset. Sign-out stops half-way on a denial.
- **Root cause:** The keyring 3 delete path needs a data read, and clear_session treats the Keychain delete as a precondition.
- **Evidence:** keyring-3.6.3 macos.rs:101-106 delete_credential -> find_generic_password WITH data, then item.delete(); auth/mod.rs:590 `self.secrets.delete(CLERK_OAUTH_TOKENS_KEY).await?;` returns early, before the local user state is cleared; :577 load_tokens read beforehand for revocation; commands/data.rs:62-80 reset deletes every provider key + Exa/Firecrawl/Agent/Clerk x2 + accounts.reset_all -> one decrypt per existing item; kc-probe: an attribute-only delete by an untrusted build returns 0 with no prompt; keyring 4 / apple-native-keyring-store 1.0.2 keychain.rs:87-96 switched delete to ItemSearchOptions.delete() (attribute-only)
- **Proposed solution:** Implement delete as attribute-only SecItemDelete (security-framework ItemSearchOptions ... .delete()), or adopt keyring 4's apple-native store for delete only. In clear_session, always clear local state, then surface a Keychain delete failure as a warning with the category and code.
- **Test plan:** Fake backend where delete fails: clear_session still clears the user row and returns a warning; reset reports the exact categories that failed. Manual row R1: reset after an update -> expect 0 dialogs.
- **Verification (confirmed):** keyring-3.6.3 macos.rs:101-106 delete_credential calls find_generic_password with data before item.delete(). auth/mod.rs:590 uses `?` before clearing the user state at :591-597. accounts/mod.rs:546 and data.rs:62-78 prompt once per item. The audit also missed that item.delete() discards the OSStatus from SecKeychainItemDelete (security-framework passwords.rs:81-85), so a failed delete after a successful find is reported as success. See the missed finding delete-status-discarded.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/delete-requires-data-read-and-signout-aborts)_

#### FEATURE-001

**There is no UI to remove a stored key, and deleting a provider leaves its key item behind**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: SecretKeyField has a tri-state and a confirmed Remove. Deleting a provider deletes its API key (settings side_effects, mock dropRemovedProviderKeys) (9630789, 8fe047a).

- **User impact:** Stale keys accumulate. If such a provider is still configured, it is read at boot and on every save, and it prompts because only an old build is trusted.
- **Root cause:** The backend supports delete but the product flow never uses it.
- **Evidence:** commands/settings.rs:51-56 secrets_delete exists; the src/lib/tauri/api.ts:191 delete wrapper has no caller; ACL dump: provider:azure-foundry:api_key and provider:provider_<custom-id> still present, trusted only for old build cdhash 44be; src-tauri/src/commands/settings.rs:51-56 secrets_delete validates the key, deletes it and refreshes provider flags; registered in lib.rs and wrapped at api.ts:191; `call_sites_v3.txt: 0 callers of bluey.secrets.delete outside api.ts/mock`; src/features/settings/SecretKeyField.tsx:61-71 save only; :84 shows 'Key saved ••••' + Replace; there is no remove action; The only way to erase a key is data_reset_all (commands/data.rs:60-77), which wipes everything
- **Proposed solution:** Add a 'Remove key' action to SecretKeyField, and delete provider:<id>:api_key when a custom provider is removed (attribute-only delete, no prompt).
- **Test plan:** vitest: the SecretKeyField remove action calls api.secrets.delete with the SECRET_KEYS key. Rust: provider removal deletes its key (counting fake).
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed that api.ts:191 secrets.delete has no UI caller (rg). The only backend deletes are data.rs, auth, accounts and commands/settings.rs:54. The severity is higher than reported: data.rs:63-68 'reset all data' deletes provider keys only for providers still in settings. The API key of a custom provider the user already removed survives a full data reset, so a promised wipe leaves a credential behind.
- **Also reported as:** surface-parity/secrets-delete-no-ui
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/no-ui-to-remove-provider-keys-orphans)_

#### DEBT-003

**Keychain delete reports success even when SecKeychainItemDelete fails**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: The new security-framework backend checks the status of every delete. A failure returns a storage.keychain_* error instead of Ok (secrets/keychain.rs).

- **Root cause:** keyring 3's macOS delete path calls a security-framework delete that returns () and drops the OSStatus.
- **Evidence:** ~/.cargo/registry/src/*/keyring-3.6.3/src/macos.rs:101-106 delete_credential: `item.delete(); Ok(())`; security-framework-3.7.0/src/os/macos/passwords.rs:81-85 `pub fn delete(self) { unsafe { SecKeychainItemDelete(..); } }`: the OSStatus is discarded; `src-tauri/Cargo.lock: keyring 3.6.3 depends on security-framework 3.7.0`; callers that trust the result: auth/mod.rs:590 (sign-out), accounts/mod.rs:546 (disconnect), commands/data.rs:62-78 (reset all data)
- **Proposed solution:** Delete with attribute-only SecItemDelete (security_framework ItemSearchOptions::delete(), which returns a Result) or with keyring 4's apple-native store, and check the status. After a reset or sign-out, confirm with an attribute-only lookup that the item is gone and report any leftover item.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (credentials-keychain/delete-status-discarded)_

#### DEBT-004

**'Reset all data' leaves API keys of removed providers in the Keychain**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Reset all data enumerates every item in Bluey's service (delete_all) instead of known keys only, so keys of removed providers go too (6b14460). Fix pass: reset also clears each account's origin record.

- **Root cause:** Reset enumerates keys from the current settings instead of from the Keychain service.
- **Evidence:** `commands/data.rs:62-68 iterates only `core.settings.get().ai.providers``; No code path deletes provider:<id>:api_key when a provider is removed (rg secrets.delete: only data.rs, auth:517/590, accounts:546, commands/settings.rs:54); The auditor's ACL dump lists provider:azure-foundry:api_key and provider:provider_<custom-id> still present
- **Proposed solution:** In reset, use an attribute-only kSecMatchLimitAll query on service com.codewithabdul.bluey to find every Bluey-owned account (validate_key allow-list) and delete each one. Also delete the key when a provider is removed.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (credentials-keychain/reset-misses-orphaned-provider-keys)_

#### DEBT-005

**Interactive Keychain reads run synchronously on tokio workers in async settings paths**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Keychain I/O runs on spawn_blocking (SecretsStore::blocking), and async settings paths use cached presence instead of synchronous reads (79ff256).

- **Root cause:** The has_api_key refresh uses has_sync from async contexts.
- **Evidence:** `settings/mod.rs:57-66 async update() -> replace() -> has_sync per provider (:150-153)`; commands/settings.rs:37-43/51-56 async secrets_set/secrets_delete -> sync refresh_provider_keys (:160-176); secrets/mod.rs:159-165 shows the intended pattern (spawn_blocking) that these paths bypass
- **Proposed solution:** Compute the flags in spawn_blocking (or from an in-memory cache or an attribute-only lookup) before taking the settings lock.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (credentials-keychain/sync-keychain-on-async-executor)_

#### TEST-013

**SecretsStore is hard-wired to keyring, so no test can assert how many Keychain reads/writes an action performs**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: KeychainBackend trait with CountingFake (secrets/backend.rs), injected through SecretsStore::with_backend, so tests can pin Read/Add/Remove/Probe counts per action.

- **User impact:** Regressions that add Keychain reads or writes on hot paths (like live suggestions in PR #45) are invisible in CI, and they show up to users as password dialogs.
- **Root cause:** There is no abstraction between SecretsStore policy (allow-list, caching, error mapping) and the platform backend.
- **Evidence:** `secrets/mod.rs tests :244-288 cover only the allow-list mirror`; `secrets/mod.rs:113 keyring::Entry::new is used directly; no trait/backend injection`; `PR #18 precedent: a mock-only transport let real-boundary failures ship`
- **Proposed solution:** `trait SecretBackend: Send + Sync { fn read(&self, key) -> Result<Option<Zeroizing<String>>, SecretAccessError>; fn exists(&self, key) -> Result<bool, _>; fn write(&self, key, value) -> Result<(), _>; fn remove(&self, key) -> Result<(), _>; fn list(&self) -> Result<Vec<String>, _>; }` with KeychainBackend (the production one) and a CountingFake (atomic counters per op and key, injectable errors). SecretsStore::with_backend for tests. Add per-action budget tests: boot, settings save, ask (API key), ask (subscription), live suggestion, research, listening start, sign-out.
- **Test plan:** The tests themselves are the deliverable. Keep them in src-tauri/src/secrets/tests.rs and in the managers' test modules, and run them through the existing AppCore constructors where possible, not only through the store.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Accurate: secrets/mod.rs:112-115 uses keyring::Entry directly and has no backend trait. This is test infrastructure with no direct user impact, so Low. It is still the recommended way to regression-test prompt budgets.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/no-secret-backend-seam-for-access-count-tests)_

#### UX-018

**Denying the macOS prompt during Claude/Antigravity import is reported as 'not signed in' / 'not found'**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-A: A denied prompt while reading the other app's item on import maps to account.import_denied ('macOS blocked Bluey from reading <App>'s sign-in — click Import again and choose Allow'), via accounts::read_foreign_secret and present.ts copy (79ff256). Device check: Click Import for Claude Code, deny the prompt, and confirm the copy.

- **User impact:** The user clicks Deny or Cancel on the foreign-item prompt (expected and legitimate) and is told the other app isn't signed in. They may sign in again in Claude Code or Antigravity for no reason.
- **Root cause:** Foreign reads discard the keyring error kind.
- **Evidence:** accounts/antigravity.rs:451-457 get_password(...).ok() -> 'Antigravity is not signed in on this Mac'; accounts/claude.rs:301-307 `if let Ok(..)` -> next candidate -> credentials file -> import_not_found
- **Proposed solution:** Map -25293/-128 to 'macOS blocked access to Claude Code's Keychain item. Click Import again and choose Allow' (reuse SecretAccessError). Keep NoEntry as 'not found'.
- **Test plan:** Unit test the mapping function with synthesized error codes. Manual row F1/F2: Deny during import.
- **Verification (confirmed):** antigravity.rs:450-457 `.ok()` on get_password turns a denial into 'not signed in'. claude.rs:301-306 `if let Ok` falls through to the file and then to import_not_found.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/foreign-import-denial-misreported)_

#### PERF-016

**Opportunity: consolidate Bluey-owned secrets into one Keychain item (vault) so an identity change costs one prompt instead of N**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — WS-A: Merging the secrets into a single vault item is a larger migration and a product decision. The cache, attribute-only probes and Allow access already cap the cost of an identity change at one prompt per used item. Recorded in ADR 0011.

- **User impact:** While builds stay ad-hoc, users would see a single 'Always Allow' dialog per update instead of one per stored secret.
- **Root cause:** The one-item-per-secret layout multiplies the per-identity cost.
- **Evidence:** Today there are 6-8 separate items (the ACL dump lists provider x4, auth:clerk, account:chatgpt, account:antigravity), and each needs its own dialog after every ad-hoc update; `securityd prompts per item (acls.cpp validate per ACL object)`
- **Proposed solution:** Store a JSON map in one generic-password item (account 'vault:v1'), guarded by a process mutex and written atomically (delete+add), and loaded once at boot into the cache. Migration: enumerate legacy items with an attribute-only query, read each (one-time prompts, or one per item if not yet trusted), write the vault, then delete the legacy items attribute-only. Costs: every write rewrites the whole vault, so it needs careful error handling; there is a larger blast radius if the item is corrupted (keep a version field and validate on load); the SECRET_KEYS allow-list still applies per logical key. Not needed once Developer ID signing ships. Do it after cache + write-only-when-changed.
- **Test plan:** Counting fake: migration reads each legacy key once, writes the vault once, deletes the legacy keys; a corrupted vault surfaces an error without wiping. Manual row U5: ad-hoc update with the vault -> exactly 1 dialog.
- **Depends on:** no-secret-cache-and-data-read-has, no-secret-backend-seam-for-access-count-tests
- **Verification (confirmed):** This is a valid design option: prompts are per item, which the probe and securityd per-ACL checks agree on. It is only worth doing if Developer ID signing stays unavailable for a long time. It adds atomicity and blast-radius risk.
- _Source: auditor (credentials-keychain/vault-consolidation)_

#### FEATURE-005

**Credential health: show which saved credentials this build can use silently, and repair them without guesswork**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence verified · effort M · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-A: Settings → Privacy → Saved credentials lists names and states (secrets_health, secrets/health.rs, SavedCredentials.tsx) with Allow access and Remove. Fix pass (dbcb485): secrets_allow_access goes through health::allow_access. It accepts only listed keys (WebView API keys, auth:clerk:oauth_tokens, account:<id>:oauth_tokens) and probes silently first. It does an interactive read only when the item is Locked; Present or Absent return with no read. The mock transport enforces the same list. SECURITY.md and the secrets module doc name the exception. Device check: Allow access on a locked item shows the macOS prompt once, and after Always Allow the row turns Present.

- **User impact:** After an ad-hoc update the user sees a string of password dialogs with no explanation, and after a Cancel, providers silently look keyless.
- **Root cause:** Bluey never tells the user which of its saved credentials need macOS approval, or why.
- **Evidence:** Phase 4 probe: an attribute-only lookup never prompts; a data read with user interaction disabled returns -25293 exactly when macOS would prompt; `No UI today explains a Keychain prompt; a denial reads as 'no key' (SEC-002)`
- **Proposed solution:** Settings > Privacy (or Advanced) gets a 'Saved credentials' list: category and provider name only, never values. Each row shows present, can be read silently, or needs approval, from a non-interactive probe (SecKeychainSetUserInteractionAllowed(false) on the native side). Actions: 'Allow access' (one interactive read, which caches the value and suggests Always Allow) and 'Remove'. The copy explains ad-hoc updates and Developer ID. Built on the new native store; no secret crosses IPC.
- **Test plan:** Rust: the counting fake reports a Locked state for denied items; command returns categories only (no values). UI test with the mock: rows render states and actions. Manual macOS QA row E2.
- **Depends on:** credentials-keychain/no-secret-backend-seam-for-access-count-tests
- **Verification (confirmed):** Added by the audit lead from direct measurement.
- _Source: audit lead (empirical) (credentials-keychain/credential-health-panel)_

### Security & privacy

#### DATA-001

**Every screen capture is written to ~/Library/Caches as a JPEG and never deleted, even with 'Store screenshots' off (default); Delete/Reset don't remove them**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: Frame files now follow a full lifecycle. FrameCache returns evicted frames and their files are deleted. A context snapshot that has OCR'd and inlined a frame releases its temp file (src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs). With Store screenshots on, the frame is first copied into <data dir>/screenshots (AppPaths.screenshots_dir) and the stored row points at that copy. data_delete_screenshots and data_reset_all empty both the frames and screenshots folders (Storage::clear_dir). The Swift helper TempFrames now deletes frames older than 10 minutes at startup and every 5 minutes (HelperApp.swift). Docs updated in HELPER_PROTOCOL.md. Device check: After a few captures with Store screenshots off, check that ~/Library/Caches/com.codewithabdul.bluey/frames empties. With it on, check that stored screenshots survive the helper's sweep.

- **User impact:** The docs and UI say screenshots are off by default and deletion really deletes. In reality every ⌘↵ leaves a full-screen JPEG in a folder any non-sandboxed process of the user can read, with no TCC protection. Files stay there for days while the app runs, and 'Delete screenshots' and 'Reset all' leave them in place. Users who opt in to storing screenshots silently lose them after a helper restart.
- **Root cause:** The helper always writes a file so OCR can work by path. No Rust path ever discards frames: cache eviction forgets the path, and no caller invokes the discard command. The 'stored' screenshot just reuses the temp path, and deletion code only knows DB-referenced paths.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/ScreenCaptureService.swift:267-275 — "The temp file is always written" to tempFrames.url(id:); src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/TempFrames.swift:3-4,30-44 — cleanupStale(maxAge: 3600) only; HelperApp.swift:42 calls it once at helper startup; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:31-34 — "the helper's temp files are its own concern (stale ones go at helper startup)"; FrameCache::insert (53-62) evicts map entries without deleting files; src/lib/tauri/api.ts:72 discardFrame → capture_discard_frame (capture/mod.rs:291-303) has no caller in src/ (grep); crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/snapshots.rs:36-39 image_path saved only when store_image; retention.rs:103-129 and snapshots.rs:88-100 unlink only DB-referenced image_paths; commands/data.rs:25-30,84-86; Local evidence (this Mac): ~/Library/Caches/com.codewithabdul.bluey/frames holds 46 files / 5.1 MB dated Sep 27 04:12-07:56, while settings privacy.storeScreenshots=0 and screen_snapshots has 3 rows with 0 image_path; With storeScreenshots=true the stored image_path points into the same temp dir, which cleanupStale later deletes, leaving dangling rows; ScreenCaptureService.swift finalize() L268-286: the temp file is always written, even when inline=true; capture/mod.rs:31-33 comment 'the helper's temp files are its own concern (stale ones go at helper startup)'; FrameCache::insert L55-62 evicts only the map entry; TempFrames.swift:29-31 cleanupStale(maxAge 3600) is called only at helper startup (HelperApp.swift:42); capture_discard_frame exists (commands/capture.rs:37) but the frontend never calls api.capture.discardFrame (only src/lib/tauri/api.ts:72); bluey-storage snapshots.rs:36-37 stores frame.path (the temp path) when store_screenshots=true, and the startup sweep later deletes those files; Live: ~/Library/Caches/com.codewithabdul.bluey/frames holds 49 entries (5.1 MB), oldest dated Sep 27 04:12 (more than 24 h old); Live: ~/Library/Caches/com.codewithabdul.bluey/frames holds 46 JPEGs (5.2 MB) from 2026-09-27 04:12-04:14, still present 1.3 days later while helper pid 6565 (started 04:12) runs; src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/TempFrames.swift:29-45 cleanupStale(maxAge 3600) is called only from HelperApp.swift:42 (startup); src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:32-35,55-63 FrameCache capacity 8 evicts `entries.remove(&oldest)`, which frees memory but never deletes the file; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:291-302 discard_frame deletes files, but `discardFrame` is only defined (src/lib/tauri/api.ts:72) and never called; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/snapshots.rs:36-37 `image_path = if store_image { frame.path.clone() }`, i.e. the temp file in Caches, which the next helper start deletes once it is older than 1 h; docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:17-18 promises 'Frames are written to ~/Library/Caches/…/frames/ and discarded after use'; commands/data.rs:25-30 data_delete_screenshots removes only DB-referenced paths, never frames_dir; src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/ScreenCaptureService.swift:266-276 'The temp file is always written' to ~/Library/Caches/<bundle>/frames; src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/TempFrames.swift:28-47 cleanupStale (files older than 1 h) runs only at helper startup (HelperApp.swift:42); src/lib/tauri/api.ts:72 discardFrame is defined but never called by the frontend; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:54-62 FrameCache eviction drops the entry without deleting the file; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/snapshots.rs:36-38 image_path = frame.path (the temp file); src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:247 is the only screen_snapshots writer; no reader anywhere; OCR is passed as None (L250); src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:200-210 'Keep screenshots — Store captured frames with sessions.'
- **Proposed solution:** (1) In CaptureManager: when FrameCache evicts an entry, and after build_snapshot has finished OCR and inlined the image, call the helper's capture.discard (or remove_file) unless store_screenshots is on. (2) When store_screenshots is on, copy the frame into an app-owned dir under data_dir/screenshots and store that path. (3) data_delete_screenshots and data_reset_all also empty paths.frames_dir. (4) The helper also runs cleanupStale on a timer (e.g. every 5 min with a 5-min maxAge), not only at startup. (5) Longer term, pass OCR the inline image bytes and skip the temp file when inline=true.
- **Test plan:** Rust integration test with a fake helper that writes real files into a temp frames dir: after 9 captures, fewer than or equal to 8 files remain (eviction deletes); after build_snapshot with store_screenshots=false, 0 files remain; data_delete_screenshots leaves the dir empty. Swift test: TempFrames periodic cleanup. On real macOS: count files in ~/Library/Caches/com.codewithabdul.bluey/frames before and after 5×⌘↵, after Delete screenshots, and after Reset.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ScreenCaptureService.swift:267-275 always writes the frame. TempFrames.cleanupStale (only files older than 1 h) runs once, at helper startup (HelperApp.swift:42). FrameCache::insert (capture/mod.rs:53-62) evicts entries without deleting the files. The only discard path is capture_discard_frame (capture/mod.rs:291), and it has no frontend caller: api.ts:72 defines discardFrame but nothing calls it outside the mock and type registry. snapshots.rs:36-39 stores frame.path (the temp path) when store_image is set, so the stored image can later be removed by cleanupStale. On this Mac the frames dir currently holds 46 files. This breaks the explicit 'store screenshots' setting, so High stands.
- **Also reported as:** capture-audio-transcription/screen-frames-never-discarded, native-macos-platform/temp-frames-never-deleted, data-sessions-history/frames-retained-keep-screenshots-dangling
- _Source: auditor (security-privacy/temp-frames-persist-despite-screenshots-off)_

#### SEC-003

**Privacy → 'Cloud AI' off does not stop live audio streaming to Gemini Live, document/query embeddings, boot re-embedding or recording uploads**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — Split across workstreams: D1 keeps live audio on the Mac when Cloud AI is off; E1 refuses stream/embed/transcribe_file with a privacy error in AiManager; FX2 extends the gate to research.

- **User impact:** A user who turns Cloud AI off expects nothing to leave the Mac. Microphone and system audio (the most sensitive data Bluey handles) keep streaming to Google whenever they listen, and resume/document text is still embedded by the cloud provider at import and at every launch.
- **Root cause:** The master switch is implemented as a TypeScript engine precondition. Rust managers that talk to cloud providers on their own (audio, documents, batch transcription) never consult it.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/cloud-gate.ts:21-28 — the gate is checked only in engine.ts:273 (runPipeline), 810 (classify), 825 (summarize); grep: privacy.cloud_ai_enabled is never read in src-tauri (only types/settings.rs:305,318); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:350-389 cloud_provider → GeminiLive whenever an enabled Gemini provider + Keychain key exist; default transcription_provider = GeminiLive (bluey-core types/settings.rs:191); src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:76 (embed on import), 141 (query embedding), 173/247 (re-embed) gated only by ai.embeddings_ready() (ai/mod.rs:789-800, no privacy check); `src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:405 documents.reembed_stale() at every boot`; src-tauri/src/commands/ai.rs:107-123 ai_transcribe_file → ai/mod.rs:731-770 uploads the recording to Gemini Files API, no privacy check; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:262-273 — 'Allow sending context to your configured cloud providers.'; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/settings.rs:305,318 is the only Rust occurrence of cloud_ai_enabled (definition and default); src/ai/cloud-gate.ts:22,26 is read only from src/ai/engine.ts:273 (ask), :810, :825 (summarize); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:350-380 cloud_provider(): Gemini Live is selected whenever an enabled Google provider has a key; there is no privacy check. The default provider is Gemini Live (settings.rs:189-191).; ai_stream / ai_transcribe_file (commands/ai.rs; ai/mod.rs:750+) and document embeddings in ai/mod.rs have no Rust gate; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:262-271 labels the switch 'Cloud AI — Allow sending context to your configured cloud providers.'; tests/integration/cloud-ai-gate.test.ts covers only the TS engine against the mock transport; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:262-270 — title "Cloud AI", description "Allow sending context to your configured cloud providers."; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/settings.rs:305,318 — cloud_ai_enabled defined/defaulted; rg over src-tauri/src finds zero consumers; src/ai/cloud-gate.ts:22-26 + src/ai/engine.ts:273,810,825 — the only enforcement, all inside the WebView engine; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:350-390 — cloud_provider(): GeminiLive branch builds GeminiLiveProvider::new(key) with no privacy check; GeminiLive is the default provider (settings.rs:191); src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:207 self.ai.embed(..); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:789-792 checks only embeddings_enabled; src-tauri/src/commands/ai.rs:105-108 ai_transcribe_file ← src/features/settings/session-import.ts:64, no gate
- **Proposed solution:** Enforce the switch in Rust, where the network calls happen. (1) AudioManager::cloud_provider returns None when cloud_ai_enabled=false, so audio falls back to Apple on-device Speech (already the fallback path). (2) AiManager::embeddings_ready() and the embed/transcribe_file/stream entry points return privacy.cloud_ai_disabled when the switch is off, and documents degrade to keyword retrieval. (3) Toggling the switch off stops an active cloud transcription session (settings side effect). (4) Optionally reflect the switch in research_search/scrape too. Update SECURITY.md.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit tests: cloud_provider(config) returns None for GeminiLive/CloudRealtime when cloud_ai_enabled=false; embeddings_ready() is false; transcribe_file returns privacy.cloud_ai_disabled. Side-effect test: flipping the switch while listening restarts audio on Apple. On real macOS: turn Cloud AI off, start listening, and confirm with nettop or Little Snitch that there is no connection to generativelanguage.googleapis.com.
- **Verification (confirmed):** cloud_ai_enabled has no reader anywhere in src-tauri (the only hits are the type definition and default, settings.rs:305,318). The TS gate is used only at engine.ts:273, 810 and 825. AudioManager::cloud_provider (audio/mod.rs:350-389) returns GeminiLive whenever a Gemini provider and key exist. Document embedding and reembed_stale (documents/mod.rs:76,141,173,248; app/mod.rs:405; settings/side_effects.rs:73) are gated only by embeddings_ready(). The PrivacyTab copy says the switch controls whether context is sent to cloud providers. The data goes to the user's own chosen provider, so this is High, not Critical. — Kept at High (security verifier): the data still goes only to the user's own configured provider. The prior-audit auditor's Critical rating for its duplicate is recorded but not adopted.
- **Also reported as:** surface-parity/cloud-ai-toggle-not-enforced-in-rust, prior-audit-docs-drift/cloud-ai-switch-rust-egress
- _Source: auditor (security-privacy/cloud-ai-switch-not-enforced-in-rust)_

#### SEC-004

**Privacy-mode note promises exclusion from ScreenCaptureKit/Zoom/Meet, but sharingType=.none is not honoured by ScreenCaptureKit on macOS 15+ (this Mac: macOS 26); native menus are never protected**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: CaptureProtection now reports a partial state on macOS 15+ or when the version is unknown (platform::parse_major_version reads sw_vers). PROTECTED_NOTE, the privacy tooltips, README, docs/SECURITY.md and an addendum to ADR 0006 now say protection hides Bluey from apps that honour macOS window protection (legacy capture), and that ScreenCaptureKit screen sharing on macOS 15+ may still show Bluey. NSMenu popups are never protected. set_content_protected is still called. The mock transport mirrors the partial field. Device check: On macOS 15+, confirm what Zoom, Meet and the macOS screenshot tools actually show with Privacy mode on, so the copy stays accurate.

- **User impact:** Users of an interview/meeting copilot may turn on Privacy mode and trust the in-app note that Bluey is hidden from screen shares. On macOS 15+ the HUD (and its dropdown menus) can be visible to the other party in Zoom, Meet, Teams or QuickTime.
- **Root cause:** The ADR was written against pre-Sequoia behaviour. The note text is static and never verified on the running OS.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:27-29 PROTECTED_NOTE: 'Bluey is excluded from most screen sharing and recording (ScreenCaptureKit and window-list capture)'; docs/adr/0006-privacy-mode-and-content-protection.md: 'excludes the panel from most screen-capture APIs (ScreenCaptureKit, CGWindowList-based capture used by Zoom/Meet/Teams/QuickTime)'; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:397-405 — only set_content_protected (NSWindow.sharingType) on webview windows; Apple Developer Forums thread 792152: sharingType .none windows still captured by ScreenCaptureKit on 15.4+, Apple: 'no public APIs for preventing screen capture'; tauri-apps/tauri#14200 (open, status: upstream); src-tauri/src/platform/hud_menu_macos.rs:12,95 — HUD menus are NSMenu popups (system windows, no sharingType); tray menu likewise; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:397-406 set_protection → `window.set_content_protected(enabled)` (NSWindow.sharingType); Apple docs: NSWindow.SharingType.none is 'A legacy constant that macOS no longer uses'; Apple Developer Forums thread 792152: on macOS 15.4+ SCK captures sharingType-none windows ('no public APIs for preventing screen capture'); tauri-apps/tauri#14200; src-tauri/src/platform/hud_menu_macos.rs:95,190 NSMenu popUpMenuPositioningItem: separate menu windows with no sharingType; documents/mod.rs:292 and commands/audio.rs:85 NSOpenPanel dialogs; this Mac runs macOS 26; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:27-29 PROTECTED_NOTE: "Bluey is excluded from most screen sharing and recording (ScreenCaptureKit and window-list capture)…"; src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:102 tooltip "Content-protected"; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:174; PR #21 body: 'Check … Spaces/pinning/protection … on macOS before merge'; docs/HUD_GEOMETRY.md:139-140 content protection is manual QA; External: tauri-apps/tauri#14200 (macOS 15+: ScreenCaptureKit ignores setContentProtection/sharingType); Apple Developer Forums thread 792152 (Apple: no public API to prevent capture); thread 808016 (QuickTime captures a sharingType .none window while the ScreenCaptureKit sample hides it); `Owner machine runs macOS 26`; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:27-29 PROTECTED_NOTE: "Bluey is excluded from most screen sharing and recording (ScreenCaptureKit and window-list capture)"; docs/adr/0006-privacy-mode-and-content-protection.md:12-15 — "excludes the panel from most screen-capture APIs (ScreenCaptureKit, CGWindowList-based capture used by Zoom/Meet/Teams/QuickTime)"; README.md:85-86; docs/SECURITY.md:143-145; src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:102 tooltip "Content-protected"; Apple DTS, developer.apple.com/forums/thread/792152: on macOS 15.4+ NSWindow.sharingType = .none windows are still captured by ScreenCaptureKit; 'no public APIs for preventing screen capture'; tauri-apps/tauri issue #14200 'macOS 15+: ScreenCaptureKit ignores setContentProtection'; this Mac runs macOS 26
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Change PROTECTED_NOTE and ADR 0006 to honest copy, e.g. 'Hidden from apps that honour macOS window protection (legacy window capture); modern screen sharing on macOS 15+ may still show Bluey', and report supported=false or a 'partial' level on macOS >= 15. (2) Add a self-test: the helper captures the display via SCK without excludeSelf and checks whether the HUD rect contains HUD pixels, then reports 'verified hidden' or 'visible' per machine. (3) Mention that native menus are not covered.
- **Test plan:** Unit test that protection() returns the macOS-version-appropriate note. On real macOS 15/26: enable Privacy mode, share the screen in Zoom, Meet (Chrome) and Teams, record with QuickTime and OBS; note which capture the HUD and its NSMenu popups; run the self-test.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Protection is only window.set_content_protected (capture/mod.rs:397-405), and PROTECTED_NOTE (capture/mod.rs:27-29) names ScreenCaptureKit explicitly. The claim that ScreenCaptureKit ignores sharingType=.none on macOS 15+ comes from the auditor's Apple forum and Tauri issue citations; I did not reproduce it. To settle it at runtime, enable Privacy mode on macOS 15/26, share the screen in Zoom/Meet (which use ScreenCaptureKit) or record with QuickTime, and check whether the HUD is visible. All three windows are declared statically in tauri.conf.json, so set_protection does reach every webview window. Native NSMenu popups are unprotected. Rated High because users of an interview-assist app rely on this copy.
- **Also reported as:** native-macos-platform/privacy-mode-ineffective-macos15, testing-verification-debt/content-protection-claim-unverified, prior-audit-docs-drift/content-protection-sck-overclaim
- _Source: auditor (security-privacy/content-protection-overclaims-sck)_

#### SEC-007

**No app ACL manifest: all 142 Bluey commands (arbitrary-file document ingest, dev_*, settings/base-URL rewrites, data_reset_all) are callable from every window**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort L · independent · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — Per-window command ACL manifest (142 commands, 3 capabilities) is defence in depth behind CSP and sanitised Markdown; large and risky for this cycle. Tracked for a dedicated change.

- **User impact:** Nothing is exploitable today without script execution in a WebView, and the CSP and markdown rendering make that hard. But the HUD renders attacker-influenced content (screen, OCR, web results, model output). Any future XSS, a mermaid or shiki bug, or a dependency compromise turns straight into reading arbitrary user files and exfiltrating API keys (by rewriting a provider base_url) with no second layer. The security doc overstates the isolation.
- **Root cause:** Tauri v2 allows every app command in every window unless the app declares an app manifest. Rust commands trust WebView-supplied paths and URLs.
- **Evidence:** `src-tauri/build.rs:38-41 `tauri_build::build()`, no Attributes::app_manifest`; ~/.cargo/registry/.../tauri-2.11.5/src/webview/mod.rs:1823 — ACL enforced only when `plugin_command.is_some() || has_app_acl_manifest || !is_local`; src-tauri/src/lib.rs generate_handler! registers 142 commands incl. commands::dev::* (198-201) unconditionally; app/dev.rs:19 simulate has no developer-mode gate; src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:321-348 validate_path only checks absolute/regular/size; crates/bluey-storage/src/documents/index.rs:44-51 honours the caller's `format`, so any file (e.g. ~/.codex/auth.json) can be ingested as text and read back via documents_get_text; src-tauri/src/settings/mod.rs:200-208 validate() checks only embeddingDimensions — provider base_url (any scheme/host) is accepted, and API keys are then sent to it; docs/SECURITY.md 'Frontend ⇄ backend boundary' claims main 'gets … the Bluey commands it needs'; tauri.conf.json:66 CSP (script-src 'self', connect-src IPC only) is the remaining barrier; core:default in all windows also grants core:image:allow-from-path + allow-rgba (read arbitrary image files) and core:event:allow-emit (spoof events to other windows); `src-tauri/build.rs:38-40 `tauri_build::build()` with no AppManifest::commands`; src-tauri/capabilities/{main,settings,onboarding}.json list only core/plugin permissions; onboarding.json:4 is described as 'Minimal surface'; Tauri docs (https://v2.tauri.app/security/capabilities/): by default all commands registered through invoke_handler are allowed for all windows unless AppManifest::commands is used; docs/SECURITY.md:112-114: 'main (HUD) gets … plus the Bluey commands it needs; … onboarding a subset'; docs/SECURITY.md:15 'Per-window Tauri capabilities' and :113-115 'main (HUD) gets ... the Bluey commands it needs; settings additionally ...; onboarding a subset'; `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:72 'capabilities/ per-window Tauri capabilities'`; `src-tauri/build.rs:40 — tauri_build::build() with no AppManifest::commands`; src-tauri/capabilities/{main,settings,onboarding}.json — only core:* and plugin permissions; Tauri v2 docs (security/capabilities, via context7): 'By default, all commands that you registered ... are allowed to be used by all the windows and webviews of the app. To change that, consider using AppManifest::commands.'
- **Proposed solution:** (1) build.rs: `tauri_build::try_build(Attributes::new().app_manifest(AppManifest::new().commands(&[...])))`, then grant per-window `allow-<command>` sets (HUD: ask/capture/audio/panel; settings: everything; onboarding: auth, permissions, secrets for gemini). (2) documents_add: accept a path only if it came from documents_pick_files (keep a short-lived set in DocumentsManager), and ignore a caller `format` for paths (derive it from the extension). (3) Gate dev_* on developer_mode or dev-tools. (4) validate(): base_url must be https unless the host is loopback. (5) Replace core:default with the specific core permissions each window needs. Update SECURITY.md.
- **Test plan:** Rust test with tauri::test mock runtime (or an ACL unit test on the generated capabilities) asserting that `documents_add`/`data_reset_all` invoked with webview label 'main' are rejected and allowed for 'settings'. DocumentsManager test: a path not returned by pick_files is rejected. settings validate test: http://evil.example rejected, http://127.0.0.1:11434 accepted. Manual real-app smoke test of all three windows after the manifest change, because PR #18 showed mock tests miss IPC failures.
- **Verification (confirmed):** build.rs calls plain tauri_build::build() and there is no app manifest. In tauri 2.11.5, webview/mod.rs:1823 skips the ACL check for local app commands. validate_path (documents/mod.rs:321-340) checks only absolute path, regular file and size, and index.rs honours a caller-supplied format. settings validate() (settings/mod.rs:199-208) checks only embeddingDimensions.
