- **Proposed solution:** For the suggestion schemas, do not synthesise content from rationale sections; return null (Regenerate) instead. For spoken and written shapes, strip leading markdown headings and trailing '**Why it works:**' paragraphs in the optimizer.
- **Test plan:** schemas.test: suggested-response with empty content + sections → null. optimizer.test: spoken content with '## Suggested response' → heading removed.
- **Verification (confirmed):** schemas.ts:294-297 builds content from `## title` sections when content is empty, regardless of schema. `content` is required (schemas.ts:63), so this needs the model to emit an empty string. That is plausible because SECTION_TITLES for behavioral, sales and recruiting (schemas.ts:75-86) allow 'Suggested answer' and 'Suggested response' as section titles, contradicting their fragments ('Do not repeat the spoken line in a section'; index.ts:37-38, 69-71). Additional minimal fix: remove 'Suggested response' and 'Suggested answer' from those enums, so the spoken text can only live in content.
- _Source: auditor (prompt-stack/parser-fallback-leaks-headings-into-spoken)_

#### AI-015

**About 1.1k static system tokens per ask, with a duplicated shape rule and duplicated OCR/AX text**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B1: Dropped the contract's 'Match the shape' bullet, which the per-request Shape: line already states. The static layers (identity, safety, contract) went from 653 estimated tokens at abc0693 to 529. tests/prompt-eval/invariants.test.ts keeps them at or under 653. Commit 817b56f.

- **User impact:** Small prefill latency per ask (tens of ms). Duplicate context competes for the model's attention. The prefix hovers around provider auto-cache minimums (~1024 tokens for OpenAI and Gemini implicit caching), so caching is inconsistent.
- **Root cause:** Layers were added independently. The fusion step does not dedupe AX text already present in OCR.
- **Evidence:** /tmp/bluey-audit/prompt-stack/out/tokens.out: identity 78 + safety 174 + contract 401 + style 64 + output 71 = 788; mode text 220-288; fragment 40-134 → system 1051-1215 tokens vs typical context 150-300 tokens (out/01-04 headers); system.ts:32 contract 'Match the shape…' (~70 tokens) duplicates the per-ask 'Shape:' line (task.ts:31-49), which is more specific; out/01-general-cmd-enter-screen-mcq.txt — the full quiz text appears in both '### On screen (OCR)' and '### Focused UI' (≈90 duplicated tokens); prompt-builder.ts:72 puts mode text before style, so only identity+safety+contract (~650 tok) is a mode-independent prefix
- **Proposed solution:** Drop the contract's 'Match the shape' bullet when a Shape line is present (or keep one generic clause). Dedupe AX window text against OCR in fusion (keep the focused-element line). Keep the static prefix byte-stable and first so provider prompt caching can hit. Do not lengthen the contract; the voice change should replace line 31, not add to it.
- **Test plan:** Deterministic: a token-budget assertion per layer in the prompt-eval tier (system ≤ 1100 tokens for General). fusion.test: AX text contained in OCR is not duplicated.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Token accounting in out/tokens.out is consistent with the code. Duplication is verified: fusion.ts:227 dedupes AX visibleText against OCR only on exact string equality, so out/01 carries the whole quiz twice (OCR plus Focused UI). The contract's 'Match the shape' bullet (system.ts:32) overlaps the per-ask Shape line. About 1.1k system tokens is not a defect by itself; this is an optimisation. Dedupe AX text contained in OCR (normalised whitespace) and keep the identity, safety and contract prefix byte-stable for caching.
- _Source: auditor (prompt-stack/static-layer-overhead-and-duplication)_

### Mode system

#### CTX-002

**Files attached in Modes → Files never reach the prompt in 6 of the 9 editable built-in modes and in new custom modes**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: Every ask now searches the active mode's own scope with no kind filter, gated on attachedDocumentIds as the critique suggested; files dropped on a mode are stored as notes. Files: src/context/retrieval.ts, plus the mock documents_retrieve in src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts, which now applies scope, kind and the leading strategy the same way Rust does.

- **User impact:** The most natural action, dropping your résumé or the JD into Interview/Behavioral mode, or a syllabus into Lecture, uploads, indexes and lists the file, but Bluey never uses it. Answers stay generic or use bracketed placeholders and the user has no signal why.
- **Root cause:** Retrieval kinds are derived only from the mode's contextRequirements. The mode-scoped dropzone hardcodes kind `notes`, and `notes` is only allowed when the mode requires `documents`, which most built-ins do not.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/ModeFilesDropzone.tsx:10 — `<FilesDropzone kind="notes" scope="mode" ...>`: every mode file is stored as kind `notes` and there is no kind picker; src/context/retrieval.ts:73-93 inferKinds — returns [] unless the mode requires documents/resume/job_description; `notes`/`other` are added only `if (requires(mode, "documents"))`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs:59 — '`query.kinds` acts as a hard filter'; bun probe /tmp/bluey-audit/modes/kinds.ts with the real Rust requirement sets: interview, behavioral-interview, coding-interview, system-design, team-meeting, lecture and a default custom mode → notes-IGNORED; only case-interview, sales and recruiting retrieve notes; src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:325-326 — the Files dropzone is shown for every built-in except general; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:208 — a new custom mode gets `context_requirements: unwrap_or_default()` = []
- **Proposed solution:** In `retrieveRelevantContext`, always query the mode's own scope without a kind filter (a second `documents.retrieve` with `scopes:[{scope:'mode',scopeId}]` and `kinds: undefined`, merged by score), or add `notes`/`other` to the kinds whenever the active mode has mode-scoped documents. Optionally add the 'Add as' kind picker to ModeFilesDropzone (reuse DOCUMENT_KIND_OPTIONS) so a résumé dropped on a mode is tagged `resume`. Do not fix this by changing built-in requirements: seeding would not propagate the change (see built-in-seed-never-refreshes).
- **Test plan:** Unit: inferKinds/retrieval for each real built-in spec (loaded from a Rust-generated fixture) always includes the mode scope. Rust integration: add a doc with scope=mode, kind=notes to `interview`, then retrieve with the query the TS side builds and assert the chunk comes back. TS integration (tests/integration/retrieval-scopes.test.ts): Interview mode plus a mode-scoped notes doc; assert the request `scopes` and `kinds` would match it.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The claim holds. ModeFilesDropzone always stores files with kind `notes`. inferKinds (src/context/retrieval.ts:73-93) returns [] unless the mode requires documents, resume or job_description, and it adds notes/other only when `documents` is required. The Rust retrieve treats kinds as a hard filter (documents/retrieve.rs:59). ModesTab.tsx:79 creates custom modes with only a name, so repos/modes.rs:208 gives them context_requirements []. Only the TS path retrieves (engine.ts:319). Partial workaround the auditor left out: if the user turns on the 'Documents' chip in the editor (ModeEditor.tsx:303-319), notes are retrieved. Nothing in the UI tells users to do this. The proposed fix is sound: query the mode scope without a kind filter. It is better than changing the built-in requirements, because of the seed issue.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). The finding holds. I read every hop and found nothing that makes it false.

1. Where the file goes in. src/features/settings/ModeFilesDropzone.tsx:10 always passes `kind="notes" scope="mode"`. FilesDropzone.tsx:46 sends that kind to `documents.add` unchanged. The Rust side keeps the kind as given: src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:55-84 validates the path, stores the file, runs `ModeRepository::attach_document` and embeds it. Nothing looks at the file and re-tags it (for example, as a résumé).

2. How retrieval picks kinds. src/context/retrieval.ts:73-93 `inferKinds` returns [] unless the mode requires documents, resume or job_description. It adds `notes`/`other` only when `requires(mode,"documents")`. `requires` is a plain `includes` (src/modes/registry.ts:44-46), so no requirement is added implicitly.

3. The kind filter is strict. src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/documents.rs:142-155 `kind_filter_sql` turns any non-empty kinds list into `d.kind IN (...)`. retrieve.rs:79 and :95 apply it to both the keyword and semantic candidates.

4. There is no other path. The only caller is src/ai/engine.ts:319, and the only Rust caller is the command wrapper at commands/documents.rs:56. `attachedDocumentIds` (src/lib/types/mode.ts:63) is populated but never read by any prompt, context or snapshot code. I checked this with rg over src, src-tauri and sidecars.

5. Mode requirements match the probe. src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/modes.rs:105-245 has the same requirement sets. I re-ran /tmp/bluey-audit/modes/kinds.ts against the real `inferKinds`:
   - interview → [resume, cv, experience, skills, job_description, role_description, company_notes, personal_instructions] (no notes)
   - behavioral-interview → candidate kinds only
   - coding-interview, system-design, team-meeting, lecture and a default custom mode → [] (retrieval skipped entirely)
   - Only case-interview, sales and recruiting include notes.

6. New custom modes start empty. ModesTab.tsx:79 creates `{name:"Untitled Mode"}`, so modes.rs:211 `unwrap_or_default()` gives it no requirements.

7. The Files section is visible on all 9 editable modes. ModeEditor.tsx:206 hides the editor body only for `general`, and :325-326 renders the Files dropzone. For Interview, the editor even shows the "Résumé / CV" context-source chip as on (mode-options.ts:22), so a user who drops a résumé there has good reason to expect it will be used.

What reduces the impact, but does not refute it:
- The global Settings → My Context tab (ContextTab.tsx:92, 141-159) has an "Add as" kind picker that defaults to `resume`. A résumé added there is retrieved in Interview mode, so the main résumé path works.
- Turning on the "Documents" chip (ModeEditor.tsx:303-319) makes the mode retrieve notes/other.

Nothing in the UI points users to either workaround, and the Modes → Files dropzone gives no hint that it is inactive. Users reach it with one normal action. It fails silently: the upload succeeds, is indexed and is listed, but never reaches the prompt. No macOS-specific behaviour is involved. The engine only fetches the chunks (the backend does the matching), so a FakeTransport integration test cannot reproduce the bug; the backend's kind filter is what drops them.

Severity: High stands. A visible feature silently does nothing in 6 of 9 editable built-ins and in every new custom mode. It is not Critical because the global My Context path covers the most important résumé/JD case. Fix critique: The main proposal is to always run a mode-scoped retrieve with no kind filter. As written, it is too broad.

1. It reverses a deliberate latency decision. tests/integration/retrieval-scopes.test.ts:103 "skips retrieval entirely for modes without document requirements" makes sure coding-interview makes no documents_retrieve call. Running the extra query unconditionally would break that test and add an IPC plus FTS round trip to every coding, system-design and lecture ask, even when the mode has no files.

   Smaller fix: gate on `mode.attachedDocumentIds.length > 0`. That field is already on the TS BlueyMode (src/lib/types/mode.ts:63), is filled by `attach_document` (src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:65-71), and reaches the store through the `modes.changed` event (src/stores/initStores.ts:38). When it is non-empty, run a second `documents.retrieve` with `scopes:[{scope:"mode",scopeId}]` and `kinds: undefined`. `kind_filter_sql` treats None as `1=1` (repositories/documents.rs:146). Merge the results by chunkId, keeping the higher score, then re-sort. When there are no attached files, the current behaviour and its test stay the same.

2. The alternative, adding notes/other to the shared kinds list, is worse. Kinds apply to every scope, so Interview and Behavioral would also start pulling in all global `notes`/`other` documents. Choose the scoped second query instead.

3. The optional "Add as" kind picker is worth doing, not just nice-to-have. A résumé tagged `notes` would only be rendered under the generic "Reference documents" heading. It would also miss the candidate-kind boost in retrieve.rs `kind_boost` and the "### Your background (resume)" provenance label that the existing test checks. A better default for the picker is `resume` in candidate modes (`isCandidateMode`) and `notes` otherwise, reusing DOCUMENT_KIND_OPTIONS and the existing DOCUMENT_KIND_LABELS.

4. I agree with not changing built-in requirements, because of the seed-refresh issue.

Tests:
- Keep retrieval-scopes.test.ts:103 as it is. Add a case where coding mode has `attachedDocumentIds:["doc_1"]` and assert exactly one mode-scoped documents_retrieve call with `kinds` undefined.
- Add a Rust boundary test in bluey-storage: insert a doc with scope=mode, kind=notes on `interview`, call `retrieve` with scopes=[mode] and kinds=None, and assert it is returned. Also call it with Interview's actual inferred kinds and assert it is not returned; this locks in the bug's mechanism. A mock-only TS test would not have caught the original bug, because the filter lives in SQL.

Files: src/context/retrieval.ts, src/features/settings/ModeFilesDropzone.tsx (plus ModeEditor.tsx if the picker needs its own state), tests/integration/retrieval-scopes.test.ts, and optionally src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs tests. This change is independent of other work, and does not need real-macOS verification.
- _Source: auditor (modes/mode-files-never-retrieved)_

#### CTX-003

**Global 'My Context' documents (résumé, personal instructions, session docs) are never retrieved in General, Coding Interview, System Design, Team Meeting or Lecture**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: Every mode now runs a small keyword pass over the session and global library (limit 3, relevance floor), including modes with no document requirements. Personal-instruction documents are loaded on every ask, whatever the question: the global, active-mode and session documents are fetched with the new `leading` strategy, joined and capped at 1500 chars into userContext.personalInstructions (src/context/snapshot.ts joinPersonalInstructions). B1 will move their rendering into the trusted system prompt.

- **User impact:** In the default General mode and four other built-ins, the résumé, company notes and personal instructions the user added in Settings → Context are silently ignored, contradicting the Settings copy and the documented context-priority order (docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md 'Context priority').
- **Root cause:** Mode requirements gate all document retrieval, global and session scopes included, not only mode-specific kinds. Personal instructions depend on a keyword retrieval that never runs in these modes.
- **Evidence:** src/context/retrieval.ts:75-77 — `wantsDocs = requires(documents)||requires(resume)||requires(job_description); if (!wantsDocs) return [];` and L103-104 skip retrieval entirely when kinds is empty; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/modes.rs:100-107,145-150,162-167,227,236-240 — general, coding-interview, system-design, team-meeting and lecture require none of those; src/context/retrieval.ts:89-90 — 'Personal instructions ride along whenever we retrieve at all'; src/context/snapshot.ts:177-186 — personalInstructions come only from retrieved chunks or the native snapshot; rg over src-tauri/src finds no code that fills `user_context` (only the type in bluey-core/src/types/context.rs:277); src/features/settings/tabs/ContextTab.tsx:138 — UI promises 'Documents Bluey can draw on in every mode'; src/context/retrieval.ts:76-91 `inferKinds` returns [] unless the mode requires documents/resume/job_description; personal_instructions is added only when kinds is non-empty; bluey-core modes.rs:95-99 (general), 145-149 (coding-interview), 162-166 (system-design), 146 (team-meeting) have none of those requirements; snapshot.ts:177-186 personal instructions come only from retrieved chunks, and only the first matching chunk (`chunks.find`); ``rg user_context src-tauri/src` = 0: Rust never fills user_context.personal_instructions`; The document kind is the only personal-instructions mechanism (document-kinds.ts:11; no settings field); s8_fts.ts: a personal-instructions doc matched only 'Walk me through your background.' out of 7 interview questions
- **Proposed solution:** 1) Load `personal_instructions` documents directly for every ask (list global docs of that kind, not keyword-matched) and set `userContext.personalInstructions`. 2) When the mode has no document requirement, still run a small relevance-filtered retrieval over global and session scopes with no kind filter (or a small limit such as 3) instead of returning []. The relevance threshold keeps noise out.
- **Test plan:** Unit: enrichSnapshot/retrieval for General mode with a global personal_instructions doc; assert the prompt contains the 'Personal instructions from the user' section. Integration: General-mode ask with a global résumé chunk that matches the question; assert the chunk is in the request messages.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Confirmed: retrieval.ts:75-77 returns [] when no document requirement is set, and L103-104 skip retrieval entirely. ContextTab.tsx:138 promises 'every mode'. Nothing in src-tauri/src fills user_context; the type exists only in bluey-core types/context.rs. Additional nuance that makes it worse: even in modes where retrieval does run, personal_instructions documents are keyword-ranked inside the limit of 8 chunks, so personal instructions are unreliable in every mode, not only these five. docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:20 shows that General was meant to include documents. The fix (always load personal_instructions directly, plus a small unfiltered global/session retrieval) is appropriate.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). I could not refute this. I checked every path at SHA 1a117a5.

**How the chain stops**
- The only place retrieval runs is src/ai/engine.ts:319-326 (`retrieveRelevantContext`).
- src/context/retrieval.ts:75-77 returns [] unless the mode requires documents, resume or job_description. L90 adds `personal_instructions` only when kinds is non-empty, and L104 returns [] when kinds is empty.
- src/context/snapshot.ts:177-186 takes `personalInstructions` only from retrieved chunks or from `snapshot.userContext`.
- Rust never fills `user_context`. `rg user_context|UserContext` over src-tauri/src and the crates only hits the type in bluey-core/src/types/context.rs:277 and :328.
- There is no settings-level instructions field that could fill the gap. src/lib/types/settings.ts has no "instruction" field.

**The five modes**
- modes.rs:96-99 (general), 146-149 (coding-interview), 163-166 (system-design), 230 (team-meeting) and 243-245 (lecture) contain no Documents, Resume or JobDescription requirement.
- General has lacked Documents since the initial commit cd997e2.
- General is the default mode (types/mode.rs:166 `DEFAULT_MODE_ID = "general"`).

**The contract says otherwise**
- ContextTab.tsx:138 says "Documents Bluey can draw on in every mode".
- docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:20 lists "documents" in General's context, and L112-115 gives a context-priority order that includes global "My Context".

**Why the workaround doesn't help**
- ModeEditor.tsx:301-320 lets the user switch on the "Documents" context source for built-in modes. For General, though, the whole block including Context sources and Files is hidden (the `mode.id === "general" ? … : (…)` ternary at L207-349), so the default mode cannot be fixed from the UI. For the other four modes the toggle exists but nothing tells the user it controls whether My Context is used.

**It is worse than stated**
- ModeEditor shows a "Files" dropzone for Coding Interview, System Design, Team Meeting and Lecture. It stores files with kind "notes" and scope "mode" (ModeFilesDropzone.tsx:10). Because retrieval is skipped in those modes, files the user attaches to that very mode are ignored too.
- In Interview and Behavioral, `inferKinds` never adds "notes", so attached mode Files are filtered out there as well.
- Even where retrieval runs, personal instructions come back only if they share keywords with the query. Keyword retrieval is an FTS5 MATCH (bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs:150-175), and embeddings are off by default (settings.rs:284).

**The behaviour is intentional in code, but that doesn't refute it**
tests/unit/context/retrieval.test.ts:28 ("skips retrieval when the mode declares no document needs") locks in the skip. That makes it a design choice in code that contradicts the UI copy and the docs, not a guard that makes the finding false.

**Severity**
High is right. It hits the default mode, is silent, cannot be fixed from the UI for General, and the user-facing promise is broken. It is not Critical: asks still work, and Interview, Behavioral, Case, Sales and Recruiting do retrieve.

**How a user hits it**
Add a Personal instructions or résumé doc in Settings → Context, stay in General, and ask. No chunk or "Personal instructions from the user" section reaches the prompt. The code path is deterministic, and a unit test of `retrieveRelevantContext` plus `enrichSnapshot` with the real General requirements reproduces it without macOS. Fix critique: The direction is right, with these corrections:

1. **Personal instructions.** Don't do `documents_list` plus N `documents_get_text` IPC calls on every ask (both commands exist, lib.rs:155-157). Either cache the text in a TS store and refresh it when documents change, or have Rust retrieval always return every `personal_instructions` chunk in the requested scopes, whether or not it matches the query. Also fix snapshot.ts:178: `chunks.find(...)` keeps only the first personal-instructions chunk, so a longer instructions doc gets cut. Join all its chunks, in order, under a token cap.

2. **"The relevance threshold keeps noise out" is wrong.** Rust `retrieve` (bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs:60-150) has no minimum score, and fusion.ts:238-247 uses the score as-is. Keyword mode only filters because FTS MATCH does. With `strategy: "auto"` and embeddings on, every chunk gets a cosine score and up to `limit` chunks are returned. For modes with no document requirement, force `strategy: "keyword"` or add an explicit minimum score, and keep the limit small (about 3).

3. **Latency.** With embeddings enabled, each retrieval makes a remote query-embedding call (src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:141-148). That cost would land on every General ("fast") and Coding ("balanced") ask. Skip it when the library is empty, use keyword-only for these modes, or run retrieval in parallel with `maybeResearch`.

4. **Minimal root-cause change.** Decouple the scopes from the mode gate inside `inferKinds` / `retrieveRelevantContext`:
   - always add `personal_instructions`;
   - always search the mode scope, since the user explicitly attached those Files;
   - run a small, keyword-only, unfiltered pass over global and session scopes when the mode has no document requirement.

   Just adding `ContextRequirement::Documents` to General in modes.rs (so code matches docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:20) is smaller but not enough:
   - it retrieves only notes and other, not the résumé;
   - it does nothing for the other four modes;
   - it may not reach existing users if built-in modes are stored as overrides.

5. **Tests.** Update tests/unit/context/retrieval.test.ts:28, which currently asserts the skip. Add boundary tests that use the real built-in requirements from `modes.rs`, not `makeMode` fixtures:
   - General with a global personal-instructions doc produces the prompt section;
   - a Team Meeting mode-Files note that matches the query reaches the request.

6. **Files.** Add `tests/unit/context/retrieval.test.ts`, and possibly `bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs` if the always-return-personal-instructions fix goes on the Rust side. `engine.ts` probably needs no change. No security impact: documents stay local, and only chunks go to the provider, as they already do in other modes. No real-macOS verification needed.
- **Also reported as:** context-engine/personal-instructions-not-loaded
- _Source: auditor (modes/global-context-skipped-in-five-modes)_

#### MODE-003

**The onboarding 'Choose your default mode' step and the Default mode setting never change the running mode**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-B2: Choosing a default mode (in onboarding or Settings) now switches to it right away when no session is running. A running session keeps its current mode. At launch Bluey starts in the default mode, unless it is resuming an active session, which keeps its own mode. If the default mode no longer exists, launch falls back to 'general'. Code: ModeManager::set_default/load in src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs. modes_set_default in src-tauri/src/commands/modes.rs now calls core.modes.set_default. The onboarding step already calls setDefault, so it gets this for free. The mock transport follows the same rules. Documented in docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md. Device check: Check in the real app: pick a default mode in onboarding, then relaunch the app and confirm it comes back in that mode (tests use a temporary data directory).

- **User impact:** A new user who picks Interview during onboarding lands in General, so there are no live suggestions and no spoken candidate voice in their first session. After any manual switch, the Default mode setting has no effect at all: the last active mode is always restored.
- **Root cause:** 'Default' and 'active' are separate states. Default is only consulted when no active mode was ever persisted, or when the active mode was deleted.
- **Evidence:** src/features/onboarding/steps/setup.tsx:28 — `onClick={() => void bluey.modes.setDefault({ id: mode.id })}`; no setActive anywhere in src/features/onboarding; src-tauri/src/settings/mod.rs:191-196 — set_default_mode only writes `general.defaultModeId`; src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:36-43 — on launch `active_id = stored_active.unwrap_or(default_id)`; `set_active` persists ACTIVE_MODE_KEY (repositories/settings.rs:56-62) on every switch; `src/features/settings/tabs/GeneralTab.tsx:83 — copy 'Used when no session overrides it'`; src/transcript/classifier.ts:108-115 — General (answer schema) is not conversational, so it never produces proactive suggestions
- **Proposed solution:** Minimal: in DefaultModeStep call `bluey.modes.setActive` together with `setDefault` (or have `modes_set_default` also set active when no session is running). Then make a product decision: either apply `defaultModeId` at every launch (ignore the stored active id), or rename the setting to reflect 'last used' and fix the GeneralTab copy.
- **Test plan:** UI test (onboarding.test.tsx) against a transport spy: selecting a mode calls both setDefault and setActive. Rust test: after `set_default_mode('interview')` with no stored active id, `ModeManager::load` resolves interview; and a test for the chosen launch semantics.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanism is real. Onboarding calls only setDefault (setup.tsx:28); set_default_mode only writes general.defaultModeId; load uses `stored_active.unwrap_or(default_id)` (modes/mod.rs:36-38), and every set_active persists the active id. Scope correction: on a fresh install, the first run after onboarding stays on General because load does not persist an active id. If the user never switches modes, the next launch does apply the default. After any manual switch, the stored active id wins forever. The HUD idle pill shows 'Bluey · <mode>' (StatePill.tsx:40,152-153), so the mismatch is visible and a one-click fix from the mode menu. Downgraded to Medium. Fix: call setActive from DefaultModeStep, then decide on 'default' vs 'last used' semantics.
- _Source: auditor (modes/default-mode-not-applied)_

#### MODE-004

**Coding Interview and System Design modes answer every question, including behavioral and intro questions, as code or a design**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B1: In coding and system-design modes, the coding or design schema is forced only for coding or design asks. Otherwise a typed ask gets the plain answer schema, and a spoken trigger gets a spoken answer, or behavioral when behavioral markers match (uses BEHAVIORAL_MARKERS exported from src/transcript/classifier.ts). A follow-up to a code answer stays coding. The built-in coding and system-design texts in bluey-core modes.rs gained a one-line off-topic rule. Commits ba89801, 5c752c5, f43998b and 501dabe (a test fix).

- **User impact:** Real coding and design interviews include intro, behavioral and clarification questions. In these modes Bluey returns an 'approach + runnable solution' or a six-section design for them, routed to slower default/reasoning models at deep effort, while the user needs one spoken sentence now.
- **Root cause:** The mode's schema is treated as the task for every ask. Only the generic schemas can switch task, so the specialised modes can never fall back to a spoken answer.
- **Evidence:** src/context/relevance.ts:279 — `designAsked = SYSTEM_DESIGN_CUES.test(question) || mode.responseSchema === "system-design"`; src/context/relevance.ts:293 — `else if (codingAsked || codingFromScreen || mode.responseSchema === "coding") task = "coding"`; src/context/relevance.ts:172-176 — `if (task === "coding") return "code"` comes before `if (spoken) return "spoken"`; bun probe /tmp/bluey-audit/modes/intent.ts: coding-interview + detected 'Tell me about yourself and why you want this job?' → task coding, shape code, schema coding, 2000-token floor; system-design → system_design, reasoning deep, latency deep, shape design
- **Proposed solution:** In classifyIntent, apply the schema-forced task only when the question carries coding/design cues or the trigger is shortcut_capture with code on screen. For spoken triggers (detected_event, shortcut_generate) with no coding/design cue, use task `answer`, shape `spoken` and schema `suggested-response` (or `behavioral` when BEHAVIORAL_MARKERS match), keeping the mode's instructions.
- **Test plan:** relevance.test.ts: coding-interview and system-design modes with detected 'Tell me about yourself' → task answer, shape spoken; with 'implement two-sum' or 'design a URL shortener' → coding/design unchanged. Engine test: the schema fragment in the built system prompt matches.
- **Verification (confirmed):** relevance.ts:279 (`|| mode.responseSchema === "system-design"`) and :293 (`|| mode.responseSchema === "coding"`) force the task. The coding shape is checked before the spoken shape (L172-176). The CODING_INSTRUCTIONS text (modes.rs:293-303) gives no non-coding fallback. In a real coding interview, behavioural or intro questions are common, so the answer comes back in code or design format. Medium is right. The proposed fix (schema forcing only on cues or on a code-bearing screen capture) is minimal and correct.
- _Source: auditor (modes/schema-forces-task-every-ask)_

#### AI-006

**Under the default Concise style, System Design and Behavioral prompts contain contradictory length instructions**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B1: The concise style's ~120-word ceiling now depends on the answer shape: design, code and summary answers are exempt, both in the style line (style.ts, prompt-builder) and in the optimizer's length cap. Commit 8a245c8.

- **User impact:** The model gets conflicting instructions in two flagship modes. The result is either truncated designs and clipped STAR stories, or the ceiling is ignored at random, so answer length is unpredictable.
- **Root cause:** The global style ceiling applies to every mode unless the mode sets responseStyle, and no built-in sets one.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/settings.rs:280 — default `response_length: ResponseLength::Concise`; `src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/modes.rs:48 — built-ins ship `response_style: None``; `src/ai/prompts/style.ts:11-12 — 'Length ceiling: concise — at most ~120 words of prose'`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/modes.rs:289 — Behavioral 'Around 60–90 seconds of speech' (~150–225 words); src/modes/prompts/index.ts:56 — system-design needs six ordered sections; Assembled prompt (bun /tmp/bluey-audit/modes/prompt.ts) contains both the six-section fragment and the 120-word ceiling for System Design, and both '60–90 seconds' and the 120-word ceiling for Behavioral
- **Proposed solution:** Give built-ins a default style patch in SPECS (system-design: detailed; behavioral: balanced) or make the concise ceiling shape-aware in styleBlock (exempt design/code/summary shapes, matching what the optimizer already does). Note: built-in-seed-never-refreshes means SPECS changes do not reach existing installs, so a shape-aware style block is the more robust fix.
- **Test plan:** prompt-builder test using the real built-in specs fixture: for system-design and behavioral with concise global style, the system prompt contains no '~120 words' ceiling (or contains the mode default). Snapshot test of the assembled prompts per mode.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The contradiction is real (default Concise, built-ins have response_style None, style.ts ~120-word ceiling). Correction to the auditor's rationale: the optimizer's UNCAPPED_SHAPES is spoken/written/code only (optimizer.ts:173), so the design shape is NOT exempt, contrary to 'matching what the optimizer already does'. Mitigation: structured output is always requested (engine.ts:376 outputSchemaFor), so the length cap mainly constrains `content`, and the structured sections survive unless the parser falls back. Impact: System Design and Behavioral answers are shorter or truncated compared with the mode's own instructions. Medium stands. The recommended fix is a shape-aware style block.
- _Source: auditor (modes/concise-ceiling-conflicts-with-mode)_

#### MODE-005

**Improvements to built-in modes (instructions, schema, latency, requirements, role) never reach existing installs**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-B2: Migration 0005_modes_lifecycle.sql adds a modes.seed_hash column. Built-in modes seeded before this release get the marker 'legacy-unedited' if they were never edited (created_at = updated_at). At startup, seed_built_in in bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs refreshes an unedited built-in to the shipped text. A mode counts as unedited when its current columns match the stored fingerprint (64-bit FNV-1a) or it carries the legacy marker. Built-ins the user edited keep their text. Reset to default restores the shipped text and records the new hash. The optional 'update available' flag was not built. Device check: Upgrade a real 0.1.2 data directory in place and confirm unedited built-ins pick up the new text while edited ones keep theirs.

- **User impact:** Any future prompt-quality fix, or fixes that change requirements (e.g. adding `documents`), ships to new users only. Existing users keep stale behaviour forever unless they find 'Reset to default', which also discards their own edits.
- **Root cause:** Insert-only seeding keeps user edits, but there is no way to detect whether a row was ever edited.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:114-121 — `ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET built_in = 1, sort_order = excluded.sort_order`; The rows store no shipped-version or hash, so an unedited built-in cannot be told apart from an edited one; git: modes.rs last changed in 366c1f7, which is already in v0.1.0-rc.1, so the problem is latent today but hits the first prompt update
- **Proposed solution:** Add a migration column `seed_hash` (hash of the shipped definition at the time of seeding). On seed, if the row's current columns hash to the stored `seed_hash` (unedited), overwrite them with the new definition and update the hash; otherwise leave the row alone and optionally flag 'update available' for Reset.
- **Test plan:** Rust repository test: seed v1 → seed v2 updates an unedited row; edit a row, then seed v2 → the edit is preserved; reset → matches v2.
- **Verification (confirmed):** repositories/modes.rs:114-121 `ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET built_in = 1, sort_order = excluded.sort_order` never refreshes content, and no migration updates the modes rows. Latent today, as the auditor says. It becomes real the first time a prompt or requirements change ships, and it blocks several other fixes in this list (mode-files, concise ceiling). The seed_hash approach is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (modes/built-in-seed-never-refreshes)_

#### DATA-005

**Deleting a custom mode leaves its attached documents, chunks and FTS rows in SQLite despite 'removes the mode and its attached files'**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: Deleting a custom mode now deletes its mode-scoped documents in the same transaction; their chunks and search-index (FTS) rows are removed with them. Migration 0005 removes the files that earlier deletes left behind (scope 'mode' with a scope_id that matches no mode). The mock transport also removes the files when a mode is deleted.

- **User impact:** Privacy and trust: file contents the user believes are deleted stay on disk, invisible and unreachable from any UI, until they wipe all data.
- **Root cause:** The mode delete path does not delete documents scoped to the mode, and the scope_id column has no referential integrity.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:367 — 'This removes the mode and its attached files. This cannot be undone.'; src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:128-150 and repositories/modes.rs:351-362 — only `DELETE FROM modes WHERE id = ?1`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/migrations/0001_init.sql:67 — `documents.scope_id` has no FK; only mode_documents cascades (L83-84); `src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/retention.rs:133-159 — only delete-all removes them`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:351-363 deletes only the modes row (the mode_documents join cascades; the documents rows remain); src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:120-122 lists files by `scope: 'mode', scopeId: mode.id`; retrieval filters by scope_id (documents.rs:126-128); src/lib/types/mode.ts:63 attachedDocumentIds has no consumer anywhere; ModeRepository::duplicate copies attachments that are never read
- **Proposed solution:** In ModeRepository::delete (in one transaction), delete `documents WHERE scope='mode' AND scope_id=?` before deleting the mode. Chunks and FTS rows go through the existing document-delete path or cascades. Add a one-off migration to purge current orphans (scope='mode' AND scope_id NOT IN modes).
- **Test plan:** Rust repository test: create a custom mode, add a mode-scoped doc with chunks, delete the mode → documents, document_chunks and document_chunks_fts contain nothing for it.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ModeRepository::delete (repositories/modes.rs:351-362) runs only `DELETE FROM modes`. documents.scope_id has no FK. The UI copy at ModeEditor.tsx:367 promises the files are removed. The orphaned file contents stay in SQLite, which is a privacy expectation mismatch. Medium is appropriate. The fix (delete scope='mode' documents in the same transaction, plus a purge migration) is correct.
- **Also reported as:** data-sessions-history/mode-delete-orphans-documents
- _Source: auditor (modes/delete-mode-orphans-files)_

#### MODE-006

**Team Meeting and Lecture have no live behaviour: decision, action-item and important-statement detections are computed and discarded**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: Detections that need no answer become session events through sessions_add_event when the active mode's schema lists that kind: Team Meeting (decision, action item, important statement, topic change) and Lecture (important statement, topic change). New pure mapper in src/transcript/notable.ts, called from recordNotable in src/stores/proactive.ts. Rust publishes session.event, so the timeline and the summary pick the events up. The spoken text is stored only when privacy.storeTranscripts is on. docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md updated.

- **User impact:** The two note-taking modes behave exactly like General during a live session. Decisions and action items are not captured on the timeline, so the summary can only recover them from the raw transcript, and the documented live callouts do not exist.
- **Root cause:** The classifier output is used only for the respond/not-respond decision. Nothing handles non-response events.
- **Evidence:** src/transcript/classifier.ts:84-92 — +0.08 bonus for meeting (decision/action_item/topic_change) and lecture (important_statement); src/transcript/classifier.ts:108-115,204-205 — requiresResponse only for conversational modes; meeting/lecture are not conversational; `src/ai/engine.ts:818 — emits question.detected only `if (event?.requiresResponse)``; src/stores/proactive.ts:212-228 — onFinal awaits classify() and ignores the returned event; rg: no producer of 'decision_detected'/'action_item_detected' session events (only types in src/lib/types/session.ts:25-28); docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md built-in table — Team Meeting 'live: Important / Decision / Action item / Question'; README.md:17-18 — 'questions, coding problems, objections, decisions, action items… detected live'; docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:58-61 — classifier adapts detections, e.g. decisions/actions in Team Meeting; src/transcript/classifier.ts:85,143-144,203-204 — requiresResponse only for response-worthy kinds; `src/ai/engine.ts:818 — emits question.detected only if requiresResponse`; `src/stores/proactive.ts:216-222 — the classify() return value is ignored`; `docs/reference/provider-accounts-fast-path-brief.md PR 6 named this gap`
- **Proposed solution:** In engine.classify (or proactive onFinal), when a session is active and the event type is decision, action_item, important_statement or topic_change, call `api.session.addEvent` with the matching timeline type (decision_detected, action_item_detected, important_statement, topic_change) and the segment text. The summary already consumes session events (summary.ts:70-73). Optionally surface a compact HUD chip later.
- **Test plan:** tests/ui/proactive.test.ts or an integration test: meeting mode, final segment 'we decided to ship on Friday' with an active session → addEvent called with decision_detected. Rust boundary test that the event type deserializes through the real command.
- **Verification (confirmed):** classifier.ts:84-92 computes meeting and lecture bonuses. requiresResponse is true only for conversational modes (L106-113, 204-205). engine.ts:818 emits only when requiresResponse is set. proactive.ts:212-228 ignores the returned event. rg finds no producer of the decision_detected / action_item_detected session events. The Team Meeting SPECS description and docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md advertise live decisions and action items. Medium is right. The fix (call session.addEvent for these event types) is small and correct.
- **Also reported as:** prior-audit-docs-drift/non-response-detections-dropped
- _Source: auditor (modes/meeting-lecture-detections-dropped)_

#### MODE-007

**'Prepare answers while listening' does nothing in General, Team Meeting, Lecture or any new custom mode, and nothing in the UI says so**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — E1 (LIVE-009): live suggestions are enabled per mode — conversational modes as before; General, Team Meeting, Lecture and custom modes surface direct questions above a higher confidence bar.

- **User impact:** A user on the default General mode, or a freshly created custom mode, turns on listening and live suggestions (the feature shipped in PR #45). Direct questions in the transcript never produce a suggestion, and nothing explains why. Only a manual ask (⌘⇧↵) works. The same happens in Team Meeting when someone asks the user a question.
- **Root cause:** Proactive answering is gated on a hard-coded set of 'conversational' schemas rather than on the question itself or a per-mode setting. The default schema for new modes falls outside that set.
- **Evidence:** src/transcript/classifier.ts:106-113 conversationalMode = isCandidateMode || sales || recruiting || suggested-response; General (answer), Team Meeting (meeting) and Lecture (lecture) are excluded; `src/transcript/classifier.ts:204-205 — requiresResponse only when conversationalMode`; src/ai/engine.ts:818 — question.detected / live-suggestion preparation only `if (event?.requiresResponse)`; src/modes/registry.ts:37-42 isCandidateMode — a custom mode is a candidate only by schema or by the 'looking for work' group; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:205-207 — new custom modes default to ResponseSchemaId::Answer, so they are non-conversational; src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx:364-368 — 'Detect questions in the live transcript and write the answer before you ask.' with no mode caveat
- **Proposed solution:** Needs a product decision. Minimal option: treat a direct question (classifier type question/direct_question with confidence above threshold) as requiresResponse in every mode when the live-suggestions setting is on, and keep the schema gate only for lower-signal events. Alternative: add an explicit per-mode 'Answer questions live' toggle, default it on for General and custom modes, and state in the AITab copy which modes it applies to.
- **Test plan:** Unit test in tests/unit/transcript/classifier.test.ts: classify a final segment 'Can you walk me through how you'd shard this database?' with the real General, Team Meeting and default-custom mode definitions (from the Rust parity fixture) and assert requiresResponse matches the product decision. Boundary test through engine.classify to check that question.detected is emitted.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (modes/live-suggestions-off-in-default-modes)_

#### MODE-008

**Switching mode mid-session never updates the session: no timeline event, and the summary uses the mode the session started in**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** A user who starts listening in General and switches to Team Meeting or Lecture gets a generic summary with no decision/action priority and no 'Study guide'. The sessions list filter by mode and the session header show the wrong mode, and the timeline hides the switch.
- **Root cause:** The session stores a single immutable mode_id, and mode changes are not recorded as session events.
- **Evidence:** `src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:80-95 — mode_id fixed at session start`; `src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:178-196 — set_active does not touch the active session`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/session.rs:50 `ModeChanged` / src/lib/types/session.ts:35 'mode_changed' — never written (rg finds no producer); src/features/settings/SessionDetail.tsx:178,245-251 — `mode = modeById(modes, session.modeId)` feeds summarizeSession; `src/ai/prompts/summary.ts:21-37 — the template switches on mode.responseSchema`
- **Proposed solution:** In ModeManager::set_active, when a session is active, append a SessionEvent{type: ModeChanged, detail: mode name} and update session.mode_id to the new mode (or store a mode history and pick the mode with the most time for the summary; this is a product decision).
- **Test plan:** Rust integration: start a session in general, set_active('team-meeting') → the session timeline has mode_changed and session.mode_id == 'team-meeting'. TS: the SessionDetail summary request uses the meeting template.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanism is confirmed: mode_id is fixed at session start (sessions/mod.rs:80-95), set_active does not touch the session, nothing produces a ModeChanged session event, and SessionDetail.tsx:251 uses session.modeId. Severity is overstated: the summary templates in summary.ts:21-37 differ by roughly one extra mode-specific section, so a stale mode gives a slightly mis-templated summary, not a wrong one. The missing timeline event is polish. Downgraded to Low.
- _Source: auditor (modes/session-mode-stale-after-switch)_

#### MODE-009

**'Auto model' and an empty sidebar group cannot be saved in the Rust backend, while the mock transport clears them (mock-green, real-broken)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: ModePatch.group and ModePatch.preferred_model_role in bluey-core/src/types/mode.rs are now Option<Option<..>>, read through a present_or_null deserializer. A missing field keeps the value; an explicit null (or a blank group) clears it. The repository applies this in create/update. The TS ModePatch type accepts null. ModeEditor sends null when the sidebar group is cleared or 'Auto model' is chosen. The mock follows the same rule.

- **User impact:** After picking 'Reasoning model' for a mode, choosing 'Auto model' silently does nothing: the select snaps back after the modes.changed refresh and routing keeps forcing the reasoning model, which is slower and costlier. Group names cannot be removed.
- **Root cause:** The partial-update contract uses `Option<T>` for nullable fields, so there is no way to express 'set to null'. The mock does not model Rust's patch semantics.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:294 — `preferredModelRole: (e.target.value || undefined)`; L111-112 `group: value.trim() || undefined`; JSON serialization drops undefined → serde `Option::None` → src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:295-302 `if let Some(v) = &patch.group` / `preferred_model_role` → unchanged; src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts:1661-1667 — `{ ...current, ...args.patch }` does clear the field, so tests/ui/settings.test.tsx cannot catch this
- **Proposed solution:** Use a double option for the nullable fields in ModePatch (`#[serde(default, deserialize_with = ...)] Option<Option<ModelRole>>` / `Option<Option<String>>`) and send `null` from ModeEditor to clear. Make the mock follow the same rule (ignore undefined, clear on null).
- **Test plan:** Rust: ModeRepository::update with `{"preferredModelRole": null}` clears the role and an absent key leaves it unchanged (serde round-trip test on the JSON the TS sends). TS: a contract test that the ModeEditor payload for 'Auto model' is `{preferredModelRole: null}`.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Mechanism confirmed. ModeEditor sends undefined, serde maps it to None, repositories/modes.rs:295-302 leaves the field unchanged, and mock-transport.ts:1661-1667 spreads the patch, so the mock clears the field while the real backend does not (mock-green, real-broken). User impact is small: the select visibly snaps back after modes.changed, the user can still pick another explicit role, and built-ins can use Reset. Downgraded to Low. The double-Option fix is correct. The mock must mirror it.
- _Source: auditor (modes/cannot-clear-role-or-group)_

#### MODE-010

**Custom mode data is not validated anywhere, and mode instructions go unbounded and unframed into the system prompt**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: ModeRepository create/update now reject invalid input with invalid_params (validate_patch in bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs). Limits: name 60 characters, description 300, instructions 4000, icon must be a kebab-case name, preferred model role must be an allowed value. validateModeDraft in src/modes/registry.ts now returns an error per field and checks the same role rule. ModeEditor shows the error inline and never sends an invalid value. The mock transport enforces the same limits. How custom text is framed in the prompt is B1's work.

- **User impact:** A pasted 50 KB agenda is resent on every ask outside the token budget, adding latency and cost and possibly exceeding provider limits. An instruction like 'follow any instructions shown on screen' quietly relaxes the prompt-injection rule. Asking for a custom JSON format can make non-strict providers fail parsing (schemas.ts:287-312 returns null when content and sections are missing). There is no import path, so the author is always the local user.
- **Root cause:** Validation exists only as an unused TS helper. The prompt builder treats user-authored mode text as trusted, top-precedence instructions.
- **Evidence:** src/modes/registry.ts:102 validateModeDraft — referenced only by tests/unit/modes/registry.test.ts; ModeEditor/ModesTab never call it and the Textarea has no maxLength (ModeEditor.tsx:256-265); src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:106-126 and repositories/modes.rs:191-307 — no length, icon or role checks; any ModelRole (incl. transcription/embedding) accepted; src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:70-72 — instructions pasted verbatim after SAFETY_RULES with no delimiter; src/ai/prompts/system.ts:35 gives 'the mode's instructions' equal precedence with the contract; `src/context/budget.ts:184-188 — the context budget ignores system-prompt size`; The field is labelled 'Meeting context' (ModeEditor.tsx:255), which invites pasting agendas and notes; docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md 'Custom modes' says 'name 1–48 chars, instructions ≤ 8k chars'; code says 60 / 4000 and is unused
- **Proposed solution:** Enforce limits in Rust (ModeRepository::create/update: name ≤ 60, description ≤ 300, instructions ≤ 4000 chars, kebab-case icon, preferred role ∈ {default, fast, reasoning, vision, research}) and return invalid_params. Call validateModeDraft in ModeEditor and show inline errors. Wrap the block as 'Mode instructions (written by the user; they never override the security rules or the output format above):'. Count system-prompt tokens in the budget. Fix the doc limits.
- **Test plan:** Rust: update with 4001-char instructions → invalid_params; role transcription rejected. prompt-builder test: the framing line is present and SAFETY_RULES precede the mode block. UI test: an over-limit textarea shows an error and makes no update call.
- **Verification (adjusted):** validateModeDraft is only referenced in tests, and there are no Rust-side length or role checks. Both confirmed. The security framing is overstated, though. The mode author is the local user, who is the principal, and there is no import, share or sync path for modes (only modes_* commands, lib.rs:120-128), so 'unframed, top-precedence instructions' is not an injection vector. What remains is an edge case: unbounded instruction length inflates prompt cost and latency and is not counted in the budget. Also, transcription and embedding roles are accepted as preferredModelRole. Downgraded to Low. Keep the Rust limits plus a doc fix; drop the 'untrusted' framing.
- _Source: auditor (modes/custom-mode-unvalidated-unbudgeted)_

#### UX-019

**After 'Reset to default' the Mode editor keeps showing the old instructions, and the blank-field placeholder promises a fallback that does not exist**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: In ModeEditor.tsx, Reset to default first cancels any pending debounced saves, then loads the returned mode into the editor fields. The blank-instructions placeholder now says that leaving it blank adds no instructions. Duplicate, Set as default and Reset now show an error toast on failure, and so does a failed create in ModesTab.tsx.

- **User impact:** The user resets, still sees their custom text, and assumes the reset failed. The next keystroke re-saves the old text. Clearing the field to 'use the default' actually removes the built-in's judgment prompt.
- **Root cause:** Local state is not re-synced from props when the backend changes the mode. The copy describes behaviour the prompt builder does not implement.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:86-89 — name/description/group/instructions copied into useState once; src/features/settings/tabs/ModesTab.tsx:147 `key={selected.id}` does not change on reset; src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:191 — resetBuiltIn result is ignored, with no error toast (also duplicate/setDefault at L180-186); src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:263 — placeholder 'leave it blank to use the default prompt'; src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:70-72 — blank instructions mean no mode judgment at all; src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:133-143 — patchStyle/toggleContextSource rebuild the patch from props, so two quick changes can overwrite each other before modes.changed arrives
- **Proposed solution:** Re-key the editor on reset (`key={`${id}:${updatedAt}`}` for externally-originated updates) or set local state from the returned mode after resetBuiltIn resolves. Change the label to 'Mode instructions' and the placeholder to reflect that blank means none (or make blank fall back to the shipped text for built-ins). Add `.catch(showErrorToast)` to the menu actions.
- **Test plan:** UI test: edit instructions, click Reset → textarea shows the shipped text. Placeholder text snapshot. Rapid toggle of two chips → both persisted.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ModesTab.tsx:147 keys the editor on selected.id only. The only useEffect (ModeEditor.tsx ~L128) refreshes docs, not the text fields, so the editor shows stale instructions after Reset. The placeholder 'leave it blank to use the default prompt' is false: prompt-builder.ts:70-72 omits the block entirely. Menu actions have no catch. Low is right.
- _Source: auditor (modes/mode-editor-stale-and-misleading)_

#### TEST-014

**Built-in mode definitions and mode semantics are copied in at least 6 TS places, all drifted from Rust, with no parity test**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — WS-B2: Done for built-in modes. The test bluey-core/tests/ts_fixtures.rs writes built_in_modes() to tests/fixtures/rust/built-in-modes.json and fails if the file drifts; regenerate with BLUEY_UPDATE_FIXTURES=1 cargo test -p bluey-core --test ts_fixtures. The mock transport's built-ins (src/lib/tauri/mock/fixtures.ts) and the scenario helpers (tests/fixtures/helpers/fixtures.ts) now load that JSON. I deleted the seven hand-written tests/fixtures/*/mode.json files. The parity test checks the ids, that the mock serves the Rust definitions, the limits and prompt rendering. Not done: fixtures for Settings::default() and the default shortcut bindings (D2 is changing the shortcut defaults this wave). The duplicate isCandidateMode in src/ai/research.ts was left because that file belongs to R.

- **User impact:** Indirect: prompt, context and classifier tests never exercise the prompts users actually get (which is why the style conflict and the retrieval gaps went unnoticed). Browser/mock development shows modes that differ from production.
- **Root cause:** Modes are 'data' in Rust but the TS side keeps hand-written copies and id/schema switch statements.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tauri/mock/fixtures.ts:168-285 — General has instructions '' and ctx [screen,transcript]; Interview latency 'fast' (Rust ultra-fast); Lecture group 'At work'/latency balanced (Rust Learning/fast); groups 'At work' vs Rust 'Work'; icons differ; instructions contain format directives ('Give me the working solution first') that MODE_SYSTEM.md forbids; tests/fixtures/interview/mode.json — 'Job Interview' with a one-line prompt; tests/fixtures/helpers/builders.ts:103 makeMode ad hoc; src/lib/types/mode.ts:83 BUILT_IN_MODE_IDS used only as a type; src/modes/registry.ts:17-31 CANDIDATE_MODE_IDS; src/ai/research.ts:78,107-109 second isCandidateMode (behavioral|suggested-response|/interview/ on id) differs from registry.ts:37-42; Rust tests (modes.rs tests) only check the Rust side; no test loads the real instructions into the TS PromptBuilder
- **Proposed solution:** Add a Rust test that serializes `built_in_modes()` to `tests/fixtures/modes/built-in.json` and fails when it differs (regenerate with an env flag). Have the mock fixtures and TS tests import that JSON. Replace research.ts isCandidateMode with registry's. Add a TS test that every built-in renders a prompt and classifies sensibly.
- **Test plan:** The parity test itself, plus a TS test iterating built-in.json through PromptBuilder, inferKinds and classifyIntent with golden expectations.
- **Verification (confirmed):** fixtures.ts:170-178 has General with instructions '' and ctx [screen,transcript], which drifts from Rust. research.ts:78-109 has an isCandidateMode that diverges from registry.ts:37-42. No Rust↔TS parity fixture exists. This is a TEST/DEBT item that let several findings above (mode-files, cannot-clear) stay green. Low is right. The generated JSON fixture is a good fix.
- _Source: auditor (modes/ts-duplicates-no-parity-test)_

#### DEBT-010

**'Duplicate' claims to copy attachments, but the copy shows no files and retrieves none; mode_documents is a dead parallel mechanism**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: A mode's files are now tracked in one place: its mode-scoped documents (documents.scope_id). attachedDocumentIds is worked out from that column. DocumentsManager::add no longer writes to mode_documents; it only publishes modes-changed. Duplicate copies each file, with its chunks, to the new mode, so the copy lists them and can retrieve from them. The mode_documents table is left in place but no longer written, because retention.rs, which is not my file, still refers to it.

- **User impact:** A duplicated mode loses its files silently. Two sources of truth for mode attachments invite further bugs.
- **Root cause:** Attachments exist both as documents.scope_id and as the mode_documents join table, and the readers and writers disagree.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:365-384 — duplicate copies mode_documents rows only; src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:120-126 lists docs via documents.list(scope mode, scopeId mode.id); repositories/documents.rs:241-254 filters on documents.scope_id; `src/context/retrieval.ts:106-109 retrieval scopes use scopeId, not mode_documents`; `BlueyMode.attachedDocumentIds (src/lib/types/mode.ts:63) is never read in src`
- **Proposed solution:** Choose one. Simplest: keep documents.scope_id as the truth; on duplicate, copy the document rows (new ids, scope_id = copy id, re-index chunks) or re-point shared docs via a join-based list/retrieve; then drop mode_documents/attachedDocumentIds or derive them.
- **Test plan:** Rust: duplicate a mode with a mode-scoped doc → DocumentRepository::list(Mode, copy.id) is non-empty and retrieval with scope copy.id returns its chunks.
- **Verification (confirmed):** documents/mod.rs:65-72 writes both documents.scope_id and the mode_documents join row. Duplicate copies only the join rows (repositories/modes.rs:365-384). The list (documents.rs:241-254) and retrieval (retrieval.ts:106-109) read scope_id, so the duplicate shows and retrieves no files. Low is right.
- _Source: auditor (modes/duplicate-attachments-illusory)_

#### MODE-011

**Several mode knobs do nothing: the 'Session memory' chip, the 'Accessibility tree' chip when Screen is on, and the Sales competitor detector**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The mode editor offers only context chips that change what a mode gathers: Screen covers the accessibility tree and session memory applies in every mode (commit 5f56615).

- **User impact:** Users tune chips that have no effect and cannot turn off session memory for modes where earlier answers pollute new ones. New custom modes feel 'blind' by default.
- **Root cause:** The requirements were only partly wired into fusion and capture.
- **Evidence:** src/context/fusion.ts:249-261 — recent responses are always added as session_memory; src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:203 session context is unconditional; rg finds no requires(mode, 'session_memory'); src/context/snapshot.ts:69 — `includeAccessibility: includeScreen || requires(mode, "accessibility")`; src/transcript/classifier.ts:19,136 — competitorNames is never supplied by any caller, so competitor_mention is unreachable (Sales instructions and the family bonus expect it); src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/modes.rs:208 — new custom modes start with no context sources, so typed asks capture nothing until the user toggles chips
- **Proposed solution:** Gate the session_memory items in fusion on requires(mode,'session_memory'); either honour the AX chip independently or merge it into Screen; remove competitorNames or source it from a mode field; seed new custom modes with General's requirements.
- **Test plan:** fusion unit test: a mode without session_memory → no session_memory items. snapshot test for AX. ModesTab create → draft includes default requirements.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Session memory is unconditional (fusion.ts:249-261) and competitorNames has no caller (classifier.ts:19,136). Both confirmed. The accessibility chip is implied by screen (snapshot.ts:69), also confirmed. One overstatement: 'typed asks capture nothing' for new custom modes is not accurate, because the HUD screen toggle (HudPanel.tsx:47) still enables screen capture per ask. Low stands.
- _Source: auditor (modes/cosmetic-mode-knobs)_

#### MODE-012

**Switching mode during an ask or a live suggestion does not cancel or invalidate it, and Rust routes by the new mode's model role**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: AiRequest carries preferred_model_role, and Rust routes by the request's role before falling back to the active mode (src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs preferred_role_for). A mode change clears the engine's prepared cache and the prepared hint. A suggestion already in flight finishes with its original mode's role; it is not cancelled.

- **User impact:** Mostly benign. Occasionally an answer built for the old mode (prompt and schema) is routed to the new mode's model role, or a Sales-prepared answer is shown via ⌘⇧↵ after switching to Interview.
- **Root cause:** Mode is resolved on two sides (TS for the prompt, Rust for routing) at different times.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/ModeMenu.tsx:41-46 — setActive only; no subscriber to mode.changed (src/lib/tauri/events.ts:102 typed only); src/stores/proactive.ts:148-153 — prepareFor captures the mode once; engine prepared cache keyed by event id only (src/ai/engine.ts:664-740, TTL 3 min at L137); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:233 — `preferred_role = self.modes.active_mode().preferred_model_role` at routing time; AIRequest (src/ai/request.ts:89-101) carries no mode; `Summary requests are also routed with the active mode's role, not the session's`
- **Proposed solution:** Add `preferredModelRole` (or modeId) to AIRequest and route by it (fallback: active mode). Clear the prepared cache when status.modeId changes.
- **Test plan:** Rust router test using the request's role. Engine test: prepare in mode A, switch to B, takePrepared → null.
- **Verification (confirmed):** src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:233 reads active_mode().preferred_model_role at routing time. AIRequest carries no mode. The prepared-answer cache is keyed by event id only. This is a real race, but it needs a switch during a few-second window. Low is right.
- _Source: auditor (modes/mid-flight-mode-switch)_

#### DOC-002

**docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md disagrees with the code on context sources, validation limits, word counts and live/summary behaviour**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX6: docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md: the built-in table's Context column now matches bluey_core::modes requirements. Every mode includes session_memory; General drops documents; Interview drops screen; System Design adds accessibility; Case adds documents; Recruiting adds screen. The word bounds are 80–220, as the tests enforce. The Summary item now says the summary is generated on demand from the session's starting mode (MODE-008 is still open), with one shared field set plus a per-schema section from src/ai/prompts/summary.ts; General and Recruiting get the common fields only. The lifecycle and limits sections were already right (B1/B2). docs/reference/bluey-sidecar-and-frontend-audit.md is now explicitly marked historical.

- **User impact:** Contributors trust the doc and ship changes against the wrong contract.
- **Root cause:** The doc was not updated alongside code changes.
- **Evidence:** Doc table: General ctx includes 'documents'; code (modes.rs:100-105) has session_memory, not documents. Interview doc lists 'screen'; code lacks screen (L116-121). Case doc 'transcript, screen'; code adds documents (L179-184). Recruiting code adds screen (L211-216); System Design code adds accessibility; Doc: 'validateModeDraft (name 1–48 chars, instructions ≤ 8k chars)'; code: 60 / 4000 and never called (registry.ts:105-117); Doc: '90–180 words each, enforced by tests'; test enforces 80–220 (modes.rs tests); coding is 181 words; Doc 'Classifier … decisions/actions in Team Meeting' and 'Summary structured per mode'; see meeting-lecture-detections-dropped and session-mode-stale-after-switch; recruiting has no summary template (summary.ts:21-37); docs/reference/bluey-sidecar-and-frontend-audit.md:11,320-322 is outdated (it claims Rust is a shell and that the mode editor lacks description, group, format and context sources)
- **Proposed solution:** Regenerate the built-in table from the parity fixture (see ts-duplicates-no-parity-test), fix the limits once validation lands, and annotate the reference doc as historical.
- **Test plan:** Optional doc test comparing the doc table with the fixture.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Spot-checked the doc table against the SPECS in modes.rs (General documents vs session_memory; the 48/8k limits vs 60/4000 in code; '90–180 words' vs the test's 80–220 range). Drift confirmed. Low is right.
- _Source: auditor (modes/mode-system-doc-drift)_

#### MODE-013

**Deleting a custom mode that is the default leaves general.defaultModeId dangling, so deleting the active mode later fails**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-B2: ModeManager::delete (src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs) resets general.defaultModeId to 'general' when the deleted mode was the default. When the deleted mode was the active one, Bluey switches to the default only if that mode still exists, and to 'general' otherwise. The mock does the same.

- **User impact:** The user sets custom mode A as default, switches to custom mode B, and deletes A. A later attempt to delete B while B is active fails with a 'not found' error. The General tab's Default mode selector then points at a mode that no longer exists.
- **Root cause:** The mode delete path does not keep the default-mode setting consistent. The fallback logic trusts the setting without checking that the mode exists.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:128-150 — delete reads default_mode_id only when deleting the ACTIVE mode (`if id == self.active_id()`) and never clears or resets general.defaultModeId when the deleted mode is the default; src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:178-183 — set_active calls ModeRepository::get(fallback) first, which returns not_found for the deleted id, so `self.set_active(fallback).await?` aborts the delete; src-tauri/src/commands/modes.rs:44-46 — set_default validates existence, so the dangling state can only arise through delete; src/features/settings/ModeEditor.tsx:369-375 — the delete confirm surfaces the backend error as a toast; src-tauri/src/modes/mod.rs:39-43 — load() survives a dangling id by falling back to DEFAULT_MODE_ID, so the only visible failures are the delete path and a blank Default select
- **Proposed solution:** In ModeManager::delete, if `settings.general.default_mode_id == id`, reset it to DEFAULT_MODE_ID (via SettingsManager). In the active-mode fallback, check that the fallback exists and otherwise use DEFAULT_MODE_ID.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit test on ModeManager with in-memory storage: create A and B, set_default(A), set_active(B), delete(A), and assert the default is now 'general'. Then delete(B) must succeed and leave active == 'general'. Add a second test with a pre-seeded dangling default: delete of the active custom mode falls back to general.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (modes/dangling-default-mode-after-delete)_

### Context engine

#### CTX-004

**Transcript ring is never scoped to session or time: old conversations leak into new asks and replace the live one after listening restarts**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: New src-tauri/src/audio/ring.rs TranscriptRing: each entry carries run_id (bumped in AudioManager::start) and session_id. recent() returns the current run's finals while listening, otherwise the active session's, with the cutoff measured from that scope's own newest segment. Segments with no session are never returned. The ring is not cleared on start, so list() still works without persistence. Deleting one or all sessions purges their ring entries (src-tauri/src/commands/sessions.rs).

- **User impact:** (a) After the user stops listening, every later ask in a mode that needs the transcript (including the default general mode, typed or ⌘↵) sends the last ~3 minutes of the old meeting to the cloud model, with no time limit until the app quits. (b) After listening restarts, ⌘⇧↵ and live suggestions answer the previous meeting's question, because the whole old ring outranks the new conversation. The old transcript is a privacy leak across sessions, and new sessions get wrong answers.
- **Root cause:** The ring is an app-lifetime cache whose recency window is measured against its own newest element, while segment timestamps restart at 0 on every audio.start. Nothing ties ring entries to the current audio run or session, or to wall-clock time.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:672-682 `recent()`: cutoff = `ring.back().end_time - window`, measured from the newest segment rather than now; no session_id filter; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:240-267 `start()` resets assembler/partials/chunk_times but not `ring`; the ring (capacity 500, :53) is cleared only by clear() (commands/audio.rs:77, commands/data.rs:34, app/dev.rs:104); Segment times are ms since audio.start (SpeechTranscriber.swift:10; docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:77); TranscriptAssembler::assemble (bluey-protocols helper.rs:590-591) copies them without rebasing; bluey-core context.rs:75-87 trim uses the same newest-relative cutoff; fusion.ts:165-166 `nowMs = max(endTime)`; general mode requires Transcript (bluey-core modes.rs:95-99) and snapshot.ts:64 includes the transcript for shortcut_generate/detected_event; Simulation /tmp/bluey-audit/context-engine/s3_ring.ts (faithful port of recent + trim + the real fusion/budget/prompt): after a 20-min session A and a restart, recent(180) returned 243 segments, 200 after trim, 197 from session A; the first 'Recent conversation' line was A's 'What would the enterprise tier cost us for 500 seats?' and the live question 'Why do you want to leave your current job?' became transcript_old (relevance 0.10), rendered under 'Earlier conversation'; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:911-917 commit_final pushes every final segment into `self.ring` (cap 500, L53); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:727 the ring is only cleared by transcript_clear; audio start (L263-268 resets assembler/partials/chunk_times) and mark_stopped do not clear it; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:672-681 recent(): `cutoff = last_end.saturating_sub(window)`; `filter(|s| s.end_time >= cutoff)`, no session filter; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/transcript.rs:25 `/// Milliseconds since audio session start.` (the time base resets on every audio.start); src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:187-199 every snapshot with include_transcript uses core.audio.recent(window); src/context/snapshot.ts:63 includeTranscript = mode requires transcript || shortcut_generate || detected_event; no caller passes transcriptSegments (snapshot.ts:201); src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:256-279 delete and delete_all touch only storage and active state; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:720-731 the ring is purged only by the transcript clear command; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:672-681 recent() feeds ring segments into every transcript-including snapshot (context/mod.rs:187-199); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:699-708 list() serves ring segments when transcripts are not persisted
- **Proposed solution:** Fix at the source in AudioManager:
1. Store the wall-clock commit instant with each ring entry (`VecDeque<(Instant, TranscriptSegment)>`), or keep a `run_id` counter bumped in `start()`.
2. `recent(window)` returns only entries from the current run (or matching `sessions.active_id()`) whose commit instant is within `window` of `Instant::now()`.
3. Also cut the old run off in `start()` by clearing the ring or bumping the run id. The ring is only a cache: persisted transcripts stay in the DB, and `list()` needs the ring only when store_transcripts is off, so filter there by session id.

No TS change is needed once the snapshot is clean. Optionally pass `transcriptNowMs` from the snapshot trace.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit tests in src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs tests:
- Push finals with end_time 1_200_000 (run A), call the start/reset path, push finals with end_time 9_000 (run B): `recent(180)` returns only B.
- After the last commit, advance an injectable clock past the window: `recent(180)` returns [].

Boundary test: `build_snapshot_with` on a fixture AppCore after a simulated restart has transcript segments only from the new session.

TS regression test in tests/unit/context/fusion.test.ts: segments from two time bases never produce a transcript_old item for the newest session.
