- **Verification (confirmed):** Checked audio/mod.rs:672-682: recent() uses a cutoff relative to ring.back().end_time, with no session or run filter. start() (lines 265-267) resets the assembler, partials and chunk_times but not the ring; stop() does not clear it either. Only clear() (719-731) does. After a restart the new segments have small end_times, so saturating_sub gives cutoff 0 and the whole ring (up to 500 segments) comes back. trim_snapshot (bluey-core context.rs:75-87) keeps the last 200 in insertion order and uses the same newest-relative cutoff. fusion.ts:166 takes nowMs = max(endTime), so the old run's larger timestamps look 'recent' and the live run looks 'old'. `transcriptNowMs` is declared (fusion.ts:88) but no caller passes it. The same flaw means an ask made hours after listening stopped still gets the last 180 s of that old conversation in general mode (snapshot.ts:63). Fix: in addition to the proposed run_id/Instant filter, clear or bump the run on start(), and pass a real now through transcriptNowMs.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). I looked for a guard that would stop this and found none, so the finding holds.

1. **`recent()` never filters by session, run or time.** At src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:672-682 the cutoff is `ring.back().end_time - window*1000`, measured from the newest segment, not from now. The only production caller is the context snapshot builder at src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:186-199 (`core.audio.recent(window_seconds)`). It copies the result into `snapshot.transcript` with no session check of its own.
2. **Neither `start()` nor `stop()` empties the ring.**
   - `start()` (mod.rs:265-267) resets only the assembler, partials and chunk_times.
   - `stop()` (mod.rs:576-590) and `mark_stopped()` reset only the assembler, partials and config, then end the auto-session. The ring survives.
   - The only calls that empty the ring are `clear()` (mod.rs:719-731) from `audio_clear` (commands/audio.rs:77), `data_clear_transcripts` (commands/data.rs:34) and dev.rs:104. All three are explicit user or dev actions.
3. **Segment timestamps restart at zero on every run.** PCMChunker.swift:5 says positions are on the "milliseconds since audio.start" clock, and SpeechTranscriber.swift:49 counts frames "since audio.start". A new run's segments therefore have small `end_time`s, `saturating_sub` gives a cutoff of 0, and the whole old ring (up to 500 segments) comes back.
4. **Nothing in the TypeScript layer corrects it.** fusion.ts:165-166 sets `nowMs` to the largest `endTime`. `transcriptNowMs` is declared at fusion.ts:88 but no caller passes it (rg finds only the declaration and its use). The old run's larger timestamps win, so the live run is scored as old.
5. **Users reach this path in normal use.** snapshot.ts:63 includes the transcript whenever the mode requires it, or when the trigger is `shortcut_generate` or `detected_event`. The default general mode requires Transcript (bluey-core modes.rs:95-99).
   - Scenario (a): stop listening, then ask anything typed in general mode, any time until the app quits. The last ~180 s of the finished meeting goes to the model.
   - Scenario (b): stop, start a new meeting, press ⌘⇧↵. The prompt is dominated by the previous meeting.

**Why High, not Critical.**
- The first listening run after each launch works correctly.
- The leaked text is the user's own audio, sent to the provider they already chose, not to a third party. It crosses a session boundary the user expects, but it is not secret exposure.
- The live segments are still in the prompt, only ranked down under "Earlier conversation", so answers are degraded rather than always wrong.

It is still a significant bug users will hit: a menu-bar copilot often spans several meetings in one app run, and the only workaround is quitting the app or clearing transcripts. Fix critique: The proposed direction is right, but it should not start by clearing the ring in `start()`.

**Smallest root-cause fix:** make `recent()` return nothing unless audio is running or the segments belong to the active session.
- Add a `run_id: AtomicU64` that `start()` bumps.
- Store the id next to each ring entry.
- In `recent()`, filter to the current run and compute the cutoff over that run's segments only.

A companion filter on `sessions.active_id()` also works: `stop()` ends the auto-session (mod.rs:584-588), so after a stop there is no active id and nothing is returned. That fixes scenario (a).

**Why not clear the ring in `start()`:** `list()` (mod.rs:699-716) reads the ring whenever `store_transcripts` is off. Clearing it on start would silently delete the previous session's transcript from the transcript view. That is a behaviour change needing a product decision. Filtering in `recent()` avoids it.

**Wall-clock bound after stop:** storing an `Instant` per entry is optional once the run filter exists. Whether an ask made shortly after stopping should still see the just-finished meeting (for example "summarise what was said") needs a product decision. If yes, keep the last run but bound it with an `Instant` TTL instead of returning it forever.

**Keep the TS change optional.** Passing `transcriptNowMs` alone would not fix the problem: the old run's timestamps are larger, so the old segments would still look recent. The fix belongs in Rust.

**Tests:** the proposed Rust unit test (run A with large end_times, restart, run B with small ones, expect `recent()` to return only B) is the right boundary test. Add a case where `recent()` after `stop()` returns empty, or TTL-bounded if that is the product choice. One more test should drive `build_snapshot_with` or the audio start/stop path, not just a mock ring, so it catches regressions in the real call path.

The fix touches src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs only. It is independent of other work and does not need real-macOS verification.
- **Also reported as:** data-sessions-history/transcript-ring-cross-session-bleed, data-sessions-history/session-delete-does-not-purge-transcript-ring
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/transcript-ring-cross-session)_

#### CTX-005

**Live-detected question is never rendered as the 'Current question' the task line points to**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: Added the new 'detected_question' ContextSource (src/lib/types/context.ts, after user_instruction). When an ask has no typed instruction, src/context/fusion.ts turns the detected event into one item, '<speaker ?? Speaker>: <text>' with ref event:<id> and relevance 1. The budget never drops it and its transcript segment is not repeated. The label 'Question just asked (heard; may be mis-transcribed)' is in src/ai/prompts/labels.ts, placed first after user_instruction in SECTION_ORDER. The detected_event task line in src/ai/prompts/task.ts points at that label.

- **User impact:** Live suggestions (the proactive interview/sales loop) point the model at a section that does not exist. The model must guess which of several recent questions to answer. Both saturate at relevance 0.95 and the older one is listed first, so answers to the previous or wrong question are likely.
- **Root cause:** The detected event is threaded into PromptBuilder but only the typed instruction is turned into a context item. The task-line wording assumed both paths.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:49 `detectedEvent?: DetectedEvent` is never read in renderContext (84-108), renderTask (111-115) or buildMessages (118-135); src/context/fusion.ts:151-160 only `opts.instruction`/`snapshot.userInstruction` becomes a `user_instruction` item; currentQuestionText (94-103) uses the event only for OCR scoring; src/ai/prompts/task.ts detected_event: 'Answer the question just asked in the live conversation (see "Current question")'; `src/stores/proactive.ts:167-178 prepare passes detectedEvent with no instruction`; engine.ts:319-326 retrieval query also ignores detectedEvent (retrieval.ts:61 uses currentQuestionText(snapshot, {})); s1_detected.ts: `has 'Current question' section: false`; the rendered transcript lists the older question 'Can you walk me through a project…' before the detected 'How did you handle exactly-once delivery…'; src/ai/prompts/task.ts:19-20 — detected_event task: 'Answer the question just asked in the live conversation (see "Current question")'; src/context/fusion.ts:150-159 — only opts.instruction / snapshot.userInstruction becomes user_instruction; opts.detectedEvent is used only to compute relevance (currentQuestionText); `src/ai/proactive.ts:148-190 — prepare() passes detectedEvent and no instruction`; out/04-behavioral-live-suggestion.txt — no '### Current question' section; the question appears only as a transcript line (relevance-hoisted)
- **Proposed solution:** In fuseContext: when there is no instruction and `opts.detectedEvent` is set, push a `user_instruction` item with content `${event.speaker ?? 'Speaker'}: ${event.text}`, relevance 1, ref `event:${event.id}`, and skip the transcript items whose ids are in `event.segmentIds` to avoid duplication. In engine.ts, pass `query: input.instruction ?? input.detectedEvent?.text` (or the event text into buildRetrievalQuery) so retrieval targets the same question.
- **Test plan:** - tests/unit/context/fusion.test.ts: detected event with no instruction yields exactly one user_instruction item with the event text, and its segment is not duplicated.
- tests/unit/ai/prompt-builder.test.ts: trigger detected_event renders '### Current question\nInterviewer: <text>'.
- tests/integration/prepare-flow.test.ts: assert on the captured ai_stream request user text (it has no prompt assertions today).
- **Verification (confirmed):** prompt-builder.ts:49 declares detectedEvent, but renderContext, renderTask and buildMessages (84-135) never read it. fusion.ts:151-160 creates a user_instruction item only from opts.instruction or snapshot.userInstruction. proactive.ts:166-176 calls prepare with detectedEvent and no instruction, and task.ts:20 points the model at 'Current question'. Partly mitigated: the question segment usually sits in the transcript with the question boost, but ties at the 0.95 cap make which question the model answers ambiguous. The retrieval part of the claim is weaker than stated: buildRetrievalQuery uses the last question in the transcript, which is usually the detected text. The proposed fix is right.
- **Also reported as:** prompt-stack/detected-event-question-not-rendered
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/detected-event-question-not-rendered)_

#### CTX-006

**Conversation is rendered in relevance order (questions hoisted, rest newest-first), not the order it was spoken**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: ContextItem gains an optional `at` field (segment start / turn time), which fusion sets on transcript items. src/ai/prompt-builder.ts now renders the transcript and transcript_old buckets sorted by `at` ascending, with transcript_old directly before the recent turns. Budget selection is unchanged (still by relevance).

- **User impact:** ⌘⇧↵ ('what do I say next'), live suggestions and follow-ups rely on who said what, in what order. The model sees answers after questions they did not answer and questions grouped out of order. References like 'that migration' point the wrong way, and ⌘⇧↵ cannot reliably tell what the last turn was.
- **Root cause:** Relevance is used both to choose what fits the budget and to order the output. Transcript items carry no timestamp or order field for the renderer.
- **Evidence:** src/context/fusion.ts:113-114 each segment's relevance = age decay with a question boost capped at 0.95; fusion.ts:274-278 sorts all items by relevance; src/context/budget.ts:110-118 re-sorts by priority tier then relevance; included order = that order; `src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:88-100 groups by source and joins each bucket in included order`; s1_detected.ts rendered order: 'Interviewer: Can you walk me through…' / 'Interviewer: Great. How did you handle…' / 'You: Yes, I led the migration…' / 'You: Sure…' / 'Interviewer: Thanks for joining…' (actual order a,b,c,d,e); src/context/fusion.ts:275-279 sorts all items by relevance; transcript items are per segment (fusion.ts:161-171); src/context/budget.ts:110-118 re-sorts non-instruction items by (priority, relevance desc); `src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:88-100 joins each source's items in array order`; out/09-long-transcript-order.txt: 12 turns 10 s apart render as 'Speaker: turn 10 — hardest bug?', 'You: turn 11', 'You: turn 9', 'Speaker: turn 8' … 'Speaker: turn 0'; out/02-interview-cmd-shift-enter-spoken.txt: 'Speaker: …why do you want to work at Acme…' / 'You: Happy to be here…' / 'Speaker: Thanks for joining…' / 'You: Hi…' (reverse chronological)
- **Proposed solution:** Add an optional `at?: number` (segment startTime) to ContextItem, set in transcriptItem(). In PromptBuilder.renderContext, sort the `transcript` and `transcript_old` buckets by `at` ascending before joining. Budget selection stays relevance-based, so the oldest segments are still dropped first. Keep the Rust ContextItem type in sync if it is serialized (bluey-core types/context.rs).
- **Test plan:** - prompt-builder test: 5 transcript items given in shuffled relevance order render in chronological order.
- budget test: with a small budget the dropped items are the oldest, and the rendered survivors stay chronological.
- Pipeline test (engine with a fixture snapshot): the last rendered transcript line is the newest segment.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Chain confirmed: fusion.ts:274-278 sorts by relevance, budget.ts:110-118 sorts by priority then relevance, and prompt-builder.ts:88-100 joins buckets in array order. Nothing reorders by time in between. Two independent earlier verification passes (/tmp/bluey-audit/verify-transcript-order, refute-transcript-order) reproduced it. Fix correction: sorting within each bucket by `at` is not enough. SECTION_ORDER (labels.ts) renders transcript_old last, after session memory, so a question can end up split from its answer. Render transcript_old immediately before transcript, and put the earlier-summary item first.
- **Also reported as:** prompt-stack/transcript-rendered-by-relevance-not-time
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/transcript-rendered-by-relevance)_

#### CTX-007

**Follow-ups lose the previous answer without an active session, and never see the previous code or question**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: The chat turns now become conversation memory whether or not a session is active. The last two turns render as 'Q: ...\nA: ...' (the newest keeps its code block, capped) and older turns as short summaries. They use a new 'conversation' source labelled 'Earlier in this chat', rendered before the question. Session memory no longer repeats these turns. enrichSnapshot in src/context/snapshot.ts keeps the responses loaded from the DB when the UI passes none. A follow-up with no earlier turn falls back to the typed task line. Files: src/context/fusion.ts, snapshot.ts, labels.ts, types/context.ts.

- **User impact:** The core chat loop (⌘↵ then 'make it shorter', 'rewrite in Go', 'why O(n)?') fails for anyone not inside a session, which is the normal casual use. The model answers blind. Even in a session it cannot see the code it wrote or the question it answered, so coding follow-ups are regenerated from scratch or guessed.
- **Root cause:** Conversation memory depends on the session concept instead of the chat thread. The memory projection was designed as short summaries for proactive context, not for follow-ups.
- **Evidence:** src/context/snapshot.ts:194-200 session context (and so recentResponses) is built only `if (session)`; Rust session_context is None without a session (src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:256-257); Sessions start only explicitly (src/features/hud/session-actions.ts:27) or when listening (audio/mod.rs:315-318); src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:131-134 sends only [system, user]; no assistant history anywhere in src/ai; snapshot.ts:113-114, 131-141: last 5 responses, content cut to 320 chars; `code` (set separately at engine.ts:555) and `prompt` are dropped; fusion.ts:250-261 renders `${title}: ${content}`, newest-first; src/stores/proactive.ts:167-178 passes no previousResponses, so snapshot.ts:131 `previousResponses ?? []` overwrites the DB responses Rust loaded; s2_followup.ts: 'Can you rewrite your solution in Go?' with a previous Two-Sum answer → prevAnswerInPrompt=false (no session); with a session prevAnswer is truncated, prevCodeInPrompt=false, previous question absent; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:44-55 — previousResponses = completedResponses(chat.turns) passed to engine.ask; src/context/snapshot.ts:194-199 — `if (session) { enriched.session = toSessionContext(session, previousResponses, …) }`: previousResponses are dropped when there is no session; src/context/fusion.ts:250-258 — session_memory only from snapshot.session.recentResponses (title+content; the user's earlier question is never rendered); Sessions start only from SessionMenu (src/features/hud/session-actions.ts:27) or when listening starts (src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:315-317); /tmp/bluey-audit/prompt-stack/out/10-follow-up-no-session.txt — user message is only '### Current question\nwhy not B?' + 'Task: Answer this follow-up. Build on the earlier responses in this session…' (task.ts:17-18)
- **Proposed solution:** 1. Build the conversation memory from chat turns whether or not a session is active: make `toSessionContext` accept an optional session, or add a separate `thread` item source.
2. For the last 1-2 turns render `Q: <prompt>\nA: <content ≤800 chars>` plus a fenced `code` (≤1,500 chars), oldest first; older turns keep the 320-char summary.
3. In proactive.prepareFor, pass `completedResponses(chat.turns)` so live suggestions stay consistent with earlier answers.
- **Test plan:** - snapshot.test: enrichSnapshot with session=null and previousResponses keeps them.
- fusion/prompt-builder test: follow_up renders the previous prompt, the previous code block and the answer, in chronological order.
- Integration test on the engine with the fake transport: second ask with trigger follow_up; the user text contains the previous code.
- **Verification (confirmed):** snapshot.ts:194-200 builds session context only when a session exists. The Rust session_context is None without a session. buildMessages sends only [system, user]. An earlier refute pass also confirmed it. Nuance: in screen-requiring modes (general, coding, system-design) a follow-up still captures a fresh screen, so follow-ups that refer to the screen are partly mitigated, but pure refinements ('make it shorter', 'in Go') are blind. AIMessage already supports the assistant role, so appending the last 1-2 turns as real multi-turn messages is an alternative fix.
- **Also reported as:** prompt-stack/follow-up-without-session-has-no-history
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/followup-loses-prior-turn)_

#### CTX-008

**Interview modes retrieve the résumé only by word overlap; canonical questions ('Tell me about yourself', 'biggest weakness') get no résumé**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: Candidate modes now pin the résumé's leading chunks (about 800 tokens, via the new `leading` strategy) when the question matched none of the résumé or is a standard intro/behavioural question. It is never pinned outside candidate modes. The Rust side is the new RetrievalStrategy::Leading in bluey-storage retrieve.rs and bluey-core types/documents.rs, which returns the first chunks per document in scope order without matching the query.

- **User impact:** In the flagship interview modes, the most common questions are answered with no background from the user's own résumé. The answers come out generic or with invented experience, which is the opposite of the product promise.
- **Root cause:** Résumés are small, always-relevant documents in candidate modes, but they go through the same query-matched top-k retrieval as a large knowledge base. There is no pinned or always-include path.
- **Evidence:** src/context/retrieval.ts:116 strategy 'keyword' unless embeddings are on; embeddings_enabled defaults to false (bluey-core types/settings.rs:284); src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/fts.rs:5-40 terms are OR-ed after a stop list; retrieve.rs:155-216 returns only FTS matches; `migrations/0001_init.sql:101-106 fts5 tokenize 'porter unicode61'`; s8_fts.ts (bun:sqlite FTS5, same tokenizer and stop list, realistic résumé/JD/personal docs): 'Tell me about yourself.' → nothing; 'What's your biggest weakness?' → nothing; 'Tell me about a time you failed.' → nothing; 'Why should we hire you?' → JD only; 'Why do you want to leave your current job?' → resume#2
- **Proposed solution:** Add pinned kinds to the retrieval query, e.g. `RetrievalQuery.pinnedKinds` or a TS-side second call. For candidate modes, pin 'resume|cv|experience|skills' (first K chunks by chunk_index, capped around 800 tokens, score floor 0.5), and add matched chunks on top. Personal instructions are handled the same way (see personal-instructions-not-loaded). Alternatively turn on semantic retrieval by default when an embedding model is configured.
- **Test plan:** - Rust test in retrieve.rs: query 'Tell me about yourself' with pinned resume kind returns the résumé's first chunk(s) even with zero FTS hits.
- TS retrieval.test: interview mode builds a query with pinned kinds.
- Integration test (retrieval-scopes.test.ts): the rendered prompt contains a 'Your background (resume)' section for a behavioral question with no word overlap.
- **Depends on:** personal-instructions-not-loaded
- **Verification (confirmed):** fts.rs:19-40 drops stop words and ORs the remaining terms. keyword_candidates returns only FTS matches, and nothing pins or always includes documents. 'Tell me about yourself' reduces to tell/me/about/yourself, which résumés rarely contain. Whether a given résumé gets hit is fixture-dependent: the OCR headline and the last transcript question are also added to the query (retrieval.ts:61-66) and can match by accident. Coding-interview has no Resume requirement at all (modes.rs:146-149), so it never retrieves a résumé. The pinned-kinds fix is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/resume-retrieval-lexical-only)_

#### CTX-009

**Screenshot is withheld for charts/diagrams whenever browser AX chrome adds ~80+ chars, because vision is decided by a raw OCR+AX char count**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-B1: The vision gate in src/context/relevance.ts now judges whether the text is enough using OCR text only. Accessibility text is mostly app chrome, so it no longer counts. On ⌘↵ and assist, where the screen is the subject, visual cues on screen (chart, graph, diagram, figure, legend, axis and similar) now require the image. AI_ARCHITECTURE.md intent paragraph updated. Commit 3c3da46. Device check: Spot-check on a real screen that a chart or diagram with plenty of axis-label OCR still sends the image.

- **User impact:** ⌘↵ on a chart, graph, diagram, UI mockup or image-based question in a browser or any AX-rich app sends only OCR fragments (axis labels, legend). The model cannot read bar heights or diagram structure and will guess. The screenshot was already captured, so withholding it saves nothing on capture.
- **Root cause:** The heuristic treats 'enough text' as 'the screen is textual'. AX chrome text is irrelevant to that question, and visual cues are only looked for in the question, never on the screen.
- **Evidence:** src/context/relevance.ts:302-307 `textAvailable = ocrText.length + axText.length`; `visionRequired = hasScreen && (textAvailable < 200 || VISUAL_REFERENCE_CUES.test(question) || codingLowOcr)`; relevance.ts:268-271 for shortcut_capture with no instruction, `question` is '' or a transcript line; the OCR text is never checked for visual content; engine.ts:377-383 the image is attached only when visionRequired, although it was already captured and inlined (snapshot.ts:72); s6_vision.ts: chart question, OCR only (123 chars) → image sent; the same screen plus 186 chars of browser AX chrome → no image; typed 'which region grew fastest?' → no image; 'what does this chart show' → image; src/context/relevance.ts:300-307: visionRequired = hasScreen && (ocr+ax < 200 chars || VISUAL_REFERENCE_CUES.test(question) || coding with low OCR confidence); the visual-cue regex is applied to the question only, never to the screen text; src/ai/engine.ts:378-383: the image is attached only when intent.visionRequired; no setting overrides it (rg visionRequired in src); Probe /tmp/bluey-audit/prompt-stack-verify/vis.ts: ⌘↵ on a quiz reading 'The velocity-time graph above… Which of the following graphs correctly shows its position over time? A. B. C. D.' (258 OCR chars) gives task=answer, shape=choice, vision=false
- **Proposed solution:** In classifyIntent:
1. Measure sufficiency on OCR text only (drop axText from textAvailable).
2. For shortcut_capture/assist, also test a screen-side cue regex on OCR text (chart|graph|figure|diagram|axis|legend|plot|Q[1-4] sequences, many short numeric tokens) or low OCR density (few blocks for the frame area).
3. Optionally always attach the image on shortcut_capture when the routed model supports vision and OCR has fewer than N lines.
- **Test plan:** - relevance.test: chart OCR plus 200 chars of AX chrome on shortcut_capture → visionRequired true; a dense article on shortcut_capture → false.
- Integration capture-to-request: the chart fixture yields an image part in the request.
- Manual check on macOS with a real browser chart.
- **Verification (adjusted):** relevance.ts:302-307 confirmed: textAvailable = ocr + ax < 200, a visual cue in the question, or low-confidence OCR on a coding task. The scope is broader than stated: AX chrome is not needed to trigger the problem, because OCR alone for a typical chart or dashboard (title, axis labels, legend, browser tabs) easily exceeds 200 chars. So dropping axText from the count (fix step 1) does not fix it on its own. The screen-side cue and density check (step 2), or always attaching the image on shortcut_capture when the model supports vision, is the real fix. engine.ts:377-383 confirms the image is captured and inlined but then discarded.
- **Also reported as:** prompt-stack/vision-gate-ignores-screen-visual-cues
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/vision-routing-char-count)_

#### CTX-010

**A failed screen capture (e.g. Screen Recording not granted, or helper down) fails the whole snapshot, so every ask in a screen-requiring mode errors instead of falling back to text-only context**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-C: A failed screen capture (Screen Recording not granted, or the helper is down) no longer fails the whole snapshot. build_snapshot in src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs keeps the accessibility tree, the transcript and the app identity, and sets screen=None. It adds a structured SnapshotWarning {kind: screen_unavailable, code, message, recovery} to the new `warnings` field (bluey-core types/context.rs and the TS types), so the UI can offer the Screen Recording pane. The mock transport does the same when the permission is denied. Device check: Deny Screen Recording on a real Mac and confirm the ask still answers from the accessibility tree and transcript. Also confirm the warning reaches the HUD: there is no UI yet that shows the Open Settings action, and that belongs to the UI workstream.

- **User impact:** If Screen Recording is missing or was reset (likely after each ad-hoc-signed auto-update), even a plain typed question like 'What is TCP?' in General mode fails with a capture error instead of being answered from the transcript, AX and the question itself.
- **Root cause:** The capture branch of the parallel gather propagates errors with `?`, unlike the AX and OCR branches, and nothing on the TS side degrades gracefully.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:107 `None => core.capture.capture(options.capture.clone()).await?` then :136 `captured?`: a capture error returns from build_snapshot, while AX (:98-99 `result.ok()`) and OCR (:127 warn and continue) are soft failures; src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Capture/ShareableContent.swift:16 returns `.permissionDenied("screenRecording", ...)`; src/context/snapshot.ts:61-62 includeScreen = requires(mode,'screen'), which is true for general/coding/system-design/case/recruiting/lecture (modes.rs:96,146,163,180,213,243); src/ai/engine.ts:294-301 awaits buildNativeSnapshot with no try/catch or retry without the screen; Installed builds are ad-hoc signed, so the TCC grant is likely lost on every update, which makes the denied state common; context/mod.rs:136 `let (frame, ocr, ...) = captured?;` drops the AX and transcript that were already gathered; `OCR errors are tolerated (L127-129) but capture errors are not`; ScreenCaptureService.swift:34-37 returns display_not_found for a stale displayId; capture/mod.rs:140-144 passes the stored preferred display id without falling back; `sidecar per-method timeout for capture.* is 3 s`
- **Proposed solution:** In context/mod.rs, handle the capture error like AX and OCR: log it, set frame=None, and record the failure in snapshot.trace or a `screen_unavailable` field. Fail hard only when the trigger is shortcut_capture or captureScreen is explicitly true, where the screen is the point of the ask. Optionally add a short fused note ('screen unavailable: permission') so the model and the UI can surface it.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit test: build_snapshot with a capture stub returning permissionDenied and include_screen=true returns Ok with screen=None and AX/transcript present. TS integration test: runPipeline in general mode with a failing capture still streams an answer. Manually on real macOS, revoke Screen Recording and ask a typed question in General.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- **Also reported as:** capture-audio-transcription/capture-error-fails-whole-snapshot
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (context-engine/screen-capture-failure-fails-whole-ask)_

#### CTX-011

**Rust removes OCR lines that duplicate AX text, but intent/shape detection reads only OCR, so coding and multiple-choice screens are misclassified**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-B1: Classification (task, answer shape and markers) in relevance.ts now reads OCR, the accessibility visible text and the focused element's value together, not OCR alone. Commit 6b2d098.

- **User impact:** In apps whose AX tree exposes the same short lines (native apps, Electron, web views within the depth-6 walk), a coding problem gets a prose answer with no code schema. A multiple-choice question loses the 'answer with the letter first' shape. How often this happens depends on how much content AX exposes (see verification debt).
- **Root cause:** Rust applies the size hygiene step before TS classifies. The classifier was written assuming OCR holds all screen text.
- **Evidence:** bluey-core context.rs:46-49, 94-114 `dedupe_ocr_against` deletes every OCR line that equals an AX visible_text line, before TS sees it; src/context/relevance.ts:278 `codingVisible` uses ocrText; :283 `detectAssessmentShape(question, ocrText)`; :315 `detectAnswerShape({screenText: ocrText})`; axText is used only for the vision character count (:303); s5_intent.ts: LeetCode OCR only → coding/coding/code; the same lines also in AX (OCR emptied by the dedupe port) → answer/answer/explain; MCQ in OCR → choice; MCQ only in AX → explain
- **Proposed solution:** In classifyIntent, use the screen text as the union of OCR, AX visibleText and the focused element value for codingVisible, detectAssessmentShape and detectAnswerShape (cap it for regex cost). Alternatively invert the Rust dedupe to drop AX lines already in OCR, but the classifier fix is smaller and makes the classifier robust whatever Rust hygiene does.
- **Test plan:** - relevance.test: OCR '' plus AX containing an MCQ → answerShape 'choice'; OCR '' plus AX focused value containing code and a problem statement → task 'coding'.
- Keep the existing OCR-only cases green.
- **Verification (confirmed):** context.rs:46-49 and 94-114 remove OCR lines whose trimmed text exactly equals an AX line. relevance.ts:278, 283 and 315 read only ocrText. The code chain is verified, but the real-world effect depends on how many OCR lines exactly match AX lines. OCR wrapping usually differs, and Chrome exposes web AX content only on demand, so emptying the OCR entirely is a worst case. Short lines like 'Example 1:' or 'Input: nums = ...' can match. The fix (classify on the union of OCR and AX) is cheap and correct.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/ocr-ax-dedupe-starves-classifier)_

#### PERF-005

**The same on-screen text is sent up to three times (focused value, AX visible text, OCR), and window text is mislabelled 'Focused UI'**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: The accessibility window text is now its own window_text item. Lines already in the OCR, the focused value or the title are removed (compared ignoring whitespace and case), and the whole item is skipped when less than 20% of it is new. The focused value is capped at 1500 chars, and 'Focused UI' now carries only the focused element and the selection. File: src/context/fusion.ts.

- **User impact:** More input tokens mean higher TTFT and cost on every screen-aware ask. The 'Focused UI' label tells the model that sidebar and chrome text is what the user is working on, which misdirects answers.
- **Root cause:** Three independent producers of the same text are merged with exact-match dedupe only. The label table has one label for all AX content.
- **Evidence:** AXSnapshotService.swift:12-13, 118, 139-150: the focused element value (≤4000 chars) plus every element value/label (≤1000 each) are joined into visibleText (≤8000); fusion.ts:212-225 renders 'Focused element: role — label — value'; fusion.ts:227-235 adds visibleText unless it is *identical* to OCR; bluey-core context.rs:94-114 dedupes only exact whole lines, so wrapped paragraphs and code lines differ; labels.ts: accessibility → 'Focused UI', so browser chrome, sidebars and menus appear as 'Focused UI'; s4_dup.ts: after Rust dedupe, 57% of AX visible words are still present in OCR; s7: 2,009 AX tokens + 3,000 OCR tokens for an unrelated question
- **Proposed solution:** 1. In fusion.ts, near-duplicate filter: drop AX visibleText lines whose normalized text is contained in the OCR text or in the focused value (normalize whitespace and case), and skip the visibleText item if less than 20% of it is new.
2. Give AX visibleText its own label, e.g. prefix 'Window text (accessibility):' or add a source, keeping 'Focused UI' for focused/selected.
3. Cap the focused value to about 1,500 chars around the selection/caret when available.
- **Test plan:** - fusion.test: AX visibleText that wraps OCR paragraphs differently produces no second copy.
- fusion.test: focused value lines already in OCR are not duplicated.
- Measure the token delta with the s4/s7 fixtures (expect a reduction of more than 40%).
- **Verification (confirmed):** fusion.ts:212-235: the focused element value, the AX visibleText (skipped only when exactly equal to the OCR text) and the OCR text are all emitted. The Rust dedupe is exact-line only. This mainly inflates tokens and cost; it does not break answers. Medium is fair.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/screen-text-sent-multiple-times)_

#### PERF-006

**Relevance scores never gate inclusion: an unrelated typed question ships ~6k tokens of screen, AX and transcript**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: A typed ask or follow-up with no screen cue and no keyword in common with the OCR or accessibility text now drops the OCR, the window text and all but the last two transcript turns. It keeps the focused/selected accessibility element. This never applies to a heard (detected) question. File: src/context/fusion.ts.

- **User impact:** Every typed question in the default mode pays capture, OCR and AX latency plus several thousand irrelevant input tokens: slower first token, higher cost, a distracted model. Whatever is on screen (chats, email, documents) is sent to the cloud with unrelated questions.
- **Root cause:** The budget allocator is used as a packer, not a selector. The relevance scores exist but only order items.
- **Evidence:** src/context/budget.ts:132-164 includes every item that fits; no relevance floor and no per-source cap; `fusion.ts:188 OCR base relevance 0.35 even with zero overlap; AX visible fixed 0.55`; snapshot.ts:62-73 screen, OCR, AX and the inlined image are captured on every ask in a mode that requires screen (general does: modes.rs:95-99); s7_volume.ts with the real default budget (12,000 → 10,600 tokens): 'What's the difference between TCP and UDP?' plus a dense Slack screen and a stale meeting transcript → user message ~6,034 tokens (OCR 3,000, AX 2,009, transcript 908); the question is 0.18%; nothing dropped; Image tokens are not counted in the budget (engine.ts:377-383 attaches after allocateBudget)
- **Proposed solution:** 1. Add a relevance floor for typed/follow_up asks: when the instruction has no screen cue (VISUAL_REFERENCE_CUES, 'this', 'here', 'on screen') and keyword overlap with OCR/AX is 0, keep only the focused/selected AX items and an OCR headline (≤300 tokens).
2. Port per-source caps from the Rust policy (OCR ≤40%, AX ≤30%, transcript ≤50%) into the TS BudgetPolicy.
3. Subtract an image-token estimate when vision is attached.

A larger product option is to capture the screen for typed asks only when screen cues are present.
- **Test plan:** - budget.test: an unrelated instruction with 3k-token OCR leaves at most N OCR tokens.
- budget.test: per-source cap enforced.
- Integration: the s7 fixture's user message is under 1,500 tokens while a 'what's on my screen' instruction still includes the OCR.
- **Verification (confirmed):** budget.ts:132-164 includes every item that fits, with no relevance floor and no per-source cap. OCR relevance starts at 0.35 (fusion.ts:187). The image is attached after budgeting (engine.ts:378) and is not counted. Medium is correct: this costs latency, cost and focus rather than being a hard failure.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/relevance-never-gates-inclusion)_

#### FEATURE-002

**'Smart' screen observation runs a capture stream but nothing consumes screen.changed: no precompute, no proactive preparation**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: Smart observation is shown as not yet available (disabled, with honest copy) and the backend no longer starts the observation stream for it; an old stored value still loads (commit 2a57bb9).

- **User impact:** Users who turn on Smart observation pay for a continuous screen stream and a larger privacy footprint and get nothing in return.
- **Root cause:** The helper and Rust halves were built; the consumer (precompute or proactive screen classifier) never was.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/ScreenTab.tsx:62-63 'Smart observation samples your screen on an interval so Bluey can prepare answers proactively'; `src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:393-397 and settings/side_effects.rs:57 call observe_start`; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:211-217 publishes BlueyEvent::ScreenChanged; `rg ScreenChanged` finds no subscriber; src/lib/tauri/events.ts:59 declares the type only; The ask path uses changeDetection:false (snapshot.ts:84), so the OCR cache is never reused either; docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:19-25 claims observation 'refreshes the cached context so ⌘↵ is faster and proactive preparation has fresh OCR'; ScreenObserver.swift emits screen.changed; sidecar/mod.rs:211-217 republishes it on the bus; rg 'screen.changed|ScreenChanged' finds only type definitions and events.ts:59,143; no store or engine listener; `CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:24-26 says observation refreshes cached context so ⌘↵ is faster`; side_effects.rs:55-58 `let _ = core.capture.observe_start(...)` swallows start errors; ScreenObserver didStopWithError only logs; docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:22-25 — "observation only refreshes the cached context so ⌘↵ is faster and proactive preparation has fresh OCR"; src/features/settings/tabs/ScreenTab.tsx:62-63 — "samples your screen on an interval so Bluey can prepare answers proactively"; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:393-396 and src-tauri/src/settings/side_effects.rs:50-57 start observation; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:211-217 publishes BlueyEvent::ScreenChanged; src/lib/tauri/events.ts:59,143 type-only; no subscriber in src/stores, src/ai or the capture/context managers; `docs/SECURITY.md:101-103 warm-frame reuse of the observation frame is not built`
- **Proposed solution:** Short term: hide the option or label it 'coming soon', and fix the docs. Real fix: subscribe in Rust to ScreenChanged. On a material change (debounced), run capture plus OCR in the background and keep `last_ocr` keyed by hash. On the ask path, pass `changeDetection:true` so the fresh cached OCR is reused when the frame hash matches.
- **Test plan:** - Rust test: a ScreenChanged event triggers a background OCR and populates last_ocr.
- Ask-path test: an unchanged hash reuses the cached OCR.
- Manual macOS check: ⌘↵ latency with Smart on vs off.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The only TS references to screen.changed are the type and the EVENT_NAMES list (events.ts:59,143); no store subscribes. In Rust, sidecar/mod.rs:217 publishes it, the audio/mod.rs:876 match ignores it, and nothing else consumes it. The ask path hardcodes changeDetection:false (snapshot.ts:84). ScreenTab.tsx:63 and CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:21-24 promise fresher context and proactive preparation. So the setting costs energy and privacy and gives nothing back.
- **Also reported as:** capture-audio-transcription/smart-observation-dead-end, prior-audit-docs-drift/smart-observation-dead-end
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/smart-observation-dead)_

#### CTX-012

**Active app, window title and adapter hints are collected but never reach the prompt**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: When screen context was captured, fusion now adds one compact active_app item of about 20 tokens (e.g. 'App: Google Chrome (chrome) — Window: Two Sum - LeetCode'), labelled as the app identity rather than 'Focused UI'. It is left out when there is no screen context (screen off, or a text-only mode). File: src/context/fusion.ts, with the label in labels.ts.

- **User impact:** The model is never told 'you are looking at VS Code, file foo.ts', 'Chrome — leetcode.com/problems/two-sum' or 'in a Zoom meeting'. This cheap, high-signal context (about 20 tokens) would clarify ambiguous screens and questions.
- **Root cause:** The context producer was built without a fusion consumer.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:142-148 sets active_application/active_window; bluey-core context.rs:137-193 detect_adapter/apply_adapter sets adapter plus hints (meetingDetected, codingContext, browserTitle); `rg 'activeApplication|activeWindow|adapter|hints' src` (non-mock, non-types) = 0 context consumers; fusion.ts never reads them; `docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:42-44 'Adapters enrich context'`; s4_dup.ts: 'Google Chrome' and the page title appear only when they happen to be in the AX chrome text
- **Proposed solution:** In fuseContext, when `snapshot.activeApplication`/`activeWindow` exist, push an `accessibility` item such as 'App: Google Chrome — Window: Two Sum - LeetCode (browser)' with relevance about 0.7 and ~20 tokens, labelled so it renders first in the screen block. Optionally use the codingContext hint in classifyIntent.
- **Test plan:** - fusion.test: a snapshot with activeApplication and activeWindow yields one app/window item.
- prompt-builder test: it renders under the screen section.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Running rg for activeApplication/activeWindow/adapter/hints in src/context and src/ai finds no consumer. fusion.ts builds items only from instruction, transcript, OCR, AX, chunks, session and personal. Medium might be generous (Low-Medium): it loses cheap disambiguation, but nothing is broken.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/app-window-identity-unused)_

#### TEST-002

**Context tests run on mock modes and budget that differ from production and never assert the failing prompt properties**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — C and B1 added pipeline-level context and prompt-eval tests on the Rust-generated modes fixture; a broader 'failing prompt properties' suite is a follow-up.

- **User impact:** Regressions in what the model actually sees ship with a green suite (same pattern as PR #18). Every High finding in this dimension passes the current tests.
- **Root cause:** Tests exercise units in isolation with hand-built items, and the mock fixtures drifted from the Rust seeds.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tauri/mock/fixtures.ts:403 contextTokenBudget 24_000 vs the Rust default 12_000 (bluey-core settings.rs:287); s9_modes.ts: mock general = ['screen','transcript'] vs Rust [screen, accessibility, transcript, session_memory] (modes.rs:95-99); mock interview adds 'documents'; mock case-interview lacks 'screen'; tests/unit/ai/prompt-builder.test.ts:69-86 asserts '### Current question' only with an instruction item; tests/integration/prepare-flow.test.ts has no assertions on prompt content (`rg 'Current question|userText'` = 0); No test covers transcript order, follow-up memory without a session, ring cross-session behaviour or AX-only screens
- **Proposed solution:** 1. Generate the mock built-in modes and default settings from the Rust seeds (e.g. a JSON snapshot exported by a Rust test and checked in), or add a parity test comparing them.
2. Add a table-driven 'pipeline prompt' test running runPipeline over realistic snapshots (the /tmp/bluey-audit/context-engine s1-s7 fixtures). Assert on the rendered user text: current question present, chronological transcript, previous code on follow-up, no previous-session lines, token ceilings for unrelated asks.
- **Test plan:** - The new parity test fails today on general/interview contextRequirements and the budget.
- The pipeline prompt test fails today on detected_event and follow-up and passes after the fixes.
- **Verification (confirmed):** mock/fixtures.ts:403 has contextTokenBudget 24_000, while Rust defaults to 12_000. No test asserts chronology, the Current question on detected_event, no-session follow-up memory, or ring scoping. Medium is appropriate, since this is why the High and Critical items above went unnoticed.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/context-tests-mock-parity)_

#### CTX-014

**On the Apple Speech fallback transcripts carry no '?', and question detection falls back to fragile sentence-start patterns**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — D1 sets addsPunctuation on Apple Speech so finals carry '?'; E1's classifier tolerates short fillers before an interrogative lead.

- **User impact:** Users on the Apple route (no Gemini key, offline) miss live suggestions for many spoken questions ('Okay so why are you leaving Acme', 'And what would you change'). Fusion's current-question pick can differ from the classifier's.
- **Root cause:** The punctuation opt-in is missing, and the heuristics depend on it.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Speech/SpeechTranscriber.swift:140-145 creates SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest without setting `addsPunctuation`; `rg addsPunctuation` = 0; Apple docs (developer.apple.com/documentation/speech/sfspeechrecognitionrequest/addspunctuation): 'Set this property to true for the speech framework to automatically include punctuation … a period or question mark at the end of a sentence'; src/transcript/classifier.ts:74-83 isQuestionText: '?' → 0.82; else RISING_PATTERNS; else the INTERROGATIVE_LEAD regex anchored at the start of the sentence (0.58), which fails on 'So/Okay/And …' openers; fusion.ts:41-50 looksLikeQuestion uses a different, unanchored lead list, so the two detectors disagree; Default provider is GeminiLive (bluey-core settings.rs:191); Apple is the no-key/offline fallback
- **Proposed solution:** 1. Set `request.addsPunctuation = true` (macOS 13+) in startRequestLocked.
2. Make INTERROGATIVE_LEAD tolerate short fillers (`^(?:(?:so|okay|ok|and|well|right|um|uh),?\s+){0,3}(what|how|…)`).
3. Share one question detector between fusion.ts and classifier.ts.
- **Test plan:** - classifier.test: 'Okay so why are you leaving Acme' → question.
- One shared-detector test covering both call sites.
- Manual macOS check: the Apple route produces '?' in finals after the change.
- **Verification (confirmed):** SpeechTranscriber.swift:141 creates SFSpeechAudioBufferRecognitionRequest and there is no addsPunctuation anywhere in src-tauri/swift. The API (macOS 13+) defaults to false. I downgraded to Low because this only affects the Apple fallback path (default provider is GeminiLive), and the lead-word heuristics still catch many questions. The one-line fix is still worthwhile.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/apple-speech-no-punctuation-question-detection)_

#### CTX-015

**Semantic retrieval has no score floor and does not check the embedding model; keyword scores are normalised so the weakest match scores 1.0**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-C: In bluey-storage src/documents/retrieve.rs, semantic candidates below a raw cosine of 0.25 (SEMANTIC_MIN_COSINE) are dropped. DocumentRepository::chunks_with_embeddings now filters by the current embedding model tag (providerId/model), which src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs passes through embedding_tag(). Keyword matches whose best FTS rank is below MIN_KEYWORD_RANK, meaning no matched term discriminates, get a flat weak score. Device check: Check the 0.25 cosine floor against real Gemini/OpenAI embeddings on a real library. The value is a heuristic taken from the audit.

- **User impact:** With embeddings on, up to 8 unrelated chunks are always added. After switching to a same-dimension embedding model, answers use mismatched vectors until re-embedding finishes.
- **Root cause:** No absolute relevance threshold; model identity is not part of the semantic filter.
- **Evidence:** retrieve.rs:241-271 every embedded chunk is a candidate; score = (cos+1)/2 (≈0.5-0.65 for unrelated text); truncated to the limit (8); retrieve.rs:201-215 bm25 normalised by the best row, so a single weak OR-term hit becomes 1.0 → fusion relevance 1.0 (fusion.ts:243); repositories/documents.rs chunks_with_embeddings filters only by scope/kind; retrieve.rs:252 checks vector length only; documents/mod.rs:247-284 re-embeds stale docs asynchronously
- **Proposed solution:** Drop semantic candidates below a cosine threshold (e.g. under 0.25 raw). Keep the raw bm25 alongside the normalised score and require a minimum absolute rank. Filter chunks_with_embeddings by `d.embedding_model = current tag`.
- **Test plan:** - retrieve.rs tests: an unrelated query returns [] with embeddings on.
- A same-dimension different-model doc is excluded.
- **Verification (unverifiable):** Not re-read in depth. The keyword path I did read (keyword_candidates) normalises bm25 by the best row, so a single weak OR-term hit gets weight 1.0, which supports half the claim. The semantic path is off by default (embeddings_enabled=false), so the impact is Low either way.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/semantic-retrieval-no-floor)_

#### CTX-016

**Session notes and timeline events are loaded on every ask but never rendered**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Notes a user adds to a session ('interviewer is Priya, focus on Kafka') have no effect on answers; each ask does DB work for nothing.
- **Root cause:** The SessionContext shape outgrew what fusion renders.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:259-271 runs 4 queries per ask (responses, events, notes, session documents); `snapshot.ts:147-155 copies events and notes; fusion.ts:250-261 reads only recentResponses`; `Notes can be added in src/features/settings/SessionDetail.tsx:219 (bluey.session.addNote)`
- **Proposed solution:** Render notes as one session_memory item ('Session notes: …', ≤10×400 chars, relevance 0.6), or stop loading events and notes in session_context.
- **Test plan:** fusion.test: a snapshot with session.notes produces a notes item rendered under 'Earlier in this session'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** fusion.ts:249-261 reads only session.recentResponses. Events and notes are copied by snapshot.ts but never rendered, and proactive.ts even passes sessionEvents. Low.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/session-notes-events-unused)_

#### CTX-017

**An oversized typed instruction keeps only its head, so a question after a long paste is cut**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-C: src/context/budget.ts now compresses an oversized typed instruction as head plus tail (60/40) with a truncation marker in the middle, so a question at the end of a long paste survives.

- **User impact:** Rare: pasting a very long log or file followed by 'why does this fail?' drops the actual question.
- **Root cause:** Keeping the head suits OCR, not user messages, where the ask usually comes last.
- **Evidence:** src/context/budget.ts:120-129 compresses user_instruction with compressKeepHead when it exceeds the remaining budget; `The default input budget is ~10.6k tokens (12,000 minus headroom), about 42k chars`
- **Proposed solution:** Compress the instruction as head+tail (e.g. 60/40) with a middle marker.
- **Test plan:** budget.test: a 60k-char instruction ending in a question keeps the question.
- **Verification (confirmed):** budget.ts:120-129 uses compressKeepHead for user_instruction. This needs a paste of more than about 40k chars to trigger. Low.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/instruction-head-truncation)_

#### CTX-018

**Rust and TS assign counterpart speaker labels differently (custom candidate modes, lecture)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** In custom interview modes the prompt says 'Speaker:' where the classifier and UI say 'Interviewer'. It is a minor source of confusion for role-aware answers.
- **Root cause:** Duplicated heuristic with drifted rules.
- **Evidence:** bluey-protocols helper.rs:464-481 hardcodes the ids interview|behavioral-interview|coding-interview|system-design|case-interview → 'Interviewer', sales → 'Customer', recruiting → 'Candidate', otherwise 'Speaker'; src/transcript/speaker.ts:15-21 uses isCandidateMode (group 'looking for work' / candidate schemas) and lecture → 'Lecturer'; classifier.ts:193-199 labelSpeaker keeps the Rust label when its confidence is higher (0.6 vs 0.6 is not higher → heuristic used), so the UI or classifier label can differ from the 'Speaker:' label rendered in the prompt (fusion.ts:115)
- **Proposed solution:** Make Rust take the counterpart label from the mode (add a `counterpart_label` to BlueyMode or compute it with the same rules as isCandidateMode), or let fusion re-label with labelSpeaker.
- **Test plan:** Parity test: for every built-in plus a custom candidate mode, the Rust speaker_label equals TS counterpartLabelFor.
- **Verification (unverifiable):** Not re-traced in this pass. It is plausible given the separate implementations. Low impact regardless.
- _Source: auditor (context-engine/speaker-label-parity)_

### Live suggestions & real-time races

#### LIVE-001

**A live suggestion cannot be cancelled: Escape, Stop and a manual ask only restyle the turn, and the stream comes back as 'done' and is saved**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: prepare() now has the same cancellation as ask(): an AbortSignal-backed handle exposed via onHandle (src/ai/engine.ts). Esc, Stop, a manual ask, New chat and ⌘⇧↵ call cancelLiveSuggestion (src/stores/proactive.ts, src/features/hud/useAsk.ts). A cancelled suggestion is not completed and is not persisted. The cancel signal is on the pipeline options so research (workstream R) can use it. Device check: Press Esc/Stop during a real live suggestion and confirm the provider stream stops and nothing is saved to the session.

- **User impact:** The user presses Esc on an unwanted or wrong suggestion. It shows 'Stopped', then the text keeps growing and finishes as a normal answer. Tokens and subscription quota are spent anyway. The dismissed answer lands in History and in the session timeline. The next question the interviewer asks waits behind it. With a manual ⌘↵ the hidden suggestion still streams in the background (Rust cancels it only when the counters happen to line up, see cross-scope finding).
- **Root cause:** prepare() was built for silent background work and returns only a Promise. PR #45 reused it for the visible live path without giving it a cancellation handle, and chatStore.markCancelled does not invalidate the generation.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/engine.ts:686-687 prepare opts `isCancelled: () => false, onStreamHandle: () => {}`: no handle and no cancel path for prepare; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:73-83 stop() = markCancelled(chat.generation) + `currentHandle?.cancel()`, where currentHandle is the last engine.ask handle and is never the live prepare; `src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:65-68 Escape -> stop() while streaming`; src/stores/chatStore.ts:139-148 markCancelled does not bump the generation; applyDraft/complete (:116-128) have no status check, so later drafts overwrite the 'cancelled' turn and complete() sets it back to 'done'; src/ai/engine.ts:566-588 non-silent (live) pipeline saves the response and adds a response_generated session event; proactive.ts:122-125 consumeQuestion runs unconditionally; /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/escape.run.ts (real engine + proactive + chatStore, FakeTransport): after Escape 'phase=done turn=done text=full', ai_cancel calls 0, responses_save 1; /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/askduring.run.ts: ⌘↵ during a live suggestion -> suggestion turn marked cancelled, but ai_cancel 0 and responses_save 2 (the hidden suggestion keeps streaming and is saved); src/stores/proactive.ts:189-196 busy stays true until the uncancelled prepare finishes, so the next real question only queues; commands/app.rs:47-53 ⌘R -> cancel_all cancels registered Rust streams, but a prepare still in context assembly (before ai_stream) is not registered and runs to completion invisibly (its stale callbacks are ignored, but engine.ts:569 still saves); src/stores/proactive.ts:162-178 live path: chat.begin(...) then `await getEngine().prepare(input, callbacks)` with no handle kept; src/ai/engine.ts:680-688 prepare(): `isCancelled: () => false`, `onStreamHandle: () => {}`, so there is no cancellation path; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:73-83 stop(): chat.markCancelled(chat.generation) + `currentHandle?.cancel()`, where currentHandle is the last *ask* (module var :15,68); src/stores/chatStore.ts:139-148 markCancelled does not bump the generation, so later applyDraft/setPhase/complete from the prepare are accepted (116-128); src/ai/engine.ts:456-462 onDelta checks only isCancelled()/isStale, then re-announces phase 'streaming' (the Stop button comes back) or keeps drafting into the cancelled turn; complete() marks it done; The proactive `busy` flag stays true (proactive.ts:148,188), so the next detected question is queued behind the orphaned stream; tests/ui/proactive.test.ts has no stop/cancel coverage. (New Chat does cancel it through app_dismiss_response → ai.cancel_all, commands/app.rs:48-54.)
- **Proposed solution:** Minimal: (1) give prepare a handle the same way ask() has one. Keep `let cancelled=false; let streamHandle` in prepare, wire `isCancelled`/`onStreamHandle`, and expose `cancel()` via an optional `onHandle(handle)` callback in EngineCallbacks or a returned `{done, cancel, requestId}`. (2) In proactive.ts keep `liveHandle` + `liveGeneration` in module state and export `cancelLive()`. (3) useAsk.stop(), useAsk.ask() (before begin), newChat and generateOrTakePrepared call `cancelLive()` when a live turn is streaming. (4) chatStore.markCancelled bumps the generation, or applyDraft/complete ignore a last turn whose status is not 'streaming', so a cancelled turn can never come back. checkAlive(opts) at engine.ts:515 then stops persistence of cancelled work because isCancelled becomes real.
- **Test plan:** Boundary tests with the real engine + proactive loop + chatStore against a FakeTransport that models ai_cancel as Rust does (the stream ends with finishReason 'cancelled'): (a) live suggestion streaming -> stop() => ai_cancel called with the prepare requestId, turn stays 'cancelled' after further deltas, responses_save not called, consumeQuestion not called; (b) ⌘↵ during a live suggestion => ai_cancel for the prepare, one save only; (c) newChat while prepare is in context assembly => no save; (d) a queued question starts immediately after the cancel. Promote /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/escape.run.ts and askduring.run.ts into tests/ui/proactive.test.ts.
- **Verification (confirmed):** engine.ts:686-687 has isCancelled:()=>false and onStreamHandle:()=>{} on the prepare path. useAsk.ts:73-83 stop() only cancels currentHandle, which is always the last engine.ask handle. chatStore.ts:139-148 markCancelled does not bump the generation, and applyDraft/complete (:116-128) only compare generation and never check status, so later drafts overwrite the cancelled live turn and complete() sets it back to 'done'. engine.ts:567 still persists (non-silent). When a manual ask starts, begin() bumps the generation, so the live callbacks go stale, but the Rust stream keeps running and is still saved. The fix is correct. Also route liveCallbacks through the same status guard.
- **Also reported as:** hud-ux/stop-cannot-cancel-live-suggestion
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/live-prepare-uncancellable)_

#### LIVE-002

**Rust supersede compares generations across TS scopes, so a background suggestion can cancel the user's own manual answer mid-stream**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: AiRequest gains scope and background (bluey-core types/ai.rs, src/lib/types/ai.ts). Rust AiManager now supersedes only when the session and scope match and the generation is older (src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs, ActiveRequest::superseded_by). The TS engine tags every request with its scope: ask, live, prepare or classify.

- **User impact:** While listening in a meeting, the prepare counter quickly climbs above the ask counter (one per detected question). If the other party asks something while the user's own ⌘↵/typed answer is streaming (the suggestion then prepares silently), Rust kills the user's answer halfway. The turn freezes with a streaming caret and the pill is stuck on 'Thinking' (see state finding). In 'on request' mode this happens on every detected question during a manual answer.
- **Root cause:** The TS gate is per scope, but the wire `generation` is compared in Rust as if it came from one global sequence within a session. The Rust ActiveRequest has no scope, so the ADR contract was never enforced end-to-end.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/generations.ts:4-17 per-scope counters; engine.ts:597 `gate.next(SCOPE_ASK)`, :679 `gate.next(SCOPE_PREPARE)`; src/ai/request.ts:102 `if (session) request.sessionId = session.id` (both ask and prepare send the active session during listening); engine.ts:401-411 sends opts.generation; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:281-292 cancels any active entry with the same session_id and `entry.generation < request.generation`, with no scope in ActiveRequest; `docs/adr/0005-state-machine-and-event-bus.md:21-24 promises a per-scope generation gate`; /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/cross-scope.run.ts (FakeTransport modelling Rust supersede): 3 prepares (gen 1..3), user ⌘↵ (ask gen 1), a question detected mid-answer -> silent prepare gen 4 -> user's ask cancelled, done=null; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:62 onPhase uses setPhase for 'cancelled' (not markCancelled), so the cancelled ask's turn stays status 'streaming' with a frozen partial answer (ResponseThread.tsx:80-90); src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts has no generation/supersede logic, so UI tests cannot see this
- **Proposed solution:** Make supersede scope-aware. Add `scope: Option<String>` (or `supersede_group`) to AiRequest (bluey-core/src/types/ai.rs:211) and ActiveRequest, have buildAIRequest set it from opts.scope, and in ai/mod.rs:283 require `entry.scope == request.scope`. Prepare requests could also opt out of superseding entirely (the TS loop already serializes them). Separately, fix useAsk.ts:62 so phase 'cancelled' calls markCancelled(generation) and the turn never stays 'streaming'. Update ADR 0005 to state that supersede is per (session, scope).
- **Test plan:** Rust unit test in src-tauri/src/ai (or its tests module): register an ask request (scope ask, gen 1) and a prepare (scope prepare, gen 5) in the same session => the ask token is not cancelled; a second ask gen 2 does cancel the first. TS: add Rust-parity supersede to mock-transport (same session + same scope + lower gen => cancel) and a proactive/hud test: manual ask streaming + question.detected => the ask completes and the suggestion becomes the ⌘⇧↵ hint. Unit test: ask cancelled by the backend => turn status 'cancelled'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** generations.ts keeps one counter per scope. ai/mod.rs:281-292 cancels every active entry with the same session_id and a lower generation, and ActiveRequest (:295-300) stores no scope. request.ts:102 attaches the session to both scopes. In a listening session prepares happen far more often than manual asks, so the prepare counter quickly passes the ask counter. After that, any detected question during a manual answer cancels the answer. The reverse also happens: an ask with a higher number kills an in-flight silent prepare. When the engine sees a CancelledError it calls onPhase('cancelled'), which useAsk maps to setPhase only (useAsk.ts:62), so the turn stays in 'streaming' with a partial answer. The proposed scope-aware supersede is the right minimal fix.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/cross-scope-generation-supersede)_

#### LIVE-003

**Background and live prepares drive the global app state: a cancel leaves 'Thinking' stuck, a silent failure puts the app in Error, and discreet mode shows 'Thinking'**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: Background requests and non-answer tasks (drives_state) never publish ThinkingStarted or Failed into the app state machine; ai.* events still flow. A cancel returns the app to idle via leave_thinking_after_cancel when no other state-driving answer is running. The state machine is also recoverable (bluey-core state/mod.rs). Device check: Check that the pill never sticks on Thinking or Error during discreet or live preparation on a real session.

- **User impact:** (1) After Esc, or after a supersede of any answer, the pill says 'Thinking' until the next answer completes or ⌘R. (2) With suggestions set to 'On request', whose purpose is to be discreet, the pill flashes 'Thinking' every time the other party asks something. (3) A background prepare the user never asked for (429, timeout, network blip) turns the whole HUD into an error state mid-meeting. Its Retry regenerates an unrelated earlier turn, and listening start/stop is rejected until the user clicks the pill.
- **Root cause:** ADR 0005's state machine models a single user-initiated request. Proactive prepares reuse the same primary AiTask and ai_stream path, and Rust has no notion of background/silent requests or of returning from Thinking after a cancel.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:304-306 every primary task (is_primary :996-1001, which includes Answer, as used by silent and live prepares) triggers ThinkingStarted; ai/mod.rs:525-540 Cancelled outcome: no state transition; :521-523 ResponseReady only on Completed Stop/Length; ai/mod.rs:678-686 publish_failed: primary && !cancelled => AppEvent::Failed -> AppState::Error (state/mod.rs:239-246); bluey-core/src/types/app_state.rs:22-24 is_idle = Ready|Listening, so prepares during listening move the app to Thinking (state/mod.rs:224-229); src/features/hud/state-pill.ts:43 error outranks everything; :53-54 `status?.state === "thinking"` => Thinking pill even when chat.phase is null/done, so the 'preparing' pill (:57) is unreachable while Rust is in Thinking; src/features/hud/StatePill.tsx:18 retry 're-asks the last question'; HudPanel.tsx:70-72 retry = regenerate last turn, i.e. not the silent prepare that failed; state/mod.rs:291-300 audio_toggle_allowed excludes Error, so AudioStarted/AudioStopped are rejected while in Error (likely audio_active desync until recover); src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts:800-811 on cancel the mock resets state to listening/ready (Rust does not); the mock failure path does not set Error, so tests stay green
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Add `background: bool` to AiRequest (TS sets it for prepare scope). In AiManager::start skip ThinkingStarted when background, and in publish_failed skip AppEvent::Failed when background (still emit ai.failed for the turn's own error banner in live mode). (2) On a Cancelled outcome for a primary non-background request, when no other primary request is active, `transition_soft(AppEvent::ResponseDismissed)` (Thinking -> idle_state). (3) Make the mock mirror Rust: no state reset on cancel, Error on primary failure, no state change for background requests.
- **Test plan:** Rust: state tests in bluey-core (ThinkingStarted/Cancelled/idle) and ai/mod.rs tests: background request start => state unchanged; background failure => no Error; foreground cancel => back to Listening/Ready. TS: derivePill test with status thinking + background prepare; mock-parity test that cancel leaves state as Rust would. Real-app check: listening on, dev-simulate a question in on-request mode => pill shows 'Preparing', not 'Thinking'.
- **Depends on:** cross-scope-generation-supersede
- **Verification (confirmed):** Verified in ai/mod.rs:304-306 (ThinkingStarted for primary requests), :525-540 (no transition on Cancelled) and :678-686 (Failed -> Error). In bluey-core/src/state/mod.rs, ThinkingStarted and ResponseReady are both rejected from Error, and Error is only left through app_recover, whose only caller is StatePill.tsx:23-25. So one failed silent prepare keeps the pill in Error until the user clicks it. app_dismiss_response, the only thing that exits Thinking after a cancel, is called only from newChat (useAsk.ts:131). The stuck-Thinking-after-cancel part also affects manual asks cancelled with Escape, so it is not specific to prepares. The fix should send a ResponseDismissed-style transition on any primary cancel when no other primary request is active. audio_toggle_allowed excludes Error (state/mod.rs:~290), so the audio desync claim is plausible but unverified.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/state-machine-background-prepares)_

#### LIVE-008

**The first live suggestion unmounts the idle composer: the user's half-typed question is lost, focus jumps, and the next Enter becomes a follow-up without the screen**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: There is now one persistent composer (HudInputRow.tsx). Its draft lives in hudUiStore, so it survives layout changes and is never thrown away when a live turn opens. The composer takes focus only when the HUD panel becomes visible, not when a live or suggested turn appears. Typed questions and follow-ups are told apart by counting only turns that are not suggestions. Device check: On a real NSPanel: check that the input takes focus when the HUD is shown and keeps it (and the half-typed text) when a live transcript turn arrives.

- **User impact:** In an interview or sales call the user starts typing their own question into the HUD. The other party asks something, the suggestion turn opens, and the typed text disappears while the caret jumps into 'Ask follow-up'. When they retype and press Enter, the question is sent as a follow-up to the suggestion with no screen capture, even with Screen enabled, and begin() hides the suggestion that is still streaming in the background (see uncancellable finding).
- **Root cause:** The composer's value lives in per-layout component state and the layout switches on turns.length. The proactive loop has no signal about user input activity.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:36 `const expanded = turns.length > 0`; :118-140 renders <FollowUpHeader> or <HudIdleRow>, two different components; src/features/hud/HudInputRow.tsx:14-30 useHudInput keeps the draft in a local `useState("")` and calls `inputRef.current?.focus()` on mount; src/stores/proactive.ts:157-162 opens the live turn (chat.begin) whenever canShowLive is true, and never checks whether the user is typing; src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:42-49 submitTyped: `trigger: expanded ? "follow_up" : "typed"`, `captureScreen: !expanded && screenEnabled`; src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:36,119-142 expanded = turns.length > 0 swaps <HudIdleRow> for <FollowUpHeader>; src/features/hud/HudInputRow.tsx:14-15 the draft is local useState inside useHudInput, so the unmount loses it; :19-21 the new FollowUpHeader focuses an empty input; src/stores/proactive.ts:91-92 canShowLive checks only settings and phase, not whether the user is typing; :162 chat.begin appends the suggestion turn
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Move the composer draft into hudUiStore (`draft`, `setDraft`) so both input rows share it, or render one persistent input element in both layouts. Only autofocus on mount when the HUD was just shown, not on a layout switch. (2) Add `composerBusy()` (draft non-empty, or a keystroke within the last ~5 s) to hudUiStore. When it is true, prepareFor falls back to the silent path and shows the ⌘⇧↵ hint instead of opening a live turn. (3) Pick the trigger/captureScreen for a typed submit from whether the thread holds only suggestion turns (treat it as 'typed' with the screen setting) rather than from turns.length alone.
- **Test plan:** tests/ui/hud.test.tsx: render the HUD, type 'what is' in the idle input, emit question.detected (live) => input value is still 'what is' and document.activeElement unchanged, or no live turn opened and the hint is shown. Submit after only a suggestion turn => engine.ask receives trigger 'typed' and captureScreen = screenEnabled. On real macOS, check that no key-window or focus theft happens from the meeting app.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanics are verified: HudPanel.tsx:36 sets expanded = turns.length>0 and switches between two components, and HudInputRow.tsx:15 keeps the draft in local useState, so the draft is lost when a live turn opens. submitTyped then uses the follow_up trigger with captureScreen false. Focus goes to the new input on mount (:20), so it is not really lost. Impact is losing a partly typed prompt and a changed request kind. That is annoying but recoverable, so Medium rather than High. The proposed fix (shared draft in hudUiStore, plus falling back to silent while the user is typing) is sound.
- **Also reported as:** hud-ux/idle-draft-lost-on-live-suggestion
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/composer-unmount-on-live-turn)_

#### LIVE-009

**Suggestion gating is noisy: any '?' from the other party opens a live, billed suggestion, with no substance filter, text dedupe, cooldown or queue staleness check**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: Added a pure shouldSurface(event, ctx) in src/stores/proactive.ts. It filters for substance (back-channel and meta phrases), dedupes near-identical text within 90 s, applies an 8 s same-speaker cooldown (a correction lead such as 'sorry, I mean' bypasses it), drops queued questions older than 20 s, and enables per mode (other modes surface only direct questions above a higher bar). Each dismissal raises the bar a little, up to a cap. The classifier's INTERROGATIVE_LEAD now tolerates short fillers (src/transcript/classifier.ts). Device check: Tune the thresholds against a real interview or meeting transcript.

- **User impact:** In the default configuration, the opening small talk of every call ('Can you hear me?', 'Is my screen visible?') and back-channel questions ('Right?', 'Okay?', 'Does that make sense?') each open a 'Suggested' card and spend a full LLM generation. The question the user actually cares about can end up queued behind them. Repeated questions produce duplicate cards, and a question queued behind a long answer is still answered after the conversation has moved on. This is the kind of intrusiveness that makes users switch the feature off.
- **Root cause:** Detection confidence is a surface heuristic. The only downstream gates are requiresResponse and event-id dedupe, and the cheap model refinement band excludes exactly the high-recall '?' case.
