- **Evidence:** src/transcript/classifier.ts:74 `if (trimmed.includes("?")) return { question: true, confidence: 0.82 }`; :75 RISING 0.72; :76-78 INTERROGATIVE_LEAD 0.58; :168 CLASSIFIER_MIN_CONFIDENCE = 0.5; src/ai/engine.ts:811 the fast-model refine runs only for 0.4..0.7, so every '?' utterance bypasses it; Probe /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/classify.ts (real classifier): interview & sales modes => 'Can you hear me?', 'Right?', 'Okay?', 'Hmm?', 'What?', 'Does that make sense?', 'Is my screen visible?', 'Could you repeat that?', 'Any questions?' all question 0.82 requiresResponse=true; general mode false (conversationalMode gate); src/stores/proactive.ts:199-202 dedupes by event id only; ids are random per classification (classifier.ts:208 `evt_${idGen()}`), so a repeated or rephrased question makes a new turn; proactive.ts:203-205, 193-195 the queued question is answered however old it is (no detectedAt check); there is no cooldown anywhere; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/settings.rs:285-286 defaults: proactive_preparation=true, suggestion_display=Live, applied globally (not per mode)
- **Proposed solution:** Add a pure `shouldSurface(event, ctx)` gate in proactive.ts (adaptive and per mode, not a kill switch). (1) Substance: drop back-channel/meta phrases (a small list: right/okay/what/hmm/can you hear me/is my screen visible/does that make sense/any questions/could you repeat) and '?' utterances under ~4 content words unless the type is specific (behavioral/technical/coding/objection). (2) Per-mode thresholds, e.g. interview 0.7, sales objection 0.75, recruiting 0.7, with an optional per-mode `suggestionDisplay` override in the mode config. (3) Extend fast-model refinement to short '?' utterances (≤8 words) when a fast model is configured. (4) Near-duplicate dedupe: normalized-token Jaccard ≥ 0.8 against questions surfaced in the last 90 s. (5) Cooldown after a live turn unless the speaker changes or the type is specific. (6) Drop queued events older than ~20 s (detectedAt). (7) Adapt per session: each Esc/⌘R dismissal of a live suggestion within N s raises the threshold, and after k dismissals the session falls back to the ⌘⇧↵ hint. Taking hints lowers it again.
- **Test plan:** Table-driven unit tests for shouldSurface using the probe phrases (expect false) and real interview questions (expect true) per mode; proactive.test: identical text twice within 90 s => one turn; queued event older than 20 s is dropped; after 3 dismissals the display falls back to the hint. Replay a recorded transcript fixture to measure suggestions per minute before and after.
- **Verification (confirmed):** classifier.ts:74 gives any '?' a confidence of 0.82, and modeFamilyBonus can raise it further. engine.ts:811 refines only in the 0.4-0.7 band, so '?' utterances are never refined. requiresResponse is gated only by type, speaker != You and conversationalMode (classifier.ts ~200). Event ids are random. The prepare loop has no text dedupe, cooldown or detectedAt staleness check. Medium is right: it costs tokens and adds thread noise, but nothing is broken.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/noisy-detection-gating)_

#### LIVE-010

**Questions split by a ~0.6 s pause (default Gemini Live VAD) or by the Apple Speech 55 s rotation fire a suggestion on the fragment, and the real question queues behind it**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: Fragment coalescing in proactive onFinal: a non-'You' final with no terminal punctuation, or with a short open lead, is held for about 900 ms and merged with the same voice's next final. A different voice, Stop listening or the timeout releases it. isOpenFragment is in classifier.ts, and segmentIds are carried on ClassifyInput. Device check: Check with real Gemini Live VAD pauses and the Apple Speech 55 s rotation.

- **User impact:** A natural thinking pause ('So tell me about… your experience scaling Kafka?') produces a suggestion for 'So tell me about' first. The real question waits until that answer finishes, which adds seconds at the moment speed matters most.
- **Root cause:** Finals are treated as complete utterances. Neither the Rust assembler nor the proactive loop merges same-speaker continuations before classifying.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/transcription/gemini_live.rs:4-5 `audioStreamEnd` after 500 ms of silence; :47-48 `SERVER_SILENCE_MS: u32 = 600` (default STT provider per ADR 0007); src-tauri/crates/bluey-protocols/src/realtime.rs:36 cloud realtime uses default `server_vad`; src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Speech/SpeechTranscriber.swift:9,35 the request restarts every ~55 s of audio and after every final (no VAD alignment); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:903-929 each final is committed and published separately; the assembler never merges successive finals; src/stores/proactive.ts:210-227 each final is classified immediately, with no utterance-end wait or merge; classifier.ts:33 RISING_PATTERNS makes 'So tell me about' / 'How would you' question 0.72 => requiresResponse (probe classify.ts); `proactive.ts:203-205 the continuation question is queued behind the fragment's suggestion`
- **Proposed solution:** In proactive.onFinal, hold a final from a non-'You' speaker for a short coalescing window (~900 ms) when it lacks terminal punctuation or matches an open lead (RISING/INTERROGATIVE_LEAD with ≤5 words). Merge it with the next same-source final that arrives within the window before classifying (join text, union segmentIds). Also, when a newer question from the same speaker arrives while the current live suggestion has no draft yet, cancel and replace it (needs the handle from live-prepare-uncancellable) instead of queueing.
- **Test plan:** proactive.test with fake timers: final 'So tell me about' then 400 ms later 'your experience with Kafka?' => exactly one question.detected with the merged text; a lone final ending in '?' is not delayed beyond the window. On real macOS with Gemini Live and Apple Speech, speak questions with a 0.8 s pause and count suggestions.
- **Depends on:** live-prepare-uncancellable
- **Verification (confirmed):** gemini_live.rs:45-48 confirms 500 ms audioStreamEnd and SERVER_SILENCE_MS=600. SpeechTranscriber.swift:9,35 confirms the ~55 s restart. The proactive onFinal (proactive.ts:210-227) classifies each final on its own with no merging. How often mid-question pauses over 600 ms split a transcript final is a runtime question and needs real-session recordings to measure. Hence 'likely'.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/fragment-question-firing)_

#### LIVE-011

**⌘⇧↵ while an answer or live suggestion is streaming leaves that turn spinning forever**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: ⌘⇧↵ over a streaming turn now stops it first (useAsk generateOrTakePrepared calls cancelStreaming), then shows the prepared answer and saves it.

- **User impact:** Question A arrives while the user's answer streams, so it becomes the ⌘⇧↵ hint. Question B then streams live. When the user takes A with ⌘⇧↵, B's card stays stuck mid-thread with a spinner or caret, while B keeps generating and gets saved in the background.
- **Root cause:** showResponse was written for an idle thread and was never updated for concurrent live turns.
- **Evidence:** src/stores/chatStore.ts:150-158 showResponse bumps the generation and appends, but does not mark streaming turns cancelled (begin does at :92); src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:89-108 generateOrTakePrepared does not call stop() or cancel anything; src/features/hud/ResponseThread.tsx:79-103 a turn with status 'streaming' renders StreamingStatus or a streaming ResponseView regardless of position; its callbacks are now stale (generation bumped), so nothing ever settles it
- **Proposed solution:** In showResponse, map streaming turns to 'cancelled', as begin does. In generateOrTakePrepared, call stop() and cancelLive() before showing.
- **Test plan:** chatStore unit test: begin() then showResponse() => first turn status 'cancelled'. hud test: live turn streaming + prepared available + ⌘⇧↵ => no turn left 'streaming', ai_cancel called for the live request.
- **Depends on:** live-prepare-uncancellable
- **Verification (confirmed):** chatStore.showResponse (:150-169) bumps the generation and appends, but unlike begin (:92) it does not map streaming turns to cancelled. Turn in ResponseThread.tsx:79-103 renders StreamingStatus or a streaming view for any 'streaming' turn, and its now-stale callbacks can never settle it. This is reachable through the documented fallback: an answer is streaming, the silent prepare shows the hint, and the user presses ⌘⇧↵. The underlying Rust stream is also left running and billing.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/show-prepared-orphans-streaming-turn)_

#### TEST-003

**No tests cover live-suggestion races, and the mock transport differs from Rust exactly where these bugs live**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: Live-races suite in tests/ui/live-races.test.tsx (13 tests): Esc/Stop, manual ask, ⌘⇧↵, New chat, correction, Retry/Regenerate, previousResponses, hidden HUD with TTL, Stop listening, mode switch, hint expiry. Two rapid questions and serialization are covered in proactive.test.ts, truncation in truncation-retry.test.ts. The mock transport now matches Rust: it supersedes by (session, scope, generation), keeps background work out of app state, sets Error only for primary non-background failures, leaves the state alone on cancel, and ai_cancel/cancel_all only act on in-flight streams. Pinned in tests/ui/mock-ai-parity.test.ts.

- **User impact:** All of the High findings above ship with green CI. This is the same failure pattern as PR #18: mock-transport tests pass while real Tauri behaviour differs.
- **Root cause:** UI tests validate against a mock whose AI lifecycle (cancel, supersede, error-to-state) was written for convenience rather than to match Rust.
- **Evidence:** tests/ui/proactive.test.ts:39-177 covers the live happy path, the on-request hint, the fallback while streaming, dedupe/serialize, engine-null failure and dev simulations; nothing for Escape, manual ask, ⌘R, ⌘⇧↵ during a live turn, supersede, or typing during detection; tests/integration/staleness.test.ts:33-159 covers only ask-vs-ask and cancelAll (which has no production caller: engine.ts:837, rg); src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts:800-811 resets app state to listening/ready on cancel (Rust leaves Thinking); it has no generation supersede and no Error on primary failure
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Make mock-transport match Rust: supersede by (session, scope, generation), no state reset on cancel, Error on primary non-background failure (keep it in step with the fixes). (2) Add a 'live races' suite driven by the real engine + proactive + chatStore (promote the /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/*.run.ts scenarios): Escape during live, ⌘↵ during live, ⌘R during context assembly, ⌘⇧↵ during live, question during a manual ask (supersede), typing during detection, stop listening with a queued question. (3) Add Rust tests in ai/mod.rs for scope-aware supersede and state after cancel.
- **Test plan:** Each suite scenario asserts ai_cancel calls, responses_save count, final turn statuses, chat phase and the app state reported by the (parity) mock. Run the Rust ai/state tests for the matching transitions.
- **Depends on:** live-prepare-uncancellable, cross-scope-generation-supersede, state-machine-background-prepares
- **Verification (confirmed):** The tests/ui/proactive.test.ts case list (lines 39-177) contains no cancel, supersede or typing races. mock-transport.ts:800-811 resets state to listening/ready on cancel, but Rust does not. The only generation-related code in the mock is unrelated (:1546), so there is no supersede parity. engine cancelAll has no production caller outside the engine contract. Confirmed.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/live-race-tests-and-mock-parity)_

#### LIVE-012

**Live suggestions keep generating billed turns into a hidden HUD**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: While the HUD is hidden no live turn is generated. Only the newest question is kept (deferred) and prepared when the HUD is shown within the TTL; otherwise it is dropped (src/stores/proactive.ts). Device check: Hide the HUD during a real call and confirm no billed requests are made.

- **User impact:** If the user hides the HUD (⌘\) while listening continues, every detected question still opens a live turn, streams it and saves it. Each one is billed, and each one can push the app into Thinking or Error (see the state-machine finding). When the HUD is shown again, the thread holds a stack of stale answers instead of one current suggestion or hint.
- **Root cause:** The live/silent choice depends on settings and the chat phase, not on whether the user can see the HUD.
- **Evidence:** src/stores/proactive.ts:91-93 canShowLive checks only settings.ai.suggestionDisplay === 'live' and the chat phase. It never checks whether the panel is visible.; src/stores/proactive.ts:95-101 isHudWindow checks only the window label ('main'), not whether the window is visible; src-tauri/src/overlay/mod.rs:236-241 hide() only hides the native panel. The webview and the audio session keep running, and PanelState.visible is published as 'panel.state' (src/lib/tauri/events.ts:108), but proactive.ts never subscribes to it.
- **Proposed solution:** Mirror panel.state.visible into hudUiStore and include it in canShowLive. When the HUD is hidden, fall back to the silent path, which caches the answer and shows the ⌘⇧↵ hint. Needs a product decision on whether a hidden HUD should prepare at all.
- **Test plan:** Unit test: canShowLive(settings live, phase null, visible=false) returns false. Proactive test: emit panel.state {visible:false} and then question.detected, and assert no turn is begun and preparedEventId is set.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (live-suggestions/live-suggestions-while-hud-hidden)_

#### LIVE-014

**Live suggestions are generated with no memory of earlier answers in the thread**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: Live suggestions pass previousResponses (completedResponses of the thread) to engine.prepare (src/stores/proactive.ts).

- **User impact:** The interviewer's follow-up ('Why that approach over X?') is answered without knowing what Bluey just suggested, which leads to contradictory or repeated suggestions and weak answers to follow-up questions.
- **Root cause:** prepareFor was written for silent background preparation; PR #45 made it a thread turn without adding thread context.
- **Evidence:** `src/stores/proactive.ts:166-177 the prepare input omits previousResponses`; `src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:43,55 manual asks pass completedResponses(chat.turns)`; src/context/snapshot.ts:125-146 toSessionContext builds recentResponses only from previousResponses; unlike recentEvents it has no fallback to existing context; /tmp/bluey-audit/live-suggestions/enrich.run.ts confirmed recentResponses=[] for a proactive prepare with done turns in the thread
- **Proposed solution:** In prepareFor pass `previousResponses: completedResponses(useChatStore.getState().turns)` (bounded by the existing RECENT_RESPONSE_LIMIT), for both the live and the silent path.
- **Test plan:** proactive.test: with one done turn in the thread, emit question.detected => spy on engine.prepare input: previousResponses contains that response; snapshot unit test for recentResponses.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed: the prepare input at proactive.ts:166-177 has no previousResponses, while useAsk passes them. Impact is small because the transcript context (sessionEvents and the recent transcript) already contains what the user actually said aloud, which is the real conversational memory. Missing earlier suggestion text only affects consistency. Low. The one-line fix is correct.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/live-suggestion-no-thread-memory)_

#### LIVE-015

**When an answer hits the output limit, the retry wipes the visible draft and regrows it from scratch**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: A length retry keeps the visible draft and only replaces it once the retry's text outgrows it (src/ai/engine.ts).

- **User impact:** On long live suggestions (system design, behavioral stories), the text the user is reading collapses to a few words and restarts. It is jarring during a live conversation and doubles latency and tokens.
- **Root cause:** The draft high-water mark is reset per attempt, so the retry's first delta always overwrites the first attempt's text.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/engine.ts:450-453 streamOnce resets `lastDraftLength = -1` and the fence buffer on every attempt; engine.ts:492-499 retries on finishReason 'length' with a new stream; the first delta of the retry calls onDraft with a much shorter content, and applyDraft (chatStore.ts:116-119) replaces the text
- **Proposed solution:** Keep the first attempt's draft on screen and only emit retry drafts once they are longer than the shown text (do not reset lastDraftLength). Better still, prefer a continuation (append 'continue' with the partial as an assistant prefix) over a restart.
- **Test plan:** engine unit test with FakeTransport: first attempt ends 'length' after 200 chars, retry streams => the onDraft content length never decreases.
- **Verification (confirmed):** engine.ts:450-453 resets lastDraftLength on each attempt, and the comment there says 'drafts restart when a retry replaces the first one'. So this is a deliberate restart, not an accident. Only affects answers that hit the length limit. The behavior is real and the UX is poor, but the defect is Low and generic (not specific to live suggestions).
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/truncation-retry-rewinds-draft)_

#### LIVE-016

**The 'Bluey has a suggestion' hint and chatStore.prepared never expire, so ⌘⇧↵ can show an answer to a long-gone question**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: Prepared entries expire (PREPARED_TTL_MS in engine-contract.ts). chatStore.prepared and the ⌘⇧↵ hint expire with them.

- **User impact:** Twenty minutes later the pill still offers a suggestion, and ⌘⇧↵ shows an answer to an old question instead of generating a fresh 'Suggested response' from the current transcript.
- **Root cause:** There are two sources of truth for prepared responses with different lifetimes.
- **Evidence:** `src/stores/initStores.ts:58 response.prepared -> chatStore.setPrepared (no timestamp)`; src/ai/engine.ts:137,652-656 the engine cache has a 3-minute TTL, but useAsk.ts:92-95 falls back to `useChatStore.getState().prepared`; src/features/hud/state-pill.ts:56 the prepared hint outranks preparing/listening indefinitely
- **Proposed solution:** Store preparedAt in chatStore and treat entries older than PREPARED_TTL_MS as absent (in the derivePill input and in the generateOrTakePrepared fallback), or drop the chatStore fallback and use only engine.takePrepared.
- **Test plan:** useAsk test with fake timers: prepared at t0, ⌘⇧↵ at t0+4 min => engine.ask('shortcut_generate') called, not showResponse; the pill no longer shows prepared.
- **Verification (confirmed):** initStores.ts:58 sets chatStore.prepared with no timestamp. useAsk.ts:92-95 falls back to it after the engine cache (3-minute TTL) misses. So ⌘⇧↵ can show a response for a question long past. Low.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/stale-prepared-hint)_

#### LIVE-017

**After Stop listening, a queued question still opens a new live suggestion**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: Stop listening (audioActive going from true to false) clears the held fragment, the queued question, the hidden-HUD deferred question and the dismissal count (src/stores/proactive.ts).

- **User impact:** The user ends the call and stops listening, then a few seconds later a new 'Suggested' card appears and spends tokens.
- **Root cause:** The proactive loop does not subscribe to audio/session lifecycle events.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:575-590 stop() ends the auto-started session; nothing notifies the proactive loop; src/stores/proactive.ts:193-195 finally{} runs the queued event regardless; prepareFor reads sessionState.active at that moment (possibly null)
- **Proposed solution:** In startProactiveLoop subscribe to audio stopped / session ended: clear `queued`, and optionally let the in-flight live suggestion finish (product choice).
- **Test plan:** proactive.test: busy with question A, queue B, emit audio stopped => after A completes, no turn for B.
- **Verification (confirmed):** startProactiveLoop subscribes only to transcript.final and question.detected (proactive.ts:229-230). The finally{} block runs the queued event no matter what. At most one queued event, so Low.
- _Source: auditor (live-suggestions/stop-listening-keeps-queue)_

### Providers, routing & accounts

#### PROV-001

**A subscription account holding the Default role has no fallback: every default-role request fails with config.no_model, while the UI says 'Bluey uses your API key meanwhile'**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-F: bluey-core/src/router.rs: select() now has a Blocked tracker. When a role's assignment points at an unusable OAuth account or at a missing provider, the role falls back to the first usable API-key provider (Gemini preferred), using that kind's preset model for the role. The reason gets '→ fallback <id> (<account> unavailable)', which E1's engine.ts maps to selection.fallbackReason. ai/mod.rs with_fallback_hint puts details.fallbackProviderId on the errors of failed account requests. present.ts only says 'uses your API key meanwhile' when that detail is present. docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md routing section updated. Device check: End-to-end check with a real expired ChatGPT/Claude account plus a Gemini key: the HUD should show the 'via API key' provenance.

- **User impact:** A user connects Claude or ChatGPT and clicks 'Use recommended models', or sets up a subscription during onboarding. When the plan window runs out (a daily occurrence on Pro plans), the sign-in expires, or the extra-usage guard trips, every Answer, Coding or long-summary request fails. The first failure says the API key is being used. The next ones say 'No model assigned', even though a working Gemini key is stored.
- **Root cause:** Fallback is modelled only as role→role (fast→default, and so on), never as 'the same role on another usable provider'. The account layer's design (ADR 0009 §3.6) assumed the chain would reach API-key providers.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/router.rs:249 — `ModelRole::Default => &[ModelRole::Default]`: the Default role has no fallback candidates; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/router.rs:131-138 — when the chain is exhausted: `no usable model for role {}; add a provider and assign models in Settings → AI`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/accounts.rs:185-207 — provider_config comment: 'Not `Connected` = keyless = the router's existing fallback chain reaches the API-key providers'. That only holds for roles whose chain ends in a different provider.; Account catalogs suggest the Default role: bluey-protocols/src/claude_code.rs:408, codex.rs:1212, antigravity.rs:641; src/features/settings/accounts/AccountsSection.tsx:55-58 and AccountCard.tsx:204-207 — 'Use recommended models' calls applyProviderPresets with overwrite:true, which re-points Default to the account. The onboarding subscription path (connect.tsx:63,218) plus the catalog auto-fill (accounts/mod.rs:642-661) fills Default when it is unassigned.; src/lib/errors/present.ts:160,169,256 — 'Bluey uses your API key meanwhile' / 'fell back to your API key'; docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:58-60 promises the role is 'routed to the API-key provider'; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:577-676 — the failing request is never retried on another provider; Accounts ship on by default: src-tauri/Cargo.toml:114 feature `subscription-accounts`; settings.rs:412 `subscription_accounts: true`. Turning the switch off empties provider_configs (accounts/mod.rs:694-703), so roles still pointing at the account resolve to 'not configured', with the same dead end.; src/lib/errors/present.ts:158-161 account.needs_reauth: 'Bluey uses your API key meanwhile.'; :256 rate_limited: 'Bluey uses your API key meanwhile.'; :168-175 extra_usage / policy_blocked: 'fell back to your API key'; docs/adr/0009-provider-accounts.md:63-65 'subscription account → API-key provider for the same role → the router's existing chain'; docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:58-60 same; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/router.rs:239-253 fallback_chain is role-only (Default → [Default]); :91-138 one assignment per role, no alternate provider; AITab.tsx:240-242 switching the default provider to an account applies presets with overwrite:true to every role (→ accounts/mod.rs:664-689); bluey-core/src/accounts.rs:184-200 non-Connected account ⇒ has_api_key=false; its test (:451-470) only asserts the keyless flag, not that routing reaches an API-key provider; src-tauri/src/accounts/mod.rs:767 comment 'the adapters then fall back to the API-key provider', but ai/mod.rs has no such logic (rg account/fallback)
- **Proposed solution:** Minimal fix in router::select: once the role chain is exhausted, and a candidate was skipped because its provider is an unusable OAuth account or is missing, try one more step. Pick the first usable API-key provider in settings order that has a preset model for the role (`presets::by_kind(kind).model_for(role)`), or failing that the provider assigned to any other text role. Record '→ fallback to <provider> (account unavailable)' in the reason. Keep the order deterministic: prefer the reserved `gemini` id, as agent/mod.rs:188-196 already does. Also change present.ts so it says 'uses your API key meanwhile' only when a usable API-key provider exists (take a flag from Rust in error.details, e.g. `fallbackProviderId`), and otherwise says 'Reconnect or add an API key'. Optionally retry the failed request once in run_stream when the error is an account.* stop signal and a fallback selection exists.
- **Test plan:** Router unit tests in router.rs:
(a) default→claude account with has_api_key=false, plus gemini usable with no assignment for default: expect gemini/gemini-3.8-flash and a reason that mentions the fallback.
(b) The account is missing from providers (switch turned off): same result.
(c) No API-key provider: still config.no_model.

Integration test in ai/mod.rs with an AccountsManager holding a NeedsReauth account and a Gemini provider that has a key: `select()` returns Gemini.

Vitest for present.ts: the copy depends on details.fallbackProviderId.

On macOS: connect Claude, click 'Use recommended models', revoke the token (or wait for the plan limit), and confirm the next question is answered by Gemini with a visible note.
- **Verification (confirmed):** router.rs:238-253: fallback_chain(Default) = [Default] only. router.rs:131-138 returns config.no_model. AiManager::select (ai/mod.rs:231-243) passes settings.ai.models straight to the router with no substitution for accounts. accounts.rs:187-190 claims 'keyless = the router's existing fallback chain reaches the API-key providers', which is false for Default. present.ts:160,165,169,173 promises 'Your API key is used meanwhile/instead'. docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:58-60 says the same. It is also reachable through AITab 'Default provider' when a subscription kind is chosen (AITab.tsx:240-241, applyProviderPresets overwrite:true). The proposed fix is sound. Also add a router unit test where Default is on an unusable OAuth provider and a usable Gemini provider exists, plus a present.ts test for the copy when no fallback exists.
- **Also reported as:** journey-onboarding-settings/account-fallback-promise-unimplemented
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/account-default-role-no-fallback)_

#### PROV-002

**Microsoft Foundry's own preset models (GPT-5.6/GPT-6 reasoning family) are always sent `temperature`, which Azure reasoning models reject. Every Foundry request, including Test connection, likely fails with a generic 'usually temporary' error**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-F: bluey-protocols openai.rs decides by model family (reasoning-capable models), not by provider name. For those families it never sends temperature or other sampling knobs, on Azure, OpenAI-compatible and Anthropic API-key requests alike (providers/azure.rs, openai.rs, anthropic.rs). The Anthropic API-key path also drops context_management when thinking is off (commit c65a68f). Device check: A live call against an Azure o-series/gpt-5 deployment would confirm there is no 400 on temperature.

- **User impact:** A user who picks Microsoft Foundry and applies its recommended models (the 'Default provider' switch, the env import or 'Use recommended models') likely gets every answer rejected with HTTP 400, and even 'Test connection' fails. The HUD calls it temporary, so the user has no path to a fix.
- **Root cause:** The shared OpenAI-style body builder forwards the WebView's per-task temperature unconditionally. The Foundry presets were updated to reasoning-model deployments without adapting the Azure body (drop temperature, send reasoning_effort).
- **Evidence:** src/ai/request.ts:71-99 — temperatureFor(task) returns 0/0.2/0.4/0.6 and is always set on the AIRequest; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:599 forwards request.temperature; ai/mod.rs:837 test_connection sends `temperature: Some(0.0)`; src-tauri/src/ai/providers/azure.rs:85 `temperature: request.temperature` → bluey-protocols/src/openai.rs:69-71 inserts `temperature` whenever it is Some; there is no model-based stripping and no reasoning_effort; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/presets.rs:115-121 — Foundry preset: Default gpt-5.6-terra, Fast gpt-5.6-luna, Reasoning/Vision/Research gpt-6-astra; Microsoft Learn, 'Azure OpenAI reasoning models - GPT-6 Astra and GPT-5' (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/how-to/reasoning) and 'Work with chat completion models' (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/openai/how-to/chatgpt): GPT-5-series reasoning models do not support temperature, top_p or the penalty parameters. Microsoft Q&A 5989904 reports gpt-5.6-luna failing because temperature/top_p are not supported; src-tauri/src/ai/providers/mod.rs:381-384 — the resulting 400 becomes ai.http_400 → present.ts:47 'The model didn't answer. This is usually temporary.'; rg 'temperature' over src-tauri/src/ai/providers finds only azure.rs:85, so there is no stripping anywhere on the Azure path
- **Proposed solution:** In AzureProvider::stream and test paths, drop temperature (set ChatBodyOptions.temperature = None) when the deployment or model is a reasoning family. Match gpt-5*, gpt-6*, o1*, o3* and o4* by prefix, or simpler, never send temperature to Azure because Foundry chat presets are all reasoning models. Pair this with the reasoning_effort mapping from finding reasoning-level-ignored-some-providers. Also give the 400 a body-aware mapping (generic-http-errors-non-gemini) so an 'Unsupported parameter' error surfaces as ai.invalid_request with the provider's message.
- **Test plan:** Unit test in bluey-protocols/azure or providers/azure: build the body for model gpt-5.6-terra with temperature Some(0.6) and assert there is no 'temperature' key. Contract test with a wiremock Azure endpoint that returns 400 {error:{code:'unsupported_parameter',param:'temperature'}} when temperature is present, asserting the stream succeeds. Live check on a real Foundry gpt-5.6 deployment through 'Test connection'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (providers-routing-accounts/azure-reasoning-models-reject-temperature)_

#### PROV-004

**Azure Foundry, OpenAI-compatible and Anthropic API-key errors drop the response body: 404/400/5xx become 'usually temporary', with no retry and no retry-after**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: New bluey-protocols/src/api_error.rs (parse_error_body, retry_after, map_api_error). Azure, OpenAI-compatible and Anthropic API-key errors read a bounded body that is never logged, and map 404 DeploymentNotFound/model_not_found to config.model_not_found naming the model or deployment, 400 to invalid params with the provider's reason, 401/403 to auth, 429 with retry-after, and 5xx to specific codes. send_with_retry retries 429/5xx. present.ts copy names the missing model (commits e6481d2, 01afe1a). Shared HTTP test stub in providers/test_http.rs.

- **User impact:** A Foundry user whose deployment name differs from the model id, or whose preset model (gpt-5.6-terra) isn't deployed, is told the failure is 'usually temporary' and gets no 'Configure provider' action. The same happens when a model is retired on OpenAI-compatible or Anthropic. Anthropic 529 'overloaded' errors and brief 5xx errors fail at once instead of being retried. 429 errors show no wait time.
- **Root cause:** Only Gemini and the OAuth adapters got a provider-specific error mapper. The other three share a status-only mapper that was written before the error taxonomy existed.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/ai/providers/mod.rs:368-385 — map_http_status: `_ => BlueyError::ai(&format!("http_{status}"), …)` with no recovery; 429 has no retryAfterMs; src-tauri/src/ai/providers/openai.rs:59-61, 81-83, 100-102 and azure.rs:97-99, 121-123 — `if status >= 400 { return Err(map_http_status(status, …)) }` without reading the body; src-tauri/src/ai/providers/anthropic.rs:157-161 — 'the body is read only in OAuth mode'; `Auth::ApiKey(_) => map_http_status(status, "Anthropic")`; src/lib/errors/present.ts — KIND_MESSAGES.ai = 'The model didn't answer. This is usually temporary.'; CODE_COPY 'config.model_not_found' can only be reached from Gemini (bluey-protocols/src/gemini.rs:643-648); By contrast, Gemini: providers/gemini.rs:112-170 retries 408/429/5xx and honours retryDelay (documented in docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md:109-119)
- **Proposed solution:** Add `map_openai_style_error(status, headers, body_limited)` and `map_anthropic_api_error(...)`, reading the body the same way as `read_limited`, and never logging it.
- 404, or error.code in {DeploymentNotFound, model_not_found, not_found_error} → `config.model_not_found` with ConfigureProvider. Make the copy name the deployment or model.
- 400 → `ai.invalid_request` quoting the provider's message (present.ts already favours that message).
- 5xx and 529 → `network.http_5xx` with Retry.
- 429 → parse `retry-after`, `retry-after-ms` and `x-ratelimit-reset-requests` into details.retryAfterMs.

Then wrap `send()` in a small retry loop that runs only before the first byte (reuse the Gemini backoff constants).
- **Test plan:** Adapter tests against a local HTTP test server for openai.rs, azure.rs and anthropic.rs (API key):
- 404 with the Azure `{"error":{"code":"DeploymentNotFound"}}` body → config.model_not_found with ConfigureProvider.
- 429 with `retry-after: 7` → network.http_429 and details.retryAfterMs=7000.
- 529 twice, then 200 → the stream succeeds.
- 400 → ai.invalid_request carrying the provider's message.

Vitest: presentError(config.model_not_found) shows a 'Configure provider' action.
- **Verification (confirmed):** providers/mod.rs:365-385: map_http_status makes 401/403 config, 429 network with no retryAfter, and everything else ai.http_<status>. openai.rs:60,82,101 and azure.rs:98,122 never read the body. anthropic.rs:161 does the same for the ApiKey path (the body is read only in OAuth mode, :164). ai/mod.rs has no generic retry. present.ts:47 falls through to 'usually temporary'. This finding also hides the missed Azure temperature 400 below. Be careful with the fix: bodies can echo prompts (see the comment at mod.rs:367), so pull out only error.code and error.message, and cap their length.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/generic-http-errors-non-gemini)_

#### PROV-005

**The reasoning level is dropped for Anthropic API key, Azure Foundry and OpenAI-compatible, so the 'Reasoning' role model runs without thinking or effort**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: ReasoningLevel and latency now map to reasoning_effort (OpenAI/Azure) or thinking/effort (Anthropic API key) where the model family supports it (commit c65a68f).

- **User impact:** System-design and deep-reasoning modes on Anthropic or Foundry keys get the same shallow behaviour as normal answers, while paying frontier-model prices. If a Foundry reasoning deployment accepts only the default temperature, both 'Test connection' and normal requests fail with HTTP 400. That part is not verified for the gpt-5.6 family.
- **Root cause:** Reasoning mapping was implemented per adapter as each one was added. The shared OpenAI-style body builder and the Anthropic API-key path never got it.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/ai/providers/anthropic.rs:82-90 builds the body; claude_code::apply_thinking runs only inside `Auth::Oauth(credential)` (103-108); src-tauri/src/ai/providers/openai.rs:41-49 and azure.rs:78-86 — ChatBodyOptions has no reasoning field; bluey-protocols/src/openai.rs:52-95 never sends reasoning_effort; Presets route the Reasoning and Research roles to claude-opus-5 and gpt-6-astra (bluey-core/src/presets.rs:115-121, 136-142); Temperature is always forwarded to these three (openai.rs:69-71; anthropic.rs protocol :81-83), from request.ts:71-83 (0–0.6) and test_connection ai/mod.rs:837 (0.0)
- **Proposed solution:** Add `reasoning_effort: Option<&str>` to ChatBodyOptions. Map ReasoningLevel/LatencyBudget → none/low/medium/high (reuse the codex::reasoning_effort policy) and send it only for Azure, gated by a per-provider `supports_reasoning_effort` flag defaulting to true for Foundry. Omit temperature whenever an effort other than none is sent. On the Anthropic API-key path, call the existing `claude_code::apply_thinking` (it is model-gated through is_effort_capable), which also strips temperature.
- **Test plan:** Protocol unit tests:
- build_chat_body with reasoning Deep → `reasoning_effort: "high"` and no temperature.
- Anthropic API-key body for claude-opus-5 with Deep reasoning has `thinking.type=adaptive` and no temperature.

Mock-server adapter tests assert the request bodies. Real check: one Foundry deployment and one Anthropic key.
- **Verification (confirmed):** anthropic.rs:82-108: apply_thinking runs only in the Auth::Oauth arm. bluey-protocols/src/openai.rs:53-93 build_chat_body has no reasoning field, and the only 'reasoning' in that file is reasoning_content in the response parser (:152). The Azure half matters more than stated. Foundry's preset models are reasoning models (presets.rs:115-121), and the ChatBodyOptions change must also stop sending temperature. See the missed finding azure-reasoning-models-reject-temperature, which is the more severe consequence of the same code.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/reasoning-level-ignored-some-providers)_

#### PROV-006

**Switching 'Default provider' to Microsoft Foundry moves the Transcription role to MAI-Transcribe-1.5, after which Import recording fails with NotSupported**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: ai/mod.rs batch_transcription_target: when the transcription assignment cannot batch-transcribe (for example Foundry), transcribe_file falls back to the first enabled Gemini provider that has a key. With no such provider it raises an error that asks for one (commit 232b158).

- **User impact:** A user who picks Foundry as the default provider (the documented one-switch path) can no longer import meeting recordings, even though their Gemini key is still stored. The error tells them to undo the switch they just made.
- **Root cause:** The Foundry preset assigns a transcription model that only the live Voice Live route can use. The batch path supports Gemini only, and presets don't check whether the provider can serve each role in-app.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx:233-249 — switchDefaultProvider calls applyProviderPresets with overwrite:true; the header says models are assigned to 'chat, vision, transcription, research and embeddings'; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/presets.rs:120 — Foundry preset `(ModelRole::Transcription, Some("MAI-Transcribe-1.5"))`; apply_presets overwrites every role that has a preset (228-260); src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:741-748 — transcribe_file: `if !matches!(config.kind, GoogleGemini | Mock) { return Err(not_supported(… "assign the transcription role to Google Gemini")) }`; src/features/settings/session-import.ts:54-64 and SessionDetail.tsx:230 — Import recording calls ai.transcribeFile
- **Proposed solution:** Minimal: in transcribe_file, when the transcription assignment is not Gemini, fall back to the first enabled Gemini provider with a key and its preset batch model, the way audio/mod.rs:369-389 already does for live transcription. Say so in the result. Alternative: have apply_presets skip Transcription for kinds that have no batch transcriber, unless the live provider setting is CloudRealtime.
- **Test plan:** Unit test in ai/mod.rs: transcription = azure/MAI-Transcribe-1.5 plus a gemini provider with a key → transcribe_file uses the Gemini adapter (mock). Also test the case with no Gemini provider: NotSupported, with copy that doesn't blame the user's switch. Frontend test: switching the default provider to Foundry still lets you import.
- **Verification (confirmed):** AITab.tsx:233-249 calls applyProviderPresets with overwrite:true. presets.rs:120 assigns Transcription to MAI-Transcribe-1.5. presets.rs:228-260 overwrites every role that has a preset. ai/mod.rs:741-748 transcribe_file rejects non-Gemini kinds with not_supported. The section description at AITab.tsx:256 explicitly promises that transcription is reassigned. The proposed fallback to an enabled Gemini provider with a key mirrors the live path and is minimal.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/default-provider-switch-foundry-breaks-import)_

#### PROV-007

**A request-time 401 on an unexpired OAuth token flips the account straight to NeedsReauth; nothing forces a refresh-and-retry first**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: A 401 on an unexpired token forces one single-flight refresh (bluey-oauth TokenCache::force_refresh, accounts::refresh_rejected). ai/mod.rs drive_provider retries once, before the first byte. It falls back to NeedsReauth only if the refresh is rejected. Rotating or unknown-origin imports are never force-refreshed.

- **User impact:** Clock skew, a server-side token rotation, or signing in from the official CLI can put Bluey's account into 'Sign-in expired'. A silent refresh would have fixed it. The user has to go through the browser OAuth flow again, and meanwhile hits the dead end described in F1.
- **Root cause:** The refresh policy is purely expiry-based. Request errors never feed back into the token cache.
- **Evidence:** bluey-oauth/src/tokens.rs:101-124 — TokenCache::fresh refreshes only when `current.is_expiring(leeway, now)`; there is no invalidate or force method; bluey-protocols/src/claude_code.rs:976, codex.rs:1447, antigravity.rs:1067 — `401 => needs_reauth()`; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:462-467 → accounts/mod.rs:715-730 note_request_error sets NeedsReauth; the account stays unusable until the user signs in again in the browser; docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:94 (Codex) and :160 (Claude) — the official clients do '401 → refresh once → NeedsReauth'
- **Proposed solution:** Add `TokenCache::force_refresh(stale_access_token, refresh)`, single-flight and a no-op if the stored token already differs from the stale one. In AiManager::run_stream/drive_provider, when an OAuth adapter returns account.needs_reauth caused by HTTP 401 (mark it with details.http=401), call `accounts.credential_for_forced`, rebuild the adapter, and retry once before the first byte. Mark NeedsReauth only if the refresh itself fails with an authentication error.
- **Test plan:** bluey-oauth test: force_refresh with concurrent callers runs one refresh, and a stale-token check skips it. ai/mod.rs test with a mock OAuth adapter returning 401 once and then success: the account stays Connected and the stream completes.
- **Verification (confirmed):** bluey-oauth/src/tokens.rs:101-124: `fresh` refreshes only when is_expiring, and there is no force or invalidate method. The only `force` in accounts is refresh_catalog's cache bypass (accounts/mod.rs:586-600). Protocol mappers send 401 to needs_reauth (claude_code.rs:976, codex.rs:1447, antigravity.rs:1067). note_request_error (accounts/mod.rs:714-730) persists NeedsReauth, and after that provider_config sets has_api_key=false, so the account is never tried again until the user reconnects. The fix must be single-flight and must retry only before the first byte.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/account-401-no-forced-refresh)_

#### PROV-008

**Structured output is always requested; OpenAI-compatible endpoints that reject json_schema fail every schema-backed answer, with no plain-text fallback**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: On a 400 that rejects response_format/json_schema, OpenAI-compatible and Azure requests are sent again without it, with the schema carried in the prompt. The fallback sticks per provider+model for the life of the process (commit e6481d2).

- **User impact:** Users of endpoints that support only `json_object` or no response_format (reportedly DeepSeek's API, some OpenRouter or Groq models, older local servers) get 'The model didn't answer' on every mode that uses a schema.
- **Root cause:** The Anthropic adapter got a capability fallback; the OpenAI-compatible adapter never did.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/request.ts:87 — `structured = Boolean(args.outputSchema) && args.useStructuredOutput !== false`; rg finds no caller that sets useStructuredOutput; bluey-protocols/src/openai.rs:72-93 — always sends `response_format: {type: json_schema, strict: true}`; providers/openai.rs:59-61 — a 400 becomes a generic ai.http_400 (see the generic-errors finding); Anthropic has exactly this fallback: providers/anthropic.rs:179-197 (is_output_config_rejection → schema-in-prompt retry); `src/ai/engine.ts:489-502 — the engine retries only when the output is truncated`
- **Proposed solution:** Mirror the Anthropic approach in OpenAiProvider::stream: on a 400 whose body mentions response_format, json_schema or 'not supported', resend without response_format. The prompt already carries structuredOutputBlock (src/ai/prompt-builder.ts:79). Remember the result per provider for the process lifetime.
- **Test plan:** Mock-server test: the first POST returns 400 with `{"error":{"message":"response_format json_schema is not supported"}}`, the second succeeds, and the second body has no response_format. Real check against one OpenAI-compatible endpoint.
- **Depends on:** generic-http-errors-non-gemini
- **Verification (confirmed):** No caller sets useStructuredOutput (only its definition at request.ts:14 and the check at :87). engine.ts:376-409 passes outputSchemaFor(intent.schemaId). build_chat_body (bluey-protocols/src/openai.rs:72-92) always sends strict json_schema. openai.rs:60-61 turns any 400 into generic ai.http_400. The Anthropic adapter has the schema-in-prompt retry (anthropic.rs:179-197). Which OpenAI-compatible backends reject json_schema is a runtime fact, so confidence stays 'likely'. The impact is limited to users of such endpoints.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/structured-output-no-fallback-openai-compat)_

#### PROV-010

**Vision routing is a per-provider-kind constant (always true); the per-model vision flag in the catalog is never consulted**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Take a text-only model on the Default role with Vision unassigned: GPT-OSS through Antigravity, a local OpenAI-compatible model, or a Codex model without image input. A question about the screen then either fails with a generic HTTP 400, or the image is silently dropped and the answer ignores the screen.
- **Root cause:** Capability is modelled per provider kind rather than per model. Catalog capability data was added for accounts, but routing was never wired to it.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/router.rs:36-47 — provider_supports_vision returns true for every AiProviderKind, and the unit test all_provider_kinds_support_vision locks that in; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/accounts.rs:185-193 — ModelCapabilities.vision is populated from catalogs (bluey-protocols/src/antigravity.rs:1520 GPT-OSS vision=false; codex.rs:2232) but never read outside tests; router.rs:241 — the Vision role falls back to Default; src/ai/engine.ts:378 attaches the screenshot whenever visionRequired; src/context/relevance.ts:307 sets visionRequired from screen context
- **Proposed solution:** Change `provider_supports_vision(kind)` to `model_supports_vision(provider, model, catalogs)`. For OAuth providers, use the catalog's `capabilities.vision`. For API-key kinds, default to true, with an optional per-assignment `vision: false` override (or a small list of known text-only prefixes for OpenAI-compatible). router::select already skips candidates that fail the check, so vision→default correctly ends in config.no_model with an explicit 'no vision-capable model' message instead of a provider 400. Pass the catalogs in through RoutingInput or AiProviderConfig.
- **Test plan:** Router tests:
- Default = antigravity GPT-OSS (vision=false) and vision unassigned, with vision_required → error code config.no_vision_model (or a fallback to a vision-capable provider).
- Default = gemini → selected.

Add an integration test in ai/mod.rs with a stub catalog.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The code claim holds: router.rs:36-47 is a constant true, and ModelCapabilities.vision is read only by catalog code and tests (codex.rs:1188 uses m.vision() only to pick which model gets the Vision role). But the impact is smaller than claimed. Account catalogs assign the Vision role to vision-capable models themselves (antigravity.rs:641 `add(default, &[Default, Vision])` with Gemini flash; codex.rs:1188 vision_idx), so vision→default fallback only reaches a text-only model when the user manually picks one for Default and leaves Vision unassigned. The result is a provider 400 shown as a generic error. That makes it Low, not Medium. Minimal fix: skip in router::select using catalog capabilities for OAuth providers only.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/vision-capability-kind-level)_

#### UX-020

**Choosing an OpenAI-compatible provider as 'Default provider' changes only the label and badge; no role is re-pointed and the router ignores bootstrapProvider**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** The user sees their OpenAI-compatible endpoint marked Default, but answers keep coming from Gemini (or fail with 'No model assigned' if Gemini isn't set up). This is confusing and can send data to a provider the user thinks they switched away from.
- **Root cause:** 'Default provider' is really 'apply this kind's presets'. For a kind with no presets, the only thing that changes is a UI-only field.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/ai/provider-presets.ts:74-90 — OPENAI_PRESET has every model set to null; presetForKind still returns it; src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx:236-244 — applyProviderPresets (a no-op: presets.rs:242-249 skips roles with no recommended model), then `update({ ai: { bootstrapProvider } })` and the toast '… is now the default provider'; AITab.tsx:205-208 — defaultProviderId prefers bootstrapProvider, so the Default badge moves (ProviderCard isDefault); router.rs:61-139 reads only ai.models; bootstrap_provider is used only by the env import (presets.rs:373, 429)
- **Proposed solution:** For kinds with no preset models, disable the option or open the Models section with Default focused. Otherwise require a model: call ai_list_models, pick the first result or prompt the user, then assign Default and Fast. Never show the success toast unless ai.models.default.providerId actually equals the chosen provider. Derive the badge from ai.models.default, not bootstrapProvider.
- **Test plan:** Vitest for AITab: switching to an OpenAI-compatible provider with no models shows the model picker or an error and no success toast; the badge follows models.default. An e2e test against the real settings command (not a mock transport) checks the settings patch.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed mechanically. presetForKind('openai_compatible') returns OPENAI_PRESET (provider-presets.ts:74-90, all null), and Rust by_kind returns Some(&OPENAI) (presets.rs:168,172), so apply_presets succeeds but changes nothing. AITab.tsx:243-244 then writes bootstrapProvider and shows 'is now the default provider'. The badge follows bootstrapProvider (AITab.tsx:205-208) and the router never reads it. The impact is a misleading label and toast, not broken routing: requests keep working on the previous Default. Low rather than Medium. Also note AITab.tsx:256 says roles the provider doesn't serve keep their current model, which partly discloses this.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/default-provider-openai-compat-noop)_

#### PROV-011

**The 'Research' role is labelled 'Deep research agent', but the deep-research sidecar ignores assignments on other provider kinds and needs a separate Anthropic 'agent' key**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The Research role label and hint say it drives answers that need research and point to the Deep research backend setting (commit 1c08547).

- **User impact:** A user who assigns Research to ChatGPT, Claude subscription or Foundry believes deep research uses it, but it quietly runs on Gemini (or fails 'unavailable' without a Gemini key). Claude-backend users must paste the same Anthropic key twice.
- **Root cause:** The deep-research sidecar has its own backend selection (ADR 0004/0007), and the role picker was never reconciled with it.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx:41 — `{ role: "research", label: "Research", hint: "Deep research agent" }`; src-tauri/src/agent/mod.rs:165-185 — research_model uses the assignment only when the provider kind is GoogleGemini (Gemini backend) or Anthropic (Claude backend); otherwise it silently uses the Gemini preset or the sidecar default; agent/mod.rs:136-143 and secrets/mod.rs:29 — the Claude backend reads `agent:anthropic:api_key` (or ANTHROPIC_API_KEY from the env), not the Anthropic provider's key; AITab.tsx:457-465 shows a second 'Anthropic (agent)' key field; The router sends inline AiTask::Research to the Research role on any provider (router.rs task mapping)
- **Proposed solution:** Split the hint ('Research answers' vs the Deep research backend section). Show an inline warning in AITab when the research assignment's kind doesn't match `researchBackend`. In job_env, when `agent:anthropic:api_key` is missing, fall back to the enabled Anthropic provider's key.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit tests for research_model and job_env: an Anthropic provider key alone makes the Claude backend available(); an Azure research assignment produces a mismatch flag. Vitest for the AITab warning.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The code behaves as described (agent/mod.rs:136-143 uses AGENT_ANTHROPIC_KEY or passthrough; research_model at :165-185 ignores assignments on other kinds). But this is intentional and documented in the doc comment at agent/mod.rs:160-164 and in ADR 0004/0007, and inline AiTask::Research does use the Research role on any provider. The only problem is the misleading hint 'Deep research agent' plus a second key field. This is copy and UX clarity, so Low. The proposal to fall back to the Anthropic provider key is reasonable.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/research-role-dual-meaning)_

#### PROV-012

**Preset model ids are hard-coded and never checked against catalogs; the Azure 'catalog' is a static list; a model that disappears is not repaired automatically for API-key providers**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** When Google, Microsoft or Anthropic retire an id, every install on the defaults breaks until a release ships or the user edits the models by hand. Foundry users are offered model ids their resource doesn't have.
- **Root cause:** Presets are compile-time constants, and there is no reconciliation step against list_models.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/presets.rs:95-143 — gemini-3.8-flash, gemini-3.5-flash-lite, gpt-5.6-terra, gpt-6-astra, claude-sonnet-5, claude-haiku-4-5, claude-opus-5 …; providers/azure.rs:27-38, 128-143 — list_models = deployment keys plus COMMON_MODELS, shown as suggestions even when not deployed; Accounts do repair themselves: bluey-core/src/accounts.rs:215-238 re-points roles whose model left the catalog; API-key providers have nothing equivalent (Gemini only gives an actionable 404)
- **Proposed solution:** When config.model_not_found comes back for an assignment that equals the preset, look up ai_list_models(role) and suggest (or apply, with a toast) the closest id in the same family. For Azure, label COMMON_MODELS 'suggestions (deploy first)' in the datalist.
- **Test plan:** Unit test: a model_not_found on a preset assignment together with a stubbed list_models → a suggested replacement from the same family.
- **Depends on:** generic-http-errors-non-gemini
- **Verification (confirmed):** presets.rs:95-143 holds compile-time ids. accounts.rs apply_catalog_presets repairs stale account roles, and API-key providers have no equivalent. Low is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/hardcoded-model-ids-no-validation)_

#### UX-021

**Role fallbacks (vision→default, fast→default, research→reasoning→default) are never shown to the user; selection.reason is dropped by the WebView**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — F (PROV-001) carries the router's fallback reason into response.selection; E2 (UX-035) shows it in the provenance line under the answer.

- **User impact:** Users can't tell why an answer came from a different model, for example a screenshot question answered by the default text model. Debugging routing needs the logs.
- **Root cause:** The routing reason is produced but never used by the UI.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:446-449 — AiChunk::Started{selection} carries role and reason, e.g. '→ fallback default' (router.rs:115-117); src/ai/stream.ts:83,146 — onStarted is defined but rg finds no caller that passes it; src/ai/metrics.ts:25-27 keeps only provider and model
- **Proposed solution:** Store selection.role and reason in the response metadata. Show a subtle 'via <provider> · fallback' chip in the HUD response footer when selection.role differs from the requested role.
- **Test plan:** Vitest for stream.ts/engine: a Started chunk with role default for a vision request sets fallback metadata; the component test renders the chip.
- **Verification (confirmed):** stream.ts:83,146 defines onStarted and stores the selection in the result, but rg finds no UI code reading selection.reason or selection.role in src (outside tests). metrics.ts:17 keeps the selection object, but only provider and model are surfaced.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/silent-role-fallback)_

#### UX-034

**Provider setup is too technical: 7 role rows with free-text model ids, saving a key assigns nothing, and roles accept providers that can't serve them**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — F (FEATURE-004): the first key saved for a preset provider fills unassigned roles with its presets and becomes the default, with Undo. Key-prefix inference and collapsing Models behind Advanced are follow-ups.

- **User impact:** A normal user who adds an Anthropic or Foundry key outside onboarding gets 'No model assigned' until they find the right button. Power-user settings (Fast, Reasoning, Vision, Research) sit alongside the essential ones, and invalid combinations are only discovered when requests fail.
- **Root cause:** Role assignment is exposed as the main configuration model. Presets exist but aren't applied automatically on key save, and roles have no eligibility metadata.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx:41-49 (7 roles), 57-178 (ModelRoleRow: every routable provider in every role, free-text model, listModels errors swallowed at :97-99); ProviderCard.tsx:195-202 — the key's onSaved only clears the test result; roles are assigned only by the manual 'Use recommended models' button (ProviderCard.tsx:205-215), onboarding Gemini (connect.tsx:156) or the env import; Runtime rejections that are selectable today: embedding on Anthropic → not_supported (anthropic.rs:205-213); batch transcription on non-Gemini → not_supported (ai/mod.rs:741-748)
- **Proposed solution:** (1) When a provider's key is first saved, call apply_provider_presets with overwrite:false, as onboarding does. (2) Add role-eligibility metadata per kind (embedding, batch transcription) and filter each role's provider list with it. (3) Collapse Fast, Reasoning, Vision and Research under an 'Advanced routing' disclosure, since Default plus the presets can infer them. (4) Validate a typed model id against ai_list_models and warn inline, without blocking.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: saving a key for a new provider with empty roles triggers applyProviderPresets(overwrite:false); the embedding row hides Anthropic. Playwright visual check of the collapsed 'Advanced routing' section.
- **Verification (confirmed):** AITab.tsx:95-97 swallows listModels errors ('listing is best-effort; typing a model id always works'), and every routable provider is offered for every role. The eligibility-metadata suggestion would also prevent the Foundry transcription issue and embedding-on-Anthropic. Reasonable as an opportunity.
- _Source: auditor (providers-routing-accounts/provider-config-too-technical)_

### Onboarding & settings coherence

#### ONB-001

**Onboarding says '<name> is ready' when no role can reach a usable provider (failed key, skip, 'Use another provider', account-only)**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: New ai_readiness command: AiManager::readiness runs router select for Answer and Vision against the real provider state (keys, enabled flags, accounts, Cloud AI) and returns AiReadiness {ok, providerId, model, vision, error}. It is registered in lib.rs, commands.ts and api.ts, and the mock transport routes the same way. The useAiReadiness hook drives onboarding's TestAIStep and ReadyStep ('Bluey can't answer yet' with the cause and a fix action; it never claims ready otherwise). TestAIStep tests the provider the router picks. connect.tsx uses keyUsable. The Settings → AI tab shows a readiness banner (commit c69c75f).

- **User impact:** A new user can finish onboarding (typo'd key, skipped, or set up Anthropic/OpenAI via 'Use another provider', or a ChatGPT/Claude subscription) and be told Bluey is ready. The first ⌘↵ then fails with 'No model assigned'. The Test-AI step reinforces the confusion by testing Gemini ('API key missing') when the user configured something else.
- **Root cause:** Readiness is computed piecewise in the UI (key stored / any provider keyed / account connected) instead of asking the router whether the Default role resolves to a usable provider. Saving a key outside the Gemini step never assigns roles. TestAIStep resolves providers from settings.ai.providers only.
- **Evidence:** src/features/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx:34 `const [ready, setReady] = useState(true)`; :41-46 last step writes onboardingCompleted and opens main with no readiness check; src/features/onboarding/steps/connect.tsx:59 `hasKey = gemini?.hasApiKey` (stored, not verified); :73 `onReady(hasKey || otherProviderReady || accountReady || skipped)`. Continue is enabled while status === 'failed'; connect.tsx:91-97 always inserts an enabled, keyless `gemini` provider at the FRONT of the list; connect.tsx:201 'Use another provider' opens Settings→AI. Adding a provider and saving its key does not assign roles: ProviderCard.tsx:201 `onSaved={() => setResult(null)}`; commands/settings.rs:38-43 only refresh_provider_keys. Yet connect.tsx:60-61 `otherProviderReady` then enables Continue; src/features/onboarding/steps/tests.tsx:105-107 TestAI provider = default-role provider ?? first enabled provider, which is the keyless gemini. Account providers are never in settings.ai.providers (AITab.tsx:188-203), so an account-only user tests Gemini with the account's model id; `tests.tsx:169-179 ReadyStep always says `${blueyName} is ready``; app/checks.rs:26-28, 56-69: the AI setup check exists but is shallow and is never run by onboarding; src/features/hud/*: no provider-readiness surface (rg), so the first failure only appears on the first ask as config.no_model; tests/ui/onboarding.test.tsx:166-189 renders ConnectAIStep with `onReady={() => {}}`; the readiness gate after a failed test is not asserted
- **Proposed solution:** 1) Add a backend `ai_readiness` command (or extend `setup_checks` AiCheck) that runs `router::select` for AiTask::Answer (and Vision) against `AiManager::providers()`. It returns {ok, providerId, model, cause: missing_key|disabled|unassigned|account_<state>}. 2) TestAIStep tests exactly that selection (providerId + model) rather than guessing. 3) ReadyStep shows the result: when not ok, a warning row 'Bluey can't answer yet — <cause>' with a Fix button (open Settings → AI). Keep 'Open Bluey' allowed so skipping stays possible, but make the state honest. 4) In ConnectAIStep, when status==='failed', label the button 'Continue without a working key' or keep it disabled until Retry or Skip. 5) See infer-routing-from-keys: auto-apply presets to unassigned roles when any preset-kind provider's key is saved.
- **Test plan:** Rust: unit tests for readiness: default role → keyless provider ⇒ cause missing_key; → disabled ⇒ disabled; → account NeedsReauth ⇒ account_needs_reauth; unassigned ⇒ unassigned. Vitest (MockTransport, but assert on the command args, not mock output): ConnectAIStep with simulated config.api_key_invalid ⇒ the onReady spy receives false (or the button label changes); 'another provider' path (anthropic keyed, gemini keyless, no roles) ⇒ TestAIStep calls ai_test_connection with the provider chosen by readiness; ReadyStep renders the warning when readiness.ok=false. Manual on macOS: fresh profile, skip the key, check the Ready step warning.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Mostly holds. connect.tsx:59 hasKey = stored flag, :73 onReady(hasKey||otherProviderReady||accountReady||skipped), so Continue stays enabled after status==='failed'. connect.tsx:25-34 geminiProviderConfig() defaults enabled:true, hasApiKey:false, and :91-97 prepends it. tests.tsx:105-107 falls back to the first enabled provider. ReadyStep :173-178 always says '<name> is ready'. Secrets saved from Settings only refresh flags (commands/settings.rs:38-43); ProviderCard onSaved resets the test result only (:201). The only preset callers are connect.tsx:156, AITab.tsx:242, ProviderCard.tsx:155 and AccountsSection.tsx:57. Correction on case (4), subscription accounts: the account path DOES assign roles. accounts/mod.rs:611,642-660 apply_catalog_presets_after_fetch fills unassigned roles with the account's models, and AiManager::providers() adds account configs (ai/mod.rs:143-150). So an account-only user can actually answer. The only defect there is that TestAIStep resolves providers from settings.ai.providers alone, so it tests keyless Gemini with the account's model id and shows a false failure. The Ready copy is correct for that user. Minor summary inaccuracy: the permissions step also disables Continue until the last sub-screen (permissions.tsx:65-67). The fix proposal is sound. Build TestAIStep on a backend readiness call that uses AiManager::providers() (which includes accounts), not the settings list.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/onboarding-completes-without-usable-ai)_

#### SEC-010

**The sign-in gate only exists in the UI: sign-out leaves mic/system-audio capture running, and signed-out users can start listening from the shortcut or menu bar with no visible state**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Sign-out stops audio capture and cancels AI work (commands/auth.rs). Audio start is refused with AuthRequired when signed out (audio/mod.rs guard) (6b14460, a675eb7).

- **User impact:** If a user logs out (Settings → General or Profile) during a listening session, microphone and system-audio capture plus cloud transcription keep running. The HUD shows only 'Sign in to use Bluey' and the menu bar offers 'Start Listening'. A signed-out user can also start capture with ⌘⇧L without any sign-in. Only macOS's orange mic dot reveals it.
- **Root cause:** Auth is enforced by React gates and soft state-machine transitions. Native entry points (global shortcuts, menu bar) and the sign-out path don't consult or change the audio manager.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/auth/mod.rs:574-603 clear_session revokes tokens and transitions SignedOut but never calls audio.stop() or ai.cancel_all() (contrast commands/data.rs:54-57, which does both); bluey-core/src/state/mod.rs:181-189 SignedOut sets audio_active=false and session_id=None while AudioManager keeps its own Running state (audio/mod.rs:207-212); src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:221-227 ToggleListening → ctx.audio.start(None) in any auth state; platform/mod.rs:223-229 menu-bar item does the same; audio/mod.rs:240-336 start() has no auth check and uses transition_soft(AudioStarted); state/mod.rs:291-301 audio_toggle_allowed excludes AuthRequired, so the soft transition is dropped and the menu-bar label (platform/mod.rs:115-120, from status.audio_active) keeps saying 'Start Listening' while the mic is live; AuthGate.tsx:111 HUD shows only HudSignInPrompt, with no listening indicator or stop control
- **Proposed solution:** (a) In `commands/auth.rs::auth_clear_session` (or `AuthManager::clear_session` via a callback), stop audio when running and cancel AI streams before clearing, mirroring data_reset_all. (b) Add a guard `if core.auth.auth_required() && core.hub.state() == AppState::AuthRequired` in `AudioManager::start` (or in the shortcut / menu-bar handlers). It returns `BlueyError::authentication("required")` with the SignIn recovery, so the existing AppError toast shows it. (c) Derive the menu-bar label from `audio.is_running()` rather than status.audio_active.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit/integration: with a fake helper, start audio, call clear_session ⇒ audio.status().state == Stopped. Shortcut trigger ToggleListening in AuthRequired ⇒ start not called and an authentication error is published. listening_label with audio running but AppState::AuthRequired ⇒ 'Stop Listening'. Manual macOS: start listening, Log out, and check the orange mic indicator disappears.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The chain is verified. auth/mod.rs:574-603 clear_session never stops audio (data_reset_all at commands/data.rs:54-57 does). shortcuts/mod.rs:221-227 and platform/mod.rs:223-229 call audio.start(None) with no auth check. Shortcuts are registered at boot whatever the auth state (app/mod.rs bootstrap apply_bindings). state/mod.rs:291-301 excludes AuthRequired, so transition_soft(AudioStarted) is dropped, and listening_label (platform/mod.rs:115-120) keeps saying 'Start Listening'. Downgraded to Medium: the Clerk sign-in guards no local asset (keys and data stay on the machine for the same local user), macOS shows its own microphone-in-use indicator, and pressing the same toggle stops capture again (is_running check). It is still a real state-desync bug with a privacy flavour (cloud STT may stream while 'signed out'). Categorize as UX/state bug rather than SEC. Fix (a) plus (c) is the minimum. For (b), gate on auth_required() && !signed_in, not on hub state alone.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/signout-leaves-audio-running-auth-gate-ui-only)_

#### UX-007

**Routing failures always say 'No model assigned' even when a model IS assigned but its provider has no key, is disabled, or is an expired account**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: The router returns config.provider_unusable with {providerId, providerName, cause: missing_key|locked_key|disabled|account_<state>} instead of 'No model assigned'. AiManager::name_account_state fills in the account state. present.ts has per-cause copy and an Open AI settings action (commit 565e949).

- **User impact:** Users can't tell why Bluey can't answer. They see their model assigned in Settings → AI → Models while the error insists none is. The real fix (add or replace a key, re-enable the provider, reconnect the account) is never named.
- **Root cause:** The router collapses all failure causes into one code and throws away the per-candidate reason; the UI maps that code to text that assumes 'unassigned'.
- **Evidence:** router.rs:104-111 skips an assignment when `!provider_usable` (disabled or missing key) and records why only in `reason`; :132-138 returns config.no_model 'no usable model for role …'; present.ts:75-78 CODE_COPY config.no_model = 'No model assigned — Assign a model to this role in Settings → AI → Models…', which overrides the Rust message; `error.rs:187-194 configuration() recovery = OpenSettings{ai} (generic)`; AITab.tsx:136 role select only marks '(disabled)', never '(no key)', so the user sees an assigned model and a message saying there isn't one
- **Proposed solution:** Have router::select return a specific error for the first skipped assignment of the desired role: `config.provider_unusable` with details {providerId, providerName, cause: missing_key|disabled|account_<state>} and recovery ConfigureProvider (or ReconnectAccount for accounts). Keep `config.no_model` for truly unassigned roles. Add CODE_COPY entries (e.g. '<Provider> has no API key — add one in Settings → AI') that interpolate the provider name from details in describeError.
- **Test plan:** bluey-core router tests for each cause ⇒ code + details; src/lib/errors present tests for the new copy and recovery label; a UI test in the AI tab: role row shows '(no key)'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** router.rs:104-111 skips with a reason string only. :132-138 returns config.no_model. present.ts:75-78 renders 'No model assigned' through CODE_COPY (present.ts:272). AITab.tsx:133-136 role select labels only '(disabled)', not '(no key)'. Unused copy for 'config.missing_key'-style ('API key missing', present.ts:72-73) already exists and could be reused if the router emitted a distinct code.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/no-model-copy-hides-cause)_

#### UX-008

**API-key providers cannot be removed and keys cannot be deleted; disabling or leaving a provider keyless silently breaks roles, and no card shows why a provider is unusable**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-F: ProviderCard has a Remove action with a ConfirmDialog that lists the roles the provider serves. AITab.removeProvider sends one settings patch that drops the provider, nulls its roles and clears bootstrapProvider. The existing side_effects removed_provider_ids deletes its Keychain key. Cards show a 'No key' badge and 'Used by …'. Role pickers label keyless providers '(no key)' / '(not connected)'. The AISettings.bootstrapProvider type now allows null (commit ea8f373). A confirm before disabling a provider is not included.

- **User impact:** Users who rotate providers keep stale cards forever. They can't revoke a key from Bluey short of 'Reset Bluey' (which erases everything). Disabling a provider silently breaks the roles that point at it, and nothing explains which provider or role is broken.
- **Root cause:** Provider management UI was built for add/edit only. Removal, key deletion and role-impact feedback were left out, even though the backend already supports deleting keys and provider rows (settings/mod.rs:132-139).
