- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/ProviderCard.tsx:185-193 only 'Edit' and an enable Switch; no Remove anywhere in AITab.tsx; src/features/settings/SecretKeyField.tsx:79-91 saved state offers only 'Replace'; `secrets.delete` (api.ts:191 → commands/settings.rs:52-57) has no UI caller (rg); AITab.tsx:303-307 onToggleEnabled patches `enabled` without checking or listing roles that point at the provider; router then skips it (router.rs:104-111); AITab.tsx:127-138 role select allows providers without a key; the default-provider select (:272-273) does mark '(no key)', so the two selects are inconsistent; ProviderCard.tsx:128-146 test result is component state only; nothing persisted or shown about usability (no key / disabled / used by roles); Contrast: account disconnect unassigns roles (accounts/mod.rs:538-580); config.unknown_provider copy exists (present.ts:79-82) but no UI path creates that state; src/features/settings/ProviderCard.tsx:133-233 — only Edit, the enable Switch, SecretKeyField, 'Use recommended models' and 'Test connection'; rg for providers.filter / removeProvider / 'Remove provider' in src finds nothing; commands/ai.rs has no remove command (removal would have to be a settings patch); No delete/remove action in src/features/settings/ProviderCard.tsx or src/features/settings/tabs/AITab.tsx (rg); docs/reference/bluey-sidecar-and-frontend-audit.md B3.17/B5.8 and gemini-migration-brief PR 6 'delete provider action (with confirm) that also calls secrets_delete'
- **Proposed solution:** 1) Add 'Remove' to ProviderCard (confirm dialog listing affected roles). Frontend: `secrets.delete(providerApiKey(id))`, then one settings patch that drops the provider, nulls roles pointing at it and clears bootstrapProvider if it matches. Or add a Rust `ai_remove_provider` that does this atomically (preferred; mirrors accounts::unassign_roles). 2) Add 'Delete key' next to 'Replace' in SecretKeyField (optional prop). 3) ProviderCard badges: 'No key', 'Disabled', 'Used by: Default, Vision'. Confirm before disabling a provider that serves roles. 4) ModelRoleRow labels keyless providers '(no key)'.
- **Test plan:** Rust: ai_remove_provider unit test ⇒ provider row deleted, roles nulled, keychain delete called (fake SecretsStore), settings.changed published. Vitest AI tab: remove flow issues the expected commands; disabling a provider used by Default shows the confirmation; role select shows '(no key)'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The only secrets_delete references are the wrapper and command tables (api.ts:191, commands.ts:311,482), with no UI caller. SecretKeyField offers only 'Replace' (:87). No remove or delete control exists in ProviderCard or AITab (rg). onToggleEnabled (AITab.tsx:302-306) patches enabled blindly. The role select at :133-136 lacks a '(no key)' marker, while the default-provider select (:272-273) has one. Being unable to delete a leaked or unwanted key from the UI is the most user-relevant part.
- **Also reported as:** providers-routing-accounts/no-remove-provider, prior-audit-docs-drift/provider-delete-missing
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/provider-lifecycle-gaps)_

#### ONB-002

**Permission badges don't update after the user grants access in System Settings; the docs' refresh-on-focus, repair flow and restart offer are not implemented**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-F: New src/hooks/useLivePermissions.ts, used by the onboarding PermissionsStep and Settings → PermissionsTab. It re-reads permissions_get, which runs the Rust refresh, on mount, on window focus, and every 2 s while the window is visible, and stops on unmount. docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md refresh sentence corrected (commit d2346c5). Not done: the restart offer and mapping 'denied' to 'not requested yet'. Device check: Grant Accessibility in System Settings, return to onboarding: the badge should flip within 2 s.

- **User impact:** In onboarding the user clicks 'Open System Settings', enables Accessibility or Screen Recording and comes back to a card that still says 'Denied'/'Not granted yet'. That invites repeated clicks or a belief that it failed. First-run cards show 'Denied' before anything was asked. Revocations while idle are only discovered when a feature fails.
- **Root cause:** The permission manager was built around explicit requests and an audio-session poll. The focus-driven refresh described in the docs was never wired.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/permissions/mod.rs:58-86 refresh() only on: boot (app/mod.rs:384), permissions_get (commands/permissions.rs:10-11, once per window init), request (:113), setup checks (checks.rs:16-20), and the 30 s loop only while `hub.audio_active()` (:158-171); `No window focus/visibility listener refreshes permissions (rg in src/ and src-tauri/src)`; `src/features/settings/tabs/PermissionsTab.tsx:48 claims 'status updates live'`; docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:22-24 'refreshed on app focus, on window activation … Revocation stops the dependent subsystem … and surfaces a repair flow'; :48-49 'detects denied → granted transitions and offers Restart Bluey'. There is no Rust consumer of PermissionsChanged and no restart offer in src/ (rg 'Restart Bluey' only hits AuthGate dev copy); permissions/mod.rs:182-189, 206-215 Screen Recording / Accessibility report Denied when never asked → onboarding permissions.tsx:100 shows 'Denied' on first view; docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:25-27 — "refreshed on app focus, on window activation, every 30 s while a session is active, and after each request. Revocation stops the dependent subsystem ... repair flow"; docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:43-44 — "detects denied → granted transitions and offers Restart Bluey"; :16 notifications "for prepared suggestions and session events"; src-tauri/src/permissions/mod.rs:23-24,155-170 — the only loop is 30 s and gated on hub.audio_active(); src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:85-93 — on_window_event handles CloseRequested only; src/stores/initStores.ts:39,76 — one initial get plus a store mirror of permissions.changed; no Rust consumer of PermissionsChanged; No restart-offer code; no notification is ever posted (only request/state at permissions/mod.rs:97,133)
- **Proposed solution:** Frontend: in permissionsStore, register `getCurrentWindow().onFocusChanged(({payload}) => payload && load())` once per window (settings and onboarding). Also poll `load()` every 2 s while PermissionsStep or PermissionsTab is mounted. Map Screen Recording/Accessibility 'denied' to 'Not granted yet' until a request has been made in this install (track requested kinds in the store). Rust (optional): refresh on `WindowEvent::Focused(true)` for the settings/onboarding windows. Update MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md to match (or implement the restart offer: when screen_recording flips denied→granted, show 'Restart Bluey' calling updates_relaunch / app restart).
- **Test plan:** Vitest: PermissionsStep with a mocked window focus event ⇒ permissions_get invoked again and the badge updates from the returned state. Assert command args at the transport boundary, not store internals. Manual macOS: grant Accessibility in System Settings, return to onboarding, badge flips within 2 s.
- **Verification (confirmed):** permissionsStore.ts has only load/request, with no focus or visibility hook. The only update paths are init load, request, and the permissions.changed event (initStores.ts:39), which is emitted only by refresh(). Refresh runs from permissions_get (commands/permissions.rs:10-11), request, the 30 s loop gated on hub.audio_active() (permissions/mod.rs:158-171), and dev.rs. The only Rust Focused(true) handler is overlay geometry (overlay/mod.rs:184). MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:22-24 and :47-48 describe unimplemented focus refresh, repair flow and Restart offer. Onboarding shows 'Denied' for never-asked Screen Recording/Accessibility (permissions/mod.rs:182-189, 206-215; permissions.tsx:100). The proposed focus-listener fix is correct and small.
- **Also reported as:** prior-audit-docs-drift/permissions-refresh-doc-overclaim
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/permission-status-stale-after-system-settings)_

#### UX-009

**If auth status can't be read (e.g. keychain error), release builds show the developer 'Sign-in isn't configured — copy .env.example' screen in Settings and the HUD**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: auth status() tolerates Keychain errors, and AuthGate shows a StatusErrorPrompt instead of the developer 'Sign-in isn't configured' screen.

- **User impact:** After a keychain hiccup (locked login keychain, or the user denying an access prompt, which is likely after ad-hoc updates, see keychain-has-reads-secret-data), a normal user sees setup instructions meant for developers, in a tiny HUD panel, with no retry. In the Unknown state the HUD just doesn't appear.
- **Root cause:** The store treats 'status unknown because the call failed' the same as 'not configured', because mode defaults to unconfigured. Rust makes status() fail on a keychain read instead of degrading.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/auth/auth-store.ts:52 initial `mode: "unconfigured"`; :63-65 load() catch → `set({ loaded: true })` only; src/lib/auth/AuthGate.tsx:102 `if (loaded && mode === "unconfigured") return <ConfigurationScreen />` (developer .env instructions, :19-57), used for both variants including the HUD; src-tauri/src/auth/mod.rs:140-141 status() uses `self.secrets.has(...).await?`, so any keychain read error fails auth_get_status; secrets/mod.rs:118-133 has()/has_sync read the password data; AuthGate.tsx:104-110 state 'unknown' (tokens but no cached user, e.g. offline) → HUD renders null with no feedback
- **Proposed solution:** Rust: in `AuthManager::status`, map a keychain error to `has_stored_session: false` plus a warn log (or return state Unknown), so the command never fails for a storage hiccup. Frontend: add `error: BlueyError|null` to the auth store and set it in the load() catch without touching mode. AuthGate shows a compact 'Couldn't check your sign-in — Retry' state (HUD and full) when `error` is set, and only shows ConfigurationScreen when a status actually reported configured=false. For Unknown in the HUD, render the compact prompt with 'Checking sign-in…' instead of null.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: AuthGate with transport auth_get_status rejecting ⇒ renders the Retry state, not 'Sign-in isn't configured'; Retry re-invokes auth_get_status. Rust unit: status() with a failing secrets backend ⇒ Ok(AuthStatus{state: SignedOut|Unknown}).
- **Verification (confirmed):** auth-store.ts:52 defaults mode 'unconfigured'. The load() catch (:63-65) sets only loaded:true. AuthGate.tsx:102 then renders ConfigurationScreen for both variants. auth/mod.rs:140-141 status() propagates a secrets.has error, and has() → get() maps any non-NoEntry keyring error to storage.keychain (secrets/mod.rs:118-128). A realistic trigger is the user clicking Deny or Cancel on a keychain ACL prompt after an ad-hoc-signed update (see keychain-has-reads-secret-data), so the two findings compound. The HUD 'unknown' → null case (AuthGate.tsx:104-108) is also confirmed.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/auth-status-failure-shows-dev-config-screen)_

#### ONB-003

**Mandatory sign-in has no functional purpose yet blocks offline first run and locks Settings; signing out keeps all keys and data**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Owner decision (product policy: keep, explain or make sign-in optional). This cycle only fixed the security part (sign-out stops capture; backend auth guard on listening, SEC-010).

- **User impact:** Users without network at first launch can't use a local, BYO-key app at all. Signed-out users can't even reach Settings. 'Log out' suggests account separation that doesn't exist.
- **Root cause:** ADR 0008 introduced browser sign-in as a gate, but no feature depends on identity. The gate was applied to whole windows.
- **Evidence:** Clerk tokens are only used inside src-tauri/src/auth/ (rg `core.auth.` → commands/auth.rs, commands/data.rs:115, app/mod.rs:348); no server feature consumes them; basics.tsx:37 onboarding Continue requires signed_in when configured, so a first run offline stops at step 2; src/windows/SettingsWindow.tsx wraps everything in AuthGate, so a signed-out user cannot reach Privacy (Reset Bluey), AI keys or Permissions; auth/mod.rs:574-603 sign-out clears only Clerk tokens and the user row; provider keys, accounts, sessions and transcripts stay and are visible to the next account signing in (no user scoping in storage); HudSignInPrompt (AuthGate.tsx:68-86) opens the onboarding window at Welcome (step 0), not at sign-in
- **Proposed solution:** Product decision needed: (a) keep sign-in required but explain what it is for, let Settings (at least Privacy/Permissions/AI) open while signed out, and word 'Log out' as not deleting local data; or (b) make sign-in optional until a server feature exists. Minimum technical fix either way: HudSignInPrompt opens onboarding at the sign-in step (pass a route/step param) and the SettingsWindow gate exempts the Privacy tab.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: SettingsWindow signed_out ⇒ Privacy tab reachable (if option a); HudSignInPrompt opens onboarding with step=sign-in (command args). Manual: first run with Wi-Fi off.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Outside auth/, core.auth is only used by commands/auth.rs, commands/data.rs:115 and app/mod.rs:348 (callback). No provider or server call consumes Clerk tokens. basics.tsx:36-38 SignInStep requires signed_in in browser mode. SettingsWindow.tsx:11 and HudWindow.tsx:11 wrap everything in AuthGate. The full variant does show a SignInScreen, so Settings is not a dead end if the user is online, but Privacy/Reset is unreachable offline. HudSignInPrompt opens onboarding with no route (AuthGate.tsx:77), so it starts at step 0 (OnboardingFlow.tsx:33). This needs a product decision, and Medium is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/sign-in-hard-gate-without-purpose)_

#### ONB-004

**Onboarding progress is in-memory, so a relaunch (e.g. macOS 'Quit & Reopen' after granting Screen Recording) restarts at Welcome**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-F: New src/features/onboarding/progress.ts stores the current step id in the onboarding window's localStorage (key bluey.onboarding.step). OnboardingFlow resumes at that step, or at Welcome if the id is unknown. The saved step is cleared on completion and on 'Reset onboarding' in GeneralTab (commit d0c84f1). Not done: auto-skipping the sign-in step when the user is already signed in. Device check: Grant Screen Recording with 'Quit & Reopen' and confirm onboarding resumes at Permissions. This relies on WKWebView keeping localStorage across launches.

- **User impact:** The user reaches step 5 (Permissions), grants Screen Recording, and accepts 'Quit & Reopen'. Bluey comes back at step 1 and they must click through Welcome, Sign-in, Name and Connect again.
- **Root cause:** No persisted step pointer.
- **Evidence:** `OnboardingFlow.tsx:33 `const [index, setIndex] = useState(0)`; nothing persisted`; Screen Recording is requested in-process (permissions/mod.rs:92-95); macOS commonly asks the user to 'Quit & Reopen' the app before capture works (docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:47-48 acknowledges it); app/mod.rs:326 onboarding window opens fresh on every boot while onboardingCompleted=false
- **Proposed solution:** Persist `general.onboardingStep` (or localStorage in the onboarding window) on each `next`/`back`, restore it on mount (clamped to STEPS), and clear it on completion or 'Reset onboarding'. Skip the sign-in step automatically when already signed in.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: advance to 'permissions', unmount and remount OnboardingFlow ⇒ resumes at 'permissions'. Manual macOS: grant Screen Recording with Quit & Reopen.
- **Verification (confirmed):** OnboardingFlow.tsx:33 useState(0), nothing persisted. app/mod.rs:326-327 reopens the onboarding window at boot while the flag is false. On relaunch the user must re-walk the steps, including the 5 permission sub-screens (Continue is disabled until the last one, permissions.tsx:65-67). The macOS 'Quit & Reopen' prompt after a Screen Recording grant is a common platform behaviour but was not verified here. The same restart happens on any crash or quit mid-wizard. Medium is borderline; Low would also be defensible.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/onboarding-progress-not-persisted)_

#### DOC-003

**'Reset Bluey' erases everything but leaves the user in Settings (now signed out); the docs say it returns to onboarding**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: After a successful Reset all data, the HUD hides and onboarding opens, as the docs say (commit 1500e3e).

- **User impact:** After a full reset the user sees the Settings window flip to a sign-in card and a HUD sign-in prompt, instead of the first-run wizard.
- **Root cause:** The flag reset isn't followed by the window transition that boot performs.
- **Evidence:** `src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:155-162 run: resetAll then refreshStats only`; src-tauri/src/commands/data.rs:53-118 resets settings (onboardingCompleted=false) and clears sign-in but opens no window; settings/side_effects.rs has no onboarding handling (rg); `onboarding flag is only read at boot (app/mod.rs:275, 325-327)`; `docs/TESTING.md:51 'reset Bluey returns to onboarding'`
- **Proposed solution:** After a successful data_reset_all, hide the HUD and open the onboarding window (Rust, in data_reset_all, via platform::open_window + panel.hide), or do it from PrivacyTab after resetAll resolves.
- **Test plan:** Vitest PrivacyTab: reset ⇒ window_open{label:'onboarding'} invoked. Manual: reset on macOS.
- **Verification (confirmed):** PrivacyTab.tsx:159-162 calls resetAll then refreshStats. data_reset_all (commands/data.rs:53-118) opens no window. The onboarding flag is read only at boot (app/mod.rs:275,326) and by GeneralTab 'Reset onboarding' (GeneralTab.tsx:154-156), which does open the window. That is a ready-made pattern for PrivacyTab to copy. docs/TESTING.md:51 promises a return to onboarding.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/reset-bluey-does-not-return-to-onboarding)_

#### UX-022

**Onboarding copies Settings logic and copy and has drifted: hardcoded shortcuts on Ready, unguarded recorder and default-mode calls, 'Denied' before asking, no Notifications**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Small inconsistencies: wrong shortcut shown after a remap, silent failures, and alarming 'Denied' badges on first view.
- **Root cause:** Onboarding reimplements Settings pieces instead of sharing components and copy.
- **Evidence:** tests.tsx:176 Ready copy hardcodes '⌘\\' and '⌘↵' although ShortcutsStep (setup.tsx:47-77) lets the user remap them two steps earlier; setup.tsx:64-73 recorder async IIFE has no try/catch (KeybindsTab.tsx has one); a failed shortcuts_update leaves recording mode on and swallows keys until Esc; result ignored; `setup.tsx:28 `void bluey.modes.setDefault(...)` with no error surface`; Separate permission copy tables: steps/permissions.tsx:19-52 vs PermissionsTab.tsx:12-17 with different wording and status labels (:100 vs :19-25); Notifications permission is supported by Rust (permissions/mod.rs:96-98) and documented (MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:16) but absent from both UIs and never used to send notifications
- **Proposed solution:** Ready step renders `<Keycaps accelerator=…>` from settings.shortcuts. Extract the shortcut recorder hook from KeybindsTab and reuse it. Wrap setDefault in try/catch with showErrorToast. Share one PERMISSION_COPY module. Drop Notifications from the docs, or add it once something sends notifications.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: remap toggle_panel in ShortcutsStep, then ReadyStep shows the new keycaps; shortcuts_update rejection shows an error and exits recording.
- **Verification (confirmed):** tests.tsx:176 hardcodes ⌘\ and ⌘↵. setup.tsx:64-73 async IIFE has no try/catch around checkConflict/update. setup.tsx:28 `void bluey.modes.setDefault` has no error surface. No UI references Notifications and Rust sends no notifications (rg). All as stated.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/onboarding-settings-drift-small)_

#### ONB-005

**Finishing onboarding opens the HUD and closes the wizard even when saving onboardingCompleted failed, so the wizard silently returns on next launch**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: Finishing onboarding keeps the wizard open with an error when saving onboardingCompleted fails (commit da4d1d4).

- **User impact:** If the settings write fails (storage error), the error toast shows in the onboarding window, which is then closed right away. The user never sees it, and the full wizard reappears on the next launch with no explanation.
- **Root cause:** The last-step handler treats update() as if it throws on failure, but the store swallows errors and returns null.
- **Evidence:** src/features/onboarding/OnboardingFlow.tsx:42-46 `await update({ general: { onboardingCompleted: true } }); await bluey.window.open({ label: "main" }); await bluey.window.close({ label: "onboarding" });` ignores the result; src/stores/settingsStore.ts:33-43 update() catches, toasts inside the calling window, and returns null (never throws); `src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:326-327 reopens onboarding at every boot while the flag is false`
- **Proposed solution:** In next(): `const saved = await update(...); if (!saved) return;` so the window stays open with the toast visible and the user can retry.
- **Test plan:** Vitest on OnboardingFlow with mock transport: make settings_update reject on the final step, click 'Open Bluey', and assert window_open/window_close were not invoked and an error toast rendered.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (journey-onboarding-settings/onboarding-finish-ignores-save-failure)_

#### FEATURE-004

**Bluey makes users understand model roles: saving a key in Settings assigns nothing, and the default provider is never inferred**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence verified · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — WS-F: Implements the decided scope: src/features/settings/first-key-presets.ts adoptFirstKey, called from ProviderCard's key onSaved when the provider had no key. It applies presets with overwrite:false, sets bootstrapProvider when there was no default, and shows a toast 'Roles now use X's recommended models' with Undo, which restores the previous roles and default. Assigned roles are never touched. This runs in the frontend, not after Rust secrets_set, so Undo can use the snapshot taken before the change (commit f5c1b26). Not done: key-prefix inference, choosing the transcription provider, and collapsing Models behind Advanced.

- **User impact:** Users adding Anthropic/OpenAI/Foundry keys end up keyed but unrouted. Every non-Gemini user sees fallback notices for transcription.
- **Root cause:** The routing model is exposed directly rather than derived from what the user has configured.
- **Evidence:** commands/settings.rs:38-43 secrets_set only refreshes has_api_key flags; ProviderCard.tsx:201 onSaved resets the test result only; Only onboarding's Gemini path applies presets automatically (connect.tsx:156-159); Settings requires 'Use recommended models' (ProviderCard.tsx:153-165) or the default-provider switch (AITab.tsx:235-251); Default transcription provider is GeminiLive (bluey-core types/settings.rs:191) even for users without a Gemini key; it falls back to Apple Speech at runtime (present.ts audio.stt_fallback); `ModelRoleRow shows 7 roles (AITab.tsx:41-49) that a first-time user must understand`
- **Proposed solution:** 1) On the first key saved for a preset-kind provider (backend, after secrets_set), apply presets with overwrite:false and set bootstrapProvider if none. Show a toast 'Roles now use X's recommended models' with Undo. 2) Infer provider kind from the key prefix in a single onboarding 'Paste any key' field (AIza… → Gemini, sk-ant- → Anthropic, sk-… → OpenAI-compatible), with a manual override. 3) Choose the transcription provider from what is usable (Gemini key → GeminiLive, else Apple). 4) Collapse the Models section behind 'Advanced' when every role follows the default provider's presets.
- **Test plan:** Rust: secrets_set for provider:anthropic:api_key with no roles ⇒ roles assigned from presets, bootstrap set. Vitest: key-prefix inference unit tests.
- **Depends on:** onboarding-completes-without-usable-ai
- **Verification (confirmed):** secrets_set only calls refresh_provider_keys (commands/settings.rs:38-43). Presets are applied only from the onboarding Gemini path (overwrite:false), the default-provider switch, the 'Use recommended models' button and account catalog fetch (accounts/mod.rs:642). The transcription default is GeminiLive (types/settings.rs:189-191), with a runtime fallback to Apple Speech. Valid opportunity. Item 1 (auto-fill unassigned roles on first key save) also fixes case (3) of onboarding-completes-without-usable-ai.
- _Source: auditor (journey-onboarding-settings/infer-routing-from-keys)_

### Capture, audio & transcription

#### MAC-002

**Apple on-device route never commits utterances that the recognizer resets after a pause, so most speech never becomes a final segment**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: New pure Swift UtteranceTracker (Speech/UtteranceTracker.swift), used by SpeechTranscriber. When on-device recognition resets after a pause without isFinal, the previous partial is committed as the utterance's final. A reset needs a signal (speech metadata on the previous result, or a sharp drop in word count) plus a new opening word, so a recognizer that keeps its words never duplicates text. I used this pure-tracker seam instead of a RecognizerFactory seam. Device check: Real speech with pauses on on-device SFSpeech (macOS 14/15), to confirm the reset heuristic commits once per utterance and never duplicates.

- **User impact:** On the Apple route, only about the last utterance of every 55 s request reaches transcript.final. Everything earlier appears briefly as an italic partial and is then overwritten. The ring used for ⌘↵ context, stored transcripts and question detection (finals only) miss most of the conversation.
- **Root cause:** Finalization relies on isFinal, which the OS no longer sends at utterance boundaries for on-device recognition.
- **Evidence:** SpeechTranscriber.swift:184-195 emits transcript.final only when result.isFinal; isFinal arrives only after endAudio (55 s rotation, L129-133); SpeechTranscriber.swift has no use of speechRecognitionMetadata and no detection of the text resetting; External: on-device SFSpeechRecognizer on macOS 26.6.2 restarts bestTranscription after about 1.5 s pauses with isFinal=false; 5 resets in 127 s, last partial 53 of about 1,500 chars (github.com/sonpiaz/haynoi/pull/40); Apple forums threads 731761, 762952, 770278 report the same on iOS 17/18; Partial segment timestamps are 0 until final (Apple forums 679230/62116), so successive utterances share startMs=epochMs and the same assembler key (helper.rs:517); This Mac is on macOS 26; Apple is the fallback for every user without a Google key (audio/mod.rs:75-95; settings.rs:189)
- **Proposed solution:** In handleLocked, track the last partial text and the metadata flag per request. When a result carries speechRecognitionMetadata, or a new partial is not a continuation of the previous one, emit transcript.final for the closed utterance with a stable per-utterance key (request epoch plus an utterance counter), then continue. Longer term, adopt SpeechAnalyzer/SpeechTranscriber behind #available(macOS 26, *).
- **Test plan:** Add a seam so SpeechTranscriber takes an injected result stream. Swift test: partial 'a b', then a result with metadata, then partial 'c' must emit final 'a b' followed by partial 'c'. Real Mac: speak 2 minutes with 2-3 s pauses on the Apple route and check that the finals count matches the utterances.
- **Depends on:** speech-rotation-cascade
- **Verification (confirmed):** Code confirmed: SpeechTranscriber.swift:163-195 finalizes only on result.isFinal and never reads speechRecognitionMetadata. External evidence is real but mixed. haynoi PR #40 (gh, 2026-09-19) measured on-device resets with metadata and isFinal=false on macOS 26.6.2 (vi-VN). In Apple forum 762952 the problem appears on iOS 17 on-device and on all of iOS 18, but the OP reports it fixed on iOS 26 RC. This Mac runs 26.5.1, so an en-US check is still needed. Important interaction with speech-rotation-cascade: after the first 55 s rotation, the cascade makes requests sub-second and they finalize constantly, which hides this bug. Loss is therefore concentrated in the first 55 s, with chopped audio afterwards. Fixing the cascade alone would make this worse, because requests would run the full 55 s and lose every reset utterance. The two must ship together. The metadata-based commit fix is cheap and harmless if the OS is already fixed.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: unknown). I could not refute this. I checked every layer between the recognizer and storage and none of them saves an utterance after the recognizer resets.

Helper (Swift), src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Speech/SpeechTranscriber.swift:
- L141-143: each request sets shouldReportPartialResults=true and requiresOnDeviceRecognition = onDevice && recognizer.supportsOnDeviceRecognition.
- L163-195: handleLocked sends "transcript.final" only when result.isFinal. In every other case it sends "transcript.partial" with the full current bestTranscription.formattedString, and startMs = epochMs + first.timestamp. The file never reads speechRecognitionMetadata, keeps no previous-partial state and has no reset detection.
- L129-133: the only regular finalization is the 55 s rotation, which calls endAudio.
- L95-107: finish() ends audio and cancels the task after 1.5 s.

Rust app:
- src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:129 sends "onDevice": true unconditionally.
- route_for (audio/mod.rs:75-95) and the default config (bluey-core types/transcript.rs:151, provider GeminiLive) send every user without a Google key to the Apple route.
- on_transcript (audio/mod.rs:881-906) only inserts partials into self.partials and publishes TranscriptPartial. Only finals reach commit_final (ring plus DB, L909+). self.partials is never read, and the earlier notes show it is cleared on stop.
- The assembler (src-tauri/crates/bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs:507-520) keys by start_ms and does not merge anything.

Frontend: src/stores/transcriptStore.ts:29 replaces a single partial slot (applyPartial: set({partial})). applyFinal (L30-32) only clears a matching id. Nothing turns an overwritten partial into a final.

Swift tests: Tests/ contains only AXRoleFilter, DHash, Envelope, OCRSorter, PCMChunker and VAD tests. Nothing covers SpeechTranscriber.

External evidence that the OS behavior is current, including English:
- haynoi PR #40 measured it on macOS 26.6.2 (vi-VN): a reset about 1.5 s after each pause, metadata on the result that closes the utterance, isFinal=false, and 3/5/8 s pauses do not end the task.
- simantstha/vital PR #220 (2026-09-24, on an iOS device, in English: "it was good" … "but my calves were tight") shows the text wiped at the pause with no isFinal.
- Apple forum threads 731761 (macOS 14/iOS 17, on-device only), 762952 and 770278 (iOS 18) report the same.
- The "fixed in iOS 26 RC" claim in 762952 is anecdotal and does not state settings. Bluey always requests on-device.

How a user hits it: no Google key, Apple fallback, speaking or meeting audio with pauses of 1.5 s or more. Every utterance before the last reset in a request shows up as an italic partial and is then overwritten. It never reaches the ⌘↵ ring, the stored transcript or question detection, since proactive uses finals only.

Scope limit (it lowers current impact, not severity): because of speech-rotation-cascade (stale task finals rotate the new request, L192-194, with no generation check), requests after the first 55 s rotation last well under a second. So today the loss mostly affects the first request of each source per session, which is up to 55 s of mic and 55 s of system audio. After that, the damage comes from chopped audio. Fixing the cascade alone would spread this loss over every 55 s window. High is still correct, and the two fixes have to ship together.

Not verified: en-US behavior on this Mac (26.5.1, Intel). supportsOnDeviceRecognition could be false here, in which case the server path would be used; per forum 731761 that path was also broken on iOS 18. No runtime logs exist for the Apple route on this Mac, because the user runs GeminiLive. Fix critique: The direction is right, but the rule as written would create a new bug.

1. Do not commit on speechRecognitionMetadata alone. As written ("metadata OR non-continuation"), it duplicates text on any OS/locale where the recognizer marks the pause but keeps the words, which haynoi notes explicitly. The ring and DB would store final "a b", then later "a b c", and at rotation the endAudio final "a b c" again. So the first-pass note's "harmless if the OS is already fixed" is wrong for that version. Only commit when a reset is actually detected:
   - Required: the previous result carried metadata, or the word count dropped sharply.
   - And: the new partial is not a continuation. Use a lenient check, such as the first word changed, plus the word-count drop vital uses, rather than a strict hasPrefix. On-device partials rewrite casing and punctuation, so a strict prefix check would report false resets.

2. Minimal implementation: keep `lastPartial: TranscriptEvent?` as per-request state, reset in startRequestLocked. When a reset is detected, send the cached lastPartial as "transcript.final" with its own startMs/endMs; do not recompute them from the new result. The existing start_ms key is then enough. If partial timestamps are 0, the final for key K removes the assembler's pending id before the next partial with the same K creates a new one, and the frontend's applyFinal clears the slot because the ids match. The proposed "request epoch + utterance counter" key is optional. If it is added, it only works if Rust's assembler uses the same key, which would mean ingest_keyed plus a wire field.

3. The state must be tied to task identity. With the cascade present, stale-task callbacks arrive for a request that has already rotated. Ship this together with the generation guard from speech-rotation-cascade, and throw away lastPartial when a stale task's final arrives.

4. Test seam: a synthetic result stream means creating SFSpeechRecognitionResult/SFTranscriptionSegment objects, which is awkward in tests. Smaller option: pull out a pure `UtteranceAccumulator` struct (like haynoi's InterimTranscript or vital's isRecognizerReset) that takes (text, hasMetadata, isFinal, startMs, endMs) and returns the events to send. Unit-test it in Tests/BlueyHelperCoreTests. Cases:
   - Reset: partial "a b", then a metadata result, then partial "c" → final "a b", then partial "c".
   - Continuation: "a b", then metadata, then "a b c" → no early final and no duplicate.
   - A normal revision where the first word changes but the word count holds → no final.
   - An endAudio final after a reset → only the last utterance.
   Also add a Rust boundary test showing that consecutive finals with the same start_ms become separate segments.

5. Real-Mac check: routine rotations and resets are not logged, so add a debug log line on reset detection. Otherwise the proposed "finals count matches utterances" check cannot be read from logs.

6. The long-term SpeechAnalyzer path (#available(macOS 26, *)) needs the Xcode 26 / macOS 26 SDK in CI and in release.sh. Package.swift is tools 5.9 with a macOS 14 minimum, so SFSpeech has to stay as the fallback for 14-15.

This doesn't affect security. It also does not fix the separate loss at session stop: finish() gives only a 1.5 s window, and Rust drops partials on stop (audio/mod.rs partials map). Flushing lastPartial as a final in finish() would close that gap cheaply.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/apple-speech-drops-utterances-after-pause)_

#### MAC-003

**Terminal callbacks from retired SFSpeech tasks rotate the live request, causing endless sub-second request churn after the first rotation**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: Every recognition request is now a generation. Callbacks from a retired request may only deliver its final, under its partials' utterance id, and can never rotate or restart the live request. This ends the sub-second request churn after the first 55 s rotation. Routine kLSRErrorDomain cancellations restart quietly. Five consecutive non-routine errors report speech_unavailable instead of restarting in a hot loop (SpeechTranscriber.swift). Device check: Listen for more than 3 minutes on Apple Speech, and confirm rotations happen about every 55 s with no churn in the helper log.

- **User impact:** After the first 55 s rotation (or the first final), each retired request's final or error ends the brand-new request, which ends the next one, and so on. Transcripts come out as fragments split mid-word, with poorer accuracy and higher CPU, for the rest of the session.
- **Root cause:** There is no generation check: callbacks from a retired task are treated as events of the active request.
- **Evidence:** SpeechTranscriber.swift:150-155 the recognitionTask closure captures only epochMs, not task identity; L192-194 `if result.isFinal, !stopped { rotateRequestLocked() }` rotates the CURRENT request; L198-212 routine errors (1110 no-speech, 216/301 cancelled) from any task also call rotateRequestLocked; rotateRequestLocked L158-162 calls endAudio on the current request, which later delivers its own final or error and so rotates the next one
- **Proposed solution:** Give every request a generation number captured in the closure. In handleLocked, emit text for any generation, but rotate or restart only when generation == currentGeneration. Ignore errors from retired generations.
- **Test plan:** Swift unit test with a fake recognizer: rotate at 55 s, deliver the final of generation 1 and check that generation 2 is not rotated. Real Mac: 3 minutes of continuous speech on the Apple route; the log should show about one rotation per 55 s.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Traced SpeechTranscriber.swift:136-212. The recognitionTask closure captures only epochMs, and self.task/self.request are overwritten on rotation. A retired task's terminal callback (isFinal at L192, or any routine or non-routine error at L198-211) calls rotateRequestLocked(). That ends the current request's audio, whose own terminal callback rotates the next request, and so on. This loop starts at the first 55 s rotation (append L127-132). Only the stopped flag breaks it. A rotation triggered by the current task's own error does not cascade. Runtime confirmation: count 'speech' rotations per minute in the helper log after 60 s of listening. The generation-number fix is correct.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). I could not refute this. I read all 224 lines of src-tauri/swift/BlueyHelper/Sources/BlueyHelperCore/Speech/SpeechTranscriber.swift and found no guard.

How the cascade happens:
- L150-155: each `recognitionTask` closure captures only `epochMs`. The request and task identity are not captured. `self.request` and `self.task` are overwritten on every rotation (L145, L150).
- L192-194: `if result.isFinal, !stopped { rotateRequestLocked() }` works on whichever request is current.
- L198-212: every error from any task rotates the current request. That covers the routine list at L202-206 and the non-routine `else` at L209-211. The only exception is `recognizer?.isAvailable == false`.
- L158-162: `rotateRequestLocked` calls `endAudio()` on the live request and starts a new one.
- The handler is sent back to the serial queue with `queue.async` (L152). So a retired task's terminal callback always runs after the rotation has finished, when `self.request` is already the new request.
- The SDK header says every task ends with a final result or an error (SFSpeechRecognizer.h:183-195: "called multiple times to deliver the partial and final results ... or when an error occurs"). Every retired request therefore ends the next one.
- An empty or silent request ends with 1110 "Failed to recognize any speech" (SFSpeechRecognitionTask.h error table). That also rotates, so silence does not stop the loop.
- If the framework cancels a released task, the error is kLSRErrorDomain 301 (SFSpeechRecognitionTask.h:96). That domain is not matched by the routine check, which only looks at kAFAssistantErrorDomain. It falls into the non-routine branch, which also rotates (and logs a warning).
- The only way out is `stopped`, which only `finish()` sets (L98).

What starts it:
- AudioSession.swift:327 passes every 200 ms chunk to `append`, with no gating by the voice-activity detector (`deliver` runs on every chunk). The 55 s check at L130-132 therefore fires at 55 s of wall-clock capture in every Apple-route session, and the loop starts there.
- A task's own final or error ends only that task, so on its own it does not cascade (the first-pass note is right about this).
- The loop runs separately for the microphone and system-audio transcribers.

Who can hit it:
- src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:74-95 `route_for` picks the Apple route when the user selects Apple. It also picks Apple when the default GeminiLive has no Google AI Studio key: "no Google AI Studio key is stored for live transcription". So any user without that key is on this path.
- audio/mod.rs:128 sets `transcription.enabled` only for the Apple route.

Downstream there is no merging or protection:
- The assembler keys segments by `start_ms` (src-tauri/crates/bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs:507-530).
- Each final is committed as its own segment to the ring (RING_CAPACITY 500, audio/mod.rs:53) and to the database (audio/mod.rs:903-930). Fragments of about 0.2-1 s reach the UI and the AI context directly, and the 500-entry ring covers far less time.
- The only dedupe near a rotation is for Gemini Live (docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:49-51), not Apple.
- There is no Swift test for SpeechTranscriber. BlueyHelper/Tests only covers AXRoleFilter, DHash, Envelope, OCRSorter, PCMChunker and the voice-activity detector.
- The file is unchanged since the first commit, cd997e2.
- docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:64 claims the request is "rotated every ~55 s". That claim is documented but contradicted by the code.

Limits of what I checked:
- I did not run it. Running it would need a Speech Recognition permission prompt, which the rules forbid, and this user's local logs only use GeminiLive.
- The claim still rests on the documented terminal-callback behaviour, not on inference.

Severity: High is correct. After the first minute, the transcription loop is badly degraded for everyone on the Apple route, including every user without a Gemini key. It is not a Blocker because there is no data loss or security exposure. If most users run without a key, it could be rated Critical. Fix critique: The generation-number fix is correct, minimal and self-contained. Five refinements:

1. Smaller alternative. Capture the request itself and compare it by identity instead of adding a counter, e.g. `let req = request` in the closure, then `let isCurrent = (req === self.request)` in `handleLocked`. Allow rotation or restart only when `isCurrent`. After `finish()`, `request` is nil and `stopped` is set, so both variants handle stop the same way.

2. Don't emit partials from retired generations. Rust keeps a single partial slot per source (audio/mod.rs:906 `partials.insert(segment.source, ...)`), and the frontend `applyPartial` also has one slot. Tail partials from the retired task would flicker against the new task's partials. Emit only the retired task's final, which keeps its correct `epochMs` offset. Drop its partials and ignore its errors.

3. The real-Mac check in the test plan won't work as written. Rotations on the routine path are silent: L205-206 rotate without logging. Add a debug log line with the generation and the reason (cap, final or error) so that "about one rotation per 55 s" can actually be counted.

4. An adjacent gap the fix does not cover. A non-routine error from the current task rotates immediately, with no backoff (L209-211). Examples are kLSRErrorDomain 201 "Siri or Dictation is disabled", 102 "assets not installed" and 1700 "not authorized" (SFSpeechRecognitionTask.h:93-101). If each new task fails straight away while `isAvailable` stays true, that is a hot restart loop that logs a warning every time. The fix should add a consecutive-failure counter: back off, or call `reportUnavailable` after N quick failures. The routine domain check should also accept kLSRErrorDomain 301.

5. Testability. SFSpeechRecognizer can't be faked easily. To make the Swift unit test possible, move the "should this callback rotate?" decision into a pure function taking (generation, current generation, isFinal, error, stopped) and test it the same way as PCMChunkerTests.

None of this weakens security or affects the Gemini or cloud paths. The change stays in SpeechTranscriber.swift, apart from optionally correcting the claim at docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:64.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/speech-rotation-cascade)_

#### LIVE-004

**A few seconds of network loss permanently ends Gemini Live and Voice Live transcription for that source while listening still shows as active**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: New src-tauri/src/transcription/reconnect.rs holds one reconnect policy shared by Gemini Live and Voice Live. A lost connection is re-opened with capped exponential backoff (0.5 s doubling to 8 s) until Close arrives or the service rejects the configuration. Sends time out after 5 s. A read watchdog reconnects when speech went out but nothing came back for 15 s. The outage is reported once via TranscriptionEvent::Degraded/Recovered. Wired into gemini_live.rs and cloud_realtime.rs. Device check: Drop the network for more than 10 s during a Gemini Live / Voice Live run, and confirm transcripts resume and exactly one degraded notice is shown.

- **User impact:** Switching Wi-Fi, a VPN reconnect or sleep/wake mid-meeting shows one error toast. After that, the mic and/or system transcript stays silent until the user stops and restarts listening, and the HUD still says 'Live transcript'.
- **Root cause:** Reconnect logic treats a second failed connect as fatal, and the manager has no retry once a source has failed.
- **Evidence:** gemini_live.rs:505-540: after Reconnect it backs off, rotate() -> connect() (3 attempts with 400/800 ms sleeps; DNS failure is fast) returns Err, failures=1, then ONE more connect(), and `Err(error) => return self.fail(error).await`. MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=3 can never be reached; cloud_realtime.rs:250-263: same pattern (`Err(error) => return self.fail(error)`), with no connect timeout on connect_async (L145); `audio/mod.rs:512-519 on_stt_event Failed: stt.failed.insert(source); status stays Running`; audio/mod.rs:448 forward_pcm returns early for failed sources, so there is no re-open for the rest of the session; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:507-514 — TranscriptionEvent::Failed → `stt.failed.insert(source); stt.sessions.remove(&source)` and publishes AudioError; audio/mod.rs:448-449 — forward_pcm: `if stt.failed.contains(&source) … { return; }`, so every later chunk is dropped; `audio/mod.rs:483-486 — a failed open() also marks the source failed`; Internal reconnects are limited: transcription/gemini_live.rs:53-57 (MAX_RECONNECTS_WITHOUT_PROGRESS=5, backoff capped at 8 s), cloud_realtime.rs:250-260; `A fallback exists only when listening starts: audio/mod.rs:75-96, 334-339`
- **Proposed solution:** In the worker, loop reconnects with capped exponential backoff (up to 8 s) until Close or a Configuration error, emitting a 'degraded' AudioError once. Alternatively, in AudioManager, clear `failed` for non-configuration errors after a cooldown so the next chunk re-opens the session. Surface the degraded state in AudioStatus.
- **Test plan:** Rust test with a local tokio-tungstenite fake server: complete setup, drop the connection, refuse connects for 5 s, then accept. Expect finals to resume and no Failed event. Same for cloud_realtime. Real Mac: turn Wi-Fi off for 10 s mid-listen.
- **Verification (confirmed):** gemini_live.rs:526-540: after a failed rotate(), a second connect() Err causes return self.fail(). Any Err on that path is terminal, so MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=3 (L52) is effectively unreachable. With fast DNS failure, the outage window is about 0.5 s backoff (L56) + 2 x (3 attempts with 400/800 ms sleeps), roughly 3-4 s. cloud_realtime.rs:252-262 follows the same pattern, and connect_async there has no timeout. audio/mod.rs:512-520 then marks the source failed forever, and forward_pcm returns early (L449). Correction: this is not silent. The AudioError is published and errorSurface.ts:23 shows a toast, but status stays Running and nothing recovers until Stop/Start. AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:109-111 documents the lack of re-route but not the permanent loss.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). The finding holds, verified at SHA 1a117a5. I found nothing (no guard, retry, test or cooldown) that makes it false. One refinement follows at the end.

Gemini Live worker (src-tauri/src/transcription/gemini_live.rs):
- A Reconnect action comes from a send error (L468-470), a server close (L474-477) or a socket error (L482-485).
- It increments `reconnects`, sleeps `reconnect_backoff` (500 ms on the first reconnect, L56/L228-233), then calls `self.rotate(conn)` (L526). `rotate` calls `self.connect()` first (L404).
- `connect()` tries 3 times with 400 ms and 800 ms sleeps between tries (L247, L322-324), each try capped by CONNECT_TIMEOUT of 10 s (L49/L248).
- On Err: `failures += 1` (L532), which is below MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES=3. It then makes ONE more `connect()` (L537), and `Err(error) => return self.fail(error).await` (L539). So a single outage gets exactly 2 `connect()` calls, 6 socket tries in total.
- When connects fail fast (no route, or DNS fails at once with the interface down), the outage it tolerates is about 0.5 + 1.2 + 1.2 ≈ 3 s. When packets are silently dropped, it is up to about 60 s.
- `failures` is not reset when the fallback connect succeeds (L538). So MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURES can only be reached across separate episodes, and each later episode fails sooner.

Voice Live worker (src-tauri/src/transcription/cloud_realtime.rs):
- It is even less tolerant. One 400 ms sleep (L260), then a single `connect()` with 3 tries (L137-171), and `Err(error) => return self.fail(error)` (L263).
- `connect_async` has no timeout (L145).
- Any in-band `error` event is fatal at once, with no reconnect attempt (L234-236; bluey-protocols/src/realtime.rs:116 maps every `"error"` type to Error).

Audio manager (src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs):
- On Failed, `on_stt_event` (L512-520) inserts the source into `failed`, removes the session, sets `status.error` and publishes AudioError.
- `forward_pcm` returns early for any failed source (L449).
- `failed` is only reset when a session starts (L281-287). Pause/resume (L620-646) does not touch it.
- I found no sleep/wake or network-path observer anywhere in src-tauri/src or the Swift helper, so nothing re-opens the session.

UI:
- errorSurface.ts:23 shows one toast.
- TranscriptStrip.tsx:26-43 keeps showing a pulsing green dot and "Live transcript" whenever `audioActive` is true. It ignores the AudioStatus error, so the "listening still shows as active" claim is accurate.

Exposure:
- GeminiLive is the `#[default]` TranscriptionProviderKind (bluey-core/src/types/transcript.rs:76-77). Any user with a Gemini key is on this path.
- There are no fake-WebSocket reconnect tests for transcription. `accept_async`/`TcpListener` appear only in AI-provider, oauth and fingerprints code.
- docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:56-57 says the session will "reconnect with exponential backoff and give up after five without transcript progress". The code contradicts this: it gives up after the second failed `connect()`.

Refinement (the trigger conditions are narrower than the user_impact text suggests):
- There is no app-level ping or read/write timeout. So a short blip that keeps the same IP may never produce a socket error at all: TCP just retransmits and the session survives.
- A network switch or VPN reconnect that silently drops packets is more likely to HANG the worker than to fail it. The TCP send buffer fills within seconds at about 43 KB/s of base64 audio. After that, `forward()` blocks (L468), the mpsc fills up, and `push_audio` returns backpressure errors that are only debug-logged (audio/mod.rs:453-455). The result is silent transcript loss with no toast at all until the OS gives up on TCP. Voice Live's untimed `connect_async` adds more hangs.
- The permanent-failure path is reached when a socket error or close coincides with an outage that fails fast. Examples: Wi-Fi turned off (the kernel may return EADDRNOTAVAIL/ENETDOWN on the continuous audio writes), or wake from sleep where the server has already closed the socket (RST on first write) before Wi-Fi has rejoined. How often these happen needs a real Mac to confirm.

Severity stays High. The provider is the default, recovery needs a manual Stop/Start, and the HUD keeps looking live. Fix critique: The direction is right, but both variants need these corrections:

1. **Worker retry loop.**
   - When `connect()` returns a non-Configuration error, replace L531-540 with a loop of `reconnect_backoff` sleeps (capped at 8 s). The loop must keep polling `rx`: honour `Close` and throw away `Audio` commands. Otherwise `close()` waits DRAIN_GRACE+2 s and then aborts the task (L128-140).
   - Connect failures must not count toward MAX_RECONNECTS_WITHOUT_PROGRESS. Otherwise the loop still gives up after 5 tries, about 20 s at the cap.
   - Keep `is_fatal` as the only thing that ends a session, so a bad key or model still ends it.
   - Send one "degraded" AudioError at the start of an outage and clear `status.error` on recovery. A toast per retry would be spam.

2. **Manager cooldown as the smaller alternative.**
   - This is the most economical option: one place covers both providers, and it also covers Voice Live's fatal in-band `error` (cloud_realtime.rs:234-236).
   - In `on_stt_event` Failed, record `failed_at` for non-Configuration errors. In `forward_pcm`, allow a re-open after roughly 5-10 s.
   - Catch: `GeminiLiveProvider::open` always returns Ok and failure arrives later as an event. Repeat AudioErrors therefore need rate-limiting, or users get one toast per cooldown.

3. **Neither proposal fixes the hang case.** VPN reconnects and network switches are more likely to stall the socket than to error it.
   - Wrap `conn.send` in `forward()` (gemini_live.rs:330) and the audio send in cloud_realtime.rs:201 in a short timeout that maps to Reconnect.
   - Add the existing 10 s CONNECT_TIMEOUT around cloud_realtime `connect_async` (L145).
   - Optionally add a watchdog: no server frame for N seconds while speech is being sent triggers Reconnect.
   - Without these, the loop-with-backoff fix never runs in the hang scenarios.

4. **UI.** AudioStatus.error is already set (audio/mod.rs:518). TranscriptStrip.tsx should show a degraded or reconnecting state instead of the green pulsing "Live transcript". No new status field is needed.

5. **Docs.** Fix docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:56-57 to describe the real policy.

6. **Tests.**
   - The proposed fake tokio-tungstenite server test is the right kind of boundary test. Use `tokio::time::pause` to keep it deterministic, and add a case where Close arrives during backoff (expect a quick exit, no abort).
   - Add a case where the server accepts TCP but stops reading (expect Reconnect within the send timeout).
   - Add a manager-level test that a Failed source with a non-configuration error re-opens after the cooldown.
   - Set needs_real_macos to true to confirm the triggers: Wi-Fi off for 10 s mid-listen, and a sleep/wake cycle with listening on.

Security: none of these changes weaken security. The fatal handling for Configuration errors (bad key, 401/403) must stay terminal, or a bad key gets hammered in a loop.
- **Also reported as:** providers-routing-accounts/stt-midsession-failure-no-fallback
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/gemini-live-blip-permanently-fails-source)_

#### MAC-004

**After a helper crash and restart, AudioManager stays 'Running' and observation stays 'on' although the new helper is doing nothing**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: The sidecar supervisor (src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs) publishes typed HelperEvent::Exited{restarting} and HelperEvent::Restarted on the event channel, defined in src-tauri/crates/bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs. AudioManager (helper_exit_plan, on_helper_exited/restarted) stops a live run when the helper dies, so status never says 'running' for a dead helper. It re-issues the run once with its stored config when the replacement helper comes up. If the helper is gone for good, the auto-started session ends. Device check: Kill BlueyHelper while listening and confirm the run resumes after the supervisor restart, and that status shows Stopped if it does not come back.

- **User impact:** A helper crash during a meeting (for example from a CoreAudio or SCK fault) silently ends listening. The HUD keeps its live indicator, levels freeze, and pressing 'listen' does nothing until the user stops and starts again. Smart observation stays off for the rest of the run.
- **Root cause:** Nothing reconciles app state with the helper's state after the helper restarts.
- **Evidence:** sidecar/mod.rs:236-287 on_terminated restarts the helper and publishes only BlueyEvent::HelperStatus; HelperStatus consumers: src/stores/errorSurface.ts:24-27 (toast 'Helper restarted') and app/mod.rs:377; AudioManager subscribes only to helper events (audio/mod.rs:779) and ignores HelperEvent::Ready (L875-877); audio/mod.rs:240-244 start() returns early while is_running(), so the user cannot restart without Stop first; `capture/mod.rs:354-366 observing flag is never reset; nobody re-calls observe.start`; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:234-282 on_terminated fails pending requests and respawns the process only; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:85,364-365 the `observing` flag is set on observe_start and never reset or re-issued after a restart; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:207-211 is_running derives from its own status; rg finds no HelperStatus/restart subscriber in audio/ or capture/; `src/stores/errorSurface.ts:27 only shows a 'Helper restarted' toast`; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:236-287 on_terminated: fail_pending + publish_status(false,..,restarted=true) + respawn; no event to subscribers that the process died; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:773-826 the listener reacts only to helper events; a dead helper sends no AudioStopped, so mark_stopped never runs; audio/mod.rs:207-211 is_running = Running|Paused|Starting; :244-246 `if self.is_running() { return Ok(self.status()); }` so pressing Listen again does nothing; src-tauri/src/capture/mod.rs:364-366 observing=true set once; observe_start callers are only settings/side_effects.rs:57, app/mod.rs:396 and the command, so nothing re-arms after a restart; src/stores/errorSurface.ts:24-27 shows 'Helper restarted' for 2 s, which reads as fully recovered
- **Proposed solution:** Subscribe AudioManager and CaptureManager to HelperStatus{restarted} (or to HelperEvent::Ready with generation > 1). Audio: close_stt, then either re-issue audio.start with the stored config (auto-resume) or mark_stopped with a 'sidecar.helper_restarted' error. Capture: re-issue observe.start when observing was true.
- **Test plan:** Rust integration test with a fake helper process that exits during a running audio session. Expect AudioStopped or resumed status and an observe.start re-sent. Real Mac: `kill -9` bluey-helper while listening.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Code claim holds. on_terminated (sidecar/mod.rs:236-287) only publishes HelperStatus. No Rust subscriber reacts to it (the only other HelperStatus reference is the publish at app/mod.rs:377). AudioManager ignores HelperEvent::Ready (audio/mod.rs:875-877), and the observing flag is never reset (capture/mod.rs:354-366). Downgraded because it needs a helper crash or a manual dev restart, which are uncommon events. The user does see the 'native helper is not running' toast and then 'Helper restarted' (errorSurface.ts:24-27), although the second toast wrongly implies recovery. Recovery takes two toggles: is_running() is true, so the first toggle stops and the second starts. Observation desync has little impact because screen.changed has no consumer (see smart-observation-dead-end). The fix is fine; prefer auto-resume audio with the stored config.
- **Also reported as:** native-macos-platform/helper-restart-state-loss, error-handling-stubs/helper-crash-zombie-sessions
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/helper-restart-no-state-resync)_

#### CTX-013

**'Display with focus' (the default) captures the menu-bar display, not the display the user is working on**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: New FocusDisplay.swift: a capture without a displayId now resolves the display that contains the midpoint of the frontmost app's front window (FrontmostAppService / CGWindowList), then the display under the mouse (CGEvent location, Quartz coordinates), then the main display, then the first display. ShareableContent.display(withId:nil) uses it. Documented in CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md. A compile error found by the Swift gate (wrong type name) was fixed inside the commit. Device check: With two displays, focus a window on the secondary display, press Cmd+Return, and check the frame's displayId.

- **User impact:** On multi-monitor setups, ⌘↵ with the question on a secondary monitor sends the wrong screen to the model, so the answer ignores what the user is looking at.
- **Root cause:** 'Active' was never resolved to a focus-based display.
- **Evidence:** `ScreenTab.tsx:100 option label 'Display with focus' for value 'active'`; snapshot.ts:53 'active' => {type:'display'} with no displayId; capture/mod.rs:140-141 'active' => None; ShareableContent.swift display(withId:nil) returns CGMainDisplayID() (the display with the menu bar/origin)
- **Proposed solution:** In the helper, when displayId is nil, pick the display containing the frontmost app's focused window (AX/CGWindowList bounds, as FrontmostApp already computes), falling back to the display under NSEvent.mouseLocation and then CGMainDisplayID.
- **Test plan:** Swift unit test for a pure 'display containing rect' resolver. Real Mac with two displays: focus a window on the secondary display, press ⌘↵, and check the frame's displayId.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ScreenTab.tsx:100 labels 'active' as 'Display with focus', and it is the default (bluey-core settings.rs:215 preferred_display 'active'). snapshot.ts:53 omits displayId, capture/mod.rs:140-141 maps it to None, and ShareableContent.swift:53 resolves nil to CGMainDisplayID. Multi-monitor users working on a secondary display get the wrong screen. The fix is correct. FrontmostApp already computes window bounds that can be reused.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/display-with-focus-captures-main-display)_

#### MAC-005

**A failed mic engine restart (device switch or unplug) leaves the mic dead for the session while status says mic active**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: MicrophoneCapture.swift keeps a wantRunning flag separate from live. A rebuild that fails after a device change is retried on the next device change, and a vanished explicit device falls back to the default input. Losing or recovering the mic mid-run re-sends audio.started, so Rust's microphone_active stays in step (AudioSession.swift). audio.error forwards the HelperError unchanged, keeping permission kinds and details for the repair flow. Device check: Unplug and replug a USB/Bluetooth mic mid-run, and confirm capture resumes and the mic status line follows.

- **User impact:** Connecting or disconnecting AirPods or a USB mic at the wrong moment stops 'You' transcription for the rest of the meeting, with only a transient toast.
- **Root cause:** The restart path has no retry and no state propagation to the session or Rust.
- **Evidence:** MicrophoneCapture.swift restartLocked: on buildAndStartEngine error it sets running=false and calls onError; AudioSession.swift:186-188 mic.onError only calls emitError; the session state stays .running and no audio.started/stopped is sent; Later handleDeviceChange -> mic.restart() -> restartLocked `guard running else { return }`, so it never retries when the device returns; handleDeviceChange (AudioSession.swift:365-383) re-routes only when usingDefaultDevice; with an explicit deviceId there is no reaction; `audio/mod.rs:863-867 AudioError sets status.error but microphone_active stays true`
- **Proposed solution:** On restart failure, keep a 'wantRunning' flag separate from 'running' so the next device change or config-change notification retries. Emit an audio.started-style status update (microphone:false/true) so Rust updates microphone_active. For explicit device ids, fall back to the default device and emit deviceChanged.
- **Test plan:** Swift test with an injectable engine factory that fails once and then succeeds on the next device-change callback. Real Mac: select an explicit USB mic, unplug and replug it mid-session.
- **Verification (confirmed):** MicrophoneCapture.swift:72-78 sets running=false and calls onError when the rebuild fails. The next restart hits `guard running` and returns. AudioSession.swift:186-188 only emits an error on mic failure. handleDeviceChange (L360-383) re-routes only when usingDefaultDevice. Rust's AudioError handler (audio/mod.rs:862-866) sets status.error but leaves microphone_active true. The fix is fine.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/mic-restart-failure-never-recovers)_

#### MAC-006

**Async permission errors lose their kind, and revocation mid-session has no repair flow (contrary to the docs)**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Partially implemented** — D1 (MAC-005) forwards the helper's error kind and details unchanged, so permission errors are no longer flattened. Still open: a repair flow for permissions revoked mid-session (the post-update repair card, MAC-001, covers the update case).

- **User impact:** The user sees 'audio' errors without an 'Open System Settings' action when Screen Recording is missing or revoked. A system-audio stream error also kills mic transcription.
- **Root cause:** The event error path hard-codes kind and drops details. There is no revocation reaction.
- **Evidence:** AudioSession.swift emitError (end of file) always sends kind:"audio" and drops details; SpeechTranscriber.swift:216-223 same; jsonl.rs:33-46 into_bluey maps kind 'permission' to BlueyErrorKind::Permission; that is lost because the helper never sends it on async errors; A partial start with system audio lacking Screen Recording goes through emitError(firstError) (AudioSession.swift:222-225), so the permissionDenied becomes kind 'audio' with no Open-Settings recovery; SystemAudioCapture.swift:136-143 didStopWithError becomes .audio('system_audio_stopped') and stopInternal ends the WHOLE session (AudioSession.swift:197-201), mic included; permissions/mod.rs:158-170 refresh loop's PermissionsChanged is consumed only by permissionsStore (initStores.ts:39); comment 'revocations must stop dependent subsystems quickly' is not implemented; AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:106 claims 'Permission revoked → session stops with BlueyError{kind: permission} and a repair flow'
- **Proposed solution:** Encode HelperError's real kind and details (permission name) in audio.error. When the system stream stops, stop only the system pipeline and emit audio.started{systemAudio:false}. When PermissionsChanged shows mic or screen recording denied during a session, AudioManager stops the affected source with a permission error.
- **Test plan:** Rust test: a helper audio.error with kind permission and details.permission=screenRecording maps to recoverable OpenSystemSettings. Swift test for encoding. Real Mac: revoke Screen Recording while listening.
- **Verification (confirmed):** AudioSession.swift emitError hard-codes kind 'audio' and drops details. SpeechTranscriber reportUnavailable does the same (the speech-not-authorized case gets no Open-Settings recovery). SystemAudioCapture.swift:136-143 turns didStopWithError into a generic error, and onStopped (AudioSession.swift:197-201) stops the whole session, mic included. The only consumer of PermissionsChanged is the frontend permissionsStore; Rust only publishes it (permissions/mod.rs:152, app/dev.rs:90). Additional trigger: an SCStream stopped by the system or the user (menu-bar capture indicator) also kills the mic pipeline. The fix is correct.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/permission-errors-flattened-and-revocation-unhandled)_

#### MAC-007

**Transcription language 'auto' (the default) runs Apple Speech in en-US regardless of the user's locale**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: audio.started carries speech {locale, onDevice} from the transcriber (WireSpeechRoute in bluey-protocols). AudioStatus exposes speechLocale and speechOnDevice (bluey-core types/transcript.rs, src/lib/types/transcript.ts). When recognition runs on Apple's servers because the locale has no on-device model, a one-time audio.speech_server info notice is shown instead of it happening silently. The 'auto' language uses the Mac's locale with an en-US fallback. Device check: Pick a locale without an on-device model, and confirm the notice appears and AudioStatus.speechOnDevice is false.

- **User impact:** Non-English users without a Google key (Apple fallback) get English-model gibberish transcripts and no question detection.
- **Root cause:** 'auto' has no meaning for SFSpeech and the helper default is hard-coded.
- **Evidence:** audio/mod.rs:132-134 omits locale when language == 'auto'; the test at L949-951 asserts 'auto → no locale'; `AudioSession.swift:150 `locale: params.transcription?.locale ?? "en-US"``; `settings.rs:186 transcription_language default 'auto'`
- **Proposed solution:** In the helper, default to Locale.current (the SFSpeechRecognizer() default init) when no locale is given, and fall back to en-US only if that recognizer is unavailable. Report the chosen locale in audio.started.
- **Test plan:** Swift unit test of the locale resolver (nil gives current locale; an unsupported locale gives en-US). Keep the Rust test.
- **Verification (confirmed):** audio/mod.rs:132-134 omits locale for 'auto', and AudioSession.swift:150 defaults to "en-US". This also makes the on-device claim less reliable: a non-English system may lack en-US on-device assets and then silently use server recognition (see missed finding apple-route-silent-server-recognition). The fix of using Locale.current / SFSpeechRecognizer() is correct.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/auto-language-means-en-us-on-apple-route)_

#### UX-010

**Partial/final id correlation relies on startMs, and the store keeps one partial for both sources, leaving stale or flickering partials**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: transcript.partial and transcript.final now carry an optional utteranceId. The TranscriptAssembler in bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs prefers it and falls back to startMs for older helpers. Cloud workers get a per-source utterance id via ChunkTiming::stamp_cloud. Swift ids use the form <generation>-<counter>. src/stores/transcriptStore.ts keeps one partial per source, and a source's final clears only its own partial. The HUD strip (src/features/hud/transcript-strip.ts, TranscriptStrip.tsx) shows both partials.

- **User impact:** A duplicate italic line stays under the committed final until the next partial arrives. In cross-talk, the mic and system partials replace each other. The Rust pending map grows with orphaned keys during a session.
- **Root cause:** Utterance identity is derived from unstable timing instead of a producer-assigned id, and the UI partial state is not per source.
- **Evidence:** `helper.rs:507-517 TranscriptAssembler.ingest keys utterances by event.start_ms`; SpeechTranscriber.swift:170-178 partial startMs = epochMs + first.timestamp; partial timestamps are 0 until final (Apple forums 679230) while the final carries the real offset, so the keys differ; audio/mod.rs on_cloud_text: when utterance_start_ms is None, start = last_start_ms, which changes on every chunk; transcriptStore.ts:28-33 applyFinal clears partial only when ids match; a single `partial` slot is shared by mic and system (applyPartial overwrites); `transcript-strip.ts:51-55 renders the partial after the finals`; TS mock transport never emits transcript.partial (mock-transport.ts:851,890), so this path is untested
- **Proposed solution:** Have the helper emit an explicit utteranceId (request generation plus utterance counter) and have cloud workers pass their own ids, then use ingest_keyed. In transcriptStore, keep `partials: Record<source, segment>` and clear the source's partial on its final.
- **Test plan:** Rust assembler test: a partial with startMs 0 and a final with startMs 1200 for the same utteranceId get the same id. Vitest: applyFinal from mic clears only the mic partial; system and mic partials coexist. Add partial emission to the mock transport.
- **Depends on:** apple-speech-drops-utterances-after-pause
- **Verification (confirmed):** transcriptStore.ts:29-33 has one partial slot and clears it only when the ids match. on_cloud_text (audio/mod.rs:524-548) goes through ingest (start_ms key) rather than ingest_keyed, and falls back to last_start_ms when utterance_start_ms is None (L534). On the default Gemini path, mismatches mostly occur on the first interim after a final. The `pending` map entries for mismatched keys leak until stop. The Apple partial-timestamp=0 premise is plausible but unverified here; the likely confidence is fair. A stale partial stays visible below its final until the next partial arrives, and mic and system partials overwrite each other.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/partial-final-correlation-and-single-partial-slot)_

#### MAC-008

**'You' at 0.95 confidence for all mic audio without echo cancellation doubles remote speech and hides in-person questions**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — Echo cancellation / speaker attribution changes capture quality; needs real-meeting A/B testing on hardware.

- **User impact:** On laptop speakers, remote participants are transcribed twice (as 'You' and as 'Interviewer'), which pollutes the context. In person or in mic-only mode, every interviewer question is labelled 'You', so proactive question detection never fires.
- **Root cause:** Labels are derived purely from channel, with no AEC or cross-source dedupe.
- **Evidence:** MicrophoneCapture.swift uses a raw AVAudioEngine input tap; no setVoiceProcessingEnabled / AEC anywhere in the helper; `helper.rs:464-466 and src/transcript/speaker.ts: Microphone => ('You', 0.95)`; `classifier.ts:205 response-worthy only when speaker !== 'You'`; `AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:19-22 calls the channel split 'reliable' for You vs other party`
- **Proposed solution:** Product decision needed. Options: enable inputNode.setVoiceProcessingEnabled(true) when system audio is also captured; drop mic finals that closely match a system final within about 2 s; in mic-only mode, lower mic confidence or let the classifier consider mic questions.
- **Test plan:** Vitest classifier test for mic-only mode. Rust test for cross-source dedupe. Real Mac: a call on speakers with and without AEC.
- **Verification (confirmed):** No setVoiceProcessingEnabled anywhere in the helper. speaker_label (bluey-protocols helper.rs:464-466) and speaker.ts:30 hard-code mic as 'You' with 0.95. classifier.ts:205 excludes 'You' from response-worthy events. The effects are real: remote speech is duplicated as 'You' when the user is on speakers, and questions from an in-person interviewer are never flagged. This needs a product decision. Note that enabling voice processing changes the input format and ducks other audio, so test it on real hardware.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/speaker-labels-without-echo-cancellation)_

#### LIVE-013

**Gemini Live has no send timeout or read watchdog, so a half-open socket stalls transcription silently**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: New src-tauri/src/audio/stt_health.rs, wired into AudioManager. Each outage publishes a single audio.stt_degraded notice and sets AudioStatus.error, which clears when transcripts flow again. A source whose provider session gave up is re-opened after a 10 s cool-down instead of staying dead for the rest of the run. Configuration errors move listening to Apple Speech. Copy is in present.ts.

- **User impact:** After a Wi-Fi drop without a TCP reset, transcripts stop for minutes with no error or toast, while the HUD shows live listening.
- **Root cause:** Liveness is inferred only from socket errors.
- **Evidence:** gemini_live.rs:329-337 forward() awaits conn.send with no timeout; run() select!
