(L462-500) has no 'no server message for N s while speech was sent' branch; `No ping/keepalive; tungstenite only detects dead TCP after the OS retransmission timeout`; push_audio try_send drops chunks when the 256-command buffer fills (L113-124), logged only at debug level (audio/mod.rs forward_pcm)
- **Proposed solution:** Wrap send in tokio::time::timeout (for example 5 s, then Reconnect). Track last_server_msg and reconnect when speech was forwarded but nothing was received for about 15 s. Count backpressure drops and surface a degraded status.
- **Test plan:** Fake WS server that accepts setup, then stops reading and writing: expect a reconnect within the watchdog window.
- **Depends on:** gemini-live-blip-permanently-fails-source
- **Verification (confirmed):** gemini_live.rs:329-337: forward() awaits conn.send inside the select! arm body (L465-469). A blocked send therefore stalls the whole loop, including the rotate and silence timers. There is no ping or read watchdog. push_audio try_send drops are logged only at debug level (audio/mod.rs:452-454). How long a half-open socket blocks depends on OS TCP behaviour and needs a real-network test (for example, toggle Wi-Fi under a link conditioner). The fix is correct.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/live-socket-no-liveness-watchdog)_

#### TEST-004

**Helper restart, audio session, speech rotation and cloud reconnect have no tests; mocks never produce partials or failures**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort L · independent · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — D1 added pure-logic tests for the ring, reconnect policy, STT health, helper exit/restart plans and Swift UtteranceTracker/SpeechCapability; a fake-helper process harness and SFSpeech seam are follow-ups.

- **User impact:** Every high-severity finding above ships green. Regressions in the core listening loop surface only on a user's Mac.
- **Root cause:** The native pieces lack seams (engine, recognizer, socket) for deterministic tests.
- **Evidence:** Swift tests cover only AXRoleFilter, DHash, Envelope, OCRSorter, PCMChunker, VAD (swift/BlueyHelper/Tests); No #[test] in sidecar/mod.rs, cloud_realtime.rs or mock.rs; gemini_live.rs tests cover only pure mapping (5 tests), with no fake WebSocket; `TS mock-transport emits only transcript.final (L851,L890)`
- **Proposed solution:** Add seams: a RecognizerFactory for SpeechTranscriber, an EngineFactory for MicrophoneCapture, and a URL override for gemini_live/cloud_realtime so a local fake server can be used. Add a fake-helper process harness for HelperClient restart tests. Make the mock transport emit partials and occasional audio.error.
- **Test plan:** The tests listed in the individual findings, run in CI (Swift tests via scripts/test-helper.sh on the macos runner).
- **Verification (confirmed):** The Swift Tests folder contains only AXRoleFilter, DHash, Envelope, OCRSorter, PCMChunker and VAD tests. sidecar/mod.rs, cloud_realtime.rs and mock.rs have 0 #[test]. gemini_live.rs has 5 pure tests. Minor correction: mock-transport can simulate helper.status crash/restart (L2185-2187), but it never emits transcript.partial or audio.error. The seam proposals are appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/perception-state-machines-untested)_

#### MAC-009

**If audio.start exceeds the 5 s helper timeout, Rust marks the session Error while the helper keeps capturing the mic, and the listen toggle can no longer stop it**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: start_recovery in src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs: after an audio.start timeout, a best-effort audio.stop goes to the helper. audio_already_running is treated as a resync: stop, then start once more. A late audio.started for a start that was given up on no longer marks sources active. Device check: Force a slow helper start (e.g. first microphone permission prompt) and confirm the mic indicator turns off after the timeout.

- **User impact:** On a slow start (Bluetooth headset profile switch, first SCStream start, or a TCC prompt during start), the UI says listening failed while the macOS mic indicator stays on and transcripts may keep arriving. Pressing the listen toggle only produces 'audio session already active' errors. Only Settings > Privacy stop, a helper restart or quitting the app releases the mic. This is a privacy and trust problem for a copilot.
- **Root cause:** The Rust and helper session states are not reconciled on request timeout, and the helper has no idempotent 'start while running' behaviour.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:405 `"audio.start" => Duration::from_secs(5)`; a timed-out request just drops the pending response; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:293-304 on helper.call error: close_stt, state=Error, publish AudioError, return. No `audio.stop` is sent to the helper; AudioSession.swift:159 sets `self.state = .running` before startCaptures(); the late group.notify completion then leaves mic/SCStream running and emitting audio.level/audio.chunk/transcript.*; AudioSession.swift:114-117 any later audio.start fails with `audio_already_running` while state != .idle; audio/mod.rs:207-212 is_running() is false for Error, so shortcuts/mod.rs:221-226 and HudToolbar.tsx:67-68 call start (not stop) and hit audio_already_running; audio/mod.rs:880-900 on_transcript does not check is_running, so orphaned Apple-route transcripts are still published
- **Proposed solution:** On an audio.start Err whose code is timeout, send a best-effort `audio.stop` to the helper before returning (or always call stop() on start failure). Also treat `audio_already_running` in start() as a resync: stop, then retry once. Optionally raise the audio.start timeout to around 10 s, or have the helper reply before slow SCStream setup and report sources later through audio.started.
- **Test plan:** Rust: a fake HelperClient that delays the audio.start response past the timeout, then asserts an audio.stop request is sent and status ends Stopped/Error with no Running helper. Swift: an AudioSession unit test in which start while running returns audio_already_running, plus a stop-then-start round trip. Real Mac: start listening with AirPods connected and time audio.start in the helper log.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (capture-audio-transcription/audio-start-timeout-orphans-helper-capture)_

#### SEC-011

**'Apple (on-device)' transcription silently falls back to Apple's servers when on-device recognition is unsupported for the locale**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — D1 (MAC-007): audio.started reports {locale, onDevice}; server recognition is announced once (audio.speech_server) instead of happening silently. With Cloud AI off the route requires on-device recognition (FX2).

- **User impact:** Meeting audio (including the other party's voice from system audio) can leave the device while the UI tells the user transcription is on-device. For a privacy-positioned product this misrepresents where data goes.
- **Root cause:** The helper treats on-device as a preference, not a requirement, and never reports which path it chose.
- **Evidence:** SpeechTranscriber.swift:142 `request.requiresOnDeviceRecognition = onDevice && recognizer.supportsOnDeviceRecognition`: when unsupported, audio goes to Apple servers with no event or status; AudioTab.tsx:154 option label 'Apple (on-device)'; onboarding/steps/permissions.tsx:48 'Bluey uses Apple's on-device speech recognition.'; present.ts:110 'Bluey is transcribing on-device'; PermissionsTab.tsx:16; audio/mod.rs:132-134 + AudioSession.swift:150: 'auto' forces en-US, so non-English systems without en-US on-device assets are more likely to hit the server path; `MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md mentions server fallback, but no UI copy does`
- **Proposed solution:** Report `onDevice: bool` (and the locale) per source in audio.started and AudioStatus. Either require on-device (fail with a clear 'download the on-device model for <locale>' error) or show 'Apple (server)' in the status and copy. Put this behind an explicit setting if server fallback is wanted.
- **Test plan:** Swift: a unit test with a fake recognizer where supportsOnDeviceRecognition=false asserts either failure or an emitted onDevice=false. Rust/TS: an AudioStatus mapping test and a UI copy test. Real Mac: start the Apple route with a locale that lacks on-device assets and check the reported flag.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (capture-audio-transcription/apple-route-silent-server-recognition)_

#### DOC-004

**Audio and permission docs claim behaviours the code does not have**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-FX6: docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md VAD section: the helper feeds every chunk to the recognizer (AudioSession.deliver). isSpeech only stamps utterance starts and feeds the Gemini Live and Voice Live stall watchdogs. Failure handling now describes the start-time permission error with Open System Settings (into_bluey adds the pane), the partial start, and how a system-audio stop ends the whole session. Mid-session revocation is described as undetected because MAC-006 is not fixed. The MACOS_PERMISSIONS restart and revocation claims are fixed under DOC-009. CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE's frame deletion statement is already accurate after DATA-001, so it is unchanged. Device check: Whether macOS revoking Microphone or Screen Recording mid-session stops the SCStream or AVAudioEngine, and how; the doc only states what Bluey's own code does.

- **User impact:** Maintainers and auditors make decisions from wrong guarantees, including privacy ones.
- **Root cause:** Docs were written ahead of, or diverged from, the implementation.
- **Evidence:** AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:25-27 says non-speech chunks are dropped before on-device transcription; AudioSession.swift deliver() appends every chunk to transcribers[source]; AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:106 describes a permission-kind stop plus repair flow (see permission-errors-flattened-and-revocation-unhandled); `MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 describes a 'Restart Bluey' offer that does not exist`; CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:17-18 says frames are 'discarded after use' (see screen-frames-never-discarded)
- **Proposed solution:** Correct the docs as part of each fix, or now for the claims that will not be implemented.
- **Test plan:** Doc review only.
- **Verification (confirmed):** AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:25-27 contradicts AudioSession.swift:327 (every chunk is appended to the transcriber). AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:106 describes a permission repair flow that does not exist. MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 describes a restart offer that does not exist. CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:18 says frames are 'discarded after use', which is false.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/audio-docs-drift)_

#### UX-023

**Every listen start without a Google key shows an error toast for the expected Apple fallback**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: audio.stt_fallback (the expected Apple Speech fallback) is shown once per app run as an info notice, not as an error toast on every start (src/stores/errorSurface.ts).

- **User impact:** Privacy-minded or key-less users see an 'error' on every session even though listening works.
- **Root cause:** An informational notice uses the error channel.
- **Evidence:** audio/mod.rs:336-341 publishes BlueyEvent::AudioError(stt_fallback) on every start with the default GeminiLive provider and no key; src/stores/errorSurface.ts:23 `eventBus.on("audio.error", (error) => showErrorToast(error))` with no code filter
- **Proposed solution:** Show stt_fallback once per app run as an info notice (or in AudioStatus.provider UI), not as an error toast. Alternatively, default to Apple when no Google key exists.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: errorSurface ignores or demotes code audio.stt_fallback after the first occurrence.
- **Verification (confirmed):** audio/mod.rs:338-343 publishes AudioError(stt_fallback) on every start without a key. errorSurface.ts:23 calls showErrorToast, which pushes variant 'error' (toast-store.ts:103). The copy itself is friendly ('Using Apple Speech', present.ts:108-111), so the issue is the styling and repetition, not the wording.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/stt-fallback-shown-as-error-toast)_

#### MAC-013

**The macOS 15+/26 monthly 'bypass the system private window picker' re-approval is neither documented nor handled, and together with the fail-whole-snapshot behaviour it breaks ⌘↵ until the user approves**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort S · has dependencies · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** About once a month, a system alert interrupts the first ⌘↵ or listen. Until the user approves it, capture or system audio fails with no in-app explanation.
- **Root cause:** A platform re-authorization flow the product has not accounted for.
- **Evidence:** The helper captures directly with SCShareableContent + SCScreenshotManager and SCStream (ScreenCaptureService.swift, SystemAudioCapture.swift, ScreenObserver.swift) and does not use SCContentSharingPicker; Sequoia shows 'X is requesting to bypass the system private window picker…' with 'Allow For One Month' (9to5mac.com/2024/08/14/macos-sequoia-screen-recording-prompt-monthly; macrumors.com/2024/10/07/apple-screen-recording-popup-update; developer.apple.com/forums/thread/765103, where a minimal SCShareableContent + SCScreenshotManager app triggers it). Admin guides report it persists on Tahoe 26; `rg over docs/*.md finds no mention of the prompt`; context/mod.rs:136 turns any capture failure into a failed ask (see capture-error-fails-whole-snapshot)
- **Proposed solution:** Document the prompt in MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md and in onboarding. Map the resulting capture error to a friendly 'macOS is asking you to re-approve screen access' message with Open Settings. Ensure the ask degrades without the screen (depends on the capture-error fix).
- **Test plan:** Doc review. Real Mac: advance the ScreenCaptureApprovals date or wait for expiry, then confirm the error mapping and degraded answer.
- **Depends on:** capture-error-fails-whole-snapshot
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (capture-audio-transcription/macos-private-window-picker-reapproval-unhandled)_

#### CTX-019

**Opportunity: enable AXManualAccessibility for Electron apps so AX context works for Slack, VS Code, Teams and Notion**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — Opportunity: not scheduled in this cycle; kept on the backlog.

- **User impact:** In many work apps the AX context is nearly empty, and the answer depends entirely on OCR of a downscaled frame.
- **Root cause:** The Electron-specific opt-in attribute is not set.
- **Evidence:** `No AXManualAccessibility / AXEnhancedUserInterface in the helper (rg over swift Sources)`; AXSnapshotService.swift walks only the default tree; Electron and Chromium expose a minimal tree until an assistive app opts in (Electron accessibility docs)
- **Proposed solution:** Before traversal, set AXManualAccessibility=true on the app element once per pid when it is an Electron app (the attribute is settable), and cache that it was done.
- **Test plan:** Real Mac: AX snapshot element and text count for VS Code/Slack before and after.
- **Verification (confirmed):** rg finds no AXManualAccessibility or AXEnhancedUserInterface in the Swift or Rust sources. MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:50 acknowledges that Electron trees are empty. Setting AXManualAccessibility is Electron's documented opt-in for assistive apps. Cache it per pid and measure the extra latency within the 250 ms AX budget.
- _Source: auditor (capture-audio-transcription/electron-apps-ax-tree-empty)_

### Native macOS, packaging & updates

#### MAC-001

**Every ad-hoc auto-update gives Bluey a new code identity, so Screen Recording, Accessibility and Microphone grants and Keychain ACLs stop matching**  
Severity **Critical** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: This covers the code side only. Signing with a Developer ID is still an owner action. PermissionManager.track_grants saves the last-known granted set together with the app version (a PermissionSnapshot in bluey-core types/permissions.rs). On the first refresh after a version change, any identity-bound grant that is now off is reported in PermissionState.lostAfterUpdate. At boot this opens Settings > Permissions, where UpdateRepairCard.tsx names the lost permissions, explains that unsigned updates reset macOS permissions and Keychain approvals, and offers Open System Settings for each one. A permission leaves the card once it is granted again. Documented in docs/UPDATES.md and docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md. Device check: Install an ad-hoc-signed update over a granted install and confirm the repair card appears once with the right permissions, and that the System Settings deep links open the right panes.

- **User impact:** After each update (daily on Nightly), screen capture, system audio, accessibility context and possibly the microphone stop working. System Settings still shows Bluey enabled, so users cannot see why. Keychain may prompt for every stored secret. The core screen-and-audio loop breaks for every user after every update.
- **Root cause:** Distributed builds are ad-hoc signed, so the designated requirement is the cdhash of that specific binary. TCC stores that requirement with each grant and legacy Keychain ACLs trust it. Any rebuild no longer matches.
- **Evidence:** codesign -d -r- /Applications/Bluey.app → `designated => cdhash H"5ac26453ccbd312059f5ac1980c37a1fb71df8ae"`, flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime), TeamIdentifier=not set; scripts/release.sh:98-103 `export APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=-` for no-credential builds; .github/workflows/nightly.yml:55 'Developer path: ad-hoc Apple signature'; docs/RELEASING.md:193-196; gh release view v0.1.2: 'not code-signed and not notarized … downloads and installs the new version in the background'; src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:191-197 auto-installs when `prefs.automatic` (default on); a nightly ships every day main changes; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:1,113 keyring v3 `apple-native` (legacy SecKeychain items; the ACL trusts the creating app's DR); docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:36-45 has no mention of this; `rg tccutil` finds nothing in code or docs; Web: ad-hoc DR is cdhash-pinned, TCC csreq mismatch → grant void while the toggle still shows ON (github.com/pathorsAI/parley/issues/75, github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/issues/86385, github.com/YARC-Official/YARG/issues/1695); `codesign -dr - /Applications/Bluey.app` => `designated => cdhash H"5ac26453..."`; bluey-helper is also cdhash-only; scripts/release.sh:100-102 sets APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- when unset; nightly.yml builds ad-hoc (RELEASING.md:194-196); TCC matches grants against the app's designated requirement; with a cdhash-only requirement every rebuilt binary is a different identity; MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 claims 'Bluey detects denied → granted transitions and offers Restart Bluey'; no such UI in PermissionsTab.tsx or onboarding/steps/permissions.tsx; codesign -d -r- /Applications/Bluey.app (v0.1.2): `designated => cdhash H"5ac26453ccbd312059f5ac1980c37a1fb71df8ae"`; bluey-helper `designated => cdhash H"f71b4c37…"`; codesign -dv: flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime), TeamIdentifier=not set; scripts/release.sh:100-104 sets APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- for every no-credential build; gh release list: 0.1.0/0.1.1/0.1.2 are 'unsigned build', nightly 0.1.3-nightly.20260912 'Unsigned developer build'; `docs/UPDATES.md:4-5,56: Automatic updates default on, downloads and installs by itself`; src-tauri/Cargo.toml:96 keyring v3 apple-native (legacy keychain ACL bound to the creating app's requirement); secrets/mod.rs:132-134 has_sync reads the secret data; settings/mod.rs:160-176 refresh_provider_keys calls has_sync for every provider; PermissionService.swift:59-60 CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess, :77 AXIsProcessTrusted: a stale grant reads as denied while System Settings still shows the toggle on; rg tccutil/cdhash over src, src-tauri, docs: no handling; docs/UPDATES.md never mentions TCC or Keychain; External: pathorsAI/parley#75, erickgnclvs/moomux#304, YARC-Official/YARG#1695 (ad-hoc cdhash voids TCC grants on every update); docs/UPDATES.md:4,56 — Automatic updates default on; available → downloading → ready/install; docs/RELEASING.md:194-196 — nightly/no-credential builds get an ad-hoc Apple signature, unsigned/un-notarized; `Machine fact: /Applications/Bluey.app v0.1.2 Signature=adhoc, no TeamIdentifier`; `rg for ad-hoc/TCC/permission in docs/UPDATES.md: no mention of the consequence`
- **Proposed solution:** 1) Sign every distributed build (nightly and any manual stable) with one stable identity: Developer ID when membership exists; until then, a self-signed code-signing certificate stored as a CI secret and imported into a temporary keychain in nightly.yml/release.sh (set APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY to it). The DR then becomes identifier + certificate leaf and stays stable across builds. Users re-grant once. 2) Add a developer-path gate that fails the build if `codesign -d -r-` contains `cdhash H"` for anything destined for a feed. 3) In-app mitigation: persist the last-known-granted permission set with the app version. On a version change where a previously granted permission now preflights as denied, show a 'macOS reset Bluey's permissions after the update' flow (open the matching Privacy pane; optionally run `tccutil reset ScreenCapture|Accessibility|Microphone com.codewithabdul.bluey`). 4) Document this in UPDATES.md and MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md. Consider turning automatic updates off by default while builds are ad-hoc.
- **Test plan:** Python test in scripts/release/tests (like test_native_gates.py) proving the gate rejects a cdhash DR and accepts a certificate DR. Rust unit test for the permission-regression detector (previous grants + version change + current denied → prompt state). Real macOS: install build N, grant all permissions and store a key, install N+1 through the updater, relaunch, confirm CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess/AXIsProcessTrusted stay true and no Keychain prompt appears (watch `log stream --predicate 'subsystem == "com.apple.TCC"'` for 'Failed to match existing code requirement').
- **Verification (confirmed):** Confirmed the installed app's DR is `cdhash H"5ac26453…"` with flags=0x10002(adhoc,runtime) and TeamIdentifier not set. scripts/release.sh:100-104 exports APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=- when no identity is given. nightly.yml:55 is the ad-hoc developer path. UpdatePrefs.automatic defaults to true (bluey-core/src/types/settings.rs:434), and updates/mod.rs:192-197 auto-installs. A cdhash-pinned DR can never match a rebuilt binary, so TCC csreq checks fail after each update. I did not observe a post-update grant loss on this machine, which is why confidence is 'likely'. Runtime proof: install nightly N, grant Screen Recording, auto-update to N+1, then preflight CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess. Fix correction: a self-signed cert gives a stable DR only if the same cert signs every build. Keep the release.sh gate. Turning automatic updates off by default while builds are ad-hoc is the cheapest immediate mitigation. — Lead override: same root cause as CRIT-001; the capture verifier rated it Critical. Screen Recording/Accessibility/Microphone grants reset on every auto-update of an ad-hoc build, and nothing detects or explains it.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: unknown). The mechanism holds. Ad-hoc builds are published to feeds users actually consume.

What I confirmed:
- `scripts/release.sh:100-104` exports `APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY=-` when no identity is given. It prints "Developer-only ad-hoc build; NOT eligible for publication", but nothing stops the result being published.
- `.github/workflows/nightly.yml:55` builds on that ad-hoc path. Its publish job at `:109-140` replaces the rolling `nightly` prerelease "and updater feed".
- `gh release list` shows `v0.1.0`, `v0.1.1` and `v0.1.2`, all "unsigned build", with v0.1.2 marked Latest. A nightly `0.1.3-nightly.20260912` also exists. So both channels ship ad-hoc builds.
- The only ad-hoc guard is `scripts/release/verify_macos.py:50-52`, which requires Developer ID and rejects `Signature=adhoc`. It runs only in the publish_release (Developer ID) path, not on the nightly or unsigned-stable path.
- `src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:192-196` auto-installs when `prefs.automatic && status.supported`.
- An ad-hoc designated requirement pins the cdhash, so TCC csreq and legacy Keychain ACLs (`keyring = { version = "3", features = ["apple-native"] }` at `src-tauri/Cargo.toml:96`) cannot match the next build.

What weakens the finding (partial counter-evidence, not a refutation):
1. **Users are not blind to it.** Screen Recording and Accessibility are re-checked live: `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` / `AXIsProcessTrusted` at `src-tauri/src/permissions/mod.rs:1-2,209` and `PermissionService.swift:60,77`. `docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:26-28` says `PermissionState` is refreshed on focus, on activation and every 30 s, and that revocation "surfaces a repair flow". So Bluey shows "denied" and links to the Privacy pane. The claim that users "cannot see why" is overstated. The real problem is narrower: the repair flow opens a pane where the toggle already looks ON, and nothing tells users to remove and re-add Bluey or run `tccutil reset`. `rg tccutil` finds nothing.
2. **Microphone is usually re-prompted**, not silently lost. When the stored csreq doesn't match, the prompt normally reappears. The Keychain impact is an "allow access" prompt the user can click through ("Always Allow"), not data loss.
3. **Nightly is not daily in practice.** Only one nightly exists (20260912). The audience is also small: every release is labelled unsigned or developer.

Net: the core loop breaks after each update until the user re-grants manually, and the in-app guidance points to a toggle that looks correct. That is a real, recurring broken flow, but it is detected in-app and limited to ad-hoc distribution. I rate it High, not Critical. I did not install N then N+1 through the updater on this machine (the app must not be launched), so runtime proof is still open. Fix critique: The long-term fix is right: sign every build with one stable identity (Developer ID, or a single self-signed code-signing certificate kept in CI secrets). A certificate-leaf DR stays stable across builds for both TCC and Keychain.

Items (2) and (3) should shrink:
- **Gate (2):** don't add a new cdhash check. `verify_macos.py:50-52` already rejects ad-hoc signatures; run that same check (or a relaxed "not adhoc" variant that accepts the self-signed identity) in `nightly.yml` before publish. As proposed, the gate would break the nightly until signing exists, so the two changes must land together.
- **Detector (3):** don't build a new version-keyed detector. The repair flow in `docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:26-28` already detects "denied". It only needs better copy and an action: "macOS may have reset this after an update. Remove Bluey from the list and re-add it", plus an optional `tccutil reset ScreenCapture|Accessibility com.codewithabdul.bluey` followed by a relaunch. Showing it only when a permission was previously granted and the version changed is a nice-to-have, not required.

Cheapest immediate mitigation: have the updater skip auto-install when the running bundle is ad-hoc (a runtime check via `SecCodeCopySigningInformation`, or a build-time flag). Still offer the update, but show a notice that permissions will need re-granting. That is better than flipping the global `automatic` default, which would also affect future properly signed builds.

Microphone and Keychain need no special handling beyond the notice, because macOS re-prompts for both.
- **Also reported as:** capture-audio-transcription/adhoc-signing-resets-tcc-on-update, testing-verification-debt/adhoc-update-voids-tcc-keychain, prior-audit-docs-drift/adhoc-auto-update-tcc-undocumented
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/adhoc-updates-reset-tcc)_

#### MAC-010

**LSUIElement is overridden at launch: Bluey runs as a Regular app with a Dock icon and a ⌘-Tab entry**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: Bootstrap sets ActivationPolicy::Accessory before the HUD is attached, so Bluey has no Dock icon and no Cmd-Tab entry (the runtime had been overriding LSUIElement). The app menu keeps the Edit key equivalents so copy, paste and select-all still work in Settings inputs. docs/ARCHITECTURE.md updated. Device check: Check there is no Dock icon and no Cmd-Tab entry, that Cmd+C/V/X/A/Z work in Settings text fields, and that the Settings window still takes focus when opened from the tray.

- **User impact:** A Dock icon and a ⌘-Tab entry appear. They are visible in every screen share, which works against Privacy mode and the menu-bar design. Clicking the Dock icon does nothing (see reopen finding).
- **Root cause:** tao applies its default Regular activation policy after launch, overriding the Info.plist key, and Bluey never sets Accessory.
- **Evidence:** `src-tauri/Info.plist and the installed Contents/Info.plist: `LSUIElement => true``; `Live `lsappinfo list`: "Bluey" pid 6547 `type="Foreground"` (Regular), version 0.1.2`; tao-0.35.3 src/platform_impl/macos/app_delegate.rs:106 `activation_policy: ActivationPolicy::Regular`; app_state.rs:284-285,455-468 `setActivationPolicy` in applicationDidFinishLaunching; No `set_activation_policy` call anywhere in src-tauri/src (rg); app/mod.rs:55-57 comments describe 'a menu-bar app'
- **Proposed solution:** In bootstrap (setup), add `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")] app.set_activation_policy(tauri::ActivationPolicy::Accessory);`. Keep the default app menu so Edit key equivalents (⌘C/⌘V/⌘A) still work in Settings inputs. Check that open_window's set_focus brings Settings and onboarding to the front, adding an NSApp activate if needed.
- **Test plan:** Real macOS: after launch, `lsappinfo info -only ApplicationType Bluey` reports UIElement and there is no Dock icon. Settings opens in front from the tray and copy/paste works in its inputs. Onboarding is visible on first run.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Info.plist has LSUIElement=true, but `lsappinfo list` shows Bluey pid 6547 type="Foreground" (the helper, pid 6565, is UIElement). There is no set_activation_policy call in src-tauri/src. Fix is as proposed. Accessory apps still process mainMenu key equivalents, so Edit shortcuts keep working. Verify that Settings and onboarding come to the front.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/activation-policy-overrides-lsuielement)_

#### MAC-011

**The helper handshake times out at every cold boot (helper.version > 2 s), so the helper is killed and respawned and an error toast appears**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D1: helper.version no longer builds an SFSpeechRecognizer on the serial read queue. New Swift SpeechCapability.swift (CachedCapability) probes speech.onDevice once on a background queue at startup, and the handshake reports whatever is known by then. The host gives helper.version 5 s (src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs) and retries the boot handshake once quietly before surfacing an error (src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs). Also adds the default locale: Locale.current, falling back to en-US. Device check: Cold boot (first launch after reboot), and confirm the helper is not killed and respawned during the handshake.

- **User impact:** Every launch on this Intel Mac shows a helper error and delays capture, permissions and audio readiness by several seconds. Captures or listening started in that window fail.
- **Root cause:** The version probe does slow Speech-framework work (XPC to speechd on a cold start) inline on the serial read queue, inside a 2 s host timeout.
- **Evidence:** ~/Library/Logs/Bluey 2026-09-16 08:58:12→14, 09-23 11:08:37→39 (3 kill/respawn cycles), 09-27 07:38:44→46 and 07:55:00→02: 'helper ready', then exactly 2.0 s later 'native helper did not start: sidecar.timeout: helper call `helper.version` timed out', then 'helper exited code None'; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:123-148 spawn_once kills on a version error; :400-408 timeout_for default `_ => Duration::from_secs(2)`; HelperApp.swift:121-155 `router.register("helper.version", inline: true)` constructs `SFSpeechRecognizer(locale: en-US)?.supportsOnDeviceRecognition` inline; Router.swift inline branch runs on the serial IO read queue and blocks every later request; src/stores/errorSurface.ts:24-26 shows a toast for helper.status with an error (published by app/mod.rs:375-382)
- **Proposed solution:** Make helper.version constant-time. Compute the speech.onDevice capability once on a background queue at startup and cache it, reporting it once ready (or as a later capabilities event). Give helper.version its own timeout_for entry of about 5 s as a safeguard. Do not surface a toast for a first-attempt handshake failure that a retry recovers from.
- **Test plan:** Swift XCTest: with an injected speech probe that sleeps 3 s, helper.version responds in under 50 ms. Rust test with a fake helper script that answers helper.version after 2.5 s: ensure_running succeeds. Real macOS: after a reboot, grep the logs for 'helper.version timed out' (expect none).
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanism is confirmed. helper.version is registered inline (HelperApp.swift:121) and builds SFSpeechRecognizer at :141. The default timeout is 2 s (sidecar/mod.rs:407), and spawn_once kills the helper on error (:145-147). But the failure is intermittent, not 'every cold boot'. Logs show clean boots on 09-22 13:36:33 and 09-27 07:40:55 and 07:42:47. Timeouts appear on 09-23 11:08 (4 ready/exit cycles), 09-27 07:38 and 07:55 (2 cycles). There is no crash_loop in the logs (MAX_RESTARTS=5). That SFSpeechRecognizer is the slow step is an inference. Confirm by timing the handler, or by stubbing capabilities and checking whether the timeouts stop. The fix is sound.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/helper-version-boot-timeout)_

#### SEC-012

**In Privacy mode the HUD is shown before content protection is applied, leaving it unprotected for several seconds after every launch**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: At boot, Privacy mode now protects every window before panel.attach first shows the HUD (app/mod.rs), not at the end of finish_boot. The tray's Toggle Privacy Mode now patches settings.privacy.displayMode, the single source of truth, and runs settings side effects (platform/mod.rs) instead of flipping the capture manager's flag. Device check: Start with Privacy mode on while screen recording and confirm the HUD is never captured unprotected at launch. Toggle from the tray and check that the HUD eye and Settings update.

- **User impact:** At login autostart or an update relaunch during a call, the HUD can be captured (legacy capture paths / macOS 14) until the helper is up.
- **Root cause:** The privacy side effect is ordered after slow async helper work instead of before the first show.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:325 `panel.attach(onboarding_completed)` shows the HUD during setup; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:388-392 set_protection(true) runs only in finish_boot, after shortcut registration (368-374), helper ensure_running (375-383: up to a 5 s ready wait plus a 2 s version timeout, ≥2 s at every boot on this Mac per the logs) and permissions.refresh (384); All three windows already exist at setup (tauri.conf.json app.windows; platform/mod.rs:84 'windows are pre-created and never destroyed')
- **Proposed solution:** In bootstrap, before `panel.attach`, call `capture.set_protection(true)` synchronously when `settings.privacy.display_mode == Privacy`, and drop the late call.
- **Test plan:** Unit test on an extracted ordering helper (e.g. `boot_window_effects(settings) -> Vec<Effect>` puts Protect before Show). Real macOS 14: launch in Privacy mode while recording with QuickTime; the HUD never appears.
- **Verification (confirmed):** panel.attach at app/mod.rs:325 applies the persisted visible state (overlay/mod.rs ~164-167 set_native_visible). set_protection runs at :388-392, after apply_bindings, helper.ensure_running and permissions.refresh in finish_boot. set_protection is sync, so moving it before attach is trivial. Value is limited on macOS 15+ (see the privacy finding) but real for legacy capture.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/privacy-protection-boot-gap)_

#### MAC-012

**A handshake timeout on respawn re-kills the helper repeatedly (ready then exit about 2 s later), and each failure counts toward the 5-restart crash-loop limit that disables the helper**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort S · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — D1 (MAC-011): helper.version no longer blocks on Speech, the handshake gets 5 s and one quiet retry, so cold boots no longer kill/respawn the helper. A backoff reset after a successful handshake was not separately tested.

- **User impact:** On a slow cold start (for example a busy Intel Mac after login), a few consecutive version timeouts can use up the restart budget. Capture, OCR and audio are then disabled until the user finds 'Restart helper' in Advanced settings.
- **Root cause:** A handshake timeout is treated like a crash. It kills the process and consumes restart budget instead of retrying the probe on the live process.
- **Evidence:** ~/Library/Logs/Bluey/bluey-2026-09-23.log 11:08:37-46: four 'helper ready' then 'helper exited code None' cycles about 2-3 s apart; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:24-25 MAX_RESTARTS=5, HEALTHY_AFTER=60s; :255-265 exceeding the limit sets desired=false and publishes crash_loop; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:145-147 a helper.version error kills the process, which then goes through on_terminated's restart counter
- **Proposed solution:** On a helper.version timeout, retry the probe once or twice on the same process (the helper did report ready) before killing it. Do not count handshake-timeout kills toward MAX_RESTARTS within the first boot window. Also apply the helper-version-boot-timeout fix.
- **Test plan:** Add a Rust unit test with a fake helper that delays the helper.version response by 3 s once. Assert that there is no kill and that the status ends running. Add a test that 5 slow handshakes do not publish crash_loop.
- **Depends on:** helper-version-boot-timeout
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (native-macos-platform/helper-timeout-respawn-loop-no-backoff-reset)_

#### UX-024

**Relaunching Bluey.app while it runs (Finder, Spotlight, Dock) does nothing: RunEvent::Reopen is not handled**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: RunEvent::Reopen (Finder or Spotlight launching Bluey.app again) now shows the onboarding wizard until it is complete, then the HUD (app/mod.rs reopen / reopen_target). Before this, it did nothing. Device check: With Bluey running, double-click Bluey.app and confirm the HUD, or onboarding, appears.

- **User impact:** A user who hid the HUD with ⌘\ (or has the shortcut hijacked or failing) and opens Bluey again from Spotlight or Applications gets no response, and may think it is broken or force-quit it.
- **Root cause:** The Reopen event is never handled.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:97-108 `app.run(|app, event| match event { ExitRequested …, Exit …, _ => {} })`; tao-0.35.3 app_delegate.rs:79-80,206-214 applicationShouldHandleReopen → AppState::reopen → tauri RunEvent::Reopen; `The tray is the only other way back (platform/mod.rs:137-230)`
- **Proposed solution:** Handle `RunEvent::Reopen { .. }` in app.run: open onboarding if it is not completed, otherwise show the HUD via open_window(app, "main", None).
- **Test plan:** Unit test a pure `reopen_target(onboarding_completed) -> &str`. Real macOS: hide the HUD, run `open -a Bluey`, and the HUD appears.
- **Verification (confirmed):** app/mod.rs:97-108 handles only ExitRequested and Exit. rg finds no Reopen anywhere in src-tauri/src. Re-rated to Low: the tray and the TogglePanel global shortcut both still work, and once the activation policy is fixed there is no Dock icon to click.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/reopen-event-ignored)_

#### MAC-014

**'Restart to update' exits without running app::shutdown: audio is not stopped and a running agent job is orphaned**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: updates_relaunch is now async. It runs app::shutdown() (stops audio, the agent sidecar and the helper) and then calls request_restart(). Before, it called restart() synchronously on the main thread, which skipped RunEvent::Exit and orphaned the helper and agent. Files: commands/updates.rs, updates/mod.rs, docs/UPDATES.md. Device check: Install an update and relaunch while listening, and confirm no orphaned bluey-helper or agent process is left.

- **User impact:** Clicking 'Restart to update' during listening skips session and transcript finalization. A research job keeps running headless, spending network and tokens, until it finishes or hits EPIPE. The relaunched instance is spawned by direct exec rather than through LaunchServices, which may confuse TCC attribution.
- **Root cause:** A synchronous command makes restart() take the main-thread path, which bypasses the run loop's Exit event where cleanup lives.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/commands/updates.rs:28-31 `pub fn updates_relaunch(...)` is sync, so Tauri v2 runs it on the main thread; `src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:311-321 relaunch() → `self.app.restart()``; tauri-2.11.5/src/app.rs:588-592 on the main thread: `cleanup_before_exit(); crate::process::restart(&self.env())`; process.rs:74-128 `Command::new(<Contents/MacOS/exe>).spawn(); exit(0)`, so RunEvent::Exit never fires; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:104-106,412-421 shutdown (agent.shutdown, audio.stop, helper.shutdown) runs only on RunEvent::Exit; tauri-plugin-shell lib.rs:132-142 kills only JS-spawned children; `sidecars/agent/src/main.ts:151-157 on stdin EOF a running job 'continues to completion'`; src-tauri/src/commands/updates.rs:28-31 `#[tauri::command] pub fn updates_relaunch` (not async, so Tauri v2 runs it on the main thread); `src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:311-320 `self.app.restart();``; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:104-106 RunEvent::Exit → block_on(shutdown(app)); :412-421 shutdown stops agent, audio and helper; Cargo.lock:4995-4996 tauri 2.11.5; docs.rs AppHandle::restart: 'When this function is called on the main thread … we skip them and directly restart the process. If you want to trigger them reliably, use request_restart'; `updates/ has 0 tests; PR #41 'No macOS, no real update yet'`
- **Proposed solution:** Make the command `async fn updates_relaunch(app: AppHandle, core: State<'_, AppCore>)` and call `crate::app::shutdown(&app).await` before `app.request_restart()`, or rely on the off-main-thread restart path, which emits ExitRequested(Some) and then Exit. Consider re-opening through LaunchServices (`open -n -a <bundle>`) rather than direct exec.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit test: UpdatesManager::relaunch calls an injected Shutdown hook before restart (fake AppHandle abstraction). Real macOS: start listening and a research job, relaunch, then check `pgrep -f bluey-agent` is empty and the session is closed in the DB.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanism is confirmed. updates_relaunch is a sync command (commands/updates.rs:28-31), so it runs on the main thread in Tauri v2. restart() then takes the main-thread path (tauri app.rs:588-592), which skips RunEvent::Exit, so shutdown() at app/mod.rs:104-106 does not run. Impact is smaller than claimed: the helper exits on stdin EOF when the parent dies, so audio capture stops anyway. The only real leak is an in-flight agent job that runs to completion and then exits. Re-rated to Low. The fix is right: make it async, await shutdown, then call request_restart.
- **Also reported as:** testing-verification-debt/relaunch-skips-shutdown
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/update-relaunch-skips-shutdown)_

#### MAC-015

**After a background auto-install, new sidecar binaries run under the old host until the user relaunches, and the agent has no protocol handshake**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Between install and relaunch (hours or days), every research job runs a newer agent against the older host. A changed JSONL contract fails in confusing ways, and a helper crash-restart can land in a protocol mismatch or crash loop.
- **Root cause:** The bundle is swapped under a live process whose sidecars are resolved by path on each spawn, with no compatibility negotiation.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs:191-197 installs automatically, then waits in `Ready` for a user relaunch (the pill only shows when the HUD is idle); tauri-plugin-updater-2.11.0 src/updater.rs:1324-1377 replaces /Applications/Bluey.app in place; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:101-106 and src-tauri/src/agent/mod.rs:229-236 resolve `sidecar(...)` on every spawn (agent: every job; helper: every crash restart); No version or protocol check for the agent (rg 'protocol|version' in agent/mod.rs finds nothing); the helper checks only the major version (bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs:255-260)
- **Proposed solution:** Add a ready/version line with a protocol number to the agent and reject a mismatch with a clear 'Restart Bluey to finish updating' error. For the helper, turn a post-install mismatch into the same message instead of a crash loop. When the phase is Ready, the UI can prompt a relaunch before starting a research job.
- **Test plan:** Rust unit tests with fake agent/helper scripts that report a different protocol: AgentManager returns a typed update.restart_required error; HelperClient does not enter crash_loop. Real macOS: install an update in the background, then run a research job before relaunching.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The code facts hold. The agent sidecar is resolved and spawned per job (agent/mod.rs:226-237) with no version handshake. The helper checks only the protocol major. Real impact requires a protocol-incompatible change between two consecutive builds while a user sits in the Ready state. That is plausible for nightlies but hypothetical, and no incident is evidenced. Re-rated to Low.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/sidecar-version-skew-after-autoinstall)_

#### MAC-016

**Persisted 'pinned' and 'always on top = off' are not applied at launch (the panel is always Floating)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: attach now applies the persisted pinned / always-on-top level through a single hud_level() function, which apply_level also uses. It no longer always sets Floating (overlay/mod.rs). docs/HUD_GEOMETRY.md updated. Device check: Turn always-on-top off, relaunch, and confirm the HUD is not floating.

- **User impact:** After a relaunch, a pinned HUD drops to Floating (below Status-level UI), and a user who turned always-on-top off gets a floating HUD again until they toggle it.
- **Root cause:** attach() hard-codes the level instead of calling apply_level().
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/overlay/mod.rs:125 attach(): `panel.set_level(PanelLevel::Floating.value())`; src-tauri/src/overlay/mod.rs:476-513 apply_level() runs only from set_pinned/set_always_on_top
- **Proposed solution:** Call `self.apply_level()` at the end of attach(), after the panel is stored.
- **Test plan:** Non-mac unit path: apply_level falls back to window.set_always_on_top. Add a test with a fake window recorder. Real macOS: pin, relaunch, check the panel level.
- **Verification (confirmed):** PanelState.pinned is persisted and loaded (overlay/mod.rs:70 get_panel_state). attach sets Floating (:125). apply_level is called only from set_pinned (:481) and set_always_on_top (:487). With always_on_top=false or pinned=true persisted, the boot level is wrong until the user toggles it.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/persisted-pin-level-ignored)_

#### MAC-017

**Per-display position memory is keyed by monitor name, so identical monitors collide**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** With two monitors of the same model, the HUD's remembered position is shared or overwritten between them.
- **Root cause:** The monitor name is not unique.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/overlay/mod.rs:560-577 `let id = monitor.name().cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| format!("{}x{}", ...))`; `overlay/mod.rs:205-219 `positions.recall(&display_id)``
- **Proposed solution:** Key by name plus origin (or CGDirectDisplayID via the NSScreen deviceDescription), and migrate old name-only keys as a fallback.
- **Test plan:** bluey-protocols geometry unit test: two work areas with the same name and different origins recall independent positions.
- **Verification (confirmed):** overlay/mod.rs:567-570 keys by monitor.name(), with position as the fallback only when the name is missing. Two identical-model displays share a name.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/monitor-name-key-collision)_

#### DOC-005

**Platform docs claim behavior that does not exist (restart offer, frames discarded after use, nightly version example)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX6: docs/UPDATES.md nightly example changed to X.Y.(Z+1)-nightly.YYYYMMDD, i.e. 0.1.3-nightly.20260913 for sources at 0.1.2 (per nightly.yml). The MACOS_PERMISSIONS restart offer was removed (see DOC-009). Two parts needed no change: CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE 'deleted after use' is now true (capture/mod.rs FRAME_CACHE_CAPACITY plus helper sweep, DATA-001), and ADR 0006 already has its 2026-09-28 addendum (SEC-004/SEC-012) about ScreenCaptureKit on macOS 15+.

- **User impact:** Maintainers and support rely on behavior that does not exist.
- **Root cause:** The docs were written ahead of the implementation and not reconciled.
- **Evidence:** docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 'Bluey detects denied → granted transitions and offers Restart Bluey': not implemented (rg only matches AuthGate.tsx:52); `docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:17-18 'discarded after use' (see temp-frames finding)`; docs/UPDATES.md:21 'Nightly versions look like 0.1.2-nightly.20260913: SemVer-greater than the current stable', which is false for current stable 0.1.2; RELEASING.md uses X.Y.(Z+1); `docs/adr/0006: ScreenCaptureKit exclusion claim (see privacy finding)`
- **Proposed solution:** Either implement the Screen Recording restart offer (via the permissions refresh transition) or remove the claim. Fix the other statements alongside their findings.
- **Test plan:** Doc review. Optionally a vitest asserting the restart CTA appears on a denied→granted screen transition if it is implemented.
- **Verification (confirmed):** MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 restart offer: no UI exists (the only matches are the update pill and AuthGate). CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md:18 says frames are 'discarded after use'. UPDATES.md:21 gives the example 0.1.2-nightly as 'greater than the current stable', while nightly.yml:4 derives X.Y.(Z+1)-nightly, so the example is stale.
- _Source: auditor (native-macos-platform/platform-doc-drift)_

### HUD & product UX

#### UX-001

**Default global hotkeys take ⌘←/→/↑/↓, ⌘⇧↑/↓, ⌘R and ⌘, away from every app while Bluey runs**  
Severity **Critical** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: New default shortcuts no longer take standard editing or app chords globally. Move is now Ctrl+Alt+arrows and Scroll is Cmd+Alt+Up/Down. NewChat and OpenSettings are HUD-local by default (enabled=false globally), and useHudShortcuts handles Cmd+R and Cmd+, locally. TogglePanel, CaptureAnalyze, GenerateResponse and ToggleListening stay global. Migration 0005_shortcut_defaults.sql moves only rows that still hold an old default and leaves customised rows alone. It runs once. Standard editing chords were added to KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS so recording one shows a warning. The mock transport (accelerators.ts, fixtures.ts) uses the same normalisation, conflict rules and defaults as bluey_core::shortcuts. keyboard.ts keeps Ctrl separate from Cmd and renders the ArrowUp/KeyR code names. The onboarding step lists only the global chords. Files: crates/bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs, bluey-storage migrations/0005 and repositories/settings.rs, src/lib/utils/keyboard.ts, src/lib/tauri/mock/*, KeybindsTab.tsx, onboarding steps/setup.tsx, useHudShortcuts.ts. Device check: Check that Cmd+arrows, Cmd+Shift+arrows, Cmd+R and Cmd+, reach other apps while Bluey runs, and that the new Ctrl+Alt / Cmd+Alt chords register on an upgraded install where migration 0005 ran.

- **User impact:** Bluey is a menu-bar app that runs all day. While it runs, ⌘←/⌘→ (start/end of line), ⌘↑/⌘↓ (top/bottom of document) and ⌘⇧↑/↓ (select to top/bottom) most likely stop working in every text field, ⌘, stops opening other apps' settings, and ⌘R stops reloading or replying. Each press moves or scrolls the HUD, opens Bluey Settings, or wipes the current chat. That is the opposite of 'invisible until useful'.
- **Root cause:** The Cluely-style defaults bind common macOS editing chords as always-on Carbon hotkeys, which take the key away from the focused app. Only the HUD needs Move/Scroll/NewChat/OpenSettings, but they are registered globally and permanently.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs:22-93 defaults: TogglePanel ⌘\, CaptureAnalyze ⌘↵, Generate ⌘⇧↵, ToggleListening ⌘⇧L, NewChat "CmdOrCtrl+KeyR", OpenSettings "CmdOrCtrl+Comma", MoveUp/Down/Left/Right "CmdOrCtrl+Arrow*", ScrollUp/Down "CmdOrCtrl+Shift+Arrow*"; mk() sets enabled: true (:15-20); src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:82-107 apply_bindings registers every enabled binding globally; called at boot (src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:370) and only re-applied on settings change, with no scoping to HUD visibility; global-hotkey 0.8.0 macOS backend uses Carbon RegisterEventHotKey (~/.cargo/registry/.../global-hotkey-0.8.0/src/platform_impl/macos/ffi.rs:112). Registering Cmd+C this way breaks copy system-wide, and returning eventNotHandledErr does not pass the key on ([alinpanaitiu.com](https://alinpanaitiu.com/blog/apps-outside-app-store/), [go-macos/hotkey](https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/go-macos/hotkey): 'you will win it globally and that application will silently stop seeing it'); KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS (shortcuts.rs:97-116) does not include ⌘←/→/↑/↓, ⌘⇧↑/↓, ⌘, or ⌘R, so the Keybinds tab never warns; shortcuts/mod.rs:227-230 NewChat publishes PanelNewChat + panel.show, so ⌘R in a browser clears the Bluey chat and shows the HUD instead of reloading; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs:32 'CmdOrCtrl+Enter', :50 'CmdOrCtrl+KeyR', :56 'CmdOrCtrl+Comma', :62-80 'CmdOrCtrl+Arrow*', :86-92 'CmdOrCtrl+Shift+ArrowUp/Down'; all `enabled: true`; src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:82-122 apply_bindings registers every enabled binding via `shortcuts.on_shortcut` (line 99) at boot (app/mod.rs:368-374), whatever the HUD visibility; global-hotkey-0.8.0 src/platform_impl/macos/mod.rs:116-123 `RegisterEventHotKey(scan_code, mods, id, GetApplicationEventTarget(), 0, …)`: a Carbon hotkey takes the keystroke before the frontmost app sees it; non-exclusive registration succeeds even when another app owns the combination; shortcuts/mod.rs:136-143: a clash with the system list is only logged ('shortcut shadows a system shortcut'); the KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS list (bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs:103-118) does not include app-level editing chords
- **Proposed solution:** Minimal change: (1) register the Move*, Scroll* and NewChat bindings only while the HUD is visible (unregister on hide in PanelManager.show/hide, or keep a 'visible-only' flag on ShortcutBinding), and move the defaults to less common chords (for example ⌃⌥ arrows for Move, ⌥⌘↑/↓ for Scroll). (2) Leave OpenSettings unbound by default, and keep NewChat as the HUD-local ⌘R that already exists (useHudShortcuts.ts:110-113). (3) Add text-navigation chords, ⌘, and ⌘R to KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS so the Keybinds tab warns. (4) Migrate stored bindings that still equal the old defaults.
- **Test plan:** Unit test in bluey-core: no default accelerator appears in a TEXT_EDITING_AND_APP_STANDARD list (Cmd+Arrow*, Cmd+Shift+Arrow*, Cmd+Comma, Cmd+KeyR). ShortcutManager test: hiding the panel unregisters the visible-only ids and showing it registers them again (fake global_shortcut). On a real Mac: with Bluey running, ⌘← in TextEdit moves the caret and ⌘, in Safari opens Safari Settings.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Checked src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs:12-93. mk() sets enabled:true, and the defaults include CmdOrCtrl+KeyR, CmdOrCtrl+Comma, CmdOrCtrl+Arrow* and CmdOrCtrl+Shift+ArrowUp/Down. KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS (:97-116) lists none of them. apply_bindings (src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:82-107) registers every enabled binding. Its only callers are boot (app/mod.rs:370), reset (:160), save (:200) and settings side_effects.rs:15, so nothing scopes these hotkeys to HUD visibility. The global-hotkey 0.8.0 macOS backend calls Carbon RegisterEventHotKey (platform_impl/macos/mod.rs:27). A Carbon hotkey consumes the chord system-wide, so ⌘←/→ (line start/end), ⌘↑/↓ (document start/end), ⌘⇧↑/↓ (select to start/end), ⌘R (reload) and ⌘, (Preferences) stop reaching the focused app for as long as Bluey runs. Pressing ⌘R also clears the Bluey chat and shows the HUD (trigger() :227-230). I kept Critical: this breaks basic text editing in every app for every user on default settings. The fix is correct. For the migration, only rewrite bindings whose accelerator still equals the old default_accelerator. Then check on a real Mac, for example in TextEdit with Bluey running.
- **Refutation attempt:** not refuted (runtime reproducible: yes). I could not refute this. Nothing in the code limits these hotkeys to when the HUD is visible.
- The defaults are defined in src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/shortcuts.rs:12-93. mk() sets enabled:true (:20). The defaults include "CmdOrCtrl+KeyR" (:50), "CmdOrCtrl+Comma" (:56), "CmdOrCtrl+Arrow{Up,Down,Left,Right}" (:62-80) and "CmdOrCtrl+Shift+Arrow{Up,Down}" (:86,:92).
- KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS (:97-116) contains none of these chords, so the Keybinds tab never warns.
- apply_bindings (src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:82-107) calls unregister_all and then on_shortcut for every enabled binding. rg finds only four callers: boot (app/mod.rs:370), reset (shortcuts/mod.rs:160), persist_and_apply (:200) and settings side_effects.rs:15. No panel show/hide path registers or unregisters anything, and nothing else in src-tauri/src unregisters.
- Stored overrides only replace the defaults (bluey-storage/src/repositories/settings.rs:120-140). A fresh install therefore gets every default.
- The plugin uses global-hotkey, whose macOS backend is Carbon RegisterEventHotKey. A registered Carbon hotkey is delivered to the registering app and not to the focused app. So on default settings, for every user, ⌘←/→/↑/↓, ⌘⇧↑/↓ (text navigation and selection), ⌘R and ⌘, stop working in other apps while Bluey is running.

The one open question is whether registration can fail for these chords. apply_bindings (:101-104) tolerates a failed registration and just reports it. But ⌘+arrow is not a reserved system hotkey, so registration should succeed. A real-Mac check in TextEdit settles it, so confidence is high but not "verified at runtime".

The finding also misses something. The General defaults ⌘↵ (CaptureAnalyze) and ⌘⇧↵ (GenerateResponse) take the "send" or "submit" chord used in many chat and email apps and in IDEs, so the same problem is broader than stated. I kept Critical: it is on by default, it affects basic editing in every app, and a menu-bar app runs all day. Fix critique: Part (1) of the fix proposes registering Move/Scroll/NewChat only while the HUD is visible. On its own that is not enough. Bluey's HUD is a non-activating overlay that stays visible while the user types in another app, such as during a meeting or while writing an answer. Visibility scoping would still take ⌘← and the other chords away exactly when the HUD is up. It would also mean re-registering on every show and hide, and each re-registration can fail.

The smaller root-cause fix:
- Change the defaults so that no chord overlaps standard text-editing or app chords. For example, use ⌃⌥ arrows for Move and ⌥⌘↑/↓ for Scroll.
- Unbind NewChat and OpenSettings globally by default (enabled:false, or an empty accelerator). The HUD-local handlers in useHudShortcuts.ts already cover these when the HUD has focus.
- Consider moving CaptureAnalyze and GenerateResponse off ⌘↵ and ⌘⇧↵.
- Visibility scoping is then an optional extra, not the main fix.

Other parts of the fix:
- Adding the editing chords to KNOWN_SYSTEM_SHORTCUTS is correct and cheap. Include ⌘↵ and ⌘⇧↵ too.
- The migration is required. ShortcutRepository::save_all (settings.rs:143-160) writes every binding once a user edits any one of them, so stored rows carry the old defaults. Rewrite only rows whose accelerator equals the old default.
- Add a bluey-core unit test asserting that no default accelerator appears in a deny-list of editing and standard-app chords. This is a real guard and does not depend on mocks.
- Keep the real-Mac check: with Bluey running, ⌘← in TextEdit should move the caret and ⌘, in Safari should open Safari's Settings.

None of these changes weaken security.
- **Also reported as:** native-macos-platform/default-global-shortcuts-hijack
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/global-hotkeys-hijack-editing)_

#### UX-002

**The HUD 'Screen off' toggle does not stop screen capture (⌘↵ and screen-requiring modes ignore it)**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: UI side. The Screen toggle state lasts for the session and shows an ImageOff icon plus aria-pressed when the screen is off. hudUiStore.screenWarning copies the screen_unavailable warning from the latest snapshot (context.updated). HudNotice.tsx then shows a compact 'Screen not included' notice with an Open Settings (Screen Recording) action. The engine side, passing screenAllowed, was already done by C. Device check: Revoke Screen Recording permission, ask a question, and check that the notice appears and that Open Settings goes to the Screen Recording pane.

- **User impact:** A user who turns Screen off (for example before sharing something sensitive) still has the screenshot, OCR and accessibility tree captured and sent to the AI provider on ⌘↵, on every typed ask and follow-up in the default mode, and on every live suggestion. The control misstates what Bluey does with the screen. In addition, 'Reading screen…' shows even when no screen is read (chatStore.ts:90 default phase "capturing"; context/mod.rs:89 CaptureStarted on every snapshot).
- **Root cause:** The toggle is only one input to an OR expression. The mode requirement and the trigger override it, so the toggle can only add the screen, never remove it.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:92-100 tooltip "Uses Screen"/"Screen off", aria-label "Screen context on/off"; the state is localStorage only (src/stores/hudUiStore.ts:22); src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:44-48 typed: captureScreen = !expanded && screenEnabled; :55-58 assist: trigger "shortcut_capture", captureScreen: screenEnabled; src/context/snapshot.ts:62-63 includeScreen = captureScreen === true || trigger === "shortcut_capture" || requires(mode, "screen"); :69 includeAccessibility follows it; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/modes.rs:88-98 General (the default) requires Screen + Accessibility; docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:19-28 Interview, Coding, System Design, Case and Lecture also list screen; src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:101-108 the only gate is options.include_screen; nothing reads the HUD toggle; Tests are mock-only: tests/ui/hud.test.tsx:69,103 assert the captureScreen flag on the fake engine; tests/unit/context/snapshot.test.ts:10-14 assert the mode override; nothing asserts that Screen off gives includeScreen === false
- **Proposed solution:** Add `screenAllowed: boolean` to AskInput/BuildNativeSnapshotArgs, filled from hudUiStore.screenEnabled in useAsk and in the proactive loop. In snapshotOptionsFor compute includeScreen = screenAllowed && (captureScreen || trigger === 'shortcut_capture' || requires(mode,'screen')). Product decision: should Screen off also drop the accessibility tree? Only enter the 'capturing' phase and label when includeScreen is true. If ⌘↵ is meant to always capture, relabel the toggle ('Screen for typed questions') instead.
- **Test plan:** Unit: snapshotOptionsFor with screenAllowed false gives includeScreen false for every trigger and mode. Boundary test (mock transport, not the fake engine): render HudPanel, toggle Screen off, press ⌘↵ and send a typed ask; assert the recorded context_build_snapshot options have includeScreen === false. Proactive test: a live suggestion with Screen off gives includeScreen false.
- **Verification (confirmed):** src/context/snapshot.ts:61-63 computes includeScreen = captureScreen===true || trigger==='shortcut_capture' || requires(mode,'screen'), and requires() reads mode.contextRequirements (src/modes/registry.ts:44-46). General requires Screen (modes.rs:95-97). HudPanel.tsx:43-57 passes screenEnabled only as captureScreen, so the toggle can add the screen but never remove it. Two further points: live suggestions (proactive.ts:167-178, trigger detected_event) in General mode also attach the screen, and suggestionDisplay defaults to Live (bluey-core settings.rs:286). So with 'Screen off' showing, screenshots and OCR are still sent to the cloud model on every detected question. This is a privacy-expectation breach. High is right, and it is borderline Critical for privacy-sensitive users. The fix is correct. Add a boundary test that snapshotOptionsFor({screenAllowed:false, trigger:'shortcut_capture', mode: general}) gives includeScreen === false and inlineImage === false.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/screen-off-toggle-cosmetic)_

#### UX-003

**After any AI failure the app stays in Error: audio start/stop is dropped (mic live with no HUD indicator) and the error pill stays over later successful answers**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E1: The state machine is recoverable (src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/state/mod.rs). CaptureStarted and ThinkingStarted leave Error and clear it. AudioStarted and AudioStopped update audio_active in every state except Booting and AuthRequired, so starting or stopping the mic after a failure is no longer dropped. Device check: After an AI failure, start and stop listening and confirm the HUD mic indicator and the pill follow.

- **User impact:** Example: an answer fails (network blip, 429, bad key) and the pill shows '! The AI request failed'. The user clicks Start Audio Session. The microphone starts (and the proactive loop can answer detected questions), but the button still says Start, there is no green dot, no Listening pill and no transcript strip. After dismissing the error the HUD says 'Bluey · General' while the mic is live, and clicking the button again does nothing. The reverse case leaves the HUD showing Listening forever after the audio stopped. Separately, the stale error pill with a Retry button stays over later answers that succeeded, and clicking it re-asks a duplicate.
- **Root cause:** Audio is documented as an 'orthogonal region', but it is gated on the main state, and new user work does not clear Error. The HUD derives listening only from AppStatus and ignores the audio manager's own events.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:678-685 publish_failed → hub.transition_soft(AppEvent::Failed) for primary tasks (is_primary :996-1001); src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/state/mod.rs:241-256 Failed → S::Error; only Recovered leaves it (257-264); CaptureStarted/ThinkingStarted require idle or ResponseReady (213-233); audio_toggle_allowed (287-300) excludes Error; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:336 transition_soft(AudioStarted) and :615 (AudioStopped) are silently rejected; start() returns early when running (:243-245) and mark_stopped when already stopped (:598-600), so it never resyncs; HUD reads only AppStatus: HudToolbar.tsx:38 audioActive; TranscriptStrip.tsx:26 returns null if !status.audioActive; state-pill.ts:43 error beats every other state; tray label platform/mod.rs:115-121; The only exit is StatePill.tsx:23-29 recover() → commands/app.rs:40-42; Recovered → idle_state() uses the stale audio_active (state/mod.rs:135-141); The same root cause on session pause: session-actions.ts:31-35 → audio.pause (audio/mod.rs:620-632) publishes only audio.paused; no frontend store listens (rg shows only errorSurface audio.error), so the HUD keeps a pulsing '● Listening' and 'Live transcript'; src-tauri/src/ai/mod.rs:678-685 publish_failed -> hub.transition_soft(AppEvent::Failed{error}) for primary requests; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/state/mod.rs:250-257 Failed -> state=Error; the only exit is Recovered (:258+); src/features/hud/StatePill.tsx:23-27,94,105 are the only callers of bluey.app.recover() (rg: no automatic caller); state/mod.rs:213-236 CaptureStarted requires is_idle()||ResponseReady; ThinkingStarted requires is_idle()||Analyzing||ResponseReady; ResponseReady requires Thinking; is_idle = Ready|Listening (types/app_state.rs:21-23); `src-tauri/src/state/mod.rs:60-71 transition_soft logs rejections at debug level only`; state/mod.rs:291-300 audio_toggle_allowed excludes Error, so AudioStarted/AudioStopped are rejected and audio_active is not updated; src/features/hud/state-pill.ts:42 `if (status?.state === "error") return { kind: "error" ... }` has top priority
- **Proposed solution:** (1) In the state machine, apply AudioStarted/AudioStopped in every state except Booting/AuthRequired (update audio_active; change `state` only from Ready/Listening). (2) Let CaptureStarted/ThinkingStarted leave Error (clear error and resume_state), or have useAsk call app_recover when an ask begins. (3) Add a small audio status store fed by audio.started/stopped/paused/resumed/error, and use it for the HUD button, pill and transcript strip (show 'Paused' when paused).
- **Test plan:** bluey-core state tests: Failed then AudioStarted gives audio_active true and still Error; Failed then ThinkingStarted gives Thinking with error None; Paused then AudioStopped updates audio_active. UI boundary test through the mock transport: status error + audio.started event → the toolbar shows 'Stop audio session' and the transcript strip renders; audio.paused → the pill reads Paused.
- **Verification (confirmed):** In state/mod.rs, Failed sets S::Error (:241-249) and only Recovered leaves it (:250-257). audio_toggle_allowed (:287-300) excludes Error, so AudioStarted/AudioStopped are rejected. CaptureStarted/ThinkingStarted also reject from Error (:213-229). The only Recovered publisher is app_recover (commands/app.rs:40-42), called only from StatePill.tsx:23-29. audio start() returns early when is_running (audio/mod.rs:244-246), and the only transition is transition_soft(AudioStarted) at :336, which is rejected silently. Pause publishes only AudioPaused (:620-632), and rg shows no frontend listener for audio.paused/started/stopped outside mock-transport. One correction: the error pill has an explicit dismiss X (StatePill.tsx:101-107) and a recovery action, so 'stays over later answers' holds only until the user dismisses it. The listening-indicator desync, a live microphone with no HUD or tray indication, is the serious part. Recovered restores idle_state() from the stale audio_active (:135-141), so after recovery the HUD still shows 'Start Audio Session' while the mic is running. High is right.
- **Also reported as:** error-handling-stubs/sticky-error-state
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/error-state-desyncs-listening)_

#### UX-004

**The HUD 'Detectable / Content-protected' eye can show the wrong stealth state, and the HUD toggle is lost on relaunch**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: The eye button in HudToolbar.tsx now reads settings.privacy.displayMode from the settings store and writes it back through settings update. The saved setting is the only source of truth, so the eye is correct after a relaunch and after a tray toggle. The mock transport's settings_update now applies content protection the same way Rust does. Device check: With D2's tray wiring merged: toggle privacy from the tray, relaunch, and check that the eye matches and the window is actually excluded from screen capture.

- **User impact:** The eye icon is the user's promise about whether the HUD appears in a screen share or recording (interviews, demos). After toggling privacy from the tray or Settings, the HUD can show 'Content-protected' while the panel is actually visible to the other side, or the reverse. Protection set from the HUD quietly goes back to 'Detectable' on the next launch while Settings shows 'Standard' the whole time.
- **Root cause:** Two sources of truth: a HUD-only direct capture command and the saved privacy.displayMode setting, plus a one-time fetch with no subscription.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:36-53 protection is read once on mount (capture.getProtection) into local useState and never refreshed; on failure it stays null → the button is disabled while the tooltip says "Detectable" (:102); src-tauri/src/platform/mod.rs:231-241 tray 'Toggle Privacy Mode' patches settings privacy.displayMode → settings/side_effects.rs:32-35 set_protection; Settings → Privacy takes the same path, and the HUD icon is not updated; HudToolbar.tsx:55-63 HUD toggle → capture_set_protection (src-tauri/src/commands/capture.rs:61-66 → capture/mod.rs:398-406) sets the live windows only and is not saved; boot re-applies settings (src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:388-390); settings.changed already reaches the WebView (src/stores/initStores.ts:37) but HudToolbar doesn't use it; src-tauri/src/platform/mod.rs:231-241 MENU_PRIVACY: `let enabled = !core.capture.protection().enabled;` (live state), then patches settings privacy.displayMode; src-tauri/src/settings/side_effects.rs:31-35 applies set_protection only `if old.privacy.display_mode != new.privacy.display_mode`; src-tauri/src/commands/capture.rs:61-66 + capture/mod.rs:398-406 HUD eye changes the live protection only and never touches settings
- **Proposed solution:** Make settings.privacy.displayMode the single source of truth. The HUD toggle calls settings update with { privacy: { displayMode } }, and the icon comes from useSettingsStore (so it stays correct through settings.changed). If a live read is still wanted, re-read on panel.state/focus and show an explicit 'unknown' state when the read fails instead of 'Detectable'.
- **Test plan:** hud.test (mock transport): emit settings.changed with displayMode 'privacy' → the button is labelled 'Content protection on'. Click the toggle → a settings_update call carries privacy.displayMode (assert the command name at the transport boundary, not a fake). On a real Mac: toggle from the tray and confirm with QuickTime or Zoom screen share that the HUD visibility matches the icon.
- **Verification (confirmed):** HudToolbar.tsx:36-53 does a one-shot getProtection into local state, with .catch(() => undefined) leaving it null. The button is then disabled while the tooltip reads 'Detectable' (:102). capture_set_protection (commands/capture.rs:61-66 → capture/mod.rs:398-406) sets only the live windows and never saves settings. Boot re-applies settings.privacy.displayMode (app/mod.rs:388-391). The two sources of truth are worse than stated: the tray MENU_PRIVACY (platform/mod.rs:231-241) derives the next mode from the LIVE core.capture.protection() and then patches settings, but side_effects.rs:31-35 applies protection only when the setting CHANGES. So after the HUD turns protection off while the setting is still Privacy, 'Toggle Privacy Mode' in the tray writes privacy→privacy, which is a no-op, and the windows stay capturable. A user can believe they are hidden during screen share when they are not. High is right.
- **Also reported as:** hud-ux/tray-privacy-toggle-noop-after-hud-toggle
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/content-protection-indicator-stale)_

#### UX-005

**Code blocks are unreadable in the Light theme (near-black box with light-theme token colors); hard-coded white hovers disappear**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: Added a --color-code-bg token in src/app/styles/theme.css: #0d0d0d in the dark theme and a light surface in the light theme. The hard-coded white hover fills are replaced with theme-aware bg-fg/N tints. A new useDocumentTheme hook (src/features/hud/useDocumentTheme.ts) makes CodeBlock.tsx pick the matching Shiki theme and MermaidDiagram.tsx the matching Mermaid theme. Documented in docs/DESIGN.md. Device check: Look at code blocks and diagrams in both the light and dark themes on a real display and check contrast.

- **User impact:** Every Light-mode Mac user on the default theme gets unreadable code in the HUD. That breaks the Coding Interview and System Design modes, whose answer is the code, and 'Copy code' becomes the only way to see it. Active and hover states (for example the selected 👍) can't be seen.
