- **Root cause:** The code surface color and the white-alpha interaction colors were written as dark-only literals instead of theme tokens, while the highlighter does follow the theme.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/CodeBlock.tsx:53 container `bg-[#0d0d0d]`; :31 picks the shiki github-light-default theme when data-theme=light; src/app/styles/theme.css:378-386 `.code-block pre { background: transparent !important }`, so dark light-theme tokens (#1f2328 body text) land on #0d0d0d, roughly 1.2:1; CodeBlock.tsx:83 fallback before shiki loads: `<pre … text-fg>` = #1d1d1f on #0d0d0d; :88 gradient from-[#0d0d0d]; ResponseView.tsx:142,155 'Writing code…' and Mermaid placeholder bg-[#0d0d0d]; src/lib/tauri/bootstrap.ts:28-30 theme 'system' → light when macOS is in Light appearance; the default is Theme::System (bluey-core types/settings.rs:156); 18 hard-coded `bg-white/*` / `hover:bg-white/*` in src/features/hud/*.tsx (e.g. ResponseActions.tsx:39-40 active state bg-white/10, ResponseView.tsx:87 bg-white/4, StatePill.tsx:90,104), which are invisible on the light hud-bg rgba(245,245,247,.82); CodeBlock highlight effect depends only on [code, language] (:29-38), so it is not re-highlighted when the theme changes at runtime; no light-theme test in tests/ui/response-view.test.tsx
- **Proposed solution:** Add a `--color-code-bg` token (#0d0d0d dark / #f6f8fa light) and use it in CodeBlock (container + gradient), ResponseView ('Writing code…', diagram placeholder) and MermaidDiagram. Replace `bg-white/N` with `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-fg) N%, transparent)` or the hud-chip token. Add the theme to the CodeBlock highlight effect's dependencies (subscribe to the data-theme change).
- **Test plan:** Unit: render CodeBlock with document.documentElement.dataset.theme='light' and assert the container has no #0d0d0d class and uses the token. Playwright visual regression of an expanded HUD with a code answer in light and dark at 690px. Contrast check that the shiki light fg on --color-code-bg (light) is at least 4.5:1.
- **Verification (confirmed):** CodeBlock.tsx:53 has the container bg-[#0d0d0d], :31 picks the light theme from data-theme, and highlighter.ts:104 maps it to github-light-default. theme.css:378-386 forces the pre background transparent, and no [data-theme=light] .code-block override exists (rg). bootstrap.ts:25-29 maps 'system' to light when the OS is in Light appearance. The effect deps are [code, language] only. Dark-on-near-black code in Coding mode is a core-surface readability failure for Light-appearance users. One correction: I count about 11 bg-white/* uses across the 5 HUD files, not 18. The substance still holds.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/code-blocks-unreadable-light-theme)_

#### UX-011

**Retry and Regenerate always re-ask the LAST turn as 'regenerate' with no screen: wrong question and lost context**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: ChatTurn stores its TurnRequest (trigger, instruction, captureScreen, detectedEvent, promptLabel) (src/stores/chatStore.ts). useAsk exposes retry(turnId) and regenerate(turnId), which re-send that turn's own request. HudPanel and ResponseThread pass the turn id.

- **User impact:** Retry after a failed screen Assist can give an answer with no context at all. Retry on an earlier failed turn re-asks a different (later) question. Regenerating a live suggestion loses its transcript anchoring. Users learn not to trust Retry, which is the main recovery button for AI errors.
- **Root cause:** The chat turn stores only prompt and label, not the original request, so every recovery rebuilds the request from the last turn with fixed defaults.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/ResponseThread.tsx:85-86 `<ErrorBanner … onRetry={onRegenerate}>` is shown on every errored turn, not just the last; :91 the truncated banner is the same; the StatePill Retry goes through HudPanel.tsx:70-72 retry → regenerate; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:117-126 regenerate: `turns[turns.length - 1]`, instruction = last.prompt (undefined for Assist), captureScreen: false, trigger "regenerate", no detectedEvent; src/ai/prompts/task.ts:21-22 the regenerate task is 'Answer again, better… not a rephrasing', even though a failed turn had no answer (completedResponses excludes it, chatStore.ts:184-186); src/context/snapshot.ts:62-63 includes the screen only when the mode requires it, so in Behavioral, Sales, Recruiting and Meeting modes Retry of a failed ⌘↵ Assist sends no screen and no instruction; src/ai/engine.ts:288-291 AskInput already supports reusing `input.snapshot`, but useAsk never keeps it; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:117-126: regenerate sends instruction = last.prompt (undefined for ⌘↵ 'Assist' and ⌘⇧↵ turns) and captureScreen:false; src/context/snapshot.ts:62-63: the screen is included only if captureScreen, trigger === 'shortcut_capture', or the mode requires Screen. Interview, Behavioral, Sales and Team Meeting do not require Screen (modes.rs requirements lists); No snapshot reuse: no cached or previous snapshot in src/ai/engine.ts or src/context/snapshot.ts (rg finds nothing); `src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:70-72,92: the error-state Retry button is regenerate()`; src/ai/prompts/task.ts:22: 'Answer again, better … the same question — not a rephrasing', but without a session the previous answer is dropped too (snapshot.ts:194 session gate); src/stores/proactive.ts:162 begin(event.text, event.text, …) stores the question as turn.prompt; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:117-126 regenerate => ask({trigger:'regenerate', instruction: last.prompt}) with no detectedEvent or suggestion meta; src/features/hud/ResponseThread.tsx:84,86 the new turn renders as a user PromptPill; the ErrorBanner retry on a failed live turn uses the same regenerate; engine.ts:418 the prompt builder receives the text as `instruction` instead of `detectedEvent`
- **Proposed solution:** Store the original AskRequest (trigger, instruction, captureScreen, detectedEvent, promptLabel, and optionally the snapshot) on ChatTurn. `retry(turnId)` re-sends the original trigger and inputs (for a failed turn). `regenerate` (for a finished last answer) uses trigger 'regenerate' with the same captureScreen/detectedEvent and reuses the snapshot. Show Retry only on the last turn, or pass the turn id.
- **Test plan:** hud.test with the fake engine: a failing Assist → click Retry → the recorded ask has trigger 'shortcut_capture' and captureScreen true. Two turns where the first failed → Retry on the first re-asks the first prompt. Regenerate on a suggestion turn keeps its detectedEvent.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ResponseThread.tsx:84-91 wires the ErrorBanner onRetry to onRegenerate on every errored or truncated turn, not just the last. useAsk.ts:116-125 regenerate uses turns.at(-1), instruction=last.prompt, captureScreen:false, trigger 'regenerate'. The regenerate task text (prompts/task.ts:21-22) is 'Answer again, better', which is wrong for a failed turn. The StatePill retry also routes to regenerate (HudPanel.tsx:70-72). One more point: the pill's action runs presented.action() then recover() (StatePill.tsx:91-95), so the retry's ThinkingStarted is issued while the machine is still in Error and is rejected. Medium is right.
- **Also reported as:** prompt-stack/regenerate-loses-original-context, live-suggestions/regenerate-suggestion-loses-provenance
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/retry-regenerate-wrong-context)_

#### UX-012

**Clearing the HUD chat (Esc, ←, ⌘R) can't be undone, and answers asked outside a session can't be found afterwards**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — WS-E2: Esc now clears a non-empty draft first; a later Esc does the existing hide or cancel. Clearing the thread shows a 'Chat cleared · Undo' row in HudNotice for UNDO_CLEAR_MS (5 s), backed by chatStore.cleared and undoNewChat. initStores resets this state. Not done: listing answers that have no session in History. That needs a change to the Rust responses_list, which I do not own.

- **User impact:** Pressing Esc to clear a half-typed follow-up, or ⌘R to reload a browser tab, wipes the whole conversation. If no session was running (typed or ⌘↵ asks without listening), those answers are gone from the UI for good.
- **Root cause:** Esc is overloaded as 'clear everything', there is no soft-delete, and the only history browser is organised by session.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:65-68 Esc on a finished thread = newChat, including from inside the follow-up input with a draft (useHudShortcuts.ts:95-98); tests/ui/hud-keyboard.test.tsx:132-142 lock this in; ← Back = newChat (HudPanel.tsx:124); ⌘R is a global hotkey (see global-hotkeys-hijack-editing); `src/stores/chatStore.ts:173-180 newChat drops all turns, with no undo`; src/ai/engine.ts:419-423,566-571 responses are saved with sessionId = input.session?.id; src-tauri/src/commands/responses.rs:20-29 responses_list requires a sessionId and no UI calls it; SessionDetail.tsx:362-370 shows only session responses
- **Proposed solution:** Esc first clears a non-empty input draft, and only a second Esc (or an explicit New Chat) clears the thread. After a clear, show a short 'Chat cleared · Undo' pill (keep the last turns in memory for about 5 s). Make session-less answers reachable (an implicit 'Quick asks' bucket, or list responses with a null sessionId in History).
- **Test plan:** hud-keyboard: Esc with a draft clears only the draft, and a second Esc calls onEscape/newChat. hud.test: newChat then Undo restores the turns. Integration: an ask with no session appears in the History list.
- **Depends on:** global-hotkeys-hijack-editing
- **Verification (confirmed):** chatStore.newChat (:173-180) drops all turns with no undo. HudPanel onEscape (:65-68) calls newChat when expanded and not streaming. responses_list requires session_id (commands/responses.rs:20-28), and its only frontend use is api.ts:165. I did not re-read the hud-keyboard test that pins Esc-with-draft behaviour. The global ⌘R part compounds with global-hotkeys-hijack-editing: ⌘R in any app wipes the thread. Medium, borderline Low if global ⌘R is fixed.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/irreversible-chat-clear)_

#### UX-013

**Error toasts render inside the auto-sized HUD window, covering the toolbar for up to 12 s and clipping when stacked**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: HUD errors now show in one inline notice row (src/features/hud/HudNotice.tsx) above the toolbar. The row is inside the measured, auto-sized frame, so errors no longer appear as a toast covering the toolbar. toast-store has inline error hosts: the HUD claims errors via claimInlineErrors, and Toasts accepts a limit prop. Documented in docs/HUD_GEOMETRY.md. Device check: Check that the HUD window grows to fit the notice row and that nothing is clipped at the screen edge.

- **User impact:** Right after an audio or helper error, the controls the user needs to fix it (the audio button, or the pill's Restart helper) are covered by the toast and can't be clicked until it is dismissed or times out. A second concurrent error is invisible.
- **Root cause:** A generic bottom-centre toast host designed for the Settings window is reused inside a tightly content-sized transparent panel.
- **Evidence:** src/windows/HudWindow.tsx:15 <Toasts/> is a sibling of HudPanel; src/components/ui/Toast.tsx:50 `fixed bottom-6 left-1/2` 360px; ErrorToast has pointer-events-auto (:12); `src/components/ui/toast-store.ts:39-41 error 6000 ms, actionable 12000 ms`; src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:40 + useAutoHeight.ts:42-65 + geometry.ts:28-32 measure only the frame, so the window never grows for toasts; DESIGN.md:152-156 idle frame 754×175 (surface y 24-135). A roughly 95px error toast at bottom 24 spans y≈56-151, covering the whole centre toolbar cluster (Screen/Eye/Mode/Audio/Session) and half the input; a second toast with a different code goes above y=0 and is clipped; Sources in the HUD: errorSurface.ts:21-28 (app.error, audio.error, helper.status), HudToolbar.tsx:61,70, ModeMenu.tsx:46
- **Proposed solution:** In the HUD, route error toasts into the state-pill row (it already renders a title plus one recovery action) or into a single inline notice row inside the surface that is included in the measured frame. Keep only non-blocking info pills ('Copied') as overlays, cap the HUD stack at one, and position it above the surface.
- **Test plan:** hud-layout test: with an error toast visible, the toolbar's audio button is still the hit target at its centre (document.elementFromPoint), or the measured frame height grows by the toast height. Playwright screenshot of the idle HUD with an actionable error.
- **Verification (unverifiable):** The code facts hold. <Toasts/> is rendered in HudWindow.tsx:15 as a fixed bottom-6 host (Toast.tsx:50). Error durations are 6000/12000 ms (toast-store.ts:39-41). The auto-height measures only the panel. Whether a toast actually covers the toolbar depends on the transparent padding and the real window height, so it needs a screenshot on a real Mac, or a Playwright capture of the HUD route at the idle frame size with a forced audio.error. Keep Medium pending that check.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/error-toasts-cover-hud)_

#### UX-014

**The panel-opacity preference fades the text as well as the background; muted and subtle text fails contrast over bright backdrops**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: Panel opacity now changes only the background alpha. .hud-surface in theme.css mixes the background with --hud-opacity, set by HudPanel, so text stays fully opaque. Added HUD-specific muted and subtle foreground tokens that pass contrast over the translucent surface. Documented in docs/HUD_GEOMETRY.md. Device check: At minimum opacity, check that text is still readable over busy wallpapers in both themes.

- **User impact:** Over a light document or web page (LeetCode, Google Docs, a white slide), section labels, speaker names and transcript partials are close to invisible. Lowering opacity to see through the panel makes the answer itself unreadable (primary text under 3:1 at 60%), right when the user needs to glance at it and say it.
- **Root cause:** Opacity is implemented as group CSS opacity instead of background alpha, and the HUD reuses the Settings text scale, which was tuned for an opaque dark background.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/HudPanel.tsx:82,112 `style={{ … opacity }}` on the role=dialog surface (text, buttons, everything); overlay/mod.rs:141-143 'Opacity is applied once, on the CSS surface'; src/features/settings/tabs/AppearanceTab.tsx:94-106 slider from PANEL_OPACITY_MIN (40%) to 100%, 'the panel keeps its blur'; default 0.92 (bluey-core types/settings.rs:157); src/app/styles/theme.css:17-33 dark fg #f2f2f2 / muted #9a9a9a / subtle #6b6b6b on hud-bg rgba(22,22,22,.78); light (111-119) subtle #98989d; Computed (/tmp/bluey-audit/hud-ux/contrast.ts, WCAG formula): dark theme at 100% over a white window: fg 8.0, muted 3.2, subtle 1.69, accent 2.47, danger 2.64. At 60%: fg 2.9. At 40%: fg 1.94. Light theme at 100% over black: muted 3.07, subtle 1.74; fg-subtle is used for readable content: section headings of spoken answers (ResponseView.tsx:73), the suggestion speaker (ResponseThread.tsx:70), partial transcript text and the 'Live transcript' label (TranscriptStrip.tsx:41,75), and 'Stopped.'
- **Proposed solution:** Drive the preference into the background alpha only (for example `--hud-bg-alpha` used by bg-hud-bg, or color-mix on the surface) and keep text at full opacity. Give the HUD its own brighter muted/subtle tokens (dark: about #b3b3b3/#8c8c8c), and add a stronger scrim when blur is off. Target at least 4.5:1 for body text and 3:1 for 11px labels over pure white and black backdrops at the minimum opacity.
- **Test plan:** Unit: the HudPanel surface has no inline `opacity` and sets the bg-alpha variable. Token test that runs the contrast math for the HUD tokens over white and black at PANEL_OPACITY_MIN. Playwright screenshots of the HUD over white and black pages in both themes at 40% and 92%.
- **Verification (confirmed):** HudPanel.tsx:112 applies style opacity to the whole role=dialog surface, and overlay/mod.rs:141-143 confirms CSS is the single opacity layer. So the 40-100% slider fades text as well as the background. I did not recompute the contrast numbers. The mechanism alone justifies Medium.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/opacity-fades-text-contrast)_

#### UX-015

**There is no keyboard way to type into the HUD: panel.focusInput is never sent and the panel is never made key**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-FX7: A second press of the toggle-panel shortcut while the HUD is visible focuses the HUD input: the non-activating panel becomes key and publishes PanelFocusInput, and the other app stays active (commit 747db34). Device check: Press the toggle-panel shortcut twice over another app and type: the text lands in the HUD composer.

- **User impact:** A keyboard-driven copilot needs the mouse to type a question: ⌘\ shows the HUD but keystrokes keep going to the other app. Keyboard-only and switch-access users can't use typed asks or follow-ups at all, and can't stop a runaway answer without clicking.
- **Root cause:** The decision not to take focus is correct for show-on-⌘↵, but no deliberate way to take focus was added (the event exists; its producer was never written).
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/overlay/mod.rs:129-139 non-activating panel with becomes_key_only_if_needed; show()/toggle() (229-261) → tauri-nspanel show() = orderFrontRegardless (tauri-nspanel c9ec213 src/panel.rs:242-244); show_and_make_key (:409) is never used; bluey-core events/mod.rs:244-245,311 declare BlueyEvent::PanelFocusInput; `rg BlueyEvent::PanelFocusInput src-tauri/src` finds no publisher; the HUD listens at HudInputRow.tsx:21 (dead code); src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:205-260 has no 'focus input / ask' shortcut and no global Stop; Esc and ⌘R are HUD-local (useHudShortcuts.ts:86-114), so after ⌘↵ from Zoom, Esc goes to Zoom and not to Stop; `src/lib/tauri/events.ts:110,180 declared; src/features/hud/HudInputRow.tsx:21 listens`; bluey-core events/mod.rs:245,311 variant exists; no `BlueyEvent::PanelFocusInput` construction in src-tauri/src; no TS emit
- **Proposed solution:** Add an 'Ask Bluey (focus input)' shortcut, or make a second ⌘\ press while visible focus the input: call panel.show_and_make_key() (the panel stays non-activating, so the other app remains active) and publish PanelFocusInput. Hand key status back on Esc or submit by ordering the panel out of key (resignKey). Optionally add a global 'stop answer' binding.
- **Test plan:** Rust: the shortcut dispatch test for the new id publishes PanelFocusInput and calls the make-key path (fake panel). UI: emitting panel.focusInput focuses the input in both layouts. Real Mac: from TextEdit press the shortcut, type, and TextEdit stays the active app (menu bar unchanged).
- **Verification (confirmed):** The panel is nonactivating with becomes_key_only_if_needed (overlay/mod.rs:126-139). show/toggle call set_native_visible → panel.show() (:229-261), and rg finds no make_key/show_and_make_key anywhere in src-tauri/src. BlueyEvent::PanelFocusInput has no publisher outside the core tests (rg shows only events/mod.rs and events/tests.rs), so HudInputRow.tsx:21's listener is dead. The HUD-local Esc/⌘R only work once the user has clicked into the panel. Medium is right.
- **Also reported as:** testing-verification-debt/panel-focusinput-dead
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/no-keyboard-path-into-hud)_

#### UX-025

**VoiceOver gets no announcement for thinking, answer ready, listening or errors in the HUD**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: Added one visually hidden aria-live=polite announcer (HudAnnouncer.tsx, announce.ts, with state from useStatePill.ts). It announces thinking, answer ready, listening and errors once per state change, not per token. Spinner.tsx is decorative (aria-hidden), and the eye button has aria-pressed. Device check: Check with VoiceOver that each state is announced once and not repeated.

- **User impact:** Screen-reader users can't tell when Bluey starts or finishes answering, when listening starts or stops, or when an error needs action. The core ask-to-answer loop is silent for them.
- **Root cause:** The HUD components were built visual-first, and the state lives in derived pills without a single announcer.
- **Evidence:** `rg aria-live src` finds nothing; the state pill variants are plain <span>s (StatePill.tsx:112-155) with no role=status; `ResponseThread.tsx:150-157 answer region role=region with no aria-live/aria-busy`; Spinner.tsx:10-13 every spinner is role=status aria-label 'Loading', so the pill and the body spinner give a duplicate, meaningless 'Loading'; `StatePill.tsx:82-110 the error pill has no role=alert (ErrorBanner.tsx:21 does)`; HudToolbar.tsx:93-113 Screen and Eye toggles use aria-label wording only; IconButton `active` is visual only (IconButton.tsx:29,42), with no aria-pressed
- **Proposed solution:** Add one visually hidden `aria-live="polite"` announcer in HudPanel, fed by derivePill kind changes and by chat completion ('Answer ready: <title or first sentence>'), not by every token. Make decorative spinners aria-hidden. Give the error pill role=alert. Use aria-pressed on the Screen and Eye toggles.
- **Test plan:** hud.test: after complete() the live region contains 'Answer ready'; on status listening it contains 'Listening'; exactly one role=status is present while thinking. axe check on the rendered HUD. Manual VoiceOver pass on a real Mac.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The claim is overstated. ErrorBanner has role=alert (ErrorBanner.tsx:21), and inline turn errors use it (ResponseThread.tsx:85). ErrorToast has role=alert (Toast.tsx:10) and info toasts have role=status (:57). Spinners are role=status 'Loading'. What is genuinely missing: no announcement of 'answer ready' or its content, no role on the pill kinds, and no aria-pressed on the Screen/Eye toggles (the aria-labels do change with state). The HUD is also a non-key panel, which limits VoiceOver focus anyway. The 'silent through the whole loop' framing is wrong. Low to Medium; I rate it Low.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/screen-reader-silent)_

#### UX-026

**HUD shortcut hints are hard-coded and the 'Bluey has a suggestion' pill can't be clicked**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E2: Shortcut hints now come from the saved settings through useShortcutAccelerator.ts and are hidden when a shortcut is disabled. keyGlyph in src/lib/utils/keyboard.ts learned KeyR. The 'Bluey has a suggestion' pill is now a real button that shows the prepared answer.

- **User impact:** After rebinding or disabling a shortcut the HUD advertises a chord that does nothing. Mouse-only users can't open a prepared suggestion.
- **Root cause:** The hints are string literals rather than values derived from the settings bindings.
- **Evidence:** `src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:165,174 literal 'CmdOrCtrl+R' in the tooltip and keycaps`; `src/features/hud/StatePill.tsx:124-133 literal '⌘⇧↵' in a non-interactive <Pill>`; Bindings can be changed or disabled (settings.shortcuts; KeybindsTab) and are available in useSettingsStore; src/features/hud/HudToolbar.tsx:165 `shortcut="CmdOrCtrl+R"`, :174 `<Keycaps accelerator="CmdOrCtrl+R" />`; Shortcuts can be rebound in KeybindsTab.tsx and onboarding setup.tsx:65; the Rust default is new_chat = CmdOrCtrl+KeyR (bluey-core shortcuts.rs)
- **Proposed solution:** Read the accelerator for new_chat and generate_response from useSettingsStore (and hide the hint when the binding is disabled). Render the prepared pill as a button that calls generateOrTakePrepared.
- **Test plan:** hud.test: rebinding new_chat in settings updates the keycaps; clicking the prepared pill shows the prepared turn.
- **Verification (confirmed):** HudToolbar.tsx:165,174 hard-code 'CmdOrCtrl+R', and StatePill.tsx:124-133 hard-codes '⌘⇧↵' in a non-interactive Pill. Low is right.
- **Also reported as:** surface-parity/hud-new-chat-hardcoded-shortcut
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/hardcoded-hints-prepared-pill)_

#### UX-027

**Auto-follow keeps the bottom of the stream in view, so the first ('say this') line of a long answer scrolls away while it streams**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-E2: When a turn starts, its top is scrolled into view. Following continues only while the turn's first line is still visible; after that the down-arrow button takes over. The logic is in src/features/hud/thread-scroll.ts and is used by ResponseThread.tsx. Device check: Check scroll-follow behaviour with real streaming in WKWebView.

- **User impact:** For answers taller than the viewport (behavioral STAR answers, sections, code) the user loses the opening sentence they are reading aloud and has to scroll back up mid-sentence.
- **Root cause:** A chat-style follow-the-tail policy is applied to a glance-and-speak surface.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/ResponseThread.tsx:131-135 scrollToBottom on every content change while at the bottom; The body viewport is the 620px surface cap minus the 56px header, 52px toolbar and up to about 110px of transcript strip (HUD_GEOMETRY.md; geometry.ts:7), so roughly 350-400px; new live suggestions are appended under older turns (proactive.ts:162)
- **Proposed solution:** When a new turn begins, scroll its top into view. Keep following only while the turn's top would stay visible, then stop and show the ↓ button.
- **Test plan:** ResponseThread test with a mocked scrollHeight: a streaming long answer keeps the turn's top in view once its height exceeds clientHeight.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ResponseThread.tsx:131-135 calls scrollToBottom on every lastContent change while at the bottom. How much actually scrolls away depends on answer length and viewport height. This is a product polish call. Low.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/auto-follow-hides-answer-start)_

#### UX-028

**'Copy answer' copies only the raw markdown body; feedback failures are silent**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: Copy answer copies the title, content and sections as plain text, and a failed rating write is reported (commit def9392).

- **User impact:** Pasted answers are incomplete or cluttered with markdown in chat or email, and feedback the user thinks was sent may be lost.
- **Root cause:** Copy was implemented as a raw-content shortcut; feedback failures aren't shown.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/ResponseActions.tsx:62-67 copyText(response.content) leaves out the title, plain sections (ResponseView.tsx:62-68 renders answer/suggestion sections, e.g. Behavioral 'Story used · Key point'), a separate response.code and citations, and pastes markdown syntax; ResponseActions.tsx:74-83 on a feedback error only console.warn; the rating stays highlighted; `ResponseThread.tsx:93 actions appear only on the last turn`
- **Proposed solution:** Build the copy text from title + content + sections (plain text, markdown stripped, with an option to keep markdown), roll back the rating and toast on feedback failure, and offer Copy on hover for earlier turns.
- **Test plan:** Unit: copy text for a response with sections includes the section titles and bodies, without '**'. hud.test: a feedback rejection resets the rating and shows an error toast.
- **Verification (confirmed):** ResponseActions.tsx:62-68 copies only response.content. :74-83 sets the rating optimistically and only logs to console.warn on failure. Low.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/copy-answer-partial)_

#### UX-035

**Opportunity: show which provider and model answered, and when an account has fallen back to the API key**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence likely · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E2: Under the answer actions there is now a muted provenance line: model and latency, plus 'via API key' or the fallback reason when there is one. The data comes from response.selection (provenance.ts, ResponseProvenance.tsx). When research failed, ResponseThread shows a subtle research note. ResponseActions uses useAsk retry(turnId) and regenerate(turnId). The code-block 'Copied' check in tests/ui/response-view.test.tsx is now deterministic (fireEvent with a fake setTimeout). Documented in docs/DESIGN.md.

- **User impact:** Users can't tell a slow or odd answer from a Flash vs Pro model, or notice that their API key (billed) is now answering instead of their subscription.
- **Root cause:** Response metadata is collected for the dev overlay but never shown in the HUD.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/types/response.ts:39-41 metrics.provider/model/totalMs are filled in by the engine; `rg metrics src/features/hud` finds nothing; src-tauri/src/accounts/mod.rs:767 'the adapters then fall back to the API-key provider' after an account error; present.ts rate-limit copy says 'Bluey uses your API key meanwhile'
- **Proposed solution:** Add a muted one-line provenance under the actions row or in a tooltip (for example 'Gemini 2.5 Flash · 1.4 s'), plus a small 'via API key' marker when the account fallback is in force. Keep it quiet (fg-subtle, only after done).
- **Test plan:** response-view test: metrics.model renders in the actions tooltip; the account-fallback flag shows the marker.
- **Verification (confirmed):** rg 'metrics' src/features/hud finds nothing, so provider and model are never shown in the HUD. This is an improvement, not a defect.
- _Source: auditor (hud-ux/answer-provenance)_

### Sessions, history & data lifecycle

#### DATA-002

**Crash, force-quit or update relaunch leaves a 'Live' zombie session that absorbs later listening forever**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-G: SessionManager::load (src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs) no longer restores an active or paused session. SessionRepository::end_interrupted (repositories/sessions.rs) marks it completed at its last event, segment or response time and adds a new session_recovered timeline event (bluey-core SessionEventType plus TS type). If history is off, the session is deleted instead. The dead restore path in app/mod.rs is removed. Device check: Force-quit mid-session, relaunch and start listening: this should create a new session.

- **User impact:** After relaunching into an update (auto-installed builds), or after a crash or force-quit during listening, the HUD shows a session as live with an ever-growing duration. Every later listening run, possibly days later, is appended to it, merging unrelated meetings into one session, summary and export. The session is never deleted even with history off, and the history row cannot be deleted from the list because it is 'Live'.
- **Root cause:** Session liveness is persisted, but the 'auto-started by listening' fact is not. Restore trusts the database status, and the update relaunch bypasses the graceful shutdown hook.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:170-181 SessionManager::load restores get_active (sessions.rs:212-218 `status IN ('active','paused')`) and marks it as the current session; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:315-319 a new listening run reuses the restored session (auto_session stays false); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:584-588 stop() ends the session only when auto_session is set, so the restored session is never ended; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:104-106,412-420 shutdown() (which ends the auto session) runs only on RunEvent::Exit; src-tauri/src/commands/updates.rs:28-31 `pub fn updates_relaunch` is a sync command (runs on the main thread in Tauri v2) → updates/mod.rs:320 `self.app.restart()`; tauri-2.11.5 app.rs:582-592: on the main thread 'we cannot guarantee the delivery of those events, so we skip them'; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/retention.rs:204-229 retention prunes only status='completed', so the zombie also escapes 'session history off'
- **Proposed solution:** (1) In SessionManager::load, end any restored active or paused session: status=completed, with ended_at set to its last event, segment or response time, or now. Add a 'session_recovered' timeline event, and delete it if store_session_history is off. Alternatively persist an `auto` flag and end auto sessions at boot. (2) Make updates_relaunch `async`, or call `request_restart()` after awaiting `app::shutdown`.
- **Test plan:** Rust storage/app test: insert an active session row, construct SessionManager::load, assert the status is completed and active() is None. Unit test: after load, AudioManager::start creates a new session. Real-Mac check: start listening, click 'Relaunch' on a pending update or kill -9, relaunch, and confirm the HUD session menu shows 'No active session'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** sessions/mod.rs:41 restores get_active at load. audio/mod.rs:315-319 reuses it with auto_session=false, so stop() (L584) never ends it. updates_relaunch is a sync command (commands/updates.rs:29), and tauri-2.11.5 app.rs:585-592 restart() on the main thread skips RunEvent::Exit, so shutdown() at app/mod.rs:104-106 never runs. Scope correction: 'forever' overstates it. The user can end the session by hand from the HUD SessionMenu (session-actions.ts:45), so it lasts until the user notices. Until then every listening run is merged into the stale session, and retention skips it because it is not 'completed'. Crash while listening is the realistic trigger. The update relaunch needs a Ready update and a user click.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/zombie-active-session-after-restart)_

#### DATA-003

**Answers asked outside a session are stored with session_id NULL and survive every deletion and retention path except Reset all**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: Answers asked outside a session (session_id NULL) and their requests are now removed by delete_all, by ending a session with history off, and by retention pruning. This is repositories/responses.rs delete_sessionless, called from sessions.rs and retention.rs; FTS rows go with them through the triggers. commands/responses.rs no longer saves a sessionless answer while history is off. The mock mirrors both. Deleting a single session does not touch sessionless rows, because they do not belong to that session.

- **User impact:** Every Cmd+Enter or screen answer given while not listening (a very common path) keeps the typed prompt and the answer, which often contains interview or coding content, in bluey.db indefinitely. The rows are invisible in the UI, are not removed by 'Delete all sessions' or 'session history off', and the Privacy stats keep counting them.
- **Root cause:** Persistence assumes every answer belongs to a session, while deletion and retention reach answers only through the sessions cascade.
- **Evidence:** src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:45-53 `session: sessionState.active` is null unless listening or a manual session is running; src/ai/engine.ts:419-428 `sessionId: input.session?.id` → engine.ts:566-569 `await api.responses.save({ response })` (no privacy gate); src-tauri/src/commands/responses.rs:11-18 responses_save has no store_session_history check; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/sessions.rs:376 delete_all = `DELETE FROM sessions` (cascade reaches only rows with a session_id); src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/retention.rs:204-229 retention prunes only completed sessions; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/search.rs:73,92 search excludes `session_id != ''`; ResponseRepository::list filters by session (responses.rs:64-92), so these rows are never shown; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:104-107 'Delete all sessions — Sessions, timelines, responses and summaries.'
- **Proposed solution:** (a) In SessionRepository::delete_all and prune_completed_sessions, also run `DELETE FROM ai_responses WHERE session_id IS NULL` and the same for ai_requests; response_feedback and FTS follow by cascade and triggers. (b) In responses_save, skip the insert when session_id is None and privacy.store_session_history is false. (c) Product: either show sessionless answers as 'Quick asks' history or stop persisting them.
- **Test plan:** Storage test: save a response with session_id None, run delete_all_sessions, and assert the ai_responses and responses_fts counts are 0. Storage test: apply_retention with store_session_history=false removes NULL-session rows. Command-level test: responses_save with history off does not insert.
- **Verification (confirmed):** responses_save (commands/responses.rs:11-18) has no privacy gate. ai_responses.session_id is nullable with ON DELETE CASCADE (0001_init.sql:189). delete_all (sessions.rs:376) and prune_completed_sessions (retention.rs:207-229) only delete sessions rows, so session_id NULL answers (with prompts and content) survive 'Delete all sessions' and 'session history off'. Only reset_all removes them. ResponseRepository::list (responses.rs:64-80) filters by session_id, so these rows are never shown either. This breaks the privacy promise in PrivacyTab. ai_requests (responses.rs:222) is presumably in the same position.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/sessionless-responses-never-deleted)_

#### DATA-006

**Deleting the live session publishes no event; the HUD keeps a deleted session id and later answers are silently not saved**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: Deleting the live session, on its own or through delete-all, now publishes session.ended so the HUD drops it (SessionManager announce_deleted_active). audio/mod.rs forget_session resets the auto-session binding. SessionDetail disables Delete for a session that has not ended, and the mock emits the same event.

- **User impact:** After deleting the current session, every HUD answer and timeline event fails its foreign-key check and is silently dropped until the HUD reloads. 'End session' shows an error toast. If audio was listening, its transcript stops being saved (session_id None), and stopping logs a failed end().
- **Root cause:** The delete paths change backend active-session state without notifying the webviews, and the storage failure is hidden by the engine's best-effort persistence.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/SessionDetail.tsx:463-476 the Delete confirm calls bluey.session.delete with no status guard (the list row guards it at SessionsTab.tsx:237; DESIGN.md:133-134 claims 'the live session cannot be deleted'); src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:104-112 'Delete all sessions' → sessions_delete_all; src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:256-279 delete/delete_all set active=None and hub SessionChanged but publish no BlueyEvent::SessionEnded; `src/stores/initStores.ts:46-50 useSessionStore changes only on session.* events`; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:45-53 and src/stores/proactive.ts:155-172 pass the stale `sessionState.active` into the engine; src/ai/engine.ts:566-583 responses.save and session.addEvent failures are swallowed (`catch {}`); PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON (db.rs:95-100) makes the insert fail
- **Proposed solution:** In SessionManager::delete/delete_all, when the active session is removed, publish SessionEnded (or a new session.deleted mapped to setActive(null)). Also stop or reset the audio auto_session, or refuse (invalid_params) to delete the active session and make delete_all skip it. Gate the detail view's Delete on status, matching the list.
- **Test plan:** Rust test: SessionManager::delete on the active id publishes session.ended on the EventBus (boundary through the real event name mapping). Vitest: SessionDetail disables Delete for status active. UI test with the fake transport: after the session.ended event, useSessionStore.active is null.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed mechanics. sessions/mod.rs:256-279 changes only hub state (the hub SessionChanged transition) and publishes no BlueyEvent. initStores.ts:46-49 updates the frontend store only on session.* events. SessionDetail.tsx:463-476 has no status guard, while SessionsTab.tsx:237 disables the list delete. engine.ts:568-583 swallows the FK failure. Extra effect: audio auto_session stays true, and live transcript finals get session_id=None (L754), so they are silently not persisted for the rest of the run. Downgraded because it takes a deliberate delete of the live session from the detail view.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/delete-active-session-stale-hud)_

#### DATA-007

**Prepared answers shown with Cmd+Shift+Enter (and cached answers opened live) are never saved to the session**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E1: engine.commitShown(response, session) persists a prepared answer shown with ⌘⇧↵ and logs response_generated. A cached answer opened live is persisted the same way (src/ai/engine.ts persistShown).

- **User impact:** With proactive preparation on, the answers the user actually saw during a meeting or interview are missing from session history, search, export, the summary input and the Rust session context.
- **Root cause:** Saving is tied to the non-silent pipeline, not to the moment a response is shown.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/engine.ts:564-569 `if (opts.silent) response.prepared = true;` … `if (!opts.silent) { await api.responses.save(...) }`; src/ai/engine.ts:670-674 when a cached prepared answer is taken live: `callbacks.onComplete?.(cached.response)`, no save; `src/ai/engine.ts:721-742 takePrepared only deletes from the in-memory map`; src/features/hud/useAsk.ts:89-108 generateOrTakePrepared → chatStore.showResponse (chatStore.ts:150-168), no save or addEvent; src/ai/engine.ts:180 'Prepared for later: no persistence, no session events'; :567 `if (!opts.silent)` guards the save; `src/stores/chatStore.ts:150-169 showResponse appends the turn and never saves`; `rg: the only responses.save call in src is engine.ts:569`; `src/sessions/timeline.ts:28 has a 'Response prepared' verb that no code path can produce`
- **Proposed solution:** Add an engine method `commitShown(response, session)`, called from takePrepared consumers and the cached-live branch, that saves the response with prepared=false and adds the response_generated event, reusing the existing best-effort block in runPipeline.
- **Test plan:** Vitest engine test with a fake StreamApi: prepare (silent) then takePrepared plus commitShown calls api.responses.save exactly once with prepared false. Boundary test through the fake transport's responses_save handler.
- **Verification (confirmed):** engine.ts:567 saves only when !silent. The cached-live branch at L670-674 calls onComplete without saving, which contradicts its own doc comment at L660 ('persisted like any shown answer'). takePrepared (L721-742) and useAsk.ts:89-108 only show the response. Answers the user saw are missing from session history and export.
- **Also reported as:** live-suggestions/prepared-suggestion-not-persisted
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/prepared-responses-not-persisted)_

#### DATA-008

**Live transcript timestamps restart at 0 on each listening run, scrambling order in multi-run sessions**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: When a run attaches to a session that already has a transcript, its live segments are offset by that session's last end time (from stored segments, or from the ring when transcripts are not persisted) via on_session_timeline. The auto-started session is bound to the id it created, so stop never ends a session the user switched to. A helper-initiated stop (device lost, stream error) now ends the auto session too (src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs).

- **User impact:** A manual session (HUD 'Start session') with listening stopped and restarted, or a restored session, shows interleaved transcript lines in Session detail and export. The 'most recent 300' window picks the wrong lines, and the summary's keep-the-tail logic keeps the wrong part.
- **Root cause:** Each run of the helper audio session uses its own time base, and the storage layer orders by that per-run time.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/types/transcript.rs:25 start_time = 'Milliseconds since audio session start'; SpeechTranscriber.swift:10 'relative to audio.start'; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:531-544 live segments take chunk.start_ms directly; no per-session offset; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/transcript.rs:176 `ORDER BY start_time_ms DESC, id DESC LIMIT ?3`; src-tauri/src/transcription/batch.rs:68-92 imports already offset by TranscriptRepository::last_end_ms (the live path does not); src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/session.rs:170-181 export prints mmss(start_time); summary.ts:55-62 joins segments in this order
- **Proposed solution:** When AudioManager::start attaches to an existing session, read TranscriptRepository::last_end_ms(session) and add it as an offset to every live segment's start and end (the same approach as batch.rs). Alternatively order by created_at, start_time_ms.
- **Test plan:** Storage test: two runs inserted with offsets list in chronological order. Rust unit test: AudioManager with a mock helper, start, final, stop, start, final; the second segment's start_time is greater than the first's end_time.
- **Depends on:** transcript-ring-cross-session-bleed
- **Verification (confirmed):** Live segments use chunk start_ms directly (audio/mod.rs:531-544). Storage orders by start_time_ms (transcript.rs:177, 221). batch.rs:69-76 offsets imports by last_end_ms but the live path does not. It shows up in sessions with more than one listening run: a manual session with stop/start, the zombie reuse above, or pause/resume if the helper restarts its clock. The segments interleave in the timeline, export and summary input.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/transcript-timebase-resets-per-run)_

#### AI-007

**Post-session summary silently ignores the first part of sessions longer than ~25-30 minutes**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: src/sessions/summary.ts keeps the start (a third of the budget) and the end of long transcripts. It marks the dropped middle with its time range in the prompt and adds a note to the stored overview, so the detail view and the export tell the user which minutes were left out.

- **User impact:** For a typical 45-60 minute meeting, lecture or interview, the overview, decisions and study guide cover only roughly the last half hour, with no indication to the user.
- **Root cause:** A single-pass summary with a fixed tail budget and no chunked (map-reduce) pass over earlier transcript.
- **Evidence:** `src/sessions/summary.ts:48 `const MAX_TRANSCRIPT_TOKENS = 6000;``; `src/sessions/summary.ts:55-62 `compressKeepTail(transcriptLines, MAX_TRANSCRIPT_TOKENS)``; `src/context/budget.ts:52-70 compressKeepTail keeps only the last lines within budget`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/router.rs:11-13,186-198 summarization goes to the fast role unless context > 24k tokens, which the 6k cap makes unreachable
- **Proposed solution:** Size the budget from the assigned model's context window, or summarize earlier chunks first (map) and feed those digests plus the tail (reduce). At minimum keep head and tail with a marker, and state truncation in the summary.
- **Test plan:** Vitest: renderSummaryInput with a synthetic 60-minute transcript containing a unique first-minute marker; assert the marker (or its chunk digest) reaches the request. Router test for escalation when context is large.
- **Verification (confirmed):** summary.ts:49 MAX_TRANSCRIPT_TOKENS=6000 with compressKeepTail (budget.ts:52-70) keeps only the tail, and a truncation marker is present. At roughly 150 words/min, anything before the last ~25-30 minutes is dropped from the summary. The model sees the marker, but the user is not told.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/summary-tail-only-transcript)_

#### UX-016

**Mode-specific summary sections (Study guide, Interview debrief, Deal notes…) and 'answers' are generated and stored but never shown or exported**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: SessionDetail.tsx now shows the summary's answers, improvements and each mode section (Study guide, Interview debrief and so on). bluey-core session.rs export_markdown includes answers, improvements and the sections.

- **User impact:** The per-mode value of summaries is invisible: a lecture session never shows its study guide and an interview never shows its debrief, even though tokens were spent generating them.
- **Root cause:** The UI and the Markdown exporter were built for the base summary fields only.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/prompts/summary.ts:16-34 the prompt asks for `answers` and mode sections ('Study guide', 'Technical review', 'Deal notes', 'Interview debrief'); src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/sessions.rs:568 sections are saved in summary_json; src/features/settings/SessionDetail.tsx ~L435-445 renders Topics/Questions/Decisions/Action items/Open items/Improvements only (rg 'sections|answers' in the file: no matches); src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/session.rs:152-161 export omits answers, improvements and sections; docs/MODE_SYSTEM.md:60 'the post-session summary is structured per mode (e.g. lecture study guide…)'
- **Proposed solution:** Render summary.answers and summary.sections (title + content) in SessionDetail, and add answers, improvements and sections to export_markdown.
- **Test plan:** UI test (fake transport) with a summary containing sections shows the section title. Rust unit test: export_markdown includes '#### Study guide'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** SessionDetail.tsx renders only the base lists (Improvements at L440). There are no sections or answers renderers. Rust export_markdown (bluey-core/session.rs:152-161) omits answers, improvements and sections. The only TS code that renders them (src/sessions/export.ts:37-42) has no importers.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/summary-sections-not-rendered)_

#### UX-017

**Session history shows at most 50 sessions and has no way to page further**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: SessionsTab.tsx requests explicit 50-row pages (limit/offset), adds a 'Load more' button that appends the next page, and shows 'Showing N of M sessions'. M comes from data_usage_stats and is shown only when no filter is set. With a filter it shows 'Showing N sessions'. The mock sessions_search now pages and sorts newest first, like Rust.

- **User impact:** Heavy users lose browse access to older sessions without any hint; 'Any time' misleadingly suggests the full history.
- **Root cause:** The backend supports limit and offset, but the UI never pages.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/SessionsTab.tsx:55-63 search() sends text/modeId/from only (no limit or offset); src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/sessions.rs:19 `DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT: u32 = 50`, L291; `src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/search.rs:136-138 `unwrap_or(50)``
- **Proposed solution:** Add 'Load more' or infinite scroll that passes offset (page size 50), plus a total count.
- **Test plan:** UI test with the fake transport returning 50 then 10 items: 'Load more' requests offset 50 and renders 60.
- **Verification (confirmed):** SessionsTab.tsx:55-63 sends no limit or offset, search.rs:137-138 defaults to 50, and the tab has no load-more control. Older sessions can be reached only through the text or date filters.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/history-capped-50-no-pagination)_

#### DEBT-006

**auto_session flag outlives the session it marks: stopping listening can end a user's later manual session**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — D1 (DATA-008): the auto-started session is bound to the id it created, so stopping listening never ends a session the user switched to.

- **Root cause:** auto_session is a bare AtomicBool, not the id of the auto-started session, so it no longer matches reality once the user ends that session and starts another.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:315-318 sets auto_session=true when listening auto-starts a session; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:584-588 stop() calls sessions.end() on whatever session is active if the flag is set; src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:189-208 end() works on the currently active session, and nothing resets AudioManager.auto_session; src/features/hud/SessionMenu.tsx:59-69 offers 'End session' and, once no session is active, 'Start session' while audio keeps running (session-actions.ts:45-47 ends only the session, not audio)
- **Proposed solution:** Store Option<session_id> for the auto-started session. In stop(), end the active session only if its id matches. Clear the value when SessionManager ends or deletes that session (subscribe to SessionEnded or call the audio manager from end() and delete()).
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (data-sessions-history/auto-session-flag-not-bound-to-session)_

#### DEBT-007

**Device loss or a helper-side stop leaves the auto-started session Live, and the next listening run merges into it**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — D1 (DATA-008): a helper-initiated stop (device lost, stream error) ends the auto-started session.

- **Root cause:** Ending the auto session happens only in the user-initiated stop() path, not in the shared mark_stopped path.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:808-827 the AudioStopped handler (device_lost or another reason) calls close_stt() and mark_stopped(error) but not the auto-session end in stop() (L584-588); src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:315 the next start() finds sessions.active() is Some and reuses the stale session; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/retention.rs:207-229 retention and the history-off prune only touch 'completed' sessions
- **Proposed solution:** Move the auto-session end into a helper called from both stop() and the AudioStopped handler, or end the session when state becomes Stopped/Error with the auto flag set.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (data-sessions-history/helper-initiated-audio-stop-leaves-auto-session-live)_

#### DATA-010

**Session and document deletions do not VACUUM or secure-delete, contrary to SECURITY.md; deleted text stays recoverable in the database file and WAL**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: db.rs Database::configure turns on PRAGMA secure_delete, and FTS5 secure-delete is enabled after migrations. Every deletion and retention pass ends with wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE), in retention.rs, commands/data.rs (plus VACUUM on bulk resets) and documents/mod.rs. SECURITY.md now describes what deletion actually does.

- **User impact:** Deleted sessions (transcripts, answers), resumes and job descriptions stay readable in free pages and the WAL of bluey.db until they are overwritten.
- **Root cause:** Vacuum was added per command, not centrally, and the doc was written for the intended design.
- **Evidence:** docs/SECURITY.md:147-151 '`sessions_delete(_all)`, `documents_delete(_all)` … remove rows and the files …, then `VACUUM`'; src-tauri/src/commands/data.rs:28,35,116 vacuum only in data_delete_screenshots, data_clear_transcripts and data_reset_all; data_clear_ai_cache (L40-42) has none; src-tauri/src/commands/sessions.rs:65-72 and src-tauri/src/commands/documents.rs:39-49 have no vacuum; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/db.rs:95-100 no `PRAGMA secure_delete`; bundled libsqlite3-sys 0.38.2 build.rs does not set SQLITE_SECURE_DELETE; Database::checkpoint is unused; docs/SECURITY.md 'Data deletion': sessions_delete(_all), documents_delete(_all) … 'then VACUUM'; src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:256-279 and src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:106-133 — no vacuum; commands/sessions.rs:65-72 thin wrappers; data_clear_ai_cache (commands/data.rs:40-42) no vacuum; crates/bluey-storage/src/db.rs:90-99 PRAGMAs (WAL, no secure_delete); 178-180 vacuum() = VACUUM only; checkpoint() (183-188) used only in tests; transcript_fts/document_chunks_fts are standalone FTS5 tables (migrations 0001:136-141, 0002 triggers); deletes leave tokens in %_data until merge; commands/data.rs:53-118 reset: no step for paths.logs_dir or paths.frames_dir; accounts/mod.rs:848-883 reset_all deletes Keychain tokens without profile.revoke (disconnect does at 543); `Local evidence: bluey.db 610 KB vs bluey.db-wal 4.1 MB`; docs/SECURITY.md:148-151: sessions_delete(_all), documents_delete(_all) ... remove rows and the files they reference (frame cache), then VACUUM; src-tauri/src/sessions/mod.rs:256-279: delete_session + Storage::remove_files(referenced paths) only; no vacuum; `VACUUM calls exist only in src-tauri/src/commands/data.rs:28,35,116,173`
- **Proposed solution:** Set `PRAGMA secure_delete = ON` in Database::configure (zeroes freed content at low cost for this workload), and run wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE) after bulk deletes. Alternatively call the existing vacuum() helper from sessions_delete(_all), documents_delete(_all) and data_clear_ai_cache. Update SECURITY.md to match.
- **Test plan:** Storage test with a file-backed tempdir database: insert a transcript with a unique marker, delete the session, checkpoint, and read the raw database bytes; assert the marker is absent.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The facts are right. SECURITY.md:147-150 promises VACUUM for sessions_delete(_all), documents_delete(_all) and data_clear_ai_cache. Only data.rs:28, 35 and 116 vacuum, and there is no secure_delete (the only PRAGMA hits are in db.rs). Downgraded because it only matters for forensic recovery by someone who already has access to the user's files, who could read live data anyway. Still fix the doc or the code.
- **Also reported as:** security-privacy/deletion-incomplete-vacuum-wal, security-privacy/security-doc-deletion-claims-drift
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/deletes-skip-vacuum-doc-mismatch)_

#### DATA-011

**Retrieval compares vectors from different embedding models when their dimensions match**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** While a re-embed is in flight, or after it fails (offline, rate limits), semantic scores for resume and job-description chunks are meaningless, for example switching between two 1536-dimension models, so the wrong context is injected into answers.
- **Root cause:** Space identity is recorded per document but not enforced at query time.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/migrations/0003_embedding_model.sql:3-8 'Vectors from different models … must never be compared'; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/documents/retrieve.rs:250-254 skips only when `chunk.embedding.len() != query_embedding.len()`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/repositories/documents.rs:418-445 chunks_with_embeddings has no embedding_model filter; src-tauri/src/documents/mod.rs:278-283 per-document re-embed failures are only logged; retried at the next boot or settings change
- **Proposed solution:** Pass the current tag and dimensions into semantic_candidates/chunks_with_embeddings and add `AND d.embedding_model = ?` (plus dimensions when set). Documents that are still stale fall back to keyword retrieval.
- **Test plan:** Storage test: document A marked with tag 'p/m1' and 4-dimension vectors, query with tag 'p/m2' and a 4-dimension vector; A is not among the semantic candidates, while keyword retrieval still returns it.
- **Verification (adjusted):** retrieve.rs:250-254 guards only on length, and chunks_with_embeddings (documents.rs:436-440) has no embedding_model filter. It only matters after a provider or model switch to a same-dimension model while re-embedding is pending or has failed. The result is noisy similarity scores that keyword retrieval partly absorbs. There is no data loss, so it is downgraded.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/embeddings-cross-model-same-dim)_

#### UX-029

**History gaps: notes cannot be deleted, notes and summaries are not searchable, search snippets are hidden, and the export prints the mode id and UTC times**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Minor friction when browsing and sharing history.
- **Root cause:** Incremental UI coverage of backend capabilities.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tauri/api.ts:159 deleteNote is wired, but SessionDetail.tsx has no delete control for notes; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/search.rs:48-106 searches titles, transcript_fts and responses_fts only; `src/lib/types/session.ts:86-87 snippet exists; SessionsTab.tsx:190-229 never renders it`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/session.rs:102 `- Mode: {session.mode_id}`; L207-212 clock() renders UTC; `src/sessions/export.ts and src/sessions/timeline.ts have no importers (dead code)`
- **Proposed solution:** Add a note delete button; add notes and summary text to search (LIKE or new FTS); render snippet under the row; export the mode name and local times; delete the dead TS composers.
- **Test plan:** UI tests for the note delete and snippet rendering; a Rust export test for the mode name.
- **Verification (confirmed):** deleteNote (api.ts:159) has no UI caller. There is no snippet rendering in SessionsTab. Export prints mode_id (session.rs:102), and clock() (L206-211) formats in the stored offset (UTC when stored with Z). src/sessions/export.ts and timeline.ts have no importers.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/export-and-history-polish)_

#### DOC-006

**Session-scoped documents are supported end to end in the backend and retrieval, but nothing can create them; the ContextTab comment says otherwise**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The ContextTab comment no longer claims sessions have their own files (commit 5612c30).

- **User impact:** You can't attach a JD or meeting brief to just one session; if added later, deleting a session would leave its files behind.
- **Root cause:** The feature is only half-built.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/ContextTab.tsx:86-87 'mode- and session-scoped files live in Modes → Files and the session itself'; FilesDropzone is used only by ContextTab (scope global) and ModeFilesDropzone.tsx:10 (scope mode); `src-tauri/src/context/mod.rs:268 and src/context/retrieval.ts:108 query the session scope`; documents.scope_id has no FK (0001_init.sql:66-67), so session delete would orphan them if ever created
- **Proposed solution:** Either add a Files section to SessionDetail/HUD (and delete session documents in SessionRepository::delete), or remove the session scope and the misleading comment.
- **Test plan:** If built: storage test that session delete removes its session-scoped documents.
- **Verification (confirmed):** FilesDropzone is used only with scope global (ContextTab.tsx:154) and scope mode (ModeFilesDropzone.tsx:10). DocumentScope::Session appears in the backend only in readers and tests (index.rs:260 and documents.rs:688 are tests). The ContextTab.tsx:84-87 comment is misleading.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/session-scoped-docs-unreachable)_

#### DATA-012

**If the database cannot open or migrate, bootstrap fails and the app aborts with no backup and no user-facing recovery**  
Severity **Opportunity** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — D2 (CRIT-003): pending migrations back the database up first (VACUUM INTO) and a boot failure shows a native dialog with the log path and Reveal Data Folder / Quit.

- **User impact:** On a corrupt database, a full disk or a future migration bug, the menu-bar app would crash on launch every time, with no message and no way to export or reset.
- **Root cause:** Storage is a hard dependency of bootstrap, with no degraded mode.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/storage/mod.rs:71-77 Storage::open → Database::open → run_migrations with `?`; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:80-94 a bootstrap error is returned from setup, then `.build(...).expect("error while building the Bluey application")`; src-tauri/crates/bluey-storage/src/db.rs:108-143 migrations are transactional, but there is no pre-migration copy of bluey.db
- **Proposed solution:** Copy bluey.db to bluey.db.bak-<version> before applying pending migrations. On open failure, show a native dialog offering 'Move database aside and start fresh' or 'Quit', and log the path.
- **Test plan:** Rust test: Database::open on a garbage file returns a storage error (not a panic); an app-level test for the fallback path; real-Mac check with a corrupted file under BLUEY_DATA_DIR.
- **Verification (confirmed):** storage/mod.rs:70-76 lets the error propagate with `?`, setup returns the error (app/mod.rs:80-84), and .build().expect() (L95) panics. There is no pre-migration backup and no user-facing recovery. Migrations are transactional, so the realistic trigger is corruption or a bad future migration.
- _Source: auditor (data-sessions-history/db-open-failure-no-recovery)_

### Research & deep-research sidecar

#### LIVE-005

**'Skip research' leaves no final event when a tool call is in flight: the ask stalls until 90 s and a stale 'Skipping research…' status sticks to later asks**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-R: The job table moved into src-tauri/src/agent/jobs.rs behind AgentProcess/DocumentSource seams. AgentJobs::cancel now publishes failed{cancelled} and kills the process if the job still exists after the 2 s grace. In the sidecar (sidecars/agent/src/agent.ts), cancel() emits the terminal cancelled event at once, and the job's abort signal reaches in-flight Exa/Firecrawl fetches (tools/errors.ts postJson uses AbortSignal.any). The researchStore skip ends local search jobs at once, so a stale 'Skipping research…' can no longer stick. Device check: Click Skip during a real deep job in the app. Confirm the HUD clears within about 2 s and the next ask shows no stale status.

- **User impact:** The user presses Skip because research is slow, and nothing happens. The answer stays blocked for the rest of the 90 s window. After that, every later ask's streaming placeholder and the status pill keep showing 'Researching · Skipping research…' with a disabled button, until some future deep-research job starts. Firecrawl scrapes (often 3-15 s) and Claude/Gemini turns routinely take longer than 2 s, so this is the common case, not a corner case.
- **Root cause:** Rust's cancel grace path removes the job and kills the child without sending a final event, and the sidecar only answers a cancel after the in-flight tool finishes because its tool fetches never see the job's abort signal. The researchStore also has no fallback that clears it when the ask ends.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/agent/mod.rs:456-476 cancel(): writes research.cancel, then after CANCEL_GRACE (2 s, :38) `if let Some(job) = jobs.lock().remove(&id) { child.kill() }` — no publish_failed; src-tauri/src/agent/mod.rs:290-301 Terminated only publishes `agent_exited` `if this.jobs.lock().remove(&job_id).is_some()` — the job is already gone; sidecars/agent/src/agent.ts:825-830 cancel() only sets cancelRequested + abortController.abort(); failed{cancelled} is emitted after the run unwinds; sidecars/agent/src/tools/errors.ts:69 fetch uses only `AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs)` (exa 20 s exa.ts:11, firecrawl 45 s firecrawl.ts:11); handlers get no job signal; Measured: /tmp/bluey-audit/research-sidecar/cancel-inflight.ts — cancel sent at 488 ms, research.failed(cancelled) at 4190 ms with a 4 s mock tool; src/ai/research.ts:270-287 runDeepAgent resolves only on completed/failed or the 90 s timer; src/stores/researchStore.ts:81-92 skip() sets cancelling:true, message 'Skipping research…'; active is cleared only by completed/failed (:74-77); clear() is only called on teardown (initStores.ts:101); src/features/hud/ResponseThread.tsx:20-40 shows 'Researching · {message}' with a disabled Skip whenever research.active is set; state-pill.ts:46 shows researching while busy
- **Proposed solution:** 1) agent/mod.rs cancel(): in the grace task, if the job is still present, call `this.publish_failed(&id, BlueyError::cancelled(...))` (same shape the sidecar would send) before killing. 2) sidecar agent.ts cancel(): call emitCancelled() immediately (guarded by `finished`), then abort. Also thread `abortController.signal` into exa.search/firecrawl.scrape (AbortSignal.any([signal, AbortSignal.timeout(ms)]) in tools/errors.ts fetchJson). 3) runDeepAgent: after deepCancel resolves true, finish(null) right away rather than waiting for the event. 4) Clear researchStore.active for the job when the owning ask finishes or is cancelled (chatStore end-of-ask hook).
- **Test plan:** Rust boundary test: spawn AgentManager against a fake sidecar (shell script that reads stdin and ignores research.cancel) and assert a DeepResearchEvent::Failed{cancelled} is published within CANCEL_GRACE+ε. Sidecar test (tests/sidecar/sidecar.test.ts): mock exa handler that sleeps 5 s; send research.cancel and assert research.failed within 500 ms and that the fetch was aborted. TS: researchStore test that a skip followed by no event is cleared when the ask ends; engine test that Skip resolves the ask in under 1 s with a bus that never emits.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The grace task in agent/mod.rs:456-476 cancel() removes the job and kills the child without calling publish_failed. The Terminated branch at 290-301 publishes only when the job is still in the map, so no terminal event is sent. The watchdog at agent/mod.rs:407-440 does publish_failed('timeout') after the same remove-then-kill steps, which shows cancel() is the odd one out. Sidecar: gemini.ts:312 runs `await handler(args)` with no signal, and tools/errors.ts postJson uses only AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs). A Firecrawl scrape in flight (up to 45 s) therefore outlives the 2 s grace. runDeepAgent (research.ts:256-297) settles only on completed/failed or the 90 s timer. researchStore.skip leaves active set with cancelling:true. The stale 'Researching · Skipping research…' line shows again in the next ask's StreamingStatus (ResponseThread.tsx:20-40) until a new 'started' event replaces it or the app tears down (initStores.ts:101). The 90 s TS timeout path (research.ts:284-287) causes the same stale store, because it also cancels mid-tool. The proposed fix is right; the smallest core change is publish_failed(cancelled) in the grace task.
- _Source: auditor (research-sidecar/skip-research-stuck)_

#### AI-002

**search_scrape research is routinely dropped from the prompt by the 12k-token budget while its citations are still shown as Sources**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-R: ResearchOutcome now holds items: one for the snippets, one per scraped page (capped at 6k chars, with a 'Source: title (url)' header and a truncation marker), and the agent report capped at 12k. Each becomes its own budget item. keptResearchCitations filters citations down to the items that budget.included kept.

- **User impact:** For typical news or docs pages (often more than 14k characters of markdown), the answer model never sees the research. It answers from stale knowledge while the HUD shows a confident 'Sources' list, so users are misled into thinking the answer is grounded. It also pays the latency of up to 3 sequential scrapes (up to 45 s each in Rust) for nothing.
- **Root cause:** The research outcome is a single, uncapped, all-or-nothing context item, and citations are attached independently of what reached the prompt.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/research.ts:213-224 concatenates every result snippet plus the FULL markdown of up to 3 scraped pages into one context string (no per-page cap, no page URL/title header); src-tauri/src/research/mod.rs:94-122 Rust scrape returns unbounded markdown (the sidecar caps at 40k, firecrawl.ts:13; the Rust path has no cap); `src/ai/engine.ts:349-356 pushes it as a single item {source:'document', ref:'research'}`; src/context/budget.ts:142-163 items that do not fit whole are dropped unless transcript/OCR; `Default contextTokenBudget 12000 (bluey-core types/settings.rs ~287)`; Measured with the real budget code (/tmp/bluey-audit/research-sidecar/budget.audit.test.ts, budget-run.log): pageChars 6000/10000 → researchInPrompt=true; 14000/20000 → researchInPrompt=false, contextTokens=9, citations=8 still attached; engine.ts:250-266 mergeCitations always attaches research citations regardless of whether research survived budgeting
- **Proposed solution:** Cap each scraped page (e.g. ~6k chars, head plus truncation marker) and emit one context item per page and one for the snippets (each with a 'Source: title (url)' header), so the budget can keep the snippets plus the best pages. Alternatively, teach budget.ts to head-truncate ref:'research' items like OCR. Only attach citations whose item survived budgeting (have allocateBudget report which refs were kept). Scrape the top N in parallel (Promise.allSettled) with a shorter per-page timeout.
- **Test plan:** Engine-level test through the real budget (not mocked) with 3 pages of 20k chars: assert the prompt contains at least the snippets plus one truncated page, and that citations only include sources present in the prompt. Unit test for the per-page cap. Latency test: 3 scrapes each resolving after 1 s finish in about 1 s, not 3 s.
- **Verification (confirmed):** research.ts:213-224 joins all snippets and the full page markdown into one string. The Rust scrape returns uncapped markdown (bluey-protocols/src/firecrawl.rs:53-73). engine.ts:349-356 pushes it as a single source:'document' item, which gets priority 2 (budget.ts:23-35), after transcript/OCR/accessibility at priority 1. budget.ts:142-163 compresses only transcript/OCR items and drops everything else that does not fit. The default budget is 12_000 (settings.rs:287). mergeCitations (engine.ts:250-266) attaches citations regardless of what survived budgeting. I did not re-run the auditor's measurement, but it follows directly from the code: three ordinary pages (10k+ chars each) plus screen OCR exceed the remaining room. The proposed fix is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (research-sidecar/scraped-research-dropped-by-budget)_

#### UX-006

**HUD citation and markdown links cannot be opened: opener:allow-open-url is granted with no URL scope**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-R: The main, settings and onboarding capabilities now scope opener:allow-open-url to https://*, http://* and mailto:*. src/lib/utils/open-external.ts refuses other schemes and shows a toast on refusal or failure, using new CODE_COPY entries in present.ts. Device check: In the packaged app, click a citation and the About help/mailto links and confirm the browser or mail app opens.

- **User impact:** Clicking a Source in the HUD, a link in a markdown answer, or the About-tab help link does nothing, with no error. Research citations become unverifiable.
- **Root cause:** The capability grants the command but no URL scope. Plugin-opener denies everything that is not explicitly allowed.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/capabilities/main.json:19 (also settings.json:18, onboarding.json:14) grant only "opener:allow-open-url"; no opener:allow-default-urls or scope entry anywhere (rg over capabilities and tauri.conf.json); tauri-plugin-opener 2.5.5 (Cargo.lock:5263) permissions/autogenerated/commands/open_url.toml: allow-open-url 'Enables the open_url command without any pre-configured scope'; default.toml adds allow-default-urls (http/https/mailto/tel) only in opener:default; plugin src/commands.rs:22-39 builds the scope from command_scope + global_scope allows; src/scope.rs:117-123 is_url_allowed returns `self.allowed.iter().any(...)`, which is false for an empty allow list ⇒ Error::ForbiddenUrl; src/lib/utils/open-external.ts:4-8 calls openUrl; src/features/hud/ResponseView.tsx:41 and :181 call it via `void openExternal(...)`, so the rejection is swallowed; Rust-side app.opener().open_url (auth/mod.rs:237, accounts/mod.rs:388, permissions/mod.rs:119) bypasses the scope, which explains why OAuth and permission deep links still work; src-tauri/capabilities/main.json:19, settings.json:18, onboarding.json:14 — bare "opener:allow-open-url" (ACL: 'Enables the open_url command without any pre-configured scope'); opener:allow-default-urls not granted; ~/.cargo/registry/.../tauri-plugin-opener-2.5.5/src/commands.rs:36-40 → scope.rs:117-123 `self.allowed.iter().any(..)` is false with no allow entries → Error::ForbiddenUrl; src/lib/utils/open-external.ts:5-8 openUrl; src/features/hud/ResponseView.tsx:36-44 (markdown links) and 181 (citations); AboutTab.tsx:61,67; Rust-side opens (auth, accounts, permissions) use app.opener() directly and are unaffected
- **Proposed solution:** Add "opener:allow-default-urls" (http/https/mailto/tel) to main.json and settings.json (and onboarding.json if it opens links). Optionally validate the scheme in openExternal (http/https only) and surface failures with showErrorToast instead of `void`.
- **Test plan:** Add a unit test that parses src-tauri/capabilities/*.json and asserts every capability granting opener:allow-open-url also grants opener:allow-default-urls or an explicit scope. Manual: in the real app, click a citation and confirm the browser opens. Unit test openExternal rejects javascript:/file: URLs.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Upgraded to verified. Cargo.lock:5263-5264 pins tauri-plugin-opener 2.5.5.
