- **Test plan:** bluey-storage unit tests: stored JSON with appearance.theme='neon' keeps ai.providers and privacy intact; a provider entry with kind 'future_kind' is dropped while the others remain; the backup key is written when a fallback happens.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The scope is overstated. Provider configs (ModelConfigRepository) and shortcuts are stored and loaded separately (settings/mod.rs:35-41), so providers survive a blob fallback. What is lost is the sections inside the settings blob: general, appearance, audio, screen, models and privacy. That can include onboarding state and privacy choices, and the next settings_update makes the loss permanent. The per-section fallback plus backup fix is still right; drop the claim about providers.
- **Also reported as:** error-handling-stubs/settings-decode-fallback-resets-all
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/settings-load-all-or-nothing-fallback)_

#### FEATURE-003

**Raw-audio retention setting (never / until session end / N minutes) is persisted and shown but nothing implements it**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: Nothing records audio to disk, so PrivacyTab.tsx now shows raw audio as 'Never kept' with honest copy (a later version may keep recordings). The selector, the custom-window row and privacy-retention.ts are removed. The settings fields stay in the schema, and a stored 'custom' value no longer makes the tab claim recordings are kept. AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md is updated.

- **User impact:** Users who choose to keep raw audio (for example to review later) get nothing. The UI implies a capability and a retention policy that do not exist. It fails safe, since no audio is ever stored.
- **Root cause:** The setting and UI shipped ahead of the recording and retention implementation.
- **Evidence:** `bluey-core types/settings.rs:302-304 store_raw_audio / raw_audio_retention_minutes`; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:225 `config.retain_raw_audio = settings.privacy.store_raw_audio;` copies to AudioSessionConfig.retain_raw_audio (bluey-core types/transcript.rs:109), which nothing reads: not helper_start_params, not Swift, not storage; `raw_audio_retention_minutes: 0 Rust readers`; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:222-241 offers the options and the copy 'Raw audio is discarded after N minutes'; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:220-260 options plus 'Raw audio is discarded after N minutes'; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:225 only copies the value into the config; no Swift consumer (rg retainRawAudio in the helper: none); `docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:28-29 'accepted in settings but not implemented'`; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:220-235 offers 'Until session ends' / 'Custom window'; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:225 is the only consumer (assigns config.retain_raw_audio, never read by helper or Rust); bluey-core types/settings.rs:306 debug_log_transcripts has no reader; docs/SECURITY.md 'Logging' cites it; `AiCacheRepository set/get used only in tests (repositories/cache.rs:108-136)`; src/features/settings/tabs/PrivacyTab.tsx:220-258 offers 'Until session ends' / 'Custom window'; src-tauri/src/audio/mod.rs:225 only copies retain_raw_audio into config; no Rust/Swift consumer; docs/AUDIO_ARCHITECTURE.md:28-30 admits it; src/lib/tauri/bootstrap.ts:33 sets root.dataset.density; no CSS selector or TS reader of data-density; AppearanceTab.tsx:80-84 'Spacing of rows and responses.'; src/features/settings/tabs/AppearanceTab.tsx:157-163 'Move with the display that has focus'; only settings.rs:149,164 define it; settings.rs:306 debug_log_transcripts: no UI, no consumer; docs/SECURITY.md:141 references it as if it worked
- **Proposed solution:** Product decision. Either hide or remove the control and the fields (the smallest honest fix: mark it 'coming soon' and disable), or implement helper-side WAV writing plus a retention sweep in bluey-storage retention.rs.
- **Test plan:** If removed: a settings parity test confirming the field is gone on both sides. If implemented: a Rust unit test that the retention sweep deletes files older than N minutes, and a Swift test that audio.start with retainRawAudio writes a file.
- **Verification (adjusted):** It is true that nothing reads retain_raw_audio or raw_audio_retention_minutes. But raw audio is never written anywhere, so 'never' is honored in practice and nothing is kept longer than the user chose. The harm is only misleading options and copy ('until session end' / 'N minutes'), not a privacy or data failure. Downgraded to Low. The fix still stands: hide or disable the control.
- **Also reported as:** data-sessions-history/raw-audio-retention-inert-ui, security-privacy/dead-privacy-settings, prior-audit-docs-drift/dead-settings-no-consumer
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/raw-audio-retention-noop)_

#### TEST-015

**Mock transport defaults and validation diverge from Rust with no parity test, hiding real bugs**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — B2 (TEST-014) generates the built-in modes fixture from Rust and the mock loads it; several lanes made mock handlers follow Rust (secrets allow-list, ai_* supersede, modes, shortcuts, protection, retrieval). Settings defaults and provider presets are still hand-copied.

- **User impact:** UI, unit and e2e tests run against a backend that behaves differently from the shipped one, so bugs that depend on real defaults and validation pass CI. PR #18 was the same failure mode (mock green, real IPC broken).
- **Root cause:** The fixtures are hand-written copies of the Rust defaults and validation, with nothing tying them together.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tauri/mock/fixtures.ts:338 opacity 1 (Rust 0.92); :359 observationIntervalMs 5000 (Rust 1500, settings.rs:214); :361 ocrLevel accurate (Rust fast); :396 responseLength balanced (Rust concise); :398-400 research/deepResearch false, embeddings true (Rust true/true/false); :403 contextTokenBudget 24000 (Rust 12000); :404 bootstrapProvider gemini (Rust None); mock-transport.ts:1994 settings_update skips Rust validate() (settings/mod.rs:200 embeddingDimensions) and shortcut reconcile; :2002 secrets_set accepts any key (Rust validate_webview_key rejects); :1668 modes_delete throws code modes.built_in/configuration (Rust modes/mod.rs:129 invalid_params); fixtures.ts:145-149 toggle_listening 'CmdOrCtrl+Shift+Backslash' / 'Start or stop a Bluey session' vs Rust 'CmdOrCtrl+Shift+KeyL' / 'Start or stop listening'; mock accelerators unnormalized ('CmdOrCtrl+Up' vs 'CmdOrCtrl+ArrowUp'); Concrete hidden bug: the mock's observationIntervalMs 5000 matches a ScreenTab option, so the unselectable 1500 default (observation-interval-default-not-selectable) never shows in tests or web dev; `src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts:2002-2021 secrets_set/has/delete store any key`; src-tauri/src/secrets/mod.rs:61-74 validate_webview_key → invalid_params for auth:*, account:*, unknown keys
- **Proposed solution:** Generate the fixture defaults from Rust: add a `cargo test` that writes `Settings::default()` and `default_bindings()` JSON to tests/fixtures/rust-defaults.json, and have fixtures.ts import it, keeping only dev overrides such as developerMode and onboardingCompleted explicit. Port the cheap validations (secret allow-list, embeddingDimensions, built-in mode delete code) into the mock.
- **Test plan:** A vitest parity test that deep-compares createDefaultSettings() minus the documented overrides against rust-defaults.json, and compares shortcut ids/accelerators/labels. A Rust test that fails when the JSON snapshot is stale.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The value differences are confirmed (fixtures.ts vs settings.rs defaults: opacity, observationIntervalMs 5000 vs 1500, ocrLevel, responseLength, contextTokenBudget, toggle_listening accelerator). The drift only affects dev, web preview and tests, and it has no direct user impact. The one real bug it hides (the observation interval select) is tracked as its own finding. Downgraded to Low. The fix (fixtures generated from Rust) is still reasonable.
- **Also reported as:** security-privacy/mock-secrets-allowlist-parity
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/mock-transport-drift)_

#### UX-031

**Observation interval default (1.5 s) is not one of the select options; the UI shows 'Every 3 seconds' and there is no range validation**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The observation-interval select offers the actual default (commit 2a57bb9).

- **User impact:** After switching Observation to Smart, the UI says 3 s while the app samples every 1.5 s, and choosing '3 seconds' does nothing. A malformed patch can set 0 ms.
- **Root cause:** The option list was not derived from the default, and there is no bounds check on the Rust side.
- **Evidence:** bluey-core types/settings.rs:214 observation_interval_ms: 1500 (docs/CAPTURE_ARCHITECTURE.md also says 1.5 s); src/features/settings/tabs/ScreenTab.tsx:79-90 native Select options 3000/5000/10000/30000 only; a controlled <select> with an unmatched value renders the first option, and picking it fires no change; src-tauri/src/settings/mod.rs:200 validate() checks only embeddingDimensions, so any u32 including 0 reaches helper observe.start (capture/mod.rs ~359)
- **Proposed solution:** Add a { value: '1500', label: 'Every 1.5 seconds (default)' } option, or change the default to 3000. Clamp observationIntervalMs to 1000..=60000 in settings validate().
- **Test plan:** RTL: with screen.observation='smart' and the default settings, the select shows the 1.5 s label. Rust: validate rejects 0 and 10^9.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The ScreenTab options are 3000, 5000, 10000 and 30000, and the default is 1500 (settings.rs:214). Rust validate() has no bounds check. Impact is further reduced because observation defaults to Manual, so the control only matters after the user switches to Smart.
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/observation-interval-default-not-selectable)_

**appearance.followActiveDisplay has a toggle but no consumer**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The Follow active display toggle, which nothing honoured, is hidden (commit 85c36da).

- **User impact:** Toggling it changes nothing. Multi-monitor users expecting the HUD to follow focus do not get that behaviour.

- **Test plan:** If hidden: an RTL snapshot. If implemented: a Rust unit test for monitor selection given window frames, plus macOS manual verification with 2 displays.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The only references are the definition and default in settings.rs and the AppearanceTab toggle. There is no reader in Rust or TS.
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/follow-active-display-dead-setting)_

#### DEBT-011

**AI cache table is never written; 'Clear AI cache' clears nothing**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The Clear AI cache action, which cleared an always-empty table, is removed with its dead path on both sides (commit 13bc3b4).

#### DEBT-012

**bluey_core::budget (364 lines) is unused and diverges from the live TS allocator**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The unused bluey_core::budget module is deleted (commit a93eb2b).

- **User impact:** None directly. It invites someone to 'fix' the wrong allocator or to trust Rust tests that do not reflect runtime behaviour.
- **Root cause:** Policy moved to TS without deleting the Rust twin.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/budget.rs: allocate() :105, float priorities + max_share, compress_threshold .45 and min_compress_tokens 48 at :60-61; no `budget::` references in src-tauri/src or other crates; Live allocator src/context/budget.ts:22-37 uses integer tiers and minCompressedTokens 24; docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md:18 cites the TS one; src-tauri/crates/bluey-core/src/budget.rs (364 lines, `max_share` per source, score = priority×relevance) has no use in src-tauri/src (`rg budget::` = 0); the only runtime use of bluey-core context is trim_snapshot/apply_adapter (context/mod.rs:11, 217-218); `src/context/budget.ts: priority tiers, no per-source caps`; docs/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md:18-20 'Over budget → compress …, summarize, drop lowest score'; context/mod.rs:196 `earlier_summary: None`; src/transcript/window.ts summarizeOlder and dedupePartials have no callers (only recentSegments is used, proactive.ts:34); `snapshot.ts:102-103 EnrichSnapshotArgs.transcriptSegments is never passed by any caller`
- **Proposed solution:** Delete budget.rs (and its lib.rs mod) or gate it behind #[cfg(test)] with a doc note.
- **Test plan:** cargo build/test after removal (no callers).
- **Verification (confirmed):** There are no budget:: references outside the module. It is only exported via bluey-core lib.rs:24 (pub mod budget). The live allocator is src/context/budget.ts.
- **Also reported as:** context-engine/dead-rust-budget-and-doc-drift
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/rust-budget-dead-duplicate)_

#### DEBT-013

**34 registered commands have no caller; several events have no listener or no emitter**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** None directly (Tauri only evals events in webviews with listeners). It enlarges the IPC attack surface (see command-capabilities-not-scoped) and misleads contributors: for example, the HUD looks like it refocuses the input on panel.focusInput, but it never does.
- **Root cause:** The surface was designed up front (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md), and the implementation took native paths without pruning.
- **Evidence:** call_sites_v3.txt: uncalled = app_pause app_resume accounts_status capture_list_windows capture_read_frame capture_discard_frame capture_observe_start ocr_recognize accessibility_snapshot accessibility_frontmost_app audio_get_status transcript_recent transcript_clear ai_embed modes_get sessions_list sessions_delete_note sessions_get_summary responses_list responses_get responses_delete documents_get documents_get_text secrets_delete shortcuts_list panel_show panel_hide panel_toggle panel_move panel_set_position panel_resize panel_set_opacity panel_set_pinned panel_start_drag; panel.focusInput: listener at HudInputRow.tsx:21; bluey-core events/mod.rs:245 variant never constructed; nothing emits it; `activeApp.changed: only emitter accessibility/mod.rs:84 poll_active_app has 0 callers`; BlueyEvent::ResponsePrepared (events/mod.rs:167) never constructed (TS emits its own at engine.ts:709); ai.chunk is published on the bus (ai/mod.rs:451,503,534,550,651,662) in addition to the Channel, with no Rust or TS subscriber; helper.ping (HelperApp.swift:111) is never called by Rust; there is no liveness probe beyond per-request timeouts
- **Proposed solution:** Decide per item. Wire user-facing ones (secrets_delete, sessions_delete_note, responses_delete). Emit PanelFocusInput from the ask/capture shortcut after panel.show(), or delete the listener. Remove the panel_* commands, app_pause/resume, ai_embed, transcript_clear, capture_read_frame and similar if they are not planned. Drop the bus copy of AiChunk.
- **Test plan:** Add a vitest to command-surface.test.ts that fails when a CommandMap name has no non-mock call site unless it is in an explicit ALLOWED_UNUSED list, so the next orphan is caught.
- **Verification (confirmed):** I sampled 16 of the listed commands and found no callers, including checks for dynamic invoke strings. The only ai.embed/ai_embed hits are settings strings, not command calls. panel.focusInput has a listener at HudInputRow.tsx:21 but nothing emits it. poll_active_app has no callers. The user-facing items (secrets_delete, sessions_delete_note, responses_delete) overlap with secrets-delete-no-ui.
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/dead-command-and-event-surface)_

#### UX-033

**Microphone picker cannot restore 'follow system default' once a device is chosen**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-FX7: The microphone picker offers System default again; it sends null and Rust accepts null (commit 58802bd).

- **User impact:** Picking 'Default — MacBook Mic' pins that device, so plugging in AirPods later does not switch input and Bluey keeps listening on the old mic.
- **Root cause:** There is no sentinel option for None.
- **Evidence:** src/features/settings/tabs/AudioTab.tsx:105-110 value = microphoneDeviceId ?? default device id; options are concrete device ids only; onChange sends the id; bluey-core types/settings.rs:489-505 merge_json only clears a field on explicit null, which the UI never sends; docs/HELPER_PROTOCOL.md:150: the helper re-routes on default-input change only when no device is pinned
- **Proposed solution:** Add a 'System default' option (value '') that sends `{ microphoneDeviceId: null }`, and make the Rust patch accept null for Option fields (merge_json already inserts null; serde maps it to None).
- **Test plan:** RTL: selecting 'System default' sends a patch with null. Rust: settings update with null clears microphone_device_id.
- **Verification (confirmed):** The AudioTab select only offers concrete device ids and never sends null. merge_json only clears a field on an explicit null.
- _Source: auditor (surface-parity/mic-cannot-return-to-system-default)_

### Stubs, swallowed errors & unfinished states

#### CRIT-003

**A bootstrap failure (DB open/migration/settings) makes the app vanish with no dialog**  
Severity **High** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: A bootstrap failure (database open, migration, settings) no longer hits .expect under panic=abort. setup keeps the event loop alive to show one native dialog with the error, the log folder and Reveal Data Folder / Quit (app/mod.rs show_boot_failure, boot_failure_message). A build failure is logged and exits with code 1. Before pending migrations run on an existing database, Database::open_with_backup copies it to bluey.db.bak-<version> with VACUUM INTO; a failed backup is logged, not fatal. Follow-up in this session: Reset all data also deletes the bluey.db.bak-* copies (Storage::remove_db_backups), because they hold all the old data. Documented in docs/UPDATES.md. Device check: Force a boot failure (for example an unreadable bluey.db) in a release build and check that the dialog appears and Reveal / Quit work.

- **User impact:** If a migration fails after an auto-installed update, or the data dir is unwritable, corrupt or full, Bluey quits on every launch with no message. The in-app updater lives inside the crashing process, so a fixed build cannot be delivered, and the panic text goes to stderr, not the log file. For affected users the product is unusable.
- **Root cause:** Bootstrap errors travel through Tauri's setup and then .expect(). Nothing turns them into user-visible UI, and panic=abort without a hook loses the message.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:78-84 setup maps bootstrap Err to a Box error after tracing::error!; src-tauri/src/app/mod.rs:94-95 `.build(tauri::generate_context!()).expect("error while building the Bluey application")`; src-tauri/Cargo.toml:123-128 [profile.release] panic = "abort", strip = true; rg finds no std::panic::set_hook in src-tauri; Fallible bootstrap steps include Storage::open + run_migrations and SettingsManager::load (app/mod.rs:116-134) and hub.transition(BootCompleted)? (app/mod.rs:339); `tauri_plugin_dialog is already registered (app/mod.rs:67)`
- **Proposed solution:** In setup's error branch, before returning Err, show a blocking native dialog (app.dialog().message(...).kind(Error).blocking_show()) with the BlueyError message, the log path and a 'Reveal data folder' option. Replace .expect with a match that logs and exits(1). Install std::panic::set_hook early that writes the panic payload through tracing to the log file.
- **Test plan:** Unit: bootstrap with a Storage pointed at a read-only temp dir returns Err with a storage kind (no panic). Integration on real macOS: create a DB with a future user_version or chmod 000 the data dir, launch, and confirm the dialog appears and the log contains the error. Verify the panic hook writes to the log with a forced panic in a debug build.
- **Verification (adjusted):** Confirmed: app/mod.rs:78-95 setup Err leads to .build().expect(), Cargo.toml [profile.release] sets panic="abort", and rg finds no set_hook. Fallible steps: Storage::open (:127), SettingsManager::load (:129-133), and BootCompleted `?` (:339). Two corrections. (1) The message is not lost. tracing::error!("bootstrap failed") at :80 writes it to the log file, because Logging::init (:117-120) runs before storage and settings. Only an AppPaths::resolve failure (:116) happens before logging exists. (2) SIGABRT from a Finder-launched app normally brings up macOS's generic 'quit unexpectedly' crash reporter, so the user does get a dialog, but a useless one, not 'no dialog'. Needs real-macOS confirmation. Severity stays High: the event is rare, but a migration failure after an auto-installed update bricks the app with no guidance. The fix is fine: a blocking tauri_plugin_dialog message in the setup error branch, and replacing expect with a logged exit.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/bootstrap-failure-silent-crash)_

#### UX-036

**Recovery buttons (Reconnect, Restart helper, Open Settings) swallow their own failures**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E2: Recovery actions now go through one runner, runRecovery in src/lib/errors/present.ts, with setRecoveryFailureReporter. If a recovery action itself fails, the runner catches the error and shows it instead of dropping it. Toast, ErrorBanner and StatePill all use the runner.

- **User impact:** The user clicks 'Reconnect' or 'Restart helper' in an error toast, the toast disappears, and if the action fails nothing happens and no message appears. In the pill, a failing action also leaves the Error state in place.
- **Root cause:** Actions are typed as returning a Promise, but no caller handles rejection, and there is no global rejection handler.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/errors/present.ts:280-340 actions return raw IPC promises (permissions.openSettings, accounts.connect, dev.restartHelper, ...); src/components/ui/Toast.tsx:24-27 `dismiss(toast.id); void toast.action?.run();` dismisses the toast before running the action, with no catch; `src/components/ui/ErrorBanner.tsx:34 `onClick={() => void presented.action?.()}``; src/features/hud/StatePill.tsx:92-95 `await presented.action?.(); await recover();` so a throw skips recover and becomes an unhandled rejection; `rg: no 'unhandledrejection' listener in src/`; Realistic sync failures: accounts.connect -> begin_connect Err (loopback ports busy / browser_open_failed, accounts/chatgpt.rs:233-235,388-395); helper restart ensure_running Err (sidecar/mod.rs:376-384)
- **Proposed solution:** Wrap once where actions are created or run: in toast-store, make `run` = async () => { try { await action() } catch (e) { showErrorToast(toBlueyError(e)) } }, and reuse that runner in ErrorBanner and StatePill (try/finally around recover). Optionally add a dev-only unhandledrejection listener that logs through the dev log.
- **Test plan:** Vitest with the real toast-store and a mock transport that rejects accounts_connect: click the toast action and assert a new error toast with the connect error. StatePill: a rejecting action still calls app_recover. Prefer a boundary test through src/lib/tauri/api.ts rather than stubbing presentError.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Confirmed. toast-store.ts:105-108 passes presented.action as `run` unwrapped. Toast.tsx:24-27 dismisses first, then `void toast.action?.run()`. ErrorBanner.tsx:34 uses `void presented.action?.()`. In StatePill.tsx:92-95, an action throw skips recover(). recover() itself does catch (:23-28). present.ts:288-340 returns raw IPC promises. rg finds no unhandledrejection listener. dev_restart_helper is registered without a feature gate (lib.rs:200) and its restart error propagates (sidecar/mod.rs:375-383). Test: render Toast with an action that rejects and assert that an error toast is pushed.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/recovery-actions-drop-failures)_

#### UX-037

**Settings side effects (Smart observation, content protection, retention sweep, autostart) fail silently**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-F: settings/side_effects.rs: failures of shortcut re-registration, content protection, launch at login, Smart observation start/stop and the retention sweep go through report(). It publishes BlueyEvent::AppError whose recovery opens the settings tab that controls the setting (keybinds, privacy, general, screen); a permission error keeps its own OpenSystemSettings action. Only error codes are logged. platform::set_autostart now returns BlueyResult, with config.autostart_failed and present.ts copy; at launch it is still only logged (commit e9cd328). Not done: an observation-status indicator in ScreenTab (ScreenTab is not a file this workstream owns); the app.error toast covers the permission case. Device check: Toggle Launch at login and Smart observation with Screen Recording denied: an actionable toast should appear.

- **User impact:** The user selects Smart observation or turns off 'store screenshots'. The toggle saves and shows the new value, but observation never starts, or the old data is not deleted, and nothing tells them. Privacy expectations (deletion, content protection) can silently diverge from reality.
- **Root cause:** apply() runs after the settings write succeeded, and its failures have no return channel to the caller.
- **Evidence:** `src-tauri/src/settings/side_effects.rs:10-12 'Errors are logged, never propagated'`; side_effects.rs:53-58 `let _ = core.capture.observe_start(..)` for Smart observation (fails without Screen Recording permission or with the helper down); side_effects.rs:34-36 content-protection failure is warn only; :22-26 panel opacity/width `let _`; side_effects.rs:99-112 retention sweep Err -> tracing::warn only: the toggle shows off but stored screenshots/transcripts are not deleted; CaptureManager::is_observing (capture/mod.rs:378-380) is not exposed by any command (rg), so there is no observation status in the UI; the boot path app/mod.rs:393-400 is also warn only
- **Proposed solution:** Have apply() collect failures into Vec<BlueyError> and publish each as BlueyEvent::AppError, with recovery set to open_system_settings(screen) for observation permission errors and a retry for retention. Expose an observation status (observing flag) through capture_get_status so ScreenTab can show 'Observation paused: permission needed'.
- **Test plan:** Rust: apply() with a helper stub that rejects observe.start publishes an AppError with a capture kind. Retention: a storage stub that errors publishes an AppError. UI: ScreenTab renders the observation-status error from the real event payload.
- **Verification (confirmed):** side_effects.rs:10-12 documents the swallowing. :53-58 does `let _ = observe_start`, :33-36 only warns on a protection failure, :22-26 uses `let _` for opacity/width. Worse than stated for retention: apply_retention is called only here (rg: side_effects.rs:101) and is never retried at boot. A failed sweep after the user turns off storing screenshots/transcripts leaves that data stored indefinitely with no notice. CaptureManager::is_observing (capture/mod.rs:378) has no caller outside the module. The fix proposal is reasonable.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/settings-side-effects-silent)_

#### UX-038

**No React error boundary: any render exception blanks the HUD, Settings or Onboarding window with no recovery**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-E2: New ErrorBoundary in src/components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx. Each window root (HudWindow, SettingsWindow, OnboardingWindow) is wrapped in one, with a compact 'Something went wrong — Reload' fallback. Each chat turn and each Mermaid diagram also has its own boundary, so one bad render no longer blanks the whole window. A separate commit adds the override modifiers needed for noImplicitOverride.

- **User impact:** If any component throws during render, React 19 unmounts the whole root. The floating HUD panel becomes an empty transparent window, and showing or hiding the panel does not remount it, so the user must quit and relaunch. Settings or onboarding would go blank the same way.
- **Root cause:** There is no top-level or per-surface error boundary, and the startup promise is fire-and-forget.
- **Evidence:** rg 'ErrorBoundary|componentDidCatch|getDerivedStateFromError' src (excluding tests) finds 0 matches; src/main.tsx:17-21 mounts a single root `<React.StrictMode><Window /></React.StrictMode>` with no boundary; src/main.tsx:24 `void start();` means a rejected bootstrap()/dynamic import leaves an empty #root with no message; The HUD renders untrusted LLM output (markdown, Mermaid via src/features/hud/MermaidDiagram.tsx, etc.), so a render-time throw on unusual content is plausible
- **Proposed solution:** Add a small ErrorBoundary component (class with getDerivedStateFromError) that renders a compact 'Something went wrong. Reload' fallback calling location.reload() and logs through the dev log. Wrap <Window /> in main.tsx. Also wrap the response thread item and MermaidDiagram so one bad message does not take down the HUD. Catch start() rejections and render the same fallback.
- **Test plan:** Vitest + RTL: render ErrorBoundary around a component that throws and assert the fallback and reload button appear. Render ResponseThread with a message whose renderer throws (mocked) and assert the rest of the HUD toolbar is still in the DOM. Manual: inject a throw in dev mode and confirm the panel shows the fallback.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (error-handling-stubs/no-react-error-boundary)_

#### UX-039

**Global shortcuts that fail to register are never shown to the user**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-D2: A binding macOS fails to register is now shown to the user. apply_bindings records the reason on the binding (ShortcutBinding.registrationError, cleared once it registers) and pushes the bindings through settings.changed. The KeybindsTab row shows a 'Not active' badge with the reason, and the onboarding shortcut step says the same. Files: shortcuts/mod.rs, bluey-core types, KeybindsTab.tsx, setup.tsx, mock. Device check: Bind a chord another app already owns and confirm the badge appears.

- **User impact:** If an accelerator is unparsable or cannot be registered, the hotkey (for example 'ask about screen') does nothing. Settings shows it as bound and enabled, and nothing explains why pressing it has no effect.
- **Root cause:** Registration results are treated as diagnostics (DevLog) instead of part of the binding state exposed to the UI.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/shortcuts/mod.rs:81-123 apply_bindings: failures pushed to `failed`, published only as BlueyEvent::DevLog, stored in self.failed, returns Ok(()); shortcuts/mod.rs:166-187 check_conflict is the only reader of self.failed and runs only while a new binding is being recorded (KeybindsTab.tsx:121, onboarding setup.tsx:65) with ignoreId = the recorded id; `settings/side_effects.rs:14-17 and the boot path (app/mod.rs ~373) only tracing::warn!`
- **Proposed solution:** Add an optional `registrationError` to the ShortcutBinding status returned by a shortcuts_status command, or include failed ids in settings.changed. Render a warning badge on the row in KeybindsTab and in onboarding setup. On boot, publish one app.error toast with recovery open_settings(keybinds) if any enabled binding failed.
- **Test plan:** Rust: apply_bindings with an invalid accelerator leaves the failed id in the status. UI: KeybindsTab renders the warning for a binding flagged with registrationError (real event payload shape, not a mock-only type). Real macOS: bind a combo held exclusively by another app and confirm the badge appears.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The code path is confirmed. apply_bindings (shortcuts/mod.rs:81-123) sends failures only to DevLog and self.failed and returns Ok. check_conflict (:166-187) is the only reader. side_effects.rs:14-17 only warns. Downgraded because real frequency on macOS is low. The keybind recorder normalizes accelerators, so invalid-accelerator failures mostly come from hand-edited or legacy data. Carbon hotkey registration generally does not fail just because another app uses the same combination (unverified here; confirm with a runtime test registering a combo held by another app). The UX gap is real but rarely hit.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/shortcut-registration-failures-invisible)_

#### AI-016

**Web research failures are invisible: the answer arrives ungrounded with no notice**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Partially implemented** — WS-R: Research failures now return an outcome with no items and a short note (configuration / timeout / generic copy via researchNote). The engine stamps it as response.researchNote (new optional BlueyResponse field). Rendering the note in ResponseThread belongs to E2 (next wave), and the field is not persisted in the Rust response struct.

- **User impact:** For time-sensitive questions the user sees 'Researching…', then an answer with no citations and no sign that research failed (for example a missing EXA/Firecrawl key or a sidecar crash). They may trust stale model knowledge.
- **Root cause:** runResearch collapses every failure into `null`, the same value as 'research not needed'.
- **Evidence:** src/ai/engine.ts:222-224 `api.research.available().catch(() => ({search:false, scrape:false, deepAgent:false}))`; src/ai/research.ts:233-236 search catch -> return null; :279-280 deep 'failed' -> finish(null) (event.code/message dropped); :284-286 timeout -> null; :295 deepStart reject -> null; `src/stores/researchStore.ts:74-76 'failed' just clears the activity`; The sidecar produces typed failures (missing_api_key, agent_empty_report, sanitized errors) at sidecars/agent/src/agent.ts:758-806
- **Proposed solution:** Return a discriminated result ({ok:false, reason: BlueyError}) and stamp response.meta.researchNote. Render a subtle line in ResponseThread ('Web research unavailable: EXA key missing') with an open_settings recovery for configuration errors.
- **Test plan:** Engine unit test with api.research.search rejecting: the response carries researchNote with the error code. Deep path: emit research.event failed with code missing_api_key and assert the note. UI test renders the note.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Confirmed: research.ts:233-236 (search catch returns null), :282-283 (failed returns finish(null)), :286-289 (timeout), :296 (deepStart catch), and engine.ts:222-224 (availability catch). A small correction to the example: a missing EXA key usually makes available() report search:false, so decideResearch picks no research and no call is attempted. The silent case is really a runtime search, scrape or agent failure, or a timeout. Low is right.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/research-failures-silent)_

#### UX-040

**Clerk session restore signs the user out on a transient network error during the refresh fallback**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-A: Restore signs out only on an authentication rejection (restore_signs_out). Network errors keep the stored sign-in. Fix pass: a locked or unprobeable item also keeps the cached user, with no read.

- **User impact:** Launching with a stale access token while the network is flaky signs the user out, and revokes the session, when a retry would have worked. Rotated tokens can also be lost, forcing a later sign-in.
- **Root cause:** Wildcard `Err(_)` arms on the refresh-fallback path.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/auth/mod.rs:548-564 after fetch_user returns Authentication: refresh `Err(_) => clear_session()` and post-refresh fetch_user `Err(_) => clear_session()` for ANY error kind; `Contrast auth/mod.rs:530-538, which defers on non-Authentication errors ('offline?')`; auth/mod.rs:553 `let _ = self.store(fresh, &user).await;` rotated tokens are not persisted on failure
- **Proposed solution:** Match `Err(e) if e.kind == Authentication => clear_session()`, otherwise defer (debug log, keep tokens). Log or surface store() failures as in the token-rotation finding.
- **Test plan:** Auth unit tests with a mocked Clerk: fetch_user 401 then refresh network error keeps the session. Refresh 400 invalid_grant signs out.
- **Verification (confirmed):** auth/mod.rs:548-564 has wildcard Err(_) arms that clear the session on both the refresh and the post-refresh fetch_user, unlike :530-538, which defers on non-Authentication errors. It needs a 401 followed by a transient network failure within the same restore, which is a narrow window. :553 uses `let _ = self.store(...)`. Low is correct.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/clerk-restore-signout-on-transient)_

#### DEBT-014

**Cluster of low-impact swallowed or console-only errors and dead stubs (about 30 sites)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Low-severity backlog: not scheduled in this cycle and unchanged on the integrated branch; the proposed solution above still applies.

- **User impact:** Each site on its own is rare or low-stakes (storage/IPC failures in a local app). Together they mean missing feedback in Settings and history, and debugging relies on devtools.
- **Root cause:** No shared 'invoke with toast' helper; each call site handles errors ad hoc.
- **Evidence:** console.warn-only store loads (10): appStore.ts:20, modesStore.ts:21, sessionStore.ts:36, permissionsStore.ts:21/28/35, panelStore.ts:49/79, updatesStore.ts:36, auth-store.ts:64; settingsStore.ts:9,29-31,45-50 lastError is set but never read outside the store (rg); every settings tab returns null when settings is null (e.g. PrivacyTab.tsx:72), so a load failure shows a blank tab with no retry; reset() failure shows no toast; Fire-and-forget IPC without catch (7): ModeEditor.tsx:180 (duplicate), :185 (setDefault), :191 (resetBuiltIn), :357 (setActive); GeneralTab.tsx:86; onboarding/steps/setup.tsx:28; ModesTab.tsx:77-83 console.warn only; AdvancedTab restart helper `.then(showToast)` with no catch; src/ai/engine.ts:566-584 responses.save and session.addEvent failures are swallowed (history lost silently; later feedback then fails with console.warn at ResponseActions.tsx:81); src/stores/engine.ts:1-30 'unavailable stub' plus a stale 'until it lands' comment: createResponseEngine never throws, so this is dead code; sessions/mod.rs: 7 `let _ =` timeline add_event_internal sites (non-critical timeline entries); Swift HelperApp.swift:192,216,227,256,263 `try? raw?.decode(...)` falls back to default params on contract drift (defaults are safe, e.g. excludeSelf=true, but the drift is silent); PrivacyTab.tsx:74-81 setDisplayMode toggles content protection even when update() returned null (not persisted); permissions/mod.rs:67 WirePermissions decode unwrap_or_default (helper drift -> default statuses)
- **Proposed solution:** Add a small `runCommand(fn, domain)` helper that awaits, catches, and calls showErrorToast(toBlueyError(e, domain)), and use it at the fire-and-forget sites. Render settingsStore.lastError with a Retry button in the Settings shell. Delete the engine stub. Make the Swift param decode errors respond with invalid_params instead of defaulting.
- **Test plan:** Vitest per store: a rejected load shows a toast or banner. The Settings shell with lastError set renders Retry, which calls settings_get. Swift unit test: capture.display with malformed params returns invalid_params.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Spot-checked the cluster. stores/engine.ts:1-45 is the stub with a stale 'until it lands' comment; it is a defensive fallback, reachable only if createResponseEngine throws. settingsStore lastError is set at :30,:40 and only reset in initStores.ts:87, never rendered. PrivacyTab.tsx:74-81 calls setProtection after an update() whose result is ignored. The backend side_effects also toggle protection on a successful update, so there is a double apply. Did not verify all ~30 sites individually. Low is appropriate.
- _Source: auditor (error-handling-stubs/swallowed-errors-debt-cluster)_

#### UX-041

**Helper auto-restart first shows a 'native helper is not running' error toast**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-D1: src/stores/errorSurface.ts shows an info toast ('restarting the helper') while the supervisor auto-restarts the helper, instead of the 'native helper is not running' error. Copy is in src/lib/errors/present.ts.

- **User impact:** Every crash that is auto-recovering shows an alarming error toast with a Restart action. If the user clicks it during the backoff window, a manual restart races the scheduled one. The spawn_lock plus the is_running check prevents a double spawn, but the messaging is contradictory.
- **Root cause:** The branch order in errorSurface does not treat restarted=true with running=false as 'restarting'.
- **Evidence:** src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs:266 `self.publish_status(false, None, None, true)` before the delayed respawn; src/stores/errorSurface.ts:25-27 checks `else if (!status.running) showErrorToast(HELPER_STOPPED_ERROR)` before `else if (status.restarted)`, so the restarted=true, running=false event shows an error with a Restart helper action
- **Proposed solution:** In errorSurface, handle `status.restarted && !status.running` as an info toast ('Helper restarting…') before the !running branch.
- **Test plan:** errorSurface unit test: emit helper.status {running:false, restarted:true}, assert an info toast rather than an error toast; then emit {running:true, restarted:true} and assert 'Helper restarted'.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (error-handling-stubs/helper-autorestart-shows-stopped-error)_

### Testing quality & verification debt

#### DEBT-001

**Stable (Latest) releases were manually uploaded developer builds, bypassing the gated publish pipeline, and they feed default auto-update**  
Severity **High** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Owner action. The Latest channel was fed by hand-uploaded ad-hoc builds. G (TEST-006) now publishes nightly only after ci.yml succeeded, and release.sh keeps its publish gates; blocking manual `gh release` uploads needs the owner to publish only through the gated pipeline, and the root fix is Developer ID signing (ADR 0011).

- **Root cause:** Without Apple credentials, the only way to ship was a manual upload of developer-path artifacts. That skipped the arch check, the pair re-verification and the provenance record the pipeline was built to enforce, and it put those artifacts on the default auto-update feed.
- **Evidence:** gh release view v0.1.2: body says 'not code-signed and not notarized … the signed pipeline (.github/workflows/release.yml) could not run. This release was published manually by the repository owner's decision, against the default policy in docs/RELEASING.md'; v0.1.2 assets include latest.json and Bluey_0.1.2_{aarch64,x64}.app.tar.gz(.sig), so the Latest feed (default channel, settings.rs:433) serves it with auto-install on (UPDATES.md:3-5); release.yml:108-152 publish path (verify_macos.py, publish_release.py re-verification) runs only when publish=='true' with Apple secrets; release.sh:122-127 skips verify_macos.py otherwise; `gh api repos/bloxy-studios/bluey/branches/main → protected=false`
- **Proposed solution:** Add a documented 'unsigned-publish' mode to release.yml that still runs verify_macos.py's arch/pair/feed checks (without the notarization parts) and records the commit and run id. Add app-crate cargo test and test-helper.sh to release.sh. Stop hand-uploading latest.json.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (testing-verification-debt/stable-feed-manually-published-dev-builds)_

#### TEST-006

**Nightly auto-publishes any main commit, whatever its CI status, without app-crate tests, Swift tests, arch checks or a launch smoke, and users auto-install it**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-G: nightly.py plan skips a commit unless its latest ci.yml push run on main concluded success. This applies even when the nightly is forced. The plan job gets actions:read. A manual ad-hoc stable upload is still possible outside the workflow; release.sh's existing plan validation is the only script-level guard. Device check: Needs a real Actions run to confirm the actions:read API call works.

- **User impact:** A commit that fails the macOS app-crate tests, crashes at launch, or bundles a wrong-arch sidecar can reach every Nightly-channel user overnight and install itself. Rollback needs a new nightly.
- **Root cause:** The nightly workflow reuses the developer-build path of release.sh, which by design skips verify_macos.py, and nothing ties it to CI results or branch protection.
- **Evidence:** gh api repos/bloxy-studios/bluey/branches/main: protected=false, required_status_checks contexts=[] enforcement_level=off; nightly.yml:27-48 plan job only runs release unit tests and `nightly.py plan` (compares main with the last nightly commit); nightly.yml:96 build = `bash scripts/release.sh`; :112 publish `if: success() && needs.plan.outputs.build == 'true'`; scripts/release.sh:85-87 runs only lint, `bun run test`, `scripts/check-rust.sh` (host crates; no `cd src-tauri && cargo test`, no scripts/test-helper.sh); scripts/release.sh:113-116 checks only that dmg/.app.tar.gz/.sig exist; verify_macos.py (arch check :92-97) runs only `if [[ "$PUBLISH_RELEASE" == true ]]` (release.sh:121-127); `docs/UPDATES.md:4-5 automatic install by default; nightly feed URL UPDATES.md:13`
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Protect main with required checks (frontend, rust, macOS). (2) In nightly.yml plan, query the check-runs for the candidate commit and skip unless ci.yml concluded success. (3) Add to release.sh for every build: `cd src-tauri && cargo test --features dev-tools --locked`, `bash scripts/test-helper.sh`, and a lipo/`file` arch check of Contents/MacOS/{bluey,bluey-helper,bluey-agent} (reuse verify_macos.py's arch helper without the notarization parts). (4) Add a headless sidecar smoke: pipe helper.ping and helper.version into the bundled helper, and send an unknown-method frame to the bundled agent.
- **Test plan:** Python unittest in scripts/release/tests: nightly plan returns build=false when the commit's CI conclusion is not success (mock gh API). Add a test_shell assertion that release.sh invokes the arch check on the developer path. In CI, push a branch with a deliberately failing app-crate test and confirm a workflow_dispatch nightly refuses to build it.
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mechanism is confirmed. `gh api …/branches/main` returns protected=false. nightly.yml plan (27-48) never checks CI status. release.sh:83-87 runs typecheck, lint, `bun run test` and check-rust.sh, and check-rust.sh:19 tests only the host crates, so there is no `cargo test` of the app crate and no test-helper.sh. verify_macos.py runs only when PUBLISH_RELEASE=true (release.sh:122). Severity drops because Nightly is opt-in: UpdateChannel::Latest is the default (settings.rs:433; UPDATES.md table). The bigger gap is the manually published stable releases (see missed).
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/nightly-ships-without-gates)_

#### TEST-007

**The helper/agent binaries are built but never executed in CI, and the 430-line helper supervisor has no tests**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — A compiled-sidecar smoke in CI is a follow-up (G added the Swift unit tests to the macOS job).

- **User impact:** Regressions in stdio framing, Bun-compiled stdin handling, the handshake or restart logic ship silently. A crash loop or a 'helper did not report ready' state shows up only on user machines.
- **Root cause:** Tests stop at pure codecs (bluey-protocols) and in-process sidecar logic. No fake-process harness exists for the Rust supervisor, and no CI step pipes a request into the real binaries.
- **Evidence:** ci.yml:89-91 `build-helper.sh host` and `build-agent.sh host`, with no step that runs either binary; src-tauri/src/sidecar/mod.rs: 0 #[test]; covers env_clear spawn (:98-108), 5 s helper.ready wait + helper.version handshake (:119-150), crash restart with backoff and a max of 5 attempts (:246-280), per-method timeouts (:400-410); tests/sidecar/sidecar.test.ts:1-12 drives startSidecar in-process with PassThrough streams under Node, not the `bun build --compile` output; tests/native/README.md: the jsonl harness is manual; helper.ping/helper.version need no TCC grant; PR #15: 'Not verified here … the cleared-environment spawn against the real helper and agent binaries'
- **Proposed solution:** (1) CI step on macOS after the builds: `printf '{"id":"1","method":"helper.ping"}\n{"id":"2","method":"helper.version"}\n' | src-tauri/binaries/bluey-helper-<triple>`, then assert the helper.ready line, pong, protocol 1 and arch. Send the agent an unknown-method frame and assert the `unknown_method` envelope. (2) Refactor HelperManager spawn to accept a command factory, then add tokio tests with a scripted fake helper (a shell script) covering the ready timeout, an incompatible protocol, a crash then restart with backoff and the 5-attempt cap, and request timeouts per method.
- **Test plan:** The new CI step fails if the binary crashes or answers wrong. The Rust tests prove the restart counter resets after 60 s healthy, stops after 5 failures, publishes helper.status with an error, and times out capture.* at 3 s (paused tokio time).
- **Verification (confirmed):** sidecar/mod.rs is 430 lines with 0 #[test]/#[tokio::test] (rg -c found none). ci.yml:88-91 builds the helper and the agent (lite) and never runs them. Sidecar TS tests are in-process. This is a coverage gap, not a known bug: the helper exits on stdin EOF (JSONLinesIO.swift:6-7,57-61), so the lifecycle has a sane fallback.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/no-compiled-sidecar-smoke-or-supervisor-tests)_

#### TEST-008

**The Swift helper unit tests are listed as an automated layer but no workflow runs them**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented — needs real-device verification** — WS-G: The macOS job in ci.yml now runs scripts/test-helper.sh after building the helper, and the docs/ci/workflows/ci.yml copy is mirrored. Device check: Needs a GitHub macOS runner to execute the swift tests.

- **User impact:** Envelope, VAD, dHash change detection and OCR ordering can regress unnoticed. VAD and dHash control what gets transcribed and when screens count as changed.
- **Root cause:** The macOS CI job was set up to build the helper, not test it.
- **Evidence:** docs/TESTING.md:23 'Swift helper | swift test (macOS) | bash scripts/test-helper.sh | dHash/VAD/envelope/ordering logic' in the Automated table; `rg over .github/workflows: no test-helper.sh or `swift test`; ci.yml:89 only builds`; git log: Tests last changed cd997e2 (2026-09-07); Sources changed d08b65a (2026-09-11, PR #34 'helper change is not compiled here')
- **Proposed solution:** Add `- run: bash scripts/test-helper.sh` to the macOS job (and to release.sh). Mark TESTING.md accordingly.
- **Test plan:** CI shows XCTest results for 6 suites. Break a DHash assertion locally and confirm CI goes red.
- **Verification (confirmed):** test-helper.sh (`swift test`) appears only in scripts/test-helper.sh:14. No workflow and not release.sh (lines 83-87) calls it. The helper's Sources changed after Tests last changed (d08b65a on 2026-09-11 vs cd997e2 on 2026-09-07), so the tests may no longer compile, and nobody would notice.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/swift-tests-never-run)_

#### TEST-009

**`tauri dev` silently runs stale helper/agent binaries; on this Mac they predate the PR #34 helper and PR #8 agent changes**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Implemented** — WS-G: build-helper.sh and build-agent.sh write <binary>.stamp with a hash of the sidecar's sources (new scripts/sidecar-stamp.sh). ensure-sidecars.sh rebuilds any sidecar that is missing, has no stamp, or whose stamp no longer matches.

- **User impact:** The owner's manual QA in `tauri dev` (research with the Gemini backend, OCR of every ⌘↵ frame) exercises old sidecar code. Results are invalid or misleading, and bugs get misattributed.
- **Root cause:** The existence check has no freshness input, and sidecars carry no build identity the app could compare.
- **Evidence:** ls src-tauri/binaries: bluey-agent-x86_64-apple-darwin (282,899,104 B = full variant) and bluey-helper-x86_64-apple-darwin, both dated Sep 7 15:01; git log --since '2026-09-07 15:01': helper d08b65a (2026-09-11 OCR inline image), agent 562f036 (Gemini backend / RESEARCH_BACKEND), a021f86; scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh:18-28 rebuilds only if `! -x` (missing); tauri.conf.json:7 beforeDevCommand uses it; HelperApp.swift:7-8 version "0.1.0" and protocol 1 are constants, never bumped; the agent has no version handshake (agent/mod.rs); src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:7 beforeDevCommand `bash scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh && bun run dev`; `scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh:19-28 only checks `-x` existence, never freshness`; src-tauri/binaries/bluey-agent-x86_64-apple-darwin built Sep 7 15:01 (282 MB, full); sidecar commits since: 562f036 (Gemini backend, Sep 8), a021f86 (Sep 9); ``LC_ALL=C grep -c RESEARCH_BACKEND` on the dev binary = 0, installed app binary = 2`; scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh: `if [[ ! -x "$OUT/${bin}-${TRIPLE}" ]]` builds only when missing; The helper handshake checks only the protocol major (bluey-protocols/src/helper.rs:255-260); the agent has no check; src-tauri/tauri.conf.json:7 beforeDevCommand 'bash scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh && bun run dev'; `scripts/ensure-sidecars.sh:18-28 — checks only [[ -x ]] existence`; src-tauri/binaries/bluey-agent-x86_64-apple-darwin (282,899,104 B, Sep 7 15:01) and bluey-helper-x86_64-apple-darwin (Sep 7 14:59) predate 562f036 (2026-09-08 Gemini research backend) and d08b65a (2026-09-11 'OCR every ⌘↵ frame'); `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md:22 'builds missing sidecars on first run'`
- **Proposed solution:** Write a stamp file (hash of Sources/** + Package.swift, and of sidecars/agent/src/** + lockfile) next to each binary in build-helper.sh / build-agent.sh. Have ensure-sidecars.sh rebuild when the stamp mismatches. Bake the git SHA into the helper version (and add an agent `sidecar.version` method), and log a warning in debug builds when it differs from the app's build SHA.
- **Test plan:** Shell test: touch a Swift source and expect ensure-sidecars to rebuild. Manual: the tauri dev log shows helper version with a SHA equal to HEAD.
- **Verification (confirmed):** src-tauri/binaries holds the helper (Sep 7 14:59) and the agent (Sep 7 15:01, 282,899,104 B). After those dates came d08b65a (2026-09-11), a021f86 and 562f036, which touched helper and agent sources. ensure-sidecars.sh:18-28 rebuilds only when `! -x`, and it is wired into beforeDevCommand (tauri.conf.json:7). So manual QA of PR #34 or the Gemini agent under `tauri dev` on this Mac would exercise old binaries. This affects dev and QA only, not shipped builds.
- **Also reported as:** research-sidecar/stale-dev-sidecar, native-macos-platform/dev-sidecars-stale, prior-audit-docs-drift/stale-dev-sidecars
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/stale-dev-sidecars)_

#### TEST-010

**All 32 UI suites run against a 2,226-line MockTransport that differs from the Rust handlers; the real TauriTransport has no test**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — A TauriTransport contract test with @tauri-apps/api mocks is a follow-up.

- **User impact:** UI flows can pass in CI and fail against Rust: settings patches, secret gating, provider-key badges, account flows, updater states. PR #18 is the precedent: every real event failed while mock tests stayed green.
- **Root cause:** The mock is a second, hand-maintained implementation of the backend, and no contract test binds the two.
- **Evidence:** src/lib/tauri/tauri-transport.ts:29-50: the only real invoke/listen/Channel code; no test imports it and no mockIPC exists in the repo; mock-transport.ts:2213-2226 mergeSettings is a one-level spread vs Rust recursive merge_json + validate + shortcut reconcile (bluey-core types/settings.rs:478-505; settings/mod.rs:57-67,200-207); mock-transport.ts:2002-2021 secrets_set accepts any key and delete doesn't clear hasApiKey; Rust commands/settings.rs:38-50 validate_webview_key rejects non-allowlisted keys; mock-transport.ts:846-862 dev 'question' simulation emits a pre-built question.detected; production detection is only engine.classify (src/ai/engine.ts:818). PR #45: 'streaming is exercised through the mock transport's dev simulation and the fake engine'; docs/UPDATES.md:63 the updater mock 'always finds 0.2.0'; PR #27: Accounts section 'exercised through the MockTransport only'
- **Proposed solution:** (1) Add tests/ui/tauri-transport.test.ts using @tauri-apps/api/mocks mockIPC. Assert invoke is called with the exact command and args, `listen` subscribes to `bluey:app/state` style names, Channel messages reach onMessage, and errors become BlueyError. (2) Move shared JSON fixtures (settings patch → expected Settings, AiChunk sequence, BlueyError, event payloads) into tests/fixtures/contract/ and consume them from vitest (mock) and cargo test (bluey-core apply_patch/serde). The pattern already exists with native-hud-menu.json. (3) Make mock mergeSettings recursive and mirror validate_webview_key.
- **Test plan:** The contract fixture test fails if either side changes merge semantics or casing. Ensure the mockIPC test would have caught PR #18 (dotted names).
- **Verification (adjusted):** The mock is at src/lib/tauri/mock/mock-transport.ts (2,226 lines), not the path the auditor cited. mergeSettings (2213-2225) is a one-level spread, and secrets_delete (2019-2021) doesn't clear hasApiKey. Both confirmed. The claim that the real TauriTransport has no test is refuted: tests/ui/native-hud-menu.test.ts:6,16-17,50,115 instantiates TauriTransport with mocked invoke/listen/Channel and asserts `invoke` is called exactly with ("hud_menu_popup", {request})`. What stays untested is listen/Channel/error mapping and contract parity for the other commands.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/mock-only-ui-suite-untested-tauri-transport)_

#### TEST-011

**Subscription-provider 'no drift' goldens compare Bluey against fixtures written from the same docs; no real capture exists**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort L · independent · needs real macOS · status **Deferred** — Needs real provider captures with the owner's accounts (fingerprints:capture) — owner action.

- **User impact:** Connecting ChatGPT, Claude or Google AI subscriptions may fail on day one (400/403, redirect rejection) or trip provider abuse detection, while the tests stay green.
- **Root cause:** The tests are self-consistency tests; the real-capture step of the runbook (PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:339-372) was never done.
- **Evidence:** tests/fixtures/fingerprints/{claude,chatgpt,antigravity}/ contain only `documented/`; no `captures/` anywhere; PR #28: 'the proxy was exercised against a fake upstream only'; PRs #29/#30/#31: 'Nothing in this PR has talked to auth.openai.com / claude.ai / accounts.google.com'; docs/PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md:410-420 open questions 1-3 'Still open until the … capture' (cch algorithm, scopes, UA, host, tier ids); PR #25: claude-code #93216 redirect-URI rejection is a 'possible day-one login blocker'; Antigravity ToS: third-party OAuth use can lead to account suspension
- **Proposed solution:** Run `fingerprints:capture` plus `bless` per provider with the owner's accounts and commit the scrubbed captures. Add a unit test that fails when a provider has no blessed capture or the capture is older than N days, and show those providers as 'Experimental' in the Accounts UI until then.
- **Test plan:** `bun run fingerprints:diff <provider>` against real captures reports only VERSION findings. accounts_probe_fingerprint succeeds per provider. Manual QA rows 7-9.
- **Verification (confirmed):** tests/fixtures/fingerprints/{antigravity,chatgpt,claude}/ contain only `documented/`, and no captures dir exists. PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md open questions 1-3 are still 'open until the … capture' as of 2026-09-11, including the claude-code #93216 redirect-URI 'possible day-one login blocker'. Real impact depends on these subscription sign-ins being exposed to users.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/subscription-goldens-circular)_

#### TEST-012

**No opt-in live/contract test tier; every real-provider and real-OS check exists only as prose**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence verified · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — An opt-in live contract tier exists for prompts (BLUEY_PROMPT_EVAL_LIVE); provider/OS contract tests are a follow-up.

- **User impact:** Provider API changes (Gemini 3.x fields, Live binary frames, Files API) are found by users. Nothing can be re-run cheaply after a dependency bump.
- **Root cause:** The team verifies by checklist in PR bodies without an executable harness for keys available on the owner's Mac.

- **Proposed solution:** Add Rust `#[ignore]` tests (`cargo test -- --ignored`, requires GEMINI_API_KEY): 1-token generateContent, streamGenerateContent, embeddings dimension, a Live handshake with a 3 s PCM fixture, and Files API upload+delete. Add `bun run smoke:live` wrapping them, and an optional manual CI workflow using repository secrets.
- **Test plan:** The live suite passes against the real endpoints on the owner's Mac. Break the model id and confirm it maps to config.model_not_found.
- **Verification (confirmed):** No #[ignore] exists in src-tauri, and there are no skipIf/runIf or env-gated tests in tests/ or src/. LATENCY.md:107 says 'Not yet measured.' This is process debt: real Gemini/Live/Files contracts are only checked by hand.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/no-live-contract-tier)_

#### DEBT-008

**The 374-line updater state machine (check/install/relaunch/channel switch) has zero tests**  
Severity **Medium** · confidence likely · effort M · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Updater state machine tests: follow-up (D2 changed relaunch to run shutdown first; the updater logic itself is unchanged).

- **Root cause:** The updater is tied to AppHandle and cfg!, with no injectable seam, so neither phase transitions nor channel-switch behavior are covered. That is on the path every user takes when auto-install is on by default.
- **Evidence:** rg -c '#[test]|#[tokio::test]' src-tauri/src/updates/ → no matches; wc -l src-tauri/src/updates/mod.rs = 374; updates/mod.rs:96-98 supported() keyed on !cfg!(debug_assertions); :311-320 relaunch phase guard; :203-216 per-channel feed query; UI side is tested only against the MockTransport updater that 'always finds 0.2.0' (docs/UPDATES.md:63)
- **Proposed solution:** Extract the phase/transition logic into a pure struct (or put the feed query behind a trait). Unit-test Idle→Available→Downloading→Ready, the relaunch guard, the channel-change reset, and the automatic-install trigger. Inject is_release instead of cfg!.
- **Verification (confirmed):** Raised by the independent verifier.
- _Source: verifier (missed by auditor) (testing-verification-debt/updates-module-untested)_

#### TEST-017

**No x86_64 artifact is ever executed; Intel builds are cross-compiled on arm64 runners and shipped untested (the owner's Mac is Intel)**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · has dependencies · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — CI has no Intel runner smoke; this audit ran the helper and app tests on an Intel Mac locally (x86_64).

- **User impact:** Intel-only regressions reach Intel users with no signal: Bun x64 runtime issues, Swift x86_64 codegen or availability paths, Speech on-device capability differences, slower first capture against the 3 s timeout.
- **Root cause:** The CI matrix is arm64-only and nothing smoke-tests the x64 slice.
- **Evidence:** ci.yml:74 runs-on macos-14 (arm64); release.yml:59,68-71 and nightly.yml:54,62-65 build x86_64-apple-darwin on macos-14; build-helper.sh / build-agent.sh:127,136 cross-build bun-darwin-x64 and swift --arch x86_64 without running them; docs/RELEASING.md:324-327 'Both signed apps install/launch … on matching Apple silicon and Intel Macs … Cross-architecture signature checking on one runner is not a launch test'; `Installed /Applications/Bluey.app is thin x86_64 (lipo -info)`
- **Proposed solution:** Add the sidecar smoke from 'no-compiled-sidecar-smoke' for the x86_64 binaries on a macos-13 (Intel) runner, or under `arch -x86_64` on macos-14 with Rosetta installed. Add an x86_64 `cargo test` job for the app crate at least weekly.
- **Test plan:** The CI job prints `arch: x86_64` from helper.version and the agent answers a protocol frame. The app-crate tests pass on the Intel runner.
- **Depends on:** no-compiled-sidecar-smoke-or-supervisor-tests
- **Verification (adjusted):** True for CI: every macOS job is macos-14 (arm64), and x86_64 is only cross-built. But the claim that no x86_64 artifact is ever executed is refuted in practice. This host is an Intel i5-7300U on macOS 26.5.1 (uname -m = x86_64), and `lipo -info` shows the installed /Applications/Bluey.app is thin x86_64. The owner runs the x64 build daily, while arm64 artifacts get no launch testing on the owner's side. A CI smoke on either arch is still worthwhile.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/x86_64-never-executed)_

#### DOC-009

**The docs promise a 'Restart Bluey' offer after a denied→granted Screen Recording change, but it isn't implemented**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · needs real macOS · status **Implemented** — WS-FX6: The fix is in docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md; no code was changed. Removed the 'Restart Bluey' offer, which does not exist. The doc now describes what is built: macOS's own Quit & Reopen, the onboarding wizard resuming at its step (ONB-004), and live refresh on focus and every 2 s on an open permissions screen. Permission-flow step 2 now lists the real refresh triggers (launch, each request, every 30 s while listening, plus useLivePermissions) and says a revocation does not stop a running session by itself. Also fixed the speech-locale bullet: Apple's servers plus a notice, not a cloud-provider fallback. The post-update repair card text (MAC-001) was already accurate.

- **User impact:** During onboarding the user grants Screen Recording and capture may keep failing (the per-process TCC cache in the helper) with no guidance. First-run experience breaks.
- **Root cause:** A documented behavior was never built, and no test checks for it.
- **Evidence:** docs/MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 'Screen Recording changes take effect only after the app restarts in some macOS versions; Bluey detects denied → granted transitions and offers *Restart Bluey*.'; rg 'Restart Bluey|relaunch' in src: only updates (describe.ts:74, :91) and AuthGate.tsx:52; permissions/mod.rs:145 only diff-publishes snapshots; `src/features/settings/tabs/AdvancedTab.tsx:78-83 only 'Restart helper' in Advanced`
- **Proposed solution:** On a permissions.changed denied→granted transition for screenRecording, restart the helper automatically (it owns the TCC check). If the next capture still fails, show a 'Restart Bluey' action (app.request_restart). Otherwise correct the doc.
- **Test plan:** UI test: emitting permissions.changed denied→granted shows the offer / calls dev.restartHelper. Manual QA row 'permission denial then later approval' on macOS 14, 15 and 26.
- **Verification (confirmed):** MACOS_PERMISSIONS.md:44-45 promises a 'Restart Bluey' offer. rg over src finds no restart UI outside updates, the AuthGate text and the 'Restart helper' row (AdvancedTab.tsx:78). The TCC check runs in the helper process (PermissionService.swift:58-60 CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess), and the helper can be restarted from Advanced, which partly mitigates this. It is mostly doc drift.
- _Source: auditor (testing-verification-debt/permission-restart-offer-doc-only)_

#### TEST-018

**Every Rust test runs debug + dev-tools, so release-only branches (updater, deep-link sign-in, dotenv paths) never execute in any test**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Release-only branches: A added release=true coverage for dotenv/OAuth config paths it touched; updater release paths remain.

- **User impact:** Sign-in redirect selection and updater enablement in shipped builds are checked only by shipping.

**CI tests with Bun 'latest' and unpinned Rust stable while shipped builds pin Bun 1.4.2**  
Severity **Low** · confidence verified · effort S · independent · provable off-device · status **Deferred** — Toolchain pinning in CI: follow-up.
